That last check counts only domains that are live and newly opened, and was confirmed to flag the Aeon Dial.

## Mastery previews outside developer mode (v374)
Simon: "make it so that when not in dev mode you can see the preview but only of items and masteries you already have". `_backup/pvown_b.js` + `_backup/p_pvown.py`, on top of v373.
- `smPvCan(kind,k,L)`: always true in developer mode. Otherwise a scroll must be owned (`meta.spells[k]`) and a building unlocked (`unlocked(k)`), and `L` must be at most the mastery already reached (`smLv` / `mastLv`); `L=0` is the picture, the item with no masteries.
- The v367/v368 gates are patched textually: `smPvShow` (scroll and building) asks `smPvCan` instead of `meta.dev`; the `smOpen`/`mastOpen` wrappers open the card's previews when the item is had; `smPvRows` hooks only reached rows (the others get a "Reach Mastery N to watch it" title); `smPvHint` words the hint for the player or the developer. The "no preview" text no longer mentions developer mode.
- The preview frame is the same no-save copy of the game as before; it is built only when a preview is opened and dropped when the card closes.
- Tests: `tests/pvown-test.js` (13, in run-all). The old "no previews without developer mode" rows of `smprev-test.js` and `bmprev-test.js` now check the new rule.

## Steady mastery power checks (tests only, 2026-10-25, on v374)
On a fresh lab account the power-ladder rows of `smast-test.js` (Commons) and `smast3-test.js` (Rares) swung from run to run, on v373 and v374 alike: Murder of Crows read 1.59-1.85 at Mastery 3 (target 1.35), Piercing Verdict 0.81-1.46. The game was fine. The tests' way of measuring had two random parts:
- **The crowd moved.** `near()` finds the road spot nearest the hero with a probe soldier, but `spawnEnemy` gives every foe a random sideways step (`e.off`, up to about 0.3). The nearest spot came out anywhere from road distance 29.4 to 30.4, so the whole test crowd stood up to a tile apart between runs. Scrolls that aim a line or reach a set distance (Verdict, Riftfang, the Beam) hit a different number of foes each run. The probe now walks the middle of the road (`e0.off=0`).
- **The hero's aura.** The lab hero is the Monarch: a foe slain within 3 tiles of him has a 15% chance (`rnd`, which is the unseeded `Math.random`) to burst for 1.5x his damage (`kill` calls `auraBoom`). The churn crowd dies right beside him, so the explosions and their chains added 0 to 560 damage a cast at random. Each kill also gives him XP, so within one suite a level-1 Monarch climbed to about level 13 and his later explosions hit harder. That was the state leaking from earlier parts of a suite. Inside a measure (`power`) his explosion is now off (`AURA[id].boom=0`, put back in a `finally`), and his record (level, XP) goes back afterwards.
- After the fix, over 3 standalone runs and 2 inside run-all, Crows read 1.34-1.37 / 1.72-1.76 and Verdict 1.17-1.18 / 1.71 (targets 1.35 / 1.74). What still moves is each scroll's own randomness: Hexspark picks a random target, Lightning Cloud strikes at random, Verdict re-aims at random while it charges. One scroll moved by up to about 15% (Cinders at Mastery 2: 1.18-1.35; Hexspark at Mastery 5: 1.82-2.07), still inside the 25% / 35% margins.
- The helpers changed in the `smast2-test.js` template, and `smast3`-`smast6` were regenerated from it. The arguments: `rare tests/smast3-test.js v370 riftfang riftfang ""`, `epic tests/smast4-test.js v371 cataclysm judgment loop`, `legendary tests/smast5-test.js v372 skyfall harvest,dslash stormcaller`, `mythic tests/smast6-test.js v373 shatter "" ""`. smast3 also picked up the generator's newer skip of casts at an empty field. `smast-cal.js` and `smast-refine.js` now measure the same way, so a recalibration matches the tests.
- Gotcha for any test that kills foes next to the hero: unless the test puts the hero record back, it levels the equipped hero in the lab account (`grantXp` saves on level-up). A fresh lab account has every hero at level 1. A separate hazard: sessions serving the game on the same origin (port) share one localStorage account, so each session should test on its own port.
- **Simon's own designs (`SM.free`):** the Commons ladder row exempts a scroll whose module sets `SM.free[k]` (Thorn Whip v378, Ember Bolt v384): at Mastery 2 and Mastery 5 it only has to reach 3/4 of the target, with no upper bound.

## Thunderclap's masteries, as Simon designed them (v375)
Simon started going through the scroll masteries one by one with his own designs. Thunderclap is first: "1st level increase the area of the blast, the original radius does same damage but extended 15% range does 15% damage / damage increases of the wider area from 15 -> 45%, lightning becomes visually tiny bit stronger / a second smaller lightning follows the main lightning dealing extra 35% of its damage / lightning becomes visually stonger and chains to one nearby foe for 50% of damage (show visual lightning passing) / keep what there is just enhance the visuals", and "enhance all visuals and dont use low effort for anything". `_backup/tclap_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_tclap.py` (args: src, module, version; needs v366).
- **Where it lives:** in the strike itself, not in add-on hooks. `castThunderclap` and `s6Clap` are replaced and read the cast's mastery level (`tclL(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`). Mastery 0 is exactly the old Thunderclap. `SMF.thunderclap` has no hooks. His numbers are exact: `SMA` and `SMG` are all 1, so no calibrated damage factor.
- **Damage (P = the scroll's power):** 130% plus a 0.25 s stun at the struck foe (and within 0.3), 45% within 1.05, everything Charged 3 s.
  - M1 extends the blast to 1.21 (15% further) with 15% on the new edge.
  - M2 makes that edge 45%.
  - M3: an afterstrike 0.18 s later repeats 35% of each foe's hit.
  - M4: a chain 0.34 s after the strike, 65% (50% of the 130%). It goes to the nearest foe at least 0.55 away (so the race is seen), else the nearest, within 2.4.
  - M5 adds no damage.
- **Power:** the smast-test ladder now reads Thunderclap at 1.10x (M2) and 1.63x (M5) against the old targets 1.35/1.82. That is lower than the other Commons but inside the test's 25%.
- **Looks:** `TCL_V` [1,1,1.15,1.2,1.45,1.85] scales the bolt (width, branches, crackles, sparks, ground light). The flash spheres stay at their base size, because scaling them made a white blob that hid everything.
  - M1: a dust wave and a smooth ring (`tclRing`) roll out to the new edge, with a thunder crack (`tclSound`).
  - M3: the afterstrike bolt, flash and shockwave.
  - M4: side bolts and glowing scorch cracks (`tclScorch`, white-hot for 0.04 s, cooling to dim blue by 0.3 s so they never compete with the chain). The chain (`tclRun`) is a three-layer ribbon revealed from the struck foe with a soft glowing head, bowed sideways (`side`), landing with its own small bolt and ring.
  - M5: a towering darker cloud with rain and inner flashes, a white-hot pillar, 7 lightning forks racing over the ground (`tclRun` with `ground`), wider scorch cracks, a crackling field for 1.5 s and a heavier chain.
- **The chain's timing and shape come from the camera:** in this tilted view every bolt rises up the screen, so a chain up the road vanished into the main bolt. The chain therefore waits until both bolts have faded, and it bows sideways rather than up.
- **Tests:** `tests/tclap-test.js` (32, in run-all). It checks every level's damage to the decimal on foes placed at set distances, the chain's target and reach, the real cast path at each mastery, that the strike draws more at each level, cleanup, and the lightning set. Frame sheets come from `tests/sm-shots.js` (`__sms.sheet(name,k,[levels],[t],{close,crop,dz,spread,set})`, one row per mastery, casts through castScrollNow so t is exact). The shot server is the launch config `shot-saver` (port 8766).

## Static Spark masteries, reworked (v376)
`_backup/smss_b.js`, inserted by `_backup/p_smss.py` (args: src html, module, version for the CLOG entry) before the boot line, on top of v366 or later. Simon is going through the scroll masteries one by one; Static Spark first: "1. keep what it has just enhance visuals; 2. when they die to this spell, leave a lingering tiny lighting sparks on the ground for the next 3.5 seconds dealing damage to enemies passing by 2% max HP every second 0.8% for bosses, make sure pretty visuals; 3. keep what it has, if effect is calculated to be triggered on the next enemy, make the spark passing more visually powerful; 4. a foe killed by static spark has a 35% chance of exploding a lightning explosion dealing 35% damage of the attack to all surrounding foes; 5. keep what there is just enhace the visuals much more", "higher the mastery the more powerful looking it should be".
- The module replaces `SM_D`/`SMF`/`SMA`/`SMG.staticspark` (the old `smSpk`/`smSpkEnd`/`smOrb` stay unused). `SMF.staticspark.cast` re-derives the scroll's own four-foe chain (`sskChain`, same rule as `castStaticSpark`) and the hand point, and rolls Static Shock for each hop up front; `.hit` finds the hop by its index in the chain.
- **Leaps** (`sskLeap` + `sskImpact`, drawn over the scroll's own bolt on every hop and on Longer Arc's two): a braided second strand and an impact pop from M1, a wide soft corridor that widens with the level, an electric ground ring (M2+), a glowing burn (M3+), forks into the ground (M4+), a crackling crown and a ring (M5). `sskHand`: the charge in the hand grows from M2.
- **Static Remnants** (M2, replaces Overcharge, `sskRemnant`): radius 0.42, 3.5s; skittering arcs between 6+L ground nodes, flickering spark points, hopping motes, a soft glow; ticks every 0.25s: 0.5% of max health (bosses 0.2%), ground foes only, a spark into the feet. At most 10 alive (the oldest fades).
- **Static Shock** (M3): `c.stun` rolled before each hop (Longer Arc rolls before each of its leaps). A leap that will stun is told by `sskCharge` (a violet-white gathering on the foe before it, or in the hand) and drawn heavy and violet (`emp`), with a violet ring; the stunned foe crackles with a spinning ring (`sskStun`).
- **Thunder Burst** (M4, replaces Discharge, `sskOnKill` → `sskBurst`): a foe killed by any of the cast's damage (its leaps through `SMF.kill`, mastery damage through `sskHurt`) bursts 35% of the time: a bolt from the sky, electric rings, radial bolts, bolts into every foe within 0.95 (flyers too), 35% x `SMA[3]` of the spark hit on that foe (`e.sskA`); at most 6 a cast; burst kills can chain.
- **Spark Orb** (M5, `sskOrb`): 25% x `SMA[4]` every 0.6s as before; a white-hot core in a shell with arcs crawling over it, two spinning rings, four orbiting motes, strikes into the ground and a ground glow; it gathers in with converging sparks and ends in a discharge and a ring.
- `sskRing` is an additive electric ring for the ground (the stock `shockwave` throws up brown dust and a dark crescent).
- Power: calibrated with `tests/smast-refine.js`: `SMG.staticspark` [1, 1.14, 1.28, 1.39, 1.05] (M2 lost Overcharge's +12%; Remnants and Bursts only add on kills).
Tests: `tests/smss-test.js` (in run-all as 'smss'). Gotcha: the Monarch's aura explosion (`AURA.monarch.boom`) fires on random kills; switch it off when measuring or framing kill effects.
- Gotcha: an effect made inside another effect's callback (a hit inside the cast's addFx) is pushed to fx after updFx has passed it, so it renders one frame before its own callback runs: give rings and scaled meshes their start scale/opacity when they are made (sskRing started at radius 1 and flashed a big white ring on every hit).

## Light Bolt masteries, redone (v377)
Simon, one scroll at a time: "1 keep what it has just enhance the visuals / 2 leave the enemy marked, next light hit triggers a tiny light beam / 3 keep whats there just enhance the visuals and damage from 20 to 25% / 4 keep whats there just enhance the visuals of it / 5 keep the sun but again enhance the visuals and other than that pretty balanced". `_backup/lbolt_b.js` + `_backup/p_lbolt.py`, on top of v376. It replaces the v366 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.lightbolt`.
- **Radiant Trail (20%):** the bolt leaves lingering golden glitter in flight (`castLightBolt` is wrapped; the bolt's group is caught from `scene.add` during the call). On impact a gold-sheathed ribbon runs from the hero to the target (`lbRibbon`), and the seared foe flares (`lbSear`).
- **Mark of Dawn (new, 20%):** replaces Dazzle. The struck foe gets a gold halo for 5s (`lbMark`, `e.smLB`). The next light hit on it calls a tiny beam of light down (`lbBeam`). That hit can be any light scroll, but not the marking cast itself (`o.smId`), a damage tick (`o.dot`) or the beam (`o.smLBeam`). An outer `hurt` wrapper reads the mark before the hit runs, so the bolt that consumes an old mark leaves a new one. If the foe died to that hit, the beam lands on the nearest foe.
- **Prism Split (25%, was 20%):** a spinning prism flashes on impact (`lbPrism`) and 2 haloed motes arc out to foes nearby (`lbMote`).
- **Sunpierce (50%):** a lance of light (`lbLance`) bursts out of the target into the foe behind.
- **Dawnbreaker (as before: 50% more, a 30% burst):** every 4th bolt flies with a rayed corona (`lbCorona`, `c.sun`) and lands in a sun burst (`lbSunBurst`).
- **Balance:** Simon chose to keep his numbers. Power is 1.16 / 1.40 / 1.81 / 1.81 / 2.14 against the Common ladder's 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82, so the Light Bolt runs above it from Mastery 3, mostly from the 25% motes. The test crowds never have a foe behind the target, so Sunpierce adds nothing there. All SMG are 1.
- Effects get their start scale and position when made (the one-frame gotcha above).
- Tests: `tests/lbolt-test.js` (14, in run-all).

## Thorn Whip masteries, reworked (v378)
Simon, one scroll at a time: "1. keep what there is but visual drops of blood appear / 2. Thorn whip whips twice, second one hits for 35% / 3. enemies hit by whips get trapped by the thorns root and stunned for 0.4 seconds (0.2 for bosses, resolve applies), visually the thorn whip has red roses over it / 4. keep what it has just enhance it much more please visualy / 5. a second whip appears slashing one selected enemy that will get "infected" a pink roses grow on his body (adjust this for different body types to avoid visual glitches) this enemy takes 4% max hp drain every second for 5 seconds and 1 tick is 1% and 1 tick is every 0.25 seconds", "higher the mastery the more appealing". `_backup/twm_b.js` + `_backup/p_twm.py`, on top of v377. It replaces the v366 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.thornwhip` and wraps `castThornWhip` (a cast without masteries draws the old whip, `TW.old`).
- **The whip** (`twLash`): a tapered vine on a curved path with thorns, a glow and a trail, lashing out and back; from M3 red roses ride on it (`twRose`, a bud, an outer cup and leaves, opening as it lands).
- **Barbs (20%):** as before, a bleed of 20% over 3s; drops of blood fly from the cut and spot the ground (`twBlood`, `twBleed`, exactly 6 ticks).
- **Double Lash (new, 35%):** a second lash from the other side, 35% x `SMA[1]` of the hit (`twLash2`).
- **Rose Snare (new):** the struck foe is rooted by coiling vines and stunned 0.4s (0.2s for bosses, through `stunE`, so Resolve applies; `twSnare`); flyers are stunned without the vines.
- **Briar Patch (5% a second, 30% slow, 3s):** as before, now a thicket of bramble canes, thorns and leaves with roses (`twBriar`, an `smZone`).
- **Rose Blight (new):** 0.32s after the hit a pink rose whip (`twRoseWhip`) slashes the toughest foe within 2.4 that isn't infected yet. Pink roses grow on its body (`twBodyPts`: points on its opaque meshes between 30% and 100% of its height, facing up where it can, spaced apart, sized by the body; `twRootRose` parents each rose to its mesh). It loses 1% of its max health every 0.25s for 5s (bosses a quarter: 0.25%), 20 ticks counted, not timed; a new infection resets the 20. The roses wither in 0.45s when it ends.
- **Developer mode:** a small "+" at the right of every mastery row (scrolls and buildings, `devMastAdd`) sets the mastery's XP to that level.
- **Patch notes:** a new batch, `NOTES_V='masteries-1'` (Simon: "start brand new patch notes batch, add only everything added after last thing never-never land scroll"): the notes keep every section above the "Never-Never Land" heading and drop it and everything older. Four suites that read the old notes (balance, dslash, tempest, tpeye) accept the new batch.
- **Balance:** Simon chose to keep his numbers (`SM.free.thornwhip`, so smast-test asks only for 3/4 of the ladder at M5). `SMA.thornwhip` [1,1,1,.84,1], `SMG.thornwhip` [1.03,1,1.02,1.02,1].
- Tests: `tests/twm-test.js` (24, in run-all as 'twm'). Gotchas: refresh a foe's world matrices (`updateWorldMatrix(true,true)`) before reading its mesh points, or the roses land off its body by its position; the top-down camera hides points on the sides, so prefer up-facing ones.

## Frost Shard masteries, as Simon designed them (v382)
PUBLISHED 2026-10-26, live MATCH (the live page is dist inside the publisher's page wrapper). It went in after v381 Gravebind, chain MATCH, and a full run-all on v382 was green (49 lines).
Simon, one scroll at a time: "1. the shard bursts into 4 splites that hit for 15% of the damage, enhance the visuals / 2. hit foes are chilled by 20% more, enhance visuals / 3. frostbite - enemies become visually paler (their skin) and all ice damage does 15% more damage to them, enhance visuals / 4. a shard now looks much sharper and stronger, a hit foe has a 25% chance to freeze solid for 0.8 seconds (0,3 to bosses and resolve applies) / 5. every 3rd cast is a shard lance that drives and pierces through a line of enemies, make the visuals much better since this is max mastery and make it feel powerful like a rare spell". `_backup/fsh_b.js` + `_backup/p_fsh.py` (args: src, module, version), on top of v374 or later. It replaces the v366 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.frostshard` and wraps `castFrostShard`.
- **Where it lives:** the wrapper reads the cast's mastery (`fshC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`). Mastery 0 and Masteries 1-3 fly the old shard, Mastery 4 flies the sharper shard (`fshShard`), and at Mastery 5 every 3rd cast (`c.n%3===0`) is the lance (`fshLance`). What a hit does to its foe runs in `SMF.frostshard.hit`, so it happens on the shard's foe and on every foe the lance pierces. The numbers live in `FSH`.
- **Splinters (15%):** 4 icicles (`fshSplinter`) arc from the impact to the 4 nearest foes within 1.6 (flyers too), each 15% of the shard's damage, with a crown of ice spikes (`fshCrown`) and a frost wave (`fshRing`, a soft additive ring: the stock `shockwave` throws up brown dust). A splinter with no foe shatters on the ground.
- **Deep Chill:** "20% more" is read as 20 points more, the way the v366 Biting Cold read "15% more": a 50% slow instead of 30%, for 1.5s. A crust of ice crystals crawls round the foe's feet while it lasts (`fshChill`, one per foe, refreshed).
- **Frostbite (15%, 4s):** sets `e.smMk.ice` (the v366 mark, merged by max) without its generic marker. The foe's skin turns pale: its meshes swap to cached paler clones of their own materials (`fshPale`/`fshPaleMat`, keyed by material, so foes that share a material are not tinted together) and swap back exactly when it ends. A slowly turning snowflake (`fshFlake`) rides over it, with cold vapour and glints. "Frostbite" is also the name of an older synergy (Withered + Chilled); Simon named this mastery.
- **Glacial Edge (25%, 0.8s, bosses 0.3s):** a long, sharp blade with a crossed second blade, a white core, three icy fins and a tapered contrail, and a crown of spikes on impact. The freeze goes through `stunE`, so Resolve applies (no freeze while it is spent): an ice block of crystals round the foe (`fshFreeze`) that shatters as it thaws.
- **Shard Lance (new numbers, Simon gave none):** a great lance forms at the hand for 0.46s (a fluted crystal, converging frost, a frost circle under the hero), then drives along the line of the most foes (`jdgAim`, the Verdict's aimer) at 12.5 tiles a second, 0.42 wide (bosses +0.22). It stops 0.75 past the last foe on its line (1.6 to 5.4 tiles), so its splinters burst among them. The first foe it reaches takes the shard's full damage and every foe after it 20% (`FSH.lance`), each once, each chilled, Frostbitten and rolling the freeze, flashing white with a tall spike under it. Ice bursts out of the road behind it every 0.3 (`fshSpike`), with a frost stripe and mist.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, and all SMA/SMG are 1. Power is 1.46 / 1.46 / 1.55 / 1.55 / 1.94 against the Common ladder's 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82: Splinters run above it, Mastery 4 adds no damage (8% under), and the lance's 20% was chosen for Mastery 5. Mastery 5 by the lance's share: 0% 1.60, 10% 1.77, 15% 1.86, 20% 1.94, 25% 2.08, 35% 2.26. Full damage to every foe gave 3.34. A flat lower share would have made a lone-foe lance weaker than Mastery 4's shard.
- Tests: `tests/fsh-test.js` (28, in run-all as 'fsh'). It checks the card and numbers, Mastery 0 as before, the splinters' damage and targets, the chill, Frostbite's 15% (with a dark hit as the other element: fire on a chilled foe is the Thermal synergy, x1.5), the pale skin swap and its exact undo, the freeze rate over 400 hits, 0.8s / 0.3s and Resolve, the lance's cast count, damage, line and splinters, clean-up at every mastery, and the ladder. The frame sheets came from `tests/sm-shots.js`; for the lance set `SM.cnt.frostshard=2` first, and for the freeze `FSH.frz=1` (put it back).

## Thunderclap's hidden damage boost (v379)
Simon chose to "add a small hidden damage boost per mastery level so Thunderclap still reaches the old ladder (about Uncommon at Mastery 2, Rare at Mastery 5) on top of my numbers". `_backup/tcboost_b.js` (by `p_tcboost.py`) sets only `SMG.thunderclap=[1.14,1.24,1.03,1.05,1.13]`, the damage factor fireScroll applies to the scroll's power, so his percentages scale with it. Measured with the factor applied (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, a generic version of smast-test's power(): set `window.__smk`, about 20 s, not in run-all): 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.49 / 1.68 / 1.81 against the ladder 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82. The mastery texts do not mention it. A changelog line and a patch note line do.

## Hexspark masteries, reworked (v380)
Simon, one scroll at a time: "1. can keep what it has just enhance the shards make them look better and the hexspark overall a tiny bit better / 2. Hexspark charges for 1 second when cast and when explsoding dealing 130% damage instead of 100 / 3. you can keep what it has just enhance visuals / 4. keep what it has but here also enhance the visual of the hex spark itselve same as of the black nova / 5. keep here aswell just maek the black lightning much more noticale and prettier", "the higher the quality the better the spell should look". `_backup/hxm_b.js` + `_backup/p_hxm.py`, on top of v378 or later. It replaces `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.hexspark` and `castHexspark` (a cast without masteries runs the old spark exactly, `HXS.old`).
- **The spark** (`hxSpark`): with any mastery, a black faceted gem with a violet heart, a ring and motes drawn in; its burst is violet and black (a dark bubble, a violet flash, a ring, black smoke; `hxBurstFx`). Damage as before: the foe takes the hit, foes within 0.71 take 35%.
- **Splinters (20%):** two obsidian shards (`hxShard`: a black crystal, a violet glow, sparks and smoke) fly on to the two nearest foes within 1.6.
- **Overcharge (new, replaces Hex Mark):** the hero's spark charges for 1s, crackling (`nsArc`), then bursts for 130%, splash included. Sparks made by masteries (Twin Spark, Hexstorm) keep the quick 0.45s fuse and 100%.
- **Twin Spark (50%):** a violet comet (`hxComet`) leaps from the burst to the next foe within 2, and the twin spark ignites where it lands.
- **Black Nova (40%):** the burst Hexes the foe for 4s (`hxHex`: a hexagram turning over its head); the spark grows a hexagram of its own; a Hexed foe that dies implodes, then blows up in a black-violet nova (`hxNova`: a rune on the ground, a dark dome, shards, smoke) for 40% within 0.9.
- **Hexstorm (60%):** every 3rd cast, black lightning (`hxBolt`: a black core in a violet glow with a white-hot thread, two branches, flickering between two shapes) strikes from the hero to 2 more foes and sparks ignite on them.
- **ringFx fix:** a ring now starts at its first size (`r*.3`); made inside another effect it used to show full size for one frame.
- **Balance:** the masteries alone measure 1.12 / 1.39 / 1.78 / 1.75 / 2.04 against the Common ladder's 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82; Overcharge's 130% (Simon's number) carries it above the ladder from Mastery 2, so only Mastery 1 is lifted: `SMG.hexspark` [1.036,1,1,1,1], `SMA` as before [1,1,.97,1,1].
- Tests: `tests/hxm-test.js` (12, in run-all as 'hxm'). Gotcha: `castHexspark` gets a random target from the list (`pick`), so the Hexstorm check uses three foes far apart and sums their damage.

## Gravebind's masteries, as Simon designed them (v381)
Simon's spec: "1. keep what it has but enhance visuals of the spell / 2. keep but make it so that enemies in this darkness are withering until they leave it / 3. a second chain lashes and circles a foe, keep all models into consideration to prevent visuals glitches, the chain should visibly be circles around the mobs (for bosses just a bit) and slow them by 60%, enhance visuals / 4. keep what it has but increase the size of the spell and make it have an area ... the targeted one gets frozen or held in place for 0.6 seconds and all enemies in the range are slowed by additional 25% / 5. the rune gets area effect and can chain up to 3 more foes with the same held effect, the rune is visually different now and looks more powerful". `_backup/gbind_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_gbind.py` (args: src, module, version, day).
- **Where it lives:** Mastery 0 is the old cast (`GB_CAST0`). From Mastery 1 `castGravebind` runs `gbCast`, which reads the level (`gbL`); the bind at 0.9s is `gbBind`. `SMF.gravebind` has no hooks.
- **Chains:** spectral links in two instanced layers (`gbLinks`), laid along the path with their long axis on the tangent and every other link turned 90 degrees. `gbFit` measures a foe's model (Box3 of its meshes, flat ones skipped, cached 2s); `gbCoil` puts the coil just outside it (radius 0.82 of the half width + 0.03), from the legs to the chest, or one loop low down on a boss.
- **The masteries:**
  - M1: 25% slower for 2.5s once the 3s bind is over, fired 0.06s after it (a slow resets to 0 when its time runs out). Before v381 it ran inside the bind and did nothing. A shackle of links rings the feet meanwhile (`gbShackle`).
  - M2: the 40% drain as 4 ticks of 10% (smDot's half-second ticks give only 3 in 2s). The darkness pools round the bound foe for the bind (`gbPool`, radius 0.85): every 0.25s the foes in it get Withered (12%/s, t 0.6, so it stops soon after they leave). The bound foe is left out, because a bound foe that withers sets off Spreading Chains by itself.
  - M3: a second chain (`gbLash`) races to the nearest foe within 1.5 and wraps it (the head wraps first): 50% and 60% slower for 2s.
  - M4: the rune grows to 1.05. The bound foe is held 0.6s (`smStun`, bosses 0.25). Every foe on the rune gets +25% slow, added to its current slow and capped at 75%, for 2.5s.
  - M5: the greater rune (1.15, nine points, counter-turning inner rune, 10 crystal spikes, 5 light pillars) erupts for 10%, and its chains bind up to 3 more foes on it for 10% each, held 0.6s.
- **Numbers left open:** the wither (12%/s), the eruption and the chain damage (10%) were tuned so the levels land on the ladder.
- **Power:** a hidden SMG factor `[1.21,1.02,1,1.05,1]`, tuned on the real power with the factor applied (`tests/sm-ladder.js`): 1.14 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.56 / 1.76 against 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82. The churn crowd makes it step-like (Gravebind's hit sits on a 600-health kill threshold), so each factor sits on the steady side of its step.
- **Tests:** `tests/gbind-test.js` (34, in run-all). It places foes and re-pins them after each update (the loop snaps foes back to their road spot) and checks every level's damage, slows, hold, withering (only while inside), the coil fit on a soldier and a boss, the real cast path, cleanup (ignoring foes' own health bars) and the dark set.

## Flame Lash masteries, reworked (v383)
`_backup/flm_b.js`, inserted by `_backup/p_flm.py` (args: src html, module, version for the CLOG entry) before the boot line, on top of v366 or later. Simon: "flame lash: 1. keep what it has but enhance visuals; 2. keep what it has but enhance visuals; 3. hit from this leaves a visual fire trail as it sweeps, hit does 35% more damage and leaves them burning for 5 seconds; 4. keep what it has but enhance more visuals; 5. keep also what it has but make it all feel more powerful and the explosion much better", "no low effort effects, better visuals as mastery goes, mastery 1 should already be looking better than no mastery".
- **The whip.** A `castFlameLash` wrapper sends a mastered cast (`flmCtx(o)`: the cast's mastery context, `o.smId` without `smx`) to `flmCast`, which keeps the reach, timing (0.12s wind-up, unfurl to 0.4s, crack, cool to 1.25s), the crack damage (80% within 0.48 of the road line) and the 3s burn of the old whip, and draws it richer with the level: a dark-red heat-haze layer, layers 7% wider a level, a fireball at the tip as it unfurls, more flames and smoke, and a harder crack (`flmCrack`: a ring of fire `flmRing` instead of the dusty `shockwave`, a glowing seared line, flames bursting up from the road from M2). Unmastered casts and echoes still go to the old function untouched. The masteries that follow the crack run in `flmAfter` (they used to wait for the first hit).
- **Long Lash** (M1): the whip itself (`flmLash`) runs on 1.05 tiles past its far tip and cracks again there; foes within 0.42 of it take 30% x `SMA[0]` of 1.2P (the old one reached from the first-hit foe toward higher road distance).
- **Searing** (M2): the same burn (12% x `SMA[1]` of 1.2P, 3s); `flmBurnFx` shows it (flames climbing the foe, a glowing ring at its feet, sparks, smoke from M3), one per foe, lengthened by a new burn.
- **Blazing Trail** (M3, replaces Backlash): +35% of each lash hit (`hurt` of `amt*.35*SMA[2]`), the Searing burn lasts 5s, and the tip leaves a streak of fire in the air (a ribbon of its last 18 positions) and flames on the road under its path (visual) for about 2s.
- **Fire Snake** (M4): the same five `smZone` fires along the line (3s, 8% x `SMA[3]` of 1.2P burn), with `flmSnake`: a fire serpent slithering back and forth along the line (a ribbon over its last 16 head positions, a fireball head) over a bed of embers and tongues of flame.
- **Inferno Cross** (M5): a real second whip across the road through the target's spot (0.22s after the crack), then `flmBoom` where they cross: a white-hot core, a layered fireball rising, 46 radial tongues of flame and a rising fire plume, two rings of fire, burning debris, a smoke plume, a burning X along both lines; 25% x `SMA[4]` of 1.2P to ground foes within 1 tile (as before).
- Power: `SMG.flamelash` [1.14, 1.22, 1, 1.09, 1.07] with `tests/smast-refine.js` (Long Lash now reaches past the far tip, Backlash is gone; M3 is Simon's numbers and lands about 2% over its target).
Tests: `tests/flm-test.js` (17, in run-all as 'flm'). Sheets: `_backup/shots/flm-cmp.jpg` (levels), `flm-L5.jpg`, `flm-boom.jpg`, `flm-L3.jpg`.

## Basic Wither masteries, as Simon designed them (v386)
Simon, the last Common before the Uncommons: "1. lingering decay ... 5. The wither bloom ...", "no low effort effects are acceptable, as the mastery goes on it should be better and better visually and feel more and more powerful" (the full text is at the top of the module). `_backup/wither_b.js` + `_backup/p_wither.py` (args: src, module, version, date), on top of v378 or later (it uses Thorn Whip's `twBodyPts`). It replaces the v366 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.wither` and wraps `castWither` and `kill`.
- **Where it lives:** from Mastery 1 the cast is a cloud (`wthCloud`), which carries every mastery; Mastery 0 is the old 0.9s mist. As before, the foes under the mist wither at 0.9s (55% a second for 5s, `e.wither`).
- **Lingering Decay:** the cloud lasts 3.5s (4.8s at Mastery 5, round the flower) and withers every foe that walks in. Simon gave no time; 3.5s is mine. The cloud is a knee-high mist of soft blot layers (`WTQ.plane` + `nsBlot`) with smoke emitted by the second. A cloud as tall as the old mist, or balls of mist, buried the foes or read as hard dark discs from this camera. Faint dark wisps drift on for 1.4-2s after it.
- **Grasp of Decay (15%):** a foe inside is slowed 15% (refreshed each frame). Dark tendrils (`wthGrasp`) rise from the ground round it and coil onto points of its own meshes (`twBodyPts`; each tip is kept in that mesh's local space, so it follows the limb). There are 3 tendrils, 5 on a boss. They are one tube mesh per foe, rebuilt each frame. They let go when the foe leaves or the cloud ends. The test checks every tip lands on the foe's own body for a soldier, knight, rider, peasant, the raven (a flyer) and the ogre (a boss).
- **Contagion (50% of 5s):** a wither from a Mastery 3+ cast (`wtC`) that is on a foe when it dies passes on (an outer `kill` wrapper, `wthSpread`). It goes to the nearest foe within 1.8 that isn't withered yet, else the nearest, for 2.5s at the original 55% (blight not carried). A black-purple wisp (`wthWisp`) bursts from the corpse and arcs into it. The passed-on wither spreads again.
- **Deepening Blight (2s, +25%):** a foe that has been in the cloud for 2s (time counted per foe) gets its wither's `dps` x1.25 for the rest of that wither (`wthBlight`). Black cracks with a violet core (`WTQ.crack`) grow over points of its body, parented to its meshes and taken off when the wither ends. A dark aura of smoke and black flakes rises off it. From Mastery 4 the cloud's ground carries a dark crack too; a bright one read as lightning.
- **The Wither Bloom (25%, 25% slow, 4s):** a black flower (`wthBloom`) with three layers of smooth petals (`WTQ.petal`, near-black with glowing violet veins), stamens, a glowing core and thorned roots. It unfolds in 0.7s. Every second for 4s its petals throw open and two rings of dark energy roll out (`wthRing`). Each pulse deals 25% of the cast's power, on top of the wither, to every foe within the cloud's radius, and slows them 25% for 1s. Then it wilts into ash.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, and SMA/SMG are all 1. Power is 1.10 / 1.09 / 1.13 / 1.27 / 1.87 against the Common ladder's 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82. Masteries 1-4 add little damage to the standing test crowds (the slow, the spread and the late-comers only pay off on moving waves); the flower brings Mastery 5 to the ladder. Mastery 2 sits 19% under its target, inside smast-test's 25%.
- Tests: `tests/wither-test.js` (23, in run-all as 'wither'). Frame sheets via `tests/sm-shots.js`; for Contagion, wrap `castScrollNow` to set the target's health low so the wither kills it on camera.

## Ember Bolt masteries, reworked (v384)
Simon, one scroll at a time: "1. ember bolt burns foes for 3 seconds 25% each second enhance visuals / 2. keep waht it has just increase embers to 5 and enhance visuals / 3. rune when casting is bigger and shoots out a much bigger bolt that leaves foes stunned for 0.1 seconds and causes a tiny explosion dealing 45% of tis damage to surrounding foes / 4. ember bolt leaves a burning ground (one tile like from cinder scatter) that burns enemies walking over them / 5. spell tuns into a fire bolt where its colour is much more red leaving a trail of fire behind it, dealing 150% damage from 100% and if it kills an enemy that has "armoured" trait, leaves molten steel on ground that slows foes by 20% and burns them for 4% max hp every time they step on it". He chose: Kindling stacks with the usual burn; the molten steel lasts 4s and hits each foe once; bosses take a quarter. `_backup/emb_b.js` + `_backup/p_emb.py`, on top of v378 or later. It replaces `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.emberbolt` and `castEmberBolt` (a cast without masteries runs the old bolt exactly, `EBM.old`).
- **The bolt** (`ebBolt`): with any mastery a rune draws sparks in and collapses into a roiling fireball (`ebFire`: myFireball's surface with a palette; a hot heart and a glow), which flies on a low arc with flame tongues and smoke and lands in a fireball (`ebBall`, `ebImpact`). Damage as before: 110% and the usual burn (25% a second for 3s).
- **Kindling (25%):** the bolt's usual burn refreshes like every burn, so the old Kindling (20%) added nothing. Now its own flame (`ebKindle`, `e.ebK`) burns 25% of the hit each second for 3s on top, 6 ticks of 0.5s (a new one restarts the 6); four flame tufts (`s6FlameMat` on `s6TuftGeo`) lick up the foe's body.
- **Ember Spray (20%):** 5 glowing coals (was 3) arc to the 5 nearest foes within 1.6, each with a streak of fiery puffs (`ebEmber`).
- **Blazing Rune (new):** the rune is 1.7x bigger with a ring of fire and casts a much bigger bolt; the hit stuns 0.1s (`stunE`, so Resolve applies) and a blast deals 45% of the hit to foes within 0.75, flyers too (`ebBlast`: a fireball, fiery puffs and `ebFireRing`, a shader ring of fire racing out, normally blended so it stays fire-coloured on grass).
- **Scorched Ground:** one tile on fire for 3.5s (`ebGround`, radius 0.45 like a Cinder Scatter cinder): glowing cracks (`myLavaTex`), six flame tufts, embers and smoke; ground foes on it catch fire for 30% of the scroll's power a second (the cinders' number). It registers in `S6FZ`, so Firestorm can drink it.
- **Fire Bolt (new):** a deep red bolt (red palettes: `EB_PAL.r`, `EB_RED_FS`) that leaves a trail of fiery puffs (`ebPuff`, `EB_PUFF_FS`) and hits for 150%, its usual burn too. An Armoured foe (the elite trait, `ebArm`) killed by any of the cast's damage (an outer `hurt` wrapper reads `o.spell`/`o.smId`) leaves molten steel for 4s (`ebMolten`, radius 0.42): a white-hot pool with flowing streaks and a metallic sheen that crusts over as it cools (`EB_STEEL_FS`), bubbles and steam; each ground foe that steps in takes 4% of its max health once (bosses 1%) and is slowed 20% while in it.
- **Balance:** Simon's own numbers (`SM.free.emberbolt`, all SMA/SMG 1). The masteries measure 1.39 / 1.88 / 2.29 / 2.93 / 3.45 against the Common ladder's 1.16 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.69 / 1.82, mostly from the stacking Kindling and the burning tile; he chose the stacking burn knowing it sits above the ladder. smast-test gives SM.free scrolls at least 3/4 of the ladder at Masteries 2 and 5.
- Tests: `tests/emb-test.js` (15, in run-all as 'emb'). Gotchas: give a group's children their real size when they are made (the fireball showed at radius 1 for its collapse phase); flat flame sheets (`s6FlameMat` on a plane) render as rectangles, use the puff shader for camera-facing fire; big additive flashes and rings turn into a lime haze over grass.

## Releasing v384-v386 after their sessions were gone (files session, 2026-10-26)
The Verdict, Tempest and test-stability sessions disappeared mid-queue. Ember Bolt was applied but not published, and Cinder Scatter and Wither were finished patch files that had never been applied. Simon asked the files session to release all three. They were applied in order (v384 on v383, v385, v386), tested in one full run-all and published as v386.
- **Fix in Ember Bolt (`emb_b.js`):** Scorched Ground's two lava discs and Molten Steel's glow disc and bubbles were added straight to the scene and removed only in their effect's end callback. `clearWorld` drops fx without calling end callbacks, so a world reset (leaving or restarting a run) could leave glowing discs on the ground for good. They now ride in the effect's own group. The run-all showed it: Ember Bolt's test left 2 tagged discs behind, which failed 3 Wither checks that run after it.
- Gotcha for effect code: anything that must disappear on a world reset belongs to the fx object (or its children), not to an end callback.
- **Test fix, `tests/hxm-test.js` (Hexspark):** the same two random helpers the test-stability session had fixed in the smast tests. The near() probe now gets `e0.off=0`, and the hero's aura boom (Monarch's random blast on kills near him) is off during the test and restored afterwards. Without that, the "Black Nova explodes for 40%" row now and then read 148.6 instead of 56.0: a no-spell 92.6 from the boom.

## Battery saver (v387)
PUBLISHED 2026-10-26 as artifact version 383, live MATCH (dist inside the publisher's page wrapper). Chain MATCH (`sh _backup/rebuild.sh` gives `_backup/kingsroad-keep.v387.html` byte for byte). Full run-all on v387: every suite green; in that run one verdict row (Dimensional Slash flash, scene child count) and nine suites that could not be fetched for a moment (twm to battery) were re-run on the same page and all passed (verdict 22/22, battery 38/38). In the lab pre-run on v386 + this patch, the wither row "raven: tendril tips on the body" failed once inside run-all (2 of 3 tips) and passed 3/3 alone; it looks order-dependent (if it comes back, look for scene leftovers from the suite before it, `scene.children` with `userData.sm`).
`_backup/battery_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_battery.py` (args: src html, module, version, date), on top of v386 (it only touches the base game's `frame`, renderer set-up, `applyShadows`, `acctEnter`, the More tab and the Settings rows). Simon asked whether the game drains a lot of battery: it drew every frame at the screen's full rate (60 or 120 Hz), in the menus and while paused too, at up to twice the pixels on sharp screens, with PCF soft shadows, on the high-performance GPU.
- **The setting:** `meta.battery`, off by default. A Settings window row right after Shadows, and a More tab toggle `#bBattery` (hidden on PC like the other More toggles, where the Settings window has it). Kept across account switches (`acctEnter`'s keep list). `setBattery(on)` saves and applies it; `batApply()` applies it, and the frame wrapper calls it on the first tick after `meta.battery` changed some other way (an imported save).
- **30 fps cap:** `frame` is wrapped; the old one is `BAT.frame0`, unchanged apart from its step limit. `batGate(now)` lets a requestAnimationFrame tick through once 1000/30 ms have passed since the last one it let through (4 ms early still counts, and the due time moves on by exactly one interval, so 60, 90, 120 and 144 Hz screens all average 30). A skipped tick only asks for the next one. `last` is untouched in between, so the drawn frame's step covers all the time since the last drawn one (33 ms, under the 50 ms limit) and game time keeps pace.
- **Menus and pause:** `batIdle()` (not playing, or paused: the home screen, the game-over screen, the pause screen) draws every 83 ms (about 12 fps), and 30 fps again for 1.2 s after any pointerdown, pointermove, wheel, keydown or touchstart (`BAT.woke`). There the step limit is .1 s (`batDt()`, read by frame's `rdt0`), so what moves in the menus keeps its speed; it is .05 everywhere else, and always while the saver is off.
- **Pixels and shadows:** `bsPR()` is 1 while it is on, otherwise the old `min(devicePixelRatio, 2)`. It is used when the renderer is made, after the thumbnail and icon renders, by the hero preview (`HPV.r`), the artifact room (`AR.r`) and the 2D map preview; `batApply` resets the live renderers and calls `psScale()` (particle sizes follow the canvas height). `shOn()` is `meta.shadows && !meta.battery`, read by `applyShadows` and at boot. The Shadows option keeps its own value for later; tapping it while the saver is on only shows a toast (`batHold`) and its button reads Off. The artifact room is made without shadows while the saver is on.
- **The graphics chip:** `bsPP()` asks for `powerPreference` 'low-power' when the saver is on as the page loads, 'high-performance' (as before) when it is off. The renderer is made once (making it again would drop every compiled program, render target and texture), so the toast asks for a reload to switch chips (`BAT.pp0` is what this page asked for).
- **Measured** in the lab (`tests/battery-measure.js`: a virtual 60 Hz display driving the game's loop, GPU time from EXT_disjoint_timer_query_webgl2 around each whole frame) on Simon's laptop GPU (Intel HD Graphics 520), in a 430x860 viewport at a 2x pixel ratio (a phone-sized canvas, 860x1720 pixels off, 430x860 on):

  | | off: fps, GPU ms a frame, GPU ms a second | on: fps, GPU ms a frame, GPU ms a second | GPU work saved | CPU ms a second (off / on) |
  |---|---|---|---|---|
  | Wave 15, ten towers, the hero (separate loads) | 60, 24.5, 1470 | 30, 13.5, 404 | 72% | 1923 / 723 |
  | Wave 20, the same board, switched off-on-off-on | 60, 22.6 and 28.6, 1358 and 1713 | 30, 15.4 and 18.6, 461 and 558 | 66-67% | 1515 and 2549 / 805 and 739 |
  | Home screen | 60, 20.3, 1218 | 12.3, 8.4, 103 | 92% | 1789 / 296 |
  | Paused mid-wave | 60, 24.2, 1450 | 12, 11.8, 142 | 90% | 1795 / 161 |

  Off, the GPU needed more than 1000 ms of work a second at 60 Hz, so this laptop ran its GPU flat out and could not really hold 60 fps; on, it is busy about half the time in battle. With the frame rate equal, the saver's pixel ratio and shadows alone take about a third off each frame (this GPU is held back by the 500-900 draw calls a frame more than by pixels); most of the saving is the frame cap. A 120 Hz screen draws 30 fps with the saver on (it was 120 off). The off build matched the v383 build within noise (wave 23.6 against 24.5 ms a frame, home 20.35 against 20.3).
- Tests: `tests/battery-test.js` (38, in run-all as 'battery'). It feeds `batGate` and the frame wrapper made-up tick times with requestAnimationFrame and the old frame stubbed, so it runs the same in a hidden pane: off, every tick of 60/120/144 Hz draws and the pixel ratio, shadows, step limit and chip are as before; on, 30 fps on 60/90/120/144 Hz screens, every tick at 24 fps, steps of 33 ms with no time lost, 12 fps in the menus, on game over and paused, 30 after input, pixel ratio 1 on a stubbed 2x screen, shadows off with the option kept, the UI rows and the account-switch keep.

## Masteries for now only on Common scrolls; Frost Spire assist XP (v388)
Simon: "only show masteries to the rest of the abilities in dev mode and when playing normally make it so that for now only the common ones are the ones that can be obtained" and "rework xp gain for frost spire tower cause it does barely any damage and doesnt earn any XP, friend stated hes at 80 xp after 300 waves". `_backup/smgate_b.js` + `_backup/p_smgate.py`, on top of v387.
- **Scroll masteries:** `smOn(k)` is true in developer mode or for a Common scroll. Wrappers on `smLv` (0 when off, so no hooks, power factor, pips or cast ring), `smGain` (no XP), `smRow` (a hidden empty element, because the scroll page inserts it) and `smOpen` (no card). Saved XP for the other rarities is untouched. The preview frame runs in developer mode, so previews work there.
- **Frost Spire:** an outer `kill` wrapper. When a foe dies to anything other than the Frost Spire that chilled it (`e.chillBy`, set by its pulse and its Blizzard), while still chilled (`slowT > 0`), and that spire is still standing, `frost` gets the same mastery XP as a kill (`MAST_KILL`: 0.5 / elite 1.5 / boss 15; `FROST_ASSIST` = 1). The spire's own kills still count once.
- **Bot measurement** (`tests/balance-sim.js`, 4 archers + 3 Frost Spires + cannon + mage):
  - v387: Frost Spire 23 XP by wave 28.
  - v388: 322 XP by wave 30, against 310 for all the archers combined.
- Tests: `tests/smgate-test.js` (19, in run-all).
- Test fix, `tests/gbind-test.js`: its foes are no longer gem or scroll carriers (`e.gem=0; e.scroll=0` in `put`). A carrier's twinkle (`sparkle`, from `update`) adds untagged meshes, which the "Mastery 5 leaves nothing behind" row counted (it failed 1 run in 3 before, 5/5 pass after).
- Known flake, not fixed: the wither row "dark tendrils ... for every kind of foe" fails sometimes inside run-all but passes alone (3/3 on v388; also seen on v387). It is order-dependent: state from an earlier suite.

## Soul Lantern and Ball Lightning nerfed (v389)
Simon: "we need to really nerf soul lantern and ball lightning, they accumulate SO MUCH damage, more than majority of legendary and epic and rare scrolls". `_backup/p_nerf389.py` (text patch of the base casts and card texts, no module), on top of v388.
- **Measured with `tests/scroll-dmg-sim.js`.** A bot plays to wave 100 with one scroll on the hero (auto-cast), with endless lives. The scroll's damage comes from the run meter (`RSTAT.heroBy`: health actually taken off foes). Load it on a dev page and run `__sds.queue([{scroll,maxW,seed,with}])` + `__sds.step(ms)`. Call step with a large `ms` (20000+): a hidden tab only wakes chained timers about once a minute.
- **Before (map seed 31, Robin level 12, scroll level 1):**
  - Soul Lantern 0.83M-1.33M.
  - Ball Lightning 1.18M-1.47M; 3.48M on the crowded seed-77 map; +67% with Lightning Cloud.
  - Other Uncommons median about 0.2M, Rares mostly 0.2M-0.5M, Epics 0.5M-2.2M, Legendaries 0.5M-0.9M.
  - Ancient domains read 0 because their damage isn't credited to the hero.
- **Soul Lantern changes:**
  - Beam 90% -> 50%, each jump 55% -> 30%.
  - Jumps to 1 more foe (2 if held) instead of 2 (3).
  - Lasts 8.5s -> 6.5s.
- **Ball Lightning changes:**
  - Zaps the nearest foe every 0.5s instead of 2 foes every 0.4s.
  - 40% -> 35% a zap.
  - The fx lasts 6.9s -> 5.4s (about 5s of zapping).
  - Storm boost: every 0.4s, x1.2, radius 1.6 (was every 0.2s, x1.3, radius 1.8).
- **After:**
  - Soul Lantern 0.51M-0.85M (median about 0.56M, -47%).
  - Ball Lightning 0.48M-0.62M (-58%); with Lightning Cloud 0.79M (was 1.97M).
  - The real damage drops less than the raw numbers: weaker hits leave foes alive longer, so less of the damage is wasted.
- Their Uncommon masteries (dev-only since v388) were re-calibrated on the new base (`tests/smast-cal.js`; the new SMA is the old one times the solved factor): `SMA.lantern=[.42,1,.31,1,.24]`, `SMA.balllightning=[.37,1,1,.68,.19]`. Otherwise their ladder read 1.70/2.68 and 1.35/2.40 against 1.35/1.74. Simon is redesigning masteries one scroll at a time anyway.
- run-all on v389: also failed the Tempest light colour (passes alone), the Wither tendrils (known flake) and the merchant's black-market reveal card. The merchant row fails the same way on v388, so it is lab-account state left by the bot runs, not this change.

## Wall of Fire masteries, redesigned by Simon (v390)
`_backup/wof_b.js`, inserted by `_backup/p_wof.py` (args: src html, module, version, day) before the boot line, on top of v384 (it uses Ember Bolt's `ebKit`, `ebFlameMat`, `ebPuff`, `ebFireRing`, `ebBall`, `EB_Q_VS` and Flame Lash's `flmBurnFx`). The first Uncommon scroll with Simon's own masteries. Since v388 a scroll above Common has masteries in developer mode only, so for now players see none of this.
Simon: "1. Scorching passage - enemies passing through the wall burn for 2 seconds longer; 2. Flashpoint - the first time each enemy crosses the wall, it erupts in a burst of flame, dealing bonus damage to that enemy and nearby foes (a sharp flare erupts at the point of contact, scattering sparks outward); 3. Trail of Embers - after the wall disappears it leaves a strip of burning ground along its length for 3 seconds (the tall flames collapse into glowing embers, leaving cracked, smouldering ground that continues to damage enemies); 4. Blazing Perdition - each enemy that crosses the wall slightly feeds the wall and slightly increases its intensity; after enough crossings the wall reaches maximum intensity and releases a powerful blast of fire; 5. Ring of Damnation - the wall bends at both ends and closes into a ring of fire around nearby enemies, applying a new effect, fear (50% slower before recovering their courage, 15% more likely to get struck critically for double damage); the ring contracts once at the end of the ability dealing heavy damage before vanishing; a straight wall visually curves into a blazing circle that snaps shut with a violent fire explosion, and the trail of embers changes with it".
- **The wall.**
  - A `castFireWall` wrapper sends a mastered cast (`wofCtx(o)`: the cast's mastery context) to `wofCast`. It keeps the old wall's numbers: the same place (1.5 tiles ahead of the lead foe), 5.4s life, 80% at a foe's first touch, a 30%/s burn while inside, a 25% slow inside, and the Firestorm ground (`S6FZ`).
  - Unmastered casts and echoes still go to the old function untouched.
  - The flames are one instanced batch (`wofFlameMat`: s6FlameMat's shader with instancing and a phase per sheet), plus one batch of glow. There are 13-28 sheets in the main row, and from Mastery 2 a lower row on each side (7-14 each).
  - The ground under it is `WOF_GR_FS` on a flat strip (`wofStripGeo`, 1.05 wide): charred, cracked earth whose cracks glow, with a molten seam under the flames and a glow spilling out beside them.
  - The cracks open first, then the flames burst up with fiery puffs. Tongues of fire lick off the crest, and embers and smoke rise, more with each level.
- **Scorching Passage** (M1): the burn lasts 5s after a foe leaves the wall (3s before). `flmBurnFx` shows it on the burning foe.
- **Flashpoint** (M2): at a foe's first touch, `wofFlare` makes a flat starburst (a spike and 6 rays) raised on the foe's side of the wall, a jet of flame, 46 sparks and a small ring of fire. It deals 25% x `SMA[1]` to that foe and the foes within 0.55 (flyers too).
- **Trail of Embers** (M3): the flames slump into the ground, throwing sparks. The strip turns into glowing cracks with instanced coals that sink as they cool, small flames and smoke, for 3s. Ground foes within 0.45 of it take 30% x `SMA[2]` each second (ticks of 0.25s, dot). It counts as Firestorm ground.
- **Blazing Perdition** (M4):
  - Each foe's first touch feeds the wall (`W.feeds`; a boss counts 3). The flames there jump up, and the wall grows taller and hotter (visual only; a damage bonus left no room in the ladder for the blast).
  - At 6, `wofBlast` fires: the whole wall flares, fireballs rise along it, and two molten lines of fire (`wofWave`) race out 1.35 tiles on both sides. It deals 80% x `SMA[3]` to all foes within 1.4 of the wall, once a cast.
- **Ring of Damnation** (M5):
  - The wall burns hellfire red (`EB_RED_FS`).
  - From 0.55s its ends bend back (`wofShape`: a line whose curvature grows until it is a circle of radius 1.05-1.4 around the spot 1.3 behind it). At 1.05s it snaps shut (`wofClose`): an explosion where the ends meet, and a crimson pulse.
  - Every foe inside is **Feared** (`wofFear`), and so is any foe that crosses the ring afterwards. Fear lasts 3s: a 50% slow, crimson wisps circling its head, a dread ring at its feet, and dark smoke. While it lasts, each hit on the foe (not damage over time) has a 15% chance to strike critically for double damage (a hurt wrapper; `wofCritFx` draws a star of light).
  - From 4.55s the ring closes in to its middle over 0.4s. It strikes each foe inside as it reaches it, for 150% x `SMA[4]`, and explodes there (`wofBoom`). The ring stops burning foes once it starts closing in.
  - The embers lie in a ring where it stood.
- **Power:** `SMA.firewall` [1, .32, .84, .48, .4], so the cards read 8%, 25%/s, 40% and 60%. `SMG` is [1,1,1,1,1].
  - Measured with `tests/smast-refine.js` (walking foes, 5 runs): 1.28/1.42/1.53/1.71/1.95 against the Uncommon ladder's 1.09/1.23/1.35/1.52/1.74.
  - Simon's +2s burn alone takes Mastery 1 to 1.28. The other masteries were scaled so each level adds its ladder step on top of that.
Tests: `tests/wof-test.js` (23, in run-all as 'wof'). Sheets: `_backup/shots/wof-*.jpg` (levels, the end, the flare, the blast, the ring snapping shut, the ring closing in, M5 wide).

## Sunflare's masteries, as Simon designed them (v391)
`_backup/sunm_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_sunm.py` (args: src html, module, version, date), on top of v390 (it needs the v369 Uncommon masteries, v357's `jvLight` and v375's `tclRing`). Simon, the second Uncommon: "1. Solar radiance ... 5. Heavens gate ..." (the full text is at the top of the module). It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.sunflare` (Corona, Solar Wind, Wider Light, Sunspots, Second Dawn) and wraps `castSunflare` and `nsSunImpact`. Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now.
- **Where it lives:** Mastery 0 is the old cast, untouched; so are casts that are not the hero's own mastered scroll (`snfC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`), such as echoes or buildings. From Mastery 1 the `castSunflare` wrapper catches the sun's group from `scene.add` during the call and dresses it (`snfDress`); the `nsSunImpact` wrapper (the old cast calls it when the sun lands) passes the power on x1.15 and then plays the masteries from the impact point. "The strike" below is the impact's damage with that 15%: 240% x 1.15 of the scroll's power. The numbers live in `SNF`.
- **Solar Radiance (M1, 15%):** a corona of golden rays (`snfRayTex`, two sprites turning opposite ways, a soft bloom, a lens streak) that grows as the sun gathers and flares as it falls, with motes drawn in. The shared effects light (`FXL`, through `jvLight`, so a spell already using it keeps it) lights the board under the sun more and more, and on impact a wide golden glow (`snfGlowDisc`, radius 3.4) and a light pulse light up the area (`snfFlashArea`). `snfLight` is one manager for all of it: each effect asks every frame, the brightest wins, and it hands the light back itself (an effect's end callback only runs in a battle, and `clearWorld` empties the effect list, so it also restarts if its entry vanished).
- **Halo of Dawn (M2, 10% of the strike, Simon left it open):** `snfRing`, a ground shader ring (`snfRingMat`: a golden band with streams and bright arcs flowing round it, a thin white leading line, a ripple and a glowing wake) plus a low curtain of light, racing out to 2.8 in 0.66s. Every foe it passes (flyers too) is hit once. Shimmering motes are left in its wake. A ring drawn as one bright circle read as a plain target circle, which Simon ruled out.
- **Solar Afterimage (M3, 40% of the strike):** `snfGhost`, a ghost of the sun 1.05 over the scorched ground 0.15s after the impact (a glassy fresnel shell `snfGhostMat`, a faint core, its own rays), hanging for 0.95s, then collapsing with motes rushing in into a second flash (`snfSecondFlash`: 40% to every foe within the impact's 1.4, a white then golden flash, two `tclRing`s, a shaft of light).
- **Zenith (M4, 35% of the strike each):** `snfZenith`, four beams in a cross lined up with the road at the impact (1.25 along it, 0.95 across), each hitting the foes within 0.55 of it (a foe at most once), with a golden cross drawn on the ground (`snfLineMat`) and a ring where each lands. They start 0.18s after the strike: landing on the impact's own flash they could not be seen. Beams 3.6 tall: taller ones met the top of the screen as slanted bars, far from the cross.
- **Heaven's Gate (M5, 10% of the strike a second, Simon left it open):** `snfGate`, a celestial halo 2.5 over the ground (two glowing tori and a band of turning rune marks, dashes and short rays, `snfHaloMat`) and a pillar of white-gold light (radius 1.3, `snfBeamMat` with a glowing rim, and a white core) for 3s: 12 ticks counted from when the pillar reaches the ground, motes rising inside, sparks falling from the halo. The pillar is kept see-through: foes stay readable in it.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.10 / 1.36 / 1.81 / 1.96 / 2.44 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74; his 40% afterimage and 35% beams carry Masteries 3-5 above it, and the two open numbers are kept small. All SMA/SMG are 1, and `SM.free.sunflare` is set; `tests/smast2-test.js` now asks a scroll in `SM.free` only for at least 3/4 of the ladder at Masteries 3 and 5, as `smast-test` does for the Commons.
- **Gotcha:** a mastery text number in braces goes through `smPct`, which rounds anything from 10 up to a multiple of 5, so a 12% shows as 10%; use numbers that survive it.
- Tests: `tests/sunm-test.js` (22, in run-all as 'sunm'). Foes stand still where they are put (only the effects are stepped), and `hurt` is wrapped to log the amount each hit is called with, so every number is checked exactly: Mastery 0 as before, 15%, the ring's reach and its one hit per foe, the second flash, the cross's geometry and one hit per foe, 12 counted ticks, clean-up and the light handed back at every mastery, other casters left alone, the real cast path. Sheets: `_backup/shots/sunm-show.jpg` (every mastery, the same moments), `sunm-gate.jpg`, `sunm-afterimage.jpg`, made with `tests/sm-shots.js`.- **Generator kept working:** the `SM.free` rule in `smast2-test.js` moved the noisy-scroll check behind it, so `tests/gen-smast-test.py` now looks for `:['darkpulse','beam'].includes(k)?`; a rarity test made from it carries the same 3/4-of-the-ladder rule for any scroll in `SM.free` (checked: `rare ... riftfang riftfang` reproduces `smast3-test.js` but for that rule).

## Rime Bloom's masteries, as Simon designed them (v392)
`_backup/rbm_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_rbm.py` (args: src html, module, version, date), on top of v391 (it needs v382's `fshFreeze` and v378's `twBodyPts`). Simon redesigned all five (his full text is at the top of the module). It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.rimebloom` (Deep Chill, Ice Petals, Brittle, Frost Grip, Winter Rose), wraps `castRimeBloom` (the masteries play from the bloom's centre) and adds one `hurt` wrapper (the Brittle shatter). Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old bloom (`rbmC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`). Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power, as in the old mastery texts (the bloom itself is 220%). The numbers live in `RBM`.
- **Permafrost (M1):** `rbmGround`, a frost of fine crystal ridges, fern-like branches and twinkling glints (`rbmFrostMat`) a little wider than the bloom (1.5 against 1.3) that lasts until 6.6s, 2s after the old frozen ground. Every 0.3s ground foes on it are slowed 25% and chilled (`e.chillT`), both set to last about 1s after they step off (slowT 1.5, chillT 1.3). The chill is the real status, so the chill synergies work on it.
- **Crystal Eruption (M2, 40%):** `rbmErupt` picks 3 random spots in the area on the road (spread at least 0.75 apart; a crystal on the grass hit nothing), erupting 0.58s, 0.75s and 0.92s after the cast, after the bloom lands at 0.4s. `rbmCrystal`: a tall jagged cluster with a cold glow thrusting up with a frost burst; 40% to the foes within 0.62, then it crumbles.
- **Brittle Ground (M3, 30% bonus, Simon left it open):** the bloom's own hit (`SMF.rimebloom.hit`) leaves foes Brittle for 4s (`e.rbBr`): frosted cracks (`rbmCracks`: white cracks with a cold blue halo, normally blended so they read on any foe, parented to points of the foe's own meshes from `twBodyPts`). The next ice scroll hit on it (`o.el==='ice'` with `o.spell` or `o.smx`, not a damage tick, not the hit that made it Brittle; this scroll's own crystals and petal rings count) shatters it (`rbmShatter`: 30% more, a burst of ice fragments) and ends it. Brittle is used up before the hit runs, so a later Rime Bloom's own hit can make the foe Brittle again.
- **Glacial Prison (M4):** `rbmPrison`, a ring of 18 tall ice walls round the bloom rising one after another; the ground foes inside freeze 1.25s (bosses 0.5s) through `fshFreeze` (`stunE`, so Resolve applies); then the walls shatter into sparkling shards. Flyers are not held.
- **Everfrost Lily (M5, 50%; Simon first named it Eternal Winter, renamed in v393 because the Mythic scroll has that name):** `rbmFlower`, a giant crystal flower from 0.4s: a six-pointed ice lily (three enormous petals alternating with three medium ones, an inner ring of small upright petals, a heart of crystals with a cold glow; faceted Phong petals with a glowing rim `rbmRimMat` and a bright vein). At the end of each second (1.4s, 2.4s, 3.4s after the cast) the next great petal trembles, brightens and shatters (`rbmPetalBurst`) and a ring of ice (`rbmWave`, `rbmRingMat`: a frosty band with crystal spikes along its front) races out to 2.2: 50% to every foe it reaches, flyers too, and their slow is set to 45% for 3s again. Then the rest of the flower shatters.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs, with the factors): 1.09 / 1.28 / 1.41 / 1.52 / 2.29 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Permafrost and Glacial Prison add no damage, so their levels get a hidden factor (`SMG.rimebloom=[1.145,1,1,1.12,1]`); the shatter is set to 30% for Mastery 3; Simon's 50% petal rings carry Mastery 5 above the ladder (`SM.free.rimebloom`).
- **Visual gotchas:** ice drawn before the ground frost (renderOrder 0 against the frost's 3) had its lower half washed out; every mastery mesh is drawn after it (5-7). A flattened crystal (scale y 0.001) still shows as a flat diamond on the ground: walls stay hidden until they rise. A cup of three wide petals read as one big triangle from this camera; six narrow petals opened outward read as a flower.
- Tests: `tests/rbm-test.js` (31, in run-all as 'rbm'). Foes stand still (only the effects are stepped, the game clock moved with them) and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount: Mastery 0 as before; the frost's reach, chill, linger and end; three crystals after the bloom, in the area, on the road, 40% each to their foes only; Brittle on the bloom's foes with cracks, the shatter (30%) by another ice scroll and not by fire, damage ticks or non-scroll hits, the cracks off after the shatter and after 4s, a later bloom shattering it; the prison's 1.25s/0.5s holds, Resolve, flyers and foes outside free, the walls breaking; three petals at 1.4/2.4/3.4s, each ring hitting every foe out to 2.2 once for 50% and refreshing the slow; nothing left behind; other casters untouched; the real cast path; no mastery name shared with a scroll.

## Soul Lantern's masteries, as Simon designed them (v394)
`_backup/slm_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_slm.py` (args: src html, module, version, date), on top of v393 (it needs v389's nerfed lantern, `tclRing` and `jvLight`). Simon redesigned all five (his full text is at the top of the module) and asked: "no low effort effects are acceptable and the spell should look cooler the higher the mastery". It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.lantern` (Brighter Wick, Haunting Light, Twin Lantern, Soul Harvest, Lantern of the Dead, whose burst still fired at the old 8.4s). Soul Lantern keeps its state inside `castLantern`, so the patch adds hooks to it: `slmStart` once the lantern is built, then `SLH.bm` (the beam's damage factor), `SLH.beam` (after each beam), `SLH.tick` (every frame, after the lantern's own animation, so it can dress and override it), `SLH.fade`/`SLH.ext` (Mastery 5 keeps the lantern for its rift) and `SLH.bind` (the mastery's materials go to the lantern's effect, which disposes them). At Mastery 0, for echoes and for other casters `SLH` is null and the lantern is exactly the old one (`slmC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`). Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the beam is 50%, its jump 30%); the numbers live in `SLM`.
- **Brighter Wick (M1, Simon's 15%):** beam i (from 0) deals 1 + 15% x i/4, its jumps too (the 5th beam on +15%), new for every lantern. The charge shows: the flame and a ghost of it grow (`slmDress`), glows sit on the frame's 11 joints and glowing seams on its bars and bands, a soft aura, and the pool (`slmPoolMat`) swirls brighter with five marks round its rim, one lit a beam, a ring pulsing out on every beam.
- **Phantom Bolt (M2, 10%):** `slmRune`, a rune drawn once (`slmRuneTex`: rings, runic marks, a hexagram, an eye) lying over the beam's target, turning faster and drawing in; `SLM.boltT` (0.55s) later it fires a thin shaft of dark light down into the foe and shatters into small violet shards. A rune whose foe died fires nothing.
- **Wandering Souls (M3, 10% each):** two spectral flames (`slmWisp`: a core, a ghost flame `slmFlameTex`, a halo, a ribbon of light `slmTrail` with a wider glow ribbon, purple smoke) orbit the lantern from 0.95s. Every 2s each (the first at 1.6s, the second 1s later) darts out on a curve to a foe within 2.6 that the other is not on and neither hit last, if it can, strikes and comes back: 5 strikes in a lantern's life.
- **Soul Harvest (M4, 25% a soul):** a foe the lantern hit (beam or jump, `SMF.lantern.hit` and `kill`) that dies while it burns sends its soul (`slmSoul`) spiralling down into the pool, where a ghostly face rises (`slmFace`, `slmFaceTex`). Up to 3 are stored, circling the flame (`slmOrbs`), which grows with them; the next beam deals 25% more per soul (x1.75 with 3) and spends them, thicker and paler, souls rushing down it and a face wailing out of the foe.
- **Lantern of the Dead (M5, 10% and a 40% slow for 1.5s):** when its 6.5s are up the lantern rises, its frame opens like a flower (`slmHinge`: the bars and bands swing out on their bottom hinges, the cap lifts and spins) and a swirling void opens beneath it (`slmVoidMat`, `slmVoidGlowMat`, rising tendrils). At 0.72s a ring of ghost fire (`slmFireMat` wall, `slmBandMat` flames and hot line on the ground, `slmScorchMat` behind it) surges out to 2.6: every foe it reaches is hit once (flyers too) and slowed. At 1.5s everything is pulled back into the void, the lantern and its flames too, and at 2s it implodes into one tiny purple spark (`slmSpark`). `slmLight` is one manager for the shared effects light (violet), as Sunflare's is.
- **Visual gotcha:** on the bright plains additive light only washes out to white: the pool, the ghost fire and most of its flame motes are painted in normal blending, and only thin hot lines and sparks are additive. The pool first came out as a flat white disc that way.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs, factors 1): 1.049 / 1.249 / 1.466 / 1.466 / 1.796 against the Uncommon ladder 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742 (Mastery 5's rift read 1.96 at 15%, so it is 10%). The hidden factors put every level on the ladder: `SMG.lantern=[1.043,1,.921,1.038,.97]` (the two flames carry Mastery 3 past it, so its factor is under 1; Soul Harvest only pays when foes die, which the measure has none of). Not in `SM.free`.
- Tests: `tests/slm-test.js` (32, in run-all as 'slm'). Foes stand still, `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount, and `window.setTimeout` (the jumps land 70ms later) is queued on the test clock; the Monarch's aura burst is off (it hits the test foes when foes die). Mastery 0 as before; the ramp on beams and jumps; the dressing; 5 runes and bolts at the right times for 10%, none for a dead foe; two flames, their strike times, 10% each, never the same foe twice running, back in orbit; souls from foes it hit (also by its own killing blow), a face, at most 3, x1.75 and spent, nothing left behind (each soul and its ribbon share one box, so both go with the effect: a ribbon added to the scene on its own stayed behind and failed the clean-up checks of later suites), none after it went out or from foes it never hit; the rift's timeline, one 10% hit within 2.6 (flyers too), none outside, the slow, everything gone and the light handed back; other casters untouched; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Road-direction fix: Sunflare's Zenith, Rime Bloom's crystals (v395)
`_backup/p_dirfix.py` (args: src html, version, date), a text patch found while building Grasping Shadows' masteries. A road segment's angle is `atan2(dx,dz)`, so (sin, cos) runs along the road and (cos, -sin) across it; the v391 and v392 modules had them swapped. Sunflare's Zenith cross (`snfZenith`) was turned a quarter, its long arms (1.25) pointing across the road onto the grass: now they run along it. Rime Bloom's crystals scattered sideways along the road: now across it (they still land on it). A "Fix:" line in the Sunflare patch-notes section and a changelog entry. `tests/sunm-test.js` and `tests/rbm-test.js` had the same swap in their helpers, so the cross check could not see it; both are corrected (the corrected sunm row fails on v394 and passes from v395).

## Grasping Shadows' masteries, as Simon designed them (v396)
`_backup/gsm_b.js`, inserted before the boot line by `_backup/p_gsm.py` (args: src html, module, version, date), on top of v395 (it needs v381's `gbLinks`). Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the module). It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.grasp` (Long Grip, Crushing Grip, Outer Ring, Drag Under, Grave Hand), wraps `castGrasp` (the base grab's own hits note who was grabbed, `c.gr`) and `pushE`. Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old cast. Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the grab is 80%); the numbers live in `GSM`. "Toward the rune" moves only foes already past it (closer to the keep): pulling the ones before it forward would help them.
- **Shackled Souls (M1):** when the hands let go (`GSM.rel` 1.45s), `gsmChains` links grabbed ground foes standing next to each other on the road (within 1.8) with violet chains for 2s (Gravebind's `gbLinks`): they shoot out, pull taut and rattle, then shatter. A `pushE` wrapper: when another effect pushes a chained foe back, the chained foes still ahead of it are pulled back toward it by half as much (`pushE` again, so Resolve applies; never forward).
- **Shadow Snare (M2, 65%):** `gsmSnare`: cracks race along the road both ways (`gsmRoadCracks`, random-walk cracks with spurs) and three great bony fingers (`gsmFinger`: three bones with dark knuckles and a claw) rise and sweep in like a closing claw. The ground foes within 2.4 that the hands did not grab take 65%; those past the rune are dragged back toward it (at most 0.6), the others are seized for 0.3s.
- **The Grasping Circle (M3, 35%):** at 2.15s `gsmCircle`: a ring of 8 shadow hands at 1.6 rising one after another and sweeping in to 0.42, trailing violet smoke: 35% to each ground foe a hand touches, once, and a nudge back toward the rune (at most 0.25).
- **Dragged Below (M4, 130%):** at 1.45s `gsmBelow` takes the cast's target if it was grabbed and is not a boss, else the toughest grabbed foe that is not. It is held (`stunE` 1.35s; if Resolve will not give 1.2s it is slammed instead), the road splits under it, hands pull it down, it is hidden and `e.burrow` (towers skip it) for 1s, then it is thrown up out of the road and lands for 130%. If it died meanwhile, a dark burst deals the 130% round the spot. A boss takes a great hand's slam for 130% (`gsmSlam`).
- **Hand of the Abyss (M5, 150% + 100%):** at 2.75s `gsmAbyss`: the rune grows to 2.0 over a vast fissure and five colossal bony fingers rise round it and close over it: 150% to every foe inside, flyers too; ground foes that are not bosses are lifted (`stunE` 1s, Resolve applies; their model raised by `gsmY`); then the hand plunges back underground for 100% more and the cracks seal.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.09 / 1.56 / 2.10 / 2.73 / 7.6 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Shackled Souls deals no damage, so its level gets a hidden factor (`SMG.grasp=[1.094,1,1,1,1]`); from Mastery 2 Simon's numbers carry it far above the ladder, the Hand of the Abyss most of all (`SM.free.grasp`). Simon, told Mastery 5 is about 4.4x the ladder: "Keep my numbers for Grasping Shadows."
- **Gotchas:** a road segment's angle is `atan2(dx,dz)`: (sin, cos) runs along the road, (cos, -sin) across it (the direction fix corrected Sunflare's Zenith and Rime Bloom's crystals, which had them swapped). An Euler yaw plus tilt on one object tilts the wrong way; turn a pivot group, tilt the child. Effect end callbacks only run during a battle: anything a foe carries (hidden, burrowed, lifted) is put back in the effect's own last frame.
- Tests: `tests/gsm-test.js` (31, in run-all as 'gsm'). Foes stand on the road (speed 1 so pushes cost Resolve as usual, but only the effects are stepped), and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount: Mastery 0 as before; the chains (who, how long), the tug (half, never forward, gone after 2s), `pushE` unchanged without chains; the snare's 65% to the missed foes, the drag back (at most 0.6) and the seize; the circle's 35% once and its nudge (never forward); Dragged Below's hiding, burrow, hold, 130% on landing, one foe only, a boss's slam, Resolve, the burst if it dies under; the Hand's 150% to everything within 2 (flyers, bosses), the lift (not bosses), the 100% plunge; nothing left behind; other casters untouched; the real cast path.

## Mastery progress in battle (v397)
Simon: "add a feature to the game where you can see your mastery progress when you're in a game". The first update made in a cloud session: written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Mastery progress in battle`, just before the boot line), with no `_backup` module or patch script.
- **Button:** `mpgSync()` (from `updHud` and `showOverlay` wrappers and a 500 ms timer) shows `#mpgHud` during a run, on the right under the top bar, under `#artHud` when the artifacts show. A green badge counts the mastery levels reached this run (`mpgUps`). It is not in the top bar itself: that is already full on a 360 px phone (the menu button is cut off there).
- **Panel:** `mpgToggle()` opens `#liveMast`, styled like `#liveDmg` (opening either closes the other: a `liveDmgToggle` wrapper). First the buildings on the board, plus any that earned XP this run and were sold (`mpgBuildings`, most XP this run first), then the battle hero's scrolls that earn mastery (`mpgScrolls`: `scrollItems()` through v388's `smOn`, plus any that earned XP this run); the hero's other scrolls are named in one line. Each row (`mpgRow`): thumbnail or scroll art, the XP this run, the diamonds and level, a bar whose part earned this run glows (`mpgBar`, `.mpg-new`), "Mastery N reached this run!" and the next upgrade. It re-renders every 500 ms while open, only when its HTML changes. A row opens `mastOpen`/`smOpen` and pauses the run until the card is closed (`mpgCard`, as v341's artifacts card).
- **Layout:** while the button column shows, `body.mpg-rail` moves `#liveDmg` and `#liveMast` to `right:52px`, beside the buttons, so neither panel covers the artifacts or mastery button (the damage panel used to cover `#artHud`).
