- **XP this run:** `RSTAT.mx = {b:{type:xp}, s:{scroll:xp}}` (`mpgRun()`), filled by `mastGain` and `smGain` wrappers with the XP they really added, so the `smOn` gate and the menu are respected. `RSTAT` was already saved with the run (`serializeRun`) and restored by `loadRun`, so a resumed run keeps it; `resetRun` starts it over.
- **Phone top bar:** at full size the bar needs about 540px when the camera button shows and the numbers are late-game (123K gold, 12.3K gems, wave 188), so on phones the buttons were squeezed to 17px and the menu button went off the right edge (even at 412px). Three `@media` tiers after the `#hud` rules: up to 560px (13px numbers, 30px buttons, the wave as "W 188"), 410px (12px, 28px) and 359px (11px, 26px; there the gems number alone may shorten with an ellipsis). The buttons never shrink. Measured in headless Chromium at 320 to 600px with all five buttons: nothing overflows; above 560px nothing changed.
- **Tests that assumed a played account** (they failed on a brand-new browser profile, as in the cloud): `tests/dev-test.js` now counts a key the account never had as its `DEV_KEYS` default, which is what `devDisable` puts back (was: 1 row failing on `combos`, `dex`, `mast`, `smast`, `mapIntro`); `tests/smast-test.js` gives the account `riftfang` before casting it, as it already did for `hexspark` (was: an exception in `fireScroll`).
- Patch notes: a section at the top of `NOTES_HTML` (still `masteries-1`) and a CLOG entry.
- Tests: `tests/mpg-test.js` (32, in run-all as 'mpg'): XP counted for the run (Common scrolls only outside developer mode, nothing outside a run), the badge, the button under the top bar and the artifacts, the rows and their order, the bar's two parts, a level reached, live updates, the cards pausing and resuming, the damage panel swap, the panel beside the buttons, saved and resumed with the run, gone in the menu.

## Spell mastery dropdown (v398)
Simon: "you should be able to click on equiped spells, a little UI will pop up under it like an extension of the UI where they see their mastery progress and level of mastery and when they click the mastery they see what it does, make it clean not chaotic". A block before the boot line, on top of v397 (after its block).
- **Chips:** a `scrollHtml` wrapper turns each equipped spell chip (`.chip.spl`, in the battle hero's sheet) into a `<button data-spm="key">`. The hero screen's scroll slots keep opening the scroll card as before, and the scroll buttons at the bottom of a run still cast.
- **Panel:** `spmHtml(k)` builds `.spm`, inserted right after the chips row with a notch pointing at the open chip (`spmPlace` sets `--nx`): the scroll's name and rarity, `smBar` (level, stars, XP), and the five masteries as one-line rows (`data-spml`: number in its `MAST_COL` colour, filled once unlocked; name; "Next" on the next one). Tapping a row unfolds its `smDesc` text, `smPowerLabel` and either "Unlocked" or the XP still needed; one row at a time. A scroll that has no masteries yet (`!smOn`, i.e. above Common outside developer mode, or no `SM_D`) gets one line saying so.
- **State:** `SPM={k,i}` (open scroll, open row), so the sheet's own re-renders keep the panel; taps redraw only that hero's `.scrolls` box (`spmRedraw`). The chip again or the x closes it; `closeSheet` forgets it. `spmTick` (every 500ms) refreshes open panels so the bar follows XP earned during a run.
- Tests: `tests/spm-test.js` (22, in run-all as 'spm'): the chips are buttons; the panel opens under the chips and marks the chip; level, bar, five rows (unlocked, next); a row's text, power and XP needed; one row at a time, folding; a sheet re-render keeps it; it follows new XP; switching chips; closing (chip, x, `closeSheet`); a Rare scroll's one line; the panel only under its own hero; developer mode; no console errors.

## Graveburst's masteries, as Simon designed them (v399)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` in a cloud session (search `Graveburst's masteries, as Simon designed them`, just before the boot line), on top of v397. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the module). It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.graveburst` (Bone Shards, Open Grave, Deathmark, Aftershock, Mass Burial) and wraps `castGraveburst`. Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old cast. Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the burst is 260%); the numbers and timings live in `GVB`. The masteries play round the crater (the spot the runes mark when it is cast), one after another so they never stand in each other's way: the fissures and the pit at the burst (1.15s), the warrior 0.2s later, the cage as the warrior crumbles (1.45s after the burst), the hands as the cage splinters.
- **Gravequake (M1, 45%):** `gvbQuake`: a shockwave and three fissures from the crater's rim, two along the road both ways and one across the ground, with spurs. Each part (three layered planes: a soft violet glow with `gvbLineTex`, a dark core, a hot line) flares as the crack front (7 u/s) reaches it, spits dark energy, then crumbles into dust. A ground foe within 0.32 of an erupting part takes 45%, once.
- **The Unburied (M2, 100%):** `gvbUnburied`: a spectral skeleton (translucent bone, a violet glow in the ribcage, sparks in the eye sockets, a notched greatsword with a rust-and-steel canvas texture, `gvbRustTex`) climbs out of the crater facing up the road, winds up over its shoulder and sweeps round about 330 degrees with a violet band trailing the blade: 100% to every foe within 1.9 the blade passes over, flyers too (the one right behind it is spared), then it breaks apart into dark particles and sinks. The arms are bones stretched between points every frame (`gvbPlace`).
- **The Open Pit (M3, 35%):** `gvbPit`: a soft violet-black hole of the crater's size, rubble slipping in round its rim, for 4s. 0.6s after the burst and then every 0.9s (4 times), cracks flash under the ground foes standing in it and 0.32s later glossy obsidian spikes thrust up there: 35% to each foe still in the crater within 0.55 of a spike. A foe that walks out in time is spared.
- **Ribcage Prison (M4, 70%):** `gvbCage`: five pairs of tapered ribs (`gvbTaper`, a tube narrowing toward its end) tear up through the road on both sides and meet a spine over it, violet energy crackling between them (`nsArc`). The ground foes inside when it closes (not bosses, not flyers) cannot walk out: one that reaches the ribs is put back at the farthest point still inside (`gvbDmax`) and stopped there with `stunE` for the time left, so Resolve applies and a foe it frees walks out. After 1.5s the cage splinters into falling bone: 70% to every foe within it, flyers too, half (35%) to bosses.
- **Hand of the Underworld (M5, 100% + 100%):** `gvbUnderworld`: the crater tears open into an abyss of 2.1 right after the burst. When the cage splinters, two colossal skeletal hands (`gvbHand`: forearm bones into the ground, a palm, four fanned fingers and a thumb from v396's `gsmFinger`) rise from both sides of the road, sweep in and hook their fingers down: 100% to every foe in the abyss, flyers and bosses too. Ground foes that are not bosses are held (`stunE`, Resolve) and dragged under on spectral chains (`gbLinks`, to the palms) as the hands plunge (hidden, `e.burrow` so towers skip them); 0.3s later the grave slams shut for 100% more to every foe in it, and the dragged ones are back on the road.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.24 / 1.48 / 1.97 / 2.38 / 3.26 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Every level is above the ladder, so no hidden factor (`SMG.graveburst` all 1); Mastery 5 is about 1.9x the ladder (Grasping Shadows' is about 4.4x). Simon's numbers are kept (`SM.free.graveburst`).
- **Visual gotchas:** additive blending washes out to white on the bright plains, so the warrior's trail and the pit are normal-blended, and the cage and the hands use an aged-ivory bone (`gvbBone`; Graveburst's own bones are ivory) instead of the lilac one. Seen from the game's high camera, fingers lying side by side read as bars: the hands need a palm and fanned, hooked fingers. Chains to a point below the ground are hidden by it, so they run to the palms.
- **Noticed, not changed:** the base Graveburst's burst damage (and its flash) is centred on its target foe at the moment of the burst, which may have walked on up to about a tile during the 1.15s, while the runes, cracks and hands stay where it was cast. The masteries use the cast spot.
- Tests: `tests/gvb-test.js` (27, in run-all as 'gvb'). Foes stand where they are put (walkers walk for real), and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount and time: Mastery 0 as before; the fissures reach foes up and down the road (45% once), none beyond them or to flyers; the warrior's 100% to foes round it and a flyer, not to the one behind it or out of reach; four warnings and 35% spikes for a foe that stays, none for one that walks out after the warning, one outside or a flyer; the cage holds a walking foe at the ribs and lets a boss and an Unstoppable foe through, then 70% (35% to the boss), nothing outside; the hands' 100% to everything in the abyss, the ground non-bosses dragged under (hidden, burrowed), the seal's 100% and everyone back on the road; the order of it all; nothing left behind; other casters untouched; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Catch Flame's masteries, as Simon designed them (v401)
A block before the boot line, on top of v398. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the block) and asked for "no low effort effects" and for the look to grow with the level; after trying it he asked for natural explosions instead of "the low effort just one coloured thing" and for a new Mastery 5. It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.catchflame` (Hotter Flame, Ember Burst, Wildfire, Ash Patch, Firestorm). A `castCatchFlame` wrapper runs every cast through `cfmCast`: the same seed (100%), leaps (60%, reach 1.35), burn (25% a second for 4s), ten-foe limit, 6s window and Wildfire combo as `castCatchFlame`, with the masteries hooked in when the cast is mastered (`cfmC(o)`).
- **Explosions (`cfmBoom`):** every ignition, Kindling's eruption and embers, Backdraft's burst, a fork's split, a vortex's blast and the eruption are 3D fireballs instead of `flash`/`shockwave`/`ringFx` (single-colour meshes). A fireball is a few lobes, each a sphere (`ico(1,4)`, shared) pushed out by animated 3D noise (`CFM_BOOM_VS`) and coloured by heat (`CFM_BOOM_FS`): white-hot, yellow, orange, deep red, then smoke that frays away along the noise and rises; the middle lobe has an additive glow shell for its hottest parts; edges fade softly. With it: sparks, falling embers, a ring of dust rolling out, a scorch mark, smoke wisps. Options: `lobes`, `T`, `mush` (a column and a mushroom cap), `skirt` (lobes rolling out along the ground), `ground:false`, `shake`. Ignitions grow with the mastery level.
- **Kindling (M1, 25%):** when the seed lands, `cfmKindle`: the foe erupts (a bigger fireball and a column of flame) and spits 3 embers (`cfmBolt`: a core, a halo and a ribbon trail, bowing out to the sides) at the nearest unlit foes within 2.2, each bursting in a small fireball. They only hurt; those foes stay unlit for the chain.
- **Backdraft (M2, 70%):** each foe the cast lights gets an `smOnDie` for its 4s of burning, set before the ignition's own hit (so a foe the ignition kills bursts too). `cfmBackdraftFx`: the flames are sucked into a shrinking core that grows hotter (0.24s), then it explodes (with a skirt of fire and `myFireRing`): 70% to every foe within 1.25; a fragment (`cfmBolt` with a helix) spirals into each unlit foe it hit while places remain, and lights it (no extra hit), so the chain goes on from it. A foe killed by a backdraft can backdraft in turn.
- **Forking Flames (M3):** every third leap of the chain (`CF.sp`, Wildfire's leaps aside) with a second unlit foe within 1.8 and two places left is a fork (`cfmFork`): the fireball flies a little way, bursts, and two helical streams curve apart onto both foes, 60% each, both counted toward the ten.
- **Firestorm (M4, 45%):** the first time each burning foe passes the fire on (leaps, forks, Wildfire), `cfmVortex`: a three-shelled fire funnel (`s6FunnelGeo` with the twisting flame shader, a smoky skin outside) twists up round it with embers whirling at its foot, blasts 45% to every foe within 0.95 at 0.3s, then collapses into a shower of embers.
- **Living Inferno (M5, 180%):** once per cast, when the tenth foe has caught fire (`CF.n` at ten and no flame still flying) or, if the chain stops short, when the 6s window ends with at least 5 lit: `cfmErupt` on the last foe lit. Its fire swells for 1.3s (`CFM.infGrow`: seven flame tufts growing wider and taller, spinning out, raging, over glowing cracks, with embers and smoke pouring up and the camera trembling), draws in for an instant, then erupts in a big `cfmBoom` (a mushroom column and a skirt of fire), `myFireRing` and burning chunks (`cfmChunk`): 180% to every foe within 2.1 of that spot, flyers too. (The first version's tornado of streams is gone.)
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.11 / 1.49 / 1.63 / 2.12 / 3.17 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Kindling alone falls short (1.02), so Mastery 1 gets a hidden factor (`SMG.catchflame[0]=1.085`), as Grasping Shadows' first level; from Mastery 2 Simon's numbers carry it above the ladder (`SM.free.catchflame`).
- Tests: `tests/cfm-test.js` (32, in run-all as 'cfm'). Foes stand in a row on the road (only the effects are stepped), and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit: Mastery 0 hits as before (only ignitions logged); the embers (3, nearest, 25%, no ignition); Backdraft on a foe the seed kills, its 70% within 1.25, the fragments' ignitions and the chain going on (at most ten), none after the fire is out; the forks (every third leap, two foes, 60% each, ten in all); one vortex per source, 45% within 0.95; the eruption on the tenth foe (once) 1.3s later where it stands, 180% within 2.1 (flyers too), at the window's end with 6 lit, none with 4; each needs its level; unmastered casts get no masteries; every level with deaths and Wildfire runs clean and frees its trails and cracks; nothing left behind.

## Dark Pulse's masteries, as Simon designed them (v400)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Dark Pulse's masteries`, just before the boot line), as v397 was, with no `_backup` module or patch script. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the block) and asked: "no low effort effects are acceptable and spell should get visually better as masteries go". It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.darkpulse` (Dread, Echo Pulse, Shadow Tendrils, Undertow, Black Tide) and wraps `castDarkPulse`. Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old pulse (`dpmC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`). Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the pulse is 60%), except Echo Pulse, which is 45% of the ring's own damage, as Simon wrote it. The numbers live in `DPM`.
- **How it hangs together:** the ring's own hits (`SMF.darkpulse.hit`, once per foe) play Masteries 1 and 4; the wrapper plays the rest from `S`, the cast: its centre (the hero), when the ring starts (`S.t0`), how long it runs (`S.T`) and its reach (`S.R`). `dpmR` gives the front's radius at any moment with the old cast's own ease (0.2 + R x ease, so it ends at 0.2 + R) and `dpmTAt` the moment it reaches a radius, so everything rides the same front, the old ring's (0.42s charge, 0.62s) or Event Horizon's.
- **Void Resonance (M1, 25%):** `dpmResonance`: the struck foe flashes violet (`dpmGlow`), and 0.1s later a ripple (`dpmRipple`: a violet band with dark tongues, `dpmBandMat`, and a low curtain) bursts round where it stands now, 25% to every other foe within 0.7 (flyers too), with a dark arc from it to each. A lone foe takes nothing; in a pack the ripples overlap into a chain.
- **Echo Pulse (M2, 45% of the ring):** `dpmEcho`: broken violet streaks and arcs (`dpmTraceMat`) are drawn on the ground behind the front; when it reaches full size they flow back to the hero, compress into a bright orb (a white core, a violet glow, a turning ring and rays) and burst out as a thinner, faster ring (0.34s against 0.62s) with lightning running along it: 45% of the ring's damage, once per foe it reaches, flyers too.
- **Rupture Wave (M3, 40%):** `dpmRupture`: three lines erupt behind the front at 34%, 63% and 92% of its reach, 0.14s after it passes (plus 0.05s each). `dpmErupt`: a jagged glowing fissure round the ring (`dpmCrackMat`), a line of obsidian spikes (one `InstancedMesh`, up to 110 spikes, `frustumCulled` off since an instanced mesh is culled by its single spike's bounds), a sharp ring of light, flashes and debris; 40% to the ground foes within 0.3 of the line; the spikes crumble into dark particles.
- **Black Sun (M4, 50%):** `dpmSun` catches the old cast's group from `scene.add` during the call, hides the orb's two violet shells and follows the orb with a black sun (`dpmSunParts`: a black body with a violet rim `dpmBodyMat`, a fresnel shell, a turning accretion disc `dpmDiscMat`, a dark corona and a violet one from `dpmRayTex`, a photon ring `dpmRingTex`), motes drawn in; it collapses as the pulse is released. `dpmWall`: a wall of violet-black energy 1.45 tall riding the ring (`dpmWallMat`: a painted body with veins and a jagged crest, and crawling lightning added on top, with `zap` arcs over it), every foe the ring hits taking 50% more (`dpmSunHit`, flyers too, as the ring hits them). When the ring is done the wall cracks apart (`uD`) into 34+ dark glass shards (instanced) that fly out and fall.
- **Event Horizon (M5, 150% + 30%):** `dpmHorizon` replaces the old cast. A dense black sphere (the black sun's parts, larger) grows over the hero for 1.05s while 14 rocks (instanced) and motes spiral into it, dust lifts, a lensing disc darkens the ground (`dpmLensMat`), the board darkens (`darkTarget` up to 0.32, a vignette) and a thin violet halo forms. Then it implodes (`dpmImplode`): 30% to every foe within the normal pulse's reach (flyers too); ground foes already past the hero (closer to the keep) are drawn back toward it (at most 0.35, `pushE`, so Resolve applies), the others are seized for 0.25s (pulling the ones before it forward would help them; held foes are left alone). A violet shell shrinks into the sphere, which collapses to a pinprick with a streak, and at 1.25s a colossal ring 30% wider than the pulse runs out in 0.8s (a band with ripples running ahead of it, `uRp`, a hot line, a scorch behind, obsidian spikes erupting where it passes, dust and arcs): 150% instead of 60%, once per foe, with the old cast's knockback (0.38, bosses 0.1), 30% slow for 1.5s and Torn Asunder (held foes take double and are not pushed). Masteries 1-4 ride it (the echo is then 45% of 150%; the wall is 1.2x as tall and more see-through, so the foes stay readable). The hero's push pose is stretched so it lands with the release (`anim.d`). The light comes back on release (`darkTarget` is put back only if it is still this effect's value).
- **Visual gotchas:** on the bright plains additive light washes out to white, so the bands, traces, cracks and the wall's body are painted (normal blending) and only thin lines, sparks and lightning are additive (the same lesson as v394). The ripples first read as plain white target circles: they are violet now, with dark tongues inside and an arc to each foe they reach. A new `InstancedMesh` draws every instance at full size until its matrices are set, so the rupture spikes and Event Horizon's rocks start hidden (`dpmHideI`), and the black sun's group stays hidden until its first frame places it (it sat at the world's origin for one frame). `flash()` with normal blending draws a flat octagon: none are used; ground glows go through Sunflare's `snfGlowDisc` (`dpmGlowDisc`) and lights through Soul Lantern's `slmLight` manager (`dpmLight`).
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, all SMA/SMG are 1 and `SM.free.darkpulse` is set. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 2.49 / 3.14 / 3.94 / 5.60 / 11.7 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Void Resonance alone is 2.3x the ladder on a pack (in a tight crowd each foe sits in several ripples), and Event Horizon's 150% ring, wider reach and everything riding it make Mastery 5 about 6.7x.
- Tests: `tests/dpm-test.js` (37, in run-all as 'dpm'). The hero stands 0.7 beside the middle of the road and foes are put on the road at chosen distances from it; they stand still (only the effects are stepped, the game clock moved with them) and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount: the card (names, numbers, factors); Mastery 0 as before; the ripples (who, 25% to neighbours only, never the source); the echo after full size, 27% once each, flyers, its reach; three ruptures at their radii, 40% on each line only, not flyers; the black sun, the wall's 50% once each (flyers), the fracture; Event Horizon: no old pulse, the darkness and its return, the implosion's 30% within the normal reach, drawn back or seized, the release, 150% to everything within the wider reach, the knockback, Masteries 1-4 riding it, Torn Asunder; nothing left behind at every mastery; other casters untouched; the real cast path at Masteries 1, 3 and 5.

## Bramble Patch's masteries, as Simon designed them (v402)
Written in a cloud session straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Bramble Patch's masteries, as Simon designed them`, just before the boot line), on top of v399. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the module). It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.bramble` (Sharper Thorns, Snagging Vines, Spreading Brambles, Thorn Burst, Rose Garden) and wraps `castBramble`. Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old patch. Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the patch itself is 45% a second); the numbers and timings live in `BPM`. Everything plays on the patch (radius 1.15, 6.4s, the spot where it was cast) and, like the patch, reaches only ground foes unless it says otherwise.
- **Barbed Thicket (M1, 35%):** `bpmSnap` at 1, 2 ... 6s: a thorny cane (`twLash`, Thorn Whip's lash, in the bramble colours) bends over each foe in the patch (up to 8 drawn) and whips down; 0.16s later 35% to every ground foe that was in it, with red thorns, leaves and green sparks.
- **Snaring Vines (M2, 0.6s; bosses 40%):** a per-cast timer adds up each ground foe's time in the patch; at 1s `bpmSnare` binds it once: three green vines spiral round its ankles and tighten, `stunE` 0.6s (Resolve applies) and `heldT` (it counts as Held, so the patch's nature ticks get Deep Roots' +40% meanwhile), then snap into leaves. A boss is not held: the vines strain round its legs (the model shakes) for 40%.
- **Thornheart (M3, 25%):** `bpmHeart`: a closed crimson bud (seven cupped petals with thorned rims, `bpmPetal`) on a thorny stem rises in the middle from 0.5s. At 2, 4 and 6s it swells and glows, unfurls with an overshoot and fires a needle (`bpmNeedle`, homing on where the foe is) at every ground foe in the patch for 25%, plus stray needles for show (`bpmStray`), then closes; it sinks after the last.
- **Wall of Thorns (M4, 100%; bosses 40%):** `bpmWall` at 3.2s: woven bramble canes with berries and six red roses (`twRose`) surge up across the road at the patch's keep-side edge (road distance + 0.98). For 1s a ground foe on that road that was not already past it is stopped there (`stunE` for the time left, Resolve applies; a foe it frees walks through) and the wall trembles while it is pressed. A boss pushes through and is cut for 40% (Simon gave no number: 40% as in Snaring Vines). Then it bursts apart into the patch: 100% to every ground foe in it.
- **Garden of Ruin (M5, 180% + 35%):** `bpmRuin` at 5.5s: the ground splits and nine thick roots arch up round the patch, eight brambles coil up and six huge crimson petals with thorned rims rise from its edge; at 6.3s they slam shut: 180% to every foe in the patch, flyers too (they are inside the flower), and the ground foes that are not bosses are held for the pause (`stunE`). The petals pulse green (their red muted: green glow on red reads yellow) and at 6.9s burst outward in a storm of red petals and leaves; a thorn flies at each foe within 3 tiles (up to 14, flyers too) for 35%; the roots sink and a dark green stain stays on the road for 3s.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.73 / 1.78 / 2.03 / 2.28 / 3.13 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Every level is above the ladder (Barbed Thicket alone adds 35% a second to the patch's 45%), so no hidden factor (`SMG.bramble` all 1); Simon's numbers are kept (`SM.free.bramble`).
- **Gotchas:** three.js r128 has no `BufferGeometry.applyQuaternion` (`applyMatrix4` with a rotation matrix instead), and no `BufferGeometryUtils` (`bpmMerge`). A rotation put on a foe's model (the boss straining) has to be put back when the effect ends.
- Tests: `tests/bpm-test.js` (27, in run-all as 'bpm'): Mastery 0 as before (its 11.25% ticks); the snaps, six of 35%, none outside or to a flyer; the snare after 1s (0.6s, Held), a boss's 40%, an Unstoppable foe not held, a flyer never snared, a foe walking in later snared 1s after it entered, once a cast; three volleys of 25%, none outside or to a flyer; the wall holding a walking foe, a boss pushing through for 40%, a freed foe and one already past walking on, its 100% collapse; the flower's 180% (a boss and a flyer too), holding the ground foes, the thorns for 35% within 3 tiles; the order of it all; nothing left behind; other casters untouched; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Basic Beam's masteries, as Simon designed them (v403)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Basic Beam's masteries`, just before the boot line), with no `_backup` module or patch script. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the block) and asked again: "NO LOW EFFORT EFFECTS are acceptable". It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.beam` (Searing Line, Prism Edge, Blinding, Lingering Light, Solar Lance) and wraps `castBeam`. Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old beam (`bemC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`). Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the beam is 225%); the numbers live in `BEM`. Built on origin/main (v397), alongside v400 (Dark Pulse, its own branch); it shares no code with it.
- **How it hangs together:** the old beam still fires (`bemFire` calls the saved `castBeam`); its hits run `SMF.beam.hit` (once per foe), which plays Shatterlight and notes who it hit (`S.hits`). `bemGeo` works out the beam's line as `castBeam` does (start at the hero's top, direction to the target, end `len` along it), and the masteries play along it, timed from the moment it fires (`BEM.TF`, 0.24s after the call). From Mastery 2 the beam ends where the prism catches it (`bemCatch`): 0.8 past the last foe on its line (within 0.42), at least 1.2 from the hero, at most the old length, so the prism and its beams are where the foes are rather than out on the grass (the old end was often far beyond them, as Solar Lance found in v369).
- **Shatterlight (M1, 35%):** `bemShatter`: a white flash, a lens streak and a small star at the struck foe, 18 glass shards (one `InstancedMesh`, `bemGlass`) bursting out to both sides of the beam with glowing trails, and a shard (`bemShard`) flying to each other foe within 0.95 (flyers too): 35% each. A foe near several struck foes takes a shard from each.
- **Prism Split (M2, 65%):** `bemPrism`: a crystal (a glass octahedron, an iridescent fresnel rim `bemIrisMat` running the colours of a prism, a white heart, a turning ring) forms at the beam's end as it fires, spins faster as it catches it and flares, then fires three thin beams (`bemRay`) 3.4 long, coloured cold blue, white and lavender: 65% to every foe within 0.3 of each, once per beam. `bemPrismDirs` aims them where each pierces the most foes (any direction but within 30 degrees of straight back along the beam, at least 24 degrees apart); any left over fan out round the beam's line. Then it cracks into glittering glass (`bemGlassBurst`).
- **Lightbound Mirrors (M3, 75%):** `bemMirrors`: three hexagonal mirrors (a glinting face `bemMirrorMat`, a double frame of light) at 28%, 52% and 76% along the beam, 0.32 to alternate sides, spinning into place; each is turned so its face's normal halves the angle between the light coming in and going out, so it truly reflects the beam onto its target. The white core ricochets hero, mirror, mirror, mirror, with lines back across them (the web), then each fires a reflected beam 2.8 long at a different group (`bemMirrorTargets`: the densest foes within 3.2 of it, at least 0.9 from another mirror's target): 75% to every foe within 0.3 of it. Then they shatter.
- **Heaven's Verdict (M4, 100%):** `bemVerdict`: a glowing line along the path on the ground (`bemStrip`, `bemStripMat`: a cold shadow beside it so the light reads on the bright plains, and the line itself), an elliptical halo the length of the path 3.1 above it (two thin tori and 28 points of light circling, light raining down from it) gathering from 0.5s after the beam, and at 0.95s a column of light (Sunflare's `snfBeamMat` on an elongated open cylinder hanging from the halo, grown downward in 0.12s) crashing down: 100% to every foe the beam hit, wherever it is now, a white flash over the board, glows and dust along the path; it thins and dissolves into drifting white and gold motes.
- **Absolute Radiance (M5, 150%):** `bemStar` charges a miniature star over the hero for 0.62s before the old beam is cast (a white heart, a spiked star `bemStarTex`, a blue corona, a lens streak, three geometric rings: a circle, a hexagon and a triangle of thin bars turning on their own axes, and a glow behind the hero, away from the camera, so the hero stands out against it); the old charge orb is hidden and the hero's push is stretched to land with it (`anim.d`). When the beam fires, `bemBlade`: its white scar widens into a corridor of light on the ground and a blade (a central sheet and two fainter walls, `bemWallMat`, soft-topped, streaked) sweeping out along the path in 0.22s: 150% to every foe within 0.95 of it as the front passes (flyers too), the screen washed white; at the end `bemStarburst` (two turning spiked stars, crossed lens streaks, ten rays of light along the ground, a faint ring) and the corridor collapses into sparkles. Masteries 1-4 ride it.
- **Visual gotchas:** a new `InstancedMesh` draws every instance at full size until its matrices are set, and a mesh made at scale 1 shows at scale 1 for the frame before its effect first runs: a 2-unit glass shard and a unit cylinder flashed as white slabs in the shots. `bemHide` starts instanced meshes hidden and `bemRay` starts its cylinders at scale 0.0001. Additive light on the bright plains turns to white blobs: the lights (`bemLight`, its own cold-white manager) are kept low, the column is see-through (only its streaks and rim), and the corridor's glow fades to nothing at the strip's sides (a hard edge read as a white rectangle).
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, all SMA/SMG are 1 and `SM.free.beam` is set. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 4 runs): 1.57 / 1.57 / 2.20 / 2.57 / 3.85 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Prism Split adds nothing there (the measure's foes stand in one line along the road, straight back along the beam from the prism); on a real board it depends on where the foes are.
- Tests: `tests/bem-test.js` (32, in run-all as 'bem'). The beam is cast at a target 1.5 east of the hero, so it runs along +x; foes are put where the test wants them and stand still (only the effects are stepped), and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount: the card; Mastery 0 as before; the shards (once per struck foe, 35% to the foes near each, never itself, flyers); the prism's three beams after the beam, 65% to the foes on them, never back toward the hero; three mirrors, three different groups, 75% after the beam; the column after the halo, 100% to every foe the beam hit even after it moved, none to the others; the star before the beam, the beam's 225%, the blade's 150% within 0.95 (flyers) and not beyond, the white wash and the starburst, Masteries 1-4 riding it; nothing left behind at every mastery; other casters untouched; the real cast path at Masteries 1, 3 and 5.

## Ball Lightning's masteries, as Simon designed them (v404)
Written in a cloud session straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Ball Lightning's masteries, as Simon designed them`, just before the boot line), on top of v402. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the module) and asked for no low-effort effects and no flat 2D explosions. It replaces the v369 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.balllightning` (with v389's re-calibration). Like every scroll above Common (v388 `smOn`), its masteries only work in developer mode for now. Percentages are of the scroll's power (a zap is 35%); the numbers and timings live in `BLM`.
- **Hooks in the cast:** the ball keeps its state inside `castBallLightning`, so the cast calls `blmStart(o,{P,sc,root,core,g1,g2,rt,sup})` once the ball is built and gets `B` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters, so it is exactly the old ball): `B.land` on landing (true: the masteries draw the landing instead of the old flash and flat ring), `B.hold` stops it rolling, `B.tick` every frame after the ball is drawn (it may move, shake, lift or hide it), `B.noPop` skips the old pop, `B.extra` lengthens its life (0.5s at Mastery 5). `BLM.last` is the last ball's `B` (for the tests).
- **The look kit (3D, no flat flashes):** lightning is soft shader ribbons (`BLM_BOLT`: white core, blue glow) along fractal paths with branches (`blmPath` on `vFract`/`vBranches`), struck out by a leader (`uRev`), struck again with a new path and fading (`blmBolt`); strips are turned to the camera or laid flat on the road (`blmStrips`). Bursts are fresnel bubbles (`BLM_SHELL`, bright at the rim) with a small soft glow and light on the road (`blmBurst`); orbs are plasma spheres with crawling veins (`BLM_ORB`); the charged patches are a shader of electric veins (`BLM_PATCH`); rings of lightning are jagged loops (`blmLoop`); scars are flat glowing strips that cool (`blmScars`). The light is the shared `FXL` through `blmLight` (each effect asks every frame, the brightest wins, handed back when idle), kept low: on the bright plains strong light and big additive glows wash the screen out to white (the first try did). The materials are pooled (`blmMat`/`blmPut`) and never disposed, so their shaders are not recompiled.
- **Thunderstrike (M1, 60%):** on landing the ball squashes, a jagged ring of lightning races out along the road with a glow under it, seven forks crawl over the road, a bubble bursts: 60% to every foe within 1.9 (flyers too; the ball lands 1.6 ahead of the foe it was cast at, so 1.25 would have missed it), a small bolt to each.
- **Static Wake (M2, 45%):** at 0.8, 1.8 and 2.8s after landing a charged patch is left where the ball is. For 2s it crackles with little arcs, faster and fiercer the nearer a foe is; the first foe within 0.6 sets it off: a branching bolt from the sky and arcs to every foe within 0.8, 45% each. A patch nobody comes to fizzles out.
- **Storm Satellites (M3, 45%):** three plasma orbs circle the ball on tilted orbits at different speeds, with arcs to the core now and then. From 1s after landing, every second one darts at a foe within 2.3 (preferring one not hit in the last 1.5s), trailing zigzag lightning, bursts on it for 45% with little bolts to the road, and forms again beside the ball 0.35s later.
- **Rolling Thunder (M4, 3 x 50%):** halfway through its life (2.35s after landing) the ball stops; for 0.8s lightning spirals into it, sparks pour in and its core and glow swell; then three rings of lightning burst out 0.12s apart with a thunderclap, arcing down to the road, out to 2.4: each 50% to every foe it passes (flyers too). It rolls on 0.4s later.
- **Tempest Incarnate (M5, 100% + 40%):** 3.75s after landing it stops and trembles, crackling; its shell splits into twelve glowing shards that circle the core, which rises a tile over the road inside three tilted rings of lightning spinning at different speeds and a swirl of storm cloud. 0.8s in, bolts strike the 6 nearest foes within 2.6 one after another (100% each, flyers too, with a burst, sparks and scars where each lands), and a second bolt hits each one that was already Charged (40%). Then the shards, the rings and the satellites contract into the core, it shrinks to a point and bursts, leaving glowing scars across the road; the ball ends there (no old pop, no more zaps).
- **Fix:** the stats line (`SPELL_FX.balllightning`) and the Supercell text still described the ball before v389 (2 foes every 0.4s for 40%; twice as often, 30% harder); they now say the nearest foe every 0.5s for 35%, and with a storm every 0.4s and 20% harder.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 3.10 / 4.77 / 5.48 / 11.08 / 13.21 against the Uncommon ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. The old ball zaps one foe at a time; every mastery but the satellites hits a whole area, so on a crowd they multiply it many times (Rolling Thunder alone is 150% to everything within 2.4). Simon was told; his numbers are kept for now (`SM.free.balllightning`, `SMG` all 1).
- Tests: `tests/blm-test.js` (23, in run-all as 'blm'): the card, the corrected texts; Mastery 0 as before (35% zaps, no hooks); the landing's 60% to its own foe, a foe where it lands and a flyer, none 2.5 away; three patches, one set off by a foe for 45% once, the others fizzling; the satellites' darts and 45% hits on foes nearby, a different foe each time; the stop, the three rings' 50% each to a foe 1.5 away, none 3.1 away, and rolling on; the storm's 100% on the 6 nearest, not a 7th or one farther out, the second 40% on Charged foes, the collapse, the ball gone, the light handed back; nothing left behind; other casters untouched; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Uncommon masteries for everyone; the patch notes start afresh (v405)
Simon: "make it so that uncommon masteries can now be obtained when playing normally (without dev mode) so normal players can start exping them and also reset patch notes so it only includes common and uncommon masteries, dont describe each mastery change just say (masteries reworked)". Shipped in the same update as v397-v404 (Simon's "update 44").
- **Open to everyone:** `SM_OPEN=['common','uncommon']` lists the rarities whose masteries work outside developer mode; `smOn` (v388) checks it. Uncommon scrolls now earn mastery XP, reach their levels and use their masteries in every game; XP they earned before v388 is still there, so it counts at once. Rare and up stay developer-mode only. The live mastery panel's line for the others now says "above Uncommon" (`smOpenTop()`).
- **Patch notes:** `NOTES_HTML` holds one short section: the 11 Common and 11 Uncommon scrolls named as reworked, and that the Uncommon masteries are open. `NOTES_V` is `masteries-2`, so every player sees the What's new popup once more. The full changelog (`CLOG`) keeps every update, with v405 on top. Older tests that read the notes (`emb`, `flm`, `grb`, `smss`, `wof`, `tpeye`, `twm`) only check them while `masteries-1` is current, as `balance` and `dslash` already did for `bosses-1`.
- Tests: `tests/smgate-test.js` now checks Common and Uncommon open and a Rare and a Mythic closed (no level, power, XP, hooks, bar, pips or card), the new notes (every Common and Uncommon scroll named, one section, no per-mastery text), the changelog, and the popup showing once for a player who saw `masteries-1`; `tests/mpg-test.js` uses a Rare as the scroll that does not earn yet.
- **Still to do (Simon, 28 Oct):** Basic Beam's Mastery 5 looks cut off at its start and end, as if the effect is drawn inside a box too small for it. Fix its visuals next.

## The Hall of Champions, a friends leaderboard, and profiles (v406)
Simon: "is it possible to add a leaderboard? without the need of servers? ... I want it implemented directly in the game, its whats gonna give it a bit of a character, and its JUST for my friends so no cheating". He chose a live board ranked by the best wave on each land.
- **Where the scores live:** Simon's free Firebase Realtime Database, through its REST API (no SDK, no sign-in). `LB.url` is the database address, `https://leaderboard-b4f46-default-rtdb.europe-west1.firebasedatabase.app` (Simon's project, Europe; with no address the Hall says it is not open yet). `GET LB.url/lb.json` reads every land at once; `PATCH LB.url/lb.json` writes this account's entries as `{"<land>/<id>":{n,w,t}}` with `t` the database's own time (`{".sv":"timestamp"}`).
- **Database rules** (Firebase console, Realtime Database, Rules): anyone may read `/lb` and write one player's entry; the name must be a string of at most 20 and the wave a number. It is only for friends, so nothing checks that a score is real.
  ```
  {"rules":{"lb":{".read":true,"$map":{"$player":{".write":true,".validate":"newData.child('n').isString() && newData.child('n').val().length <= 20 && newData.child('w').isNumber()"}}}}}
  ```
- **The account:** `meta.lb` `{id,on,sent,sentN,sentI}` holds the random board id, whether the player has joined, and what was last sent (the wave per land, the name, the portrait). The name on the board is the account's name (`meta.acct.n`) and the portrait the profile's (`meta.prof.icon`); entries carry it as `i`. It is in `meta`, so it moves with the account's save code, and not in `DEV_KEYS`, so developer mode leaves it alone. Every account on a device is its own player.
- **Sending:** joining sends the best wave of every land the account has one on (`meta.bestA`); during a run a new best goes up within `LB.gap` (30) seconds, and at once when the keep falls; four seconds after the game opens anything not yet sent goes up. Runs in developer mode never count. Names are cleaned (no `<` `>` or control characters, single spaces, 20 letters at most), and whatever comes back from the database is checked again before it is shown (`lbSane`).
- **The Hall** (`lbOpen`): a gold Hall tile in the land card's stats row (Best wave, Foes slain, Spells, Hall) shows your rank on the current land and glows at #1. The Hall has a tab per land with how many are on it, a podium for the top 3 (2nd, 1st, 3rd; medals, a crown, pillars rising in), everyone else in a list with how long ago they set it, your own pillar or row glowing, and your rank in the footer with Rename (and Leave the Hall) and Refresh. A tie goes to whoever reached the wave first. When the keep falls and you passed someone, a toast says so ("you passed Tom in Kingsroad! Now #1."). Offline, the last board read (`LB.ck` in localStorage) is shown and marked as offline.
- **Patch notes:** a short "Hall of Champions, and profiles" section above the masteries one (NOTES_V stays `masteries-2`).
- Tests: `tests/lb-test.js` (33, in run-all as 'lb'; the first async suite, so run-all now awaits each suite) runs against a fake database in place of `LB.fetch`: the tile and the Hall, the not-open message, joining (an "Account N" name is not offered, an empty name refused, the name cleaned), the best wave of every land sent with the database time, the podium order and ties, the list, names as text, broken entries left out, the glow, the footer and tile ranks, the tabs and an empty land, a new best in a run going up by itself and not in developer mode, the toast for passing a friend, the offline board and coming back, renaming and leaving, the id kept with the account.
### Profiles
Simon: "put an icon on the top left where players will be able to see their profile, change their profile icon (make like 2 default for now, a knight and a mage) and there they'll be able to change their account name aswell". Built together with the Hall, so they share one name.
- **Portraits** (`PROF.icons`): the Knight (the plate knight with the blue lion surcoat, `enemyModel('knight',0)`) and the Mage (Thaddeus the Wizard, `heroModel('thaddeus')`), rendered head and shoulders once when the game opens (`profMake`, wrapped onto `makeThumbs`, 192px PNGs on a clear background; the frame gives the colour). Adding a portrait is one more entry with its model, angle and framing. If a render fails the portrait shows its initial.
- **`meta.prof` `{icon}`**: the chosen portrait (the Knight until one is picked); not in `DEV_KEYS`, so developer mode leaves it alone, and it travels with the save code.
- **The button** (`#bProf`): a round portrait at the top left of the home screen's top bar, clear of the crowns, gems and scraps.
- **The profile** (`profOpen`): the big portrait and the name (tap to rename: cleaned like the Hall's, 20 letters), the portraits to choose from, the records (best wave, foes slain, crowns, scrolls, then the best wave and Hall rank on every open land) and a button into the Hall. The name is the account's (`meta.acct.n`): renaming here renames the account in More > Accounts and the other way round, and a new name or portrait goes up to the Hall at once. The Hall's footer shows your portrait and has a Profile button in place of the old Rename; Leave the Hall sits under the board.
- Tests: `tests/prof-test.js` (27, in run-all as 'prof'): both portraits rendered and not blank, the Knight by default, the button at the top left clear of the counters, the profile's name, records and lands, picking the Mage (saved, the big and the top-left portraits follow, an unknown one ignored), renaming (empty refused, cleaned, saved, shown in More > Accounts and back), cancel, the Hall button, the Hall ranks per land, developer mode and saves. `tests/lb-test.js` (37) now also checks the portraits on the board and that a new name or portrait from the profile goes up to every land.

## Spatial Deflection's masteries, as Simon designed them (v407)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Spatial Deflection's masteries`, just before the boot line). The first Rare scroll reworked; Simon's text, abridged, is at the top of the block, and he asked: "NO LOW EFFORT EFFECTS ARE ACCEPTABLE, no bland 2d explosions etc, this is about making something that 'feels' worth unlocking". It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.deflect` (Crushing Fold, Slipstream, Mirror Shards, Space Lock, Second Fold) and wraps `castDeflectZone`. Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old spell (`dfmC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`). Rare masteries still only work in developer mode (v405's `SM_OPEN` is Common and Uncommon). Percentages are of the scroll's power (the snap is 250%); the numbers live in `DFM`, the times are from the zone's start (the old zone: radius 2.3, snap at 7.12s, `DFM.SNAP`).
- **The warp pass, extended:** Spatial Deflection draws the real world bent (the v331-era `SDP` pass: the scene rendered to a texture and re-sampled through a world-space displacement). An `sdpInit` wrapper patches its fragment shader once with three fields, fed by an `sdpRender` wrapper: ghost copies of the world (`uEc/uEo/uEa`, from `DFM.ecS`: the zone's world sampled again at two offsets and blended in, pale and cold), a fold field (`uFd/uFe/uFf`, from `DFM.fdS`: a pinch toward the centre, clamped to the zone, with a twist stepped into facets, and a cross of creases with shear and glowing crease lines) and four smooth ripples (`uRw`, `DFM.rw`, `dfmRipple`: a ring pushing the world outward as it passes, with a chromatic split). `dfmWarp` keeps `SDP.n` up while a field is in use so the pass keeps running; every field also cleans up through `SG_ACTIVE` (a battle that ends mid-effect) and in its effect's last frame (end callbacks only run in battle). The stock `spaceQuake` is not used: its block jitter turned the stacked masteries into a checkerboard.
- **Fractured Perspective (M1, 35%):** `dfmFracture` at the snap. Simon, after the first version: the cracks and light bursts should be much more subtle and about the distortion, "the light should actually 'feel' like a distorting light explosion cracks not just white stripes". So the zone shatters like glass in the warp pass (`uSh/uSs`, `DFM.shS`: long shards from a polar Voronoi pattern, revealed outward with the front, each shifting the world behind it in its own direction and outward, with a chromatic split, held 0.55s and eased back over 0.9s; only the seams glow, faintly). Under it, at 30%, a faint fringe of cracks (`dfmCrackNet`: nine jagged radial cracks with branches and two broken polygonal rings, one mesh, `dfmCrackGeo`, revealed outward along each crack's length by `dfmCrackMat`, a cold shadow under a white-blue core) racing out in 0.42s; four main cracks bend the world along them (`SDP.sl` slashes) and the ground under each foe they reach twists for 0.35s (`SDP.pl`): 35% to every foe inside as the front reaches it.
- **Echoes of Elsewhere (M2, 50%):** `dfmEchoes`: from 5.12s two copies of the zone's world, offset 0.42 and 0.38 in different directions, fade in and flicker, each with a ghostly rim (a low scanlined curtain); after the snap they collapse into the real one at +0.08s and +0.3s (their offset shrinking to nothing), each with a ripple and a pale-blue wave of light (`dfmWave`): 50% to the foes inside that no copy has hit yet, so a foe takes it once; one that walked in after the first can be caught by the second.
- **Folded Space (M3, 75%):** `dfmFold`: from 3.56s the fold field creases the zone along its own shift axis and its normal (the road kinks into sharp angles at the creases, the sides shear, the area pinches slightly toward the centre), the creases glow, and sheets of light rise along them. Every 0.2s ground foes inside already past the centre on the road are drawn back toward it (`dfmDraw`: at most 0.07 a step, `pushE`, so Resolve applies; pulling the ones before it forward would help them), the others are slowed 50%; bosses are not moved. At the snap the fold springs open (crease and pinch ring out): 75% to every foe inside.
- **The Impossible Prison (M4, 100%):** `dfmPrison`: from 1.2s ten crystal panels rise round the zone one after another (a decagonal prism 1.25 tall, `dfmPanelMat`: a glassy sheen, a glint sweeping over it, bright edges); over the last three seconds ten roof triangles grow from the top edges into a point 2.35 up; in the last half second the cage contracts and turns white; at the snap it shatters inward (`dfmShatter`: 320 glass shards, instanced, flying to the centre, then scattering and falling) with a ripple: 100% to every foe inside. It blocks nothing (it is only drawn).
- **Paradox Collapse (M5, 60% + 150%):** `dfmParadox`: from 6.45s the fold field collapses the whole zone toward its centre (the pinch rises to 5.5, the twist to 3.2: the road and scenery spiral into ribbons and a point), foes are drawn in harder (0.16 a step, slowed 75%; bosses resist), the board darkens a little, and a singularity burns at the centre (`dfmSingularity`: an icosahedron, a dodecahedron and an octahedron of light turning against each other round a glass core, a white star). At 7.32s it ruptures into three tilted rings of fractured light (`dfmRing`: 54 shards each, instanced, racing out to 3.4 in 0.5, 0.6 and 0.7s, with ripples and pale-blue waves): 60% to each foe the first ring to reach it touches, once. At 7.82s restoration: a blinding white-blue flash, a great ripple, a glow: 150% to every foe still in the zone. Masteries 1-4 ride it.
- **Subtler light (same pass):** every flash, spark burst and wave of light in these masteries was turned down (the per-foe flashes to small cold-blue ones, the prison's whitening to half, the white screen flashes to 0.1-0.2, the rupture's rings to a translucent blue) and the ripples turned up, so the bending carries the effect.
- **Visual gotchas:** the zone's own meshes are drawn into the warp pass's texture, so they bend with it (the prison wobbles with the zone, which suits it). Ground meshes need y 0.075 here (0.03 sat under the tiles), and a ShaderMaterial is single-sided by default: the crack quads faced down and vanished until they were double-sided. Revealing cracks by their distance from the centre left the outer ends dark (a crack's length is longer than its reach): they are revealed along their own length.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, all SMA/SMG are 1 and `SM.free.deflect` is set. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 2 runs): 1.23 / 1.41 / 1.60 / 1.86 / 2.97 against the ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74 (the Rare ladder is the Uncommon one, v370).
- Patch notes: one line added to the v405 section (Rare, developer mode); a CLOG entry.
- **Older tests:** `tests/smast3-test.js` (the Rare power check) now gives a scroll in `SM.free` the same 3/4-of-the-ladder rule as `smast2-test.js` (Spatial Deflection measured 1.60 / 2.98 against 1.35 / 1.74 and failed the 35% band); `tests/smgate-test.js` accepts a later update than v405 on top of the changelog.
- Tests: `tests/dfm-test.js` (32, in run-all as 'dfm'). Foes stand on the road at chosen distances from the middle of the first route (only the effects are stepped), the cast waits for the crystal's flight, and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit's amount: the card; the shader carrying the three fields; Mastery 0 as before; the fracture at the snap, its bending, 35% inside only; the copies in the last two seconds, collapsing in turn, 50% once, a late foe caught by the second; the fold at half time, drawing past foes back, slowing the others, not moving bosses, 75% at the snap; the prison shattering at the snap, 100% inside only; the collapse drawing foes in (not bosses), rupture then restoration, 60% once (flyers), 150% inside only, Masteries 1-4 riding it, the light back; nothing left behind and the warp pass handed back (`SDP.n` 0, every field and slot cleared) at every mastery; other casters untouched; the real cast path at Masteries 1, 3 and 5.

## Riftfang's masteries, as Simon designed them (v408)
A block before the boot line, on top of v405. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the block) and asked for "no bland 2d explosions" and for it to feel more powerful with each level. It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.riftfang` (Jagged Shards, Void Mark, Rift Echo, Shard Storm, Gaping Rift). Riftfang is Rare, so its masteries stay developer-mode only (`SM_OPEN`, v405); the What's new popup (v405: Common and Uncommon only) is unchanged, the changelog has the entry.
- **Every cast:** a `castRiftfang` wrapper runs every cast through `rfmCast`: the same rift, five shards, timings and side-shard targets as `castRiftfang`. The piercing shard's line now runs where the shard flies, from the rift through the target (castRiftfang measured it from the hero, half a tile to the side, so a lone target was usually missed and took only the four 80% side shards). Every impact and the rift's snap are 3D void explosions: `cfmBoom` (v401) has a `pal:'void'` palette (`CFM_BOOM_FS_VOID`: white-lavender, magenta, violet, deep purple, black smoke; void sparks, dust and scorch), plus obsidian fragments (`rfmCrystals`, a shared jittered octahedron with glowing edges). The rift and the explosions grow with the mastery level.
- **Shardburst (M1, 30%):** when the piercing shard has hit at least one foe, it bursts 0.35 past the last foe it went through into 3 splinters (`rfmShot`: an obsidian spike with a thin violet streak and black smoke): the 3 nearest foes within 2.8 that it did not pierce, one each, fanned left to right. A splinter with no foe flies off and fades.
- **Void Pins (M2, 45%):** each side shard, hit or miss, drives a spectral blade (`rfmPin`, an extruded blade with glowing edges) into the ground round where it lands. At the fourth they flash white together and erupt one after another (a void fireball with a column, obsidian shards): 45% to every foe within 0.6 of each blade, so a foe near several blades is hit by each.
- **Deathmark (M3, 120%):** each side shard that hits the marked foe carves a rune into it (`rfmRunes`, billboarded on its body), even if that hit kills it; a dead foe's marks move to the nearest foe within 1.2 of where it fell, and the next shards mark that one. At four the runes join into a sigil, it flashes and bursts: 120% to that foe.
- **Blackglass Barrage (M4, 45%):** when the fifth shard has landed, the rift widens and crackles for 0.7s more; six blackglass spears form in it and fly one after another at different foes within 3.2 of the target (nearest first, flyers too; with fewer foes they go round again): 45% each.
- **Cataclysmic Convergence (M5, 100% + 50%):** each of the five shards leaves a fracture (`rfmFracture`) where it struck. When the fifth lands they flare, violet lightning races between them, and they explode one after another: 100% to every foe within 0.75 of each (once per foe per cast), and each sends lightning to up to 2 foes within 1.6 that it has not struck yet, 50% each. The energy then streams back into the rift, which snaps shut.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.06 / 1.43 / 1.55 / 1.84 / 2.39 against the Rare ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Mastery 1 gets a small hidden factor (`SMG.riftfang[0]=1.04`); from Mastery 2 Simon's numbers carry it above the ladder (`SM.free.riftfang`).
- Tests: `tests/rfm-test.js` (26, in run-all as 'rfm'). Foes stand on the road (only the effects are stepped), and `hurt` is wrapped to log each hit: Mastery 0 hits as before (150% now on its target) with no masteries; three splinters on three foes the shard did not pierce, none for a lone target; four blades and their 45% eruptions (also when the target dies early); four marks and the 120% detonation, and the marks passing on when the target dies (the killing shard's mark counting); six spears on six foes (flyers too), going round two; five fractures, 100% once per foe, lightning 50% once per foe; each needs its level; unmastered casts get no masteries; every level runs clean; nothing left behind.

## Piercing Verdict's masteries, as Simon designed them (v409)
Simon designed five new masteries for the Rare scroll Piercing Verdict (the line of light, 375% to every foe on it), with "no low effort effects, no bland 2D explosions, progressively better visually". Written in a cloud session straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Piercing Verdict's masteries`).
- **Where it hooks in:** the cast in use is `jvCast` (it replaced `castJudgment`, which stays only for the parity test). For a Verdict (`big` false) it calls `pvmStart(o,t,power)` and gets `V`: null at Mastery 0, for echoes, other casters and the Judgment, so they are exactly the old cast. `V.charge` while the circle compresses, `V.still` in the silent moment, `V.hit` for each foe the line pierces (where along it, at what height), `V.fire` at the shot (the palm point, the final direction, the length and slope). `V.ownBeam` (Mastery 3+) hides the old beam.
- **Timeline after the shot:** seals ignite at `PVM.m4At` (0.38s), the second beam at `PVM.m5At` (0.45s), the stars flare from `PVM.m1At` (0.6s) over `PVM.m1Sweep` (0.18s); the scar lives `PVM.m2T` (2.5s).
- **M1 Luminous Afterimage:** a small rayed star with a hot core hangs where the line pierced each foe (drawn over the foe), and motes of light all along the path; 0.6s after the shot a sweep runs from the palm to the end, dropping violet sparks, and each star it reaches bursts into a flare of rays with a ring of light: 20% to its foe.
- **M2 Severance Line:** a razor-thin violet scar on the road under the line (a shader strip: an unstable core with breaks, a pulse running along it, a dark line beneath so it reads on bright ground). A foe the line did not pierce that touches or crosses it (within `PVM.m2W`, more for a boss; flyers too) is pierced by a miniature verdict: the scar flares there and a short line of light shoots through it along the scar, 55%, once per foe per cast. Nothing past the line's end, nothing after 2.5s.
- **M3 Absolute Focus:** three violet rings collapse into the circle, faster and faster, and motes rush in; in the silent moment a pinprick so bright its heart looks black (a dark core in a white-hot rim, a violet halo, a thin flare). The line fires as a needle: a hair-fine white line flaring out to the violet beam toward its end, then a sharp violet afterimage. Foes on the first `PVM.m3Core` (half) of the line take 35% more. Simon's "enemies hit by the initial narrow core" read as: the near half of the line, where the needle is still narrow.
- **M4 Consecrated Passage:** a glowing sigil (two rings, a hexagram, studs) drawn in on each foe pierced, following it; 0.38s after the shot they all flash white at once with a ring of light and fine violet sparks: 65% to each (none for a foe already dead).
- **M5 The Unerring Verdict:** the white point stays at the palm and grows blinding over 0.45s, light rushing into it; then a second, heavier beam tears down the exact same line (the first shot's point and final direction; no retargeting): 100% once to every foe on it then, flyers too, rings at the palm and a flare at the end; the scorch under it becomes one brilliant white-violet scar that cools and fades.
- **The looks:** beams are soft shader ribbons turned to the camera (white core, violet glow, struck out from the palm); stars and flares are canvas-drawn hot points with long rays; the seals are a drawn sigil; the scars a flickering shader strip. Materials are pooled (`pvmMat`), the shared effects light takes a colour now (`blmLight(...,col)`, Ball Lightning keeps its blue).
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept (`SM.free.verdict`). They add little to a 375% line, so alone they fall short of the Rare ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs: 1.03 / 1.03 / 1.09 / 1.20 / 1.52 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74); the hidden factor `SMG.verdict` [1.08, 1.28, 1.26, 1.2, 1.04] makes up the rest (5 runs: 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.42-1.75 / 1.77; Mastery 4 sits on a kill threshold in the ladder's fight).
- Tests: `tests/pvm-test.js` (28, in run-all as 'pvm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old line; M1 stars, 20% once each in order from the hero, none to a foe the line missed; M2 the scar, 55% once to a foe crossing (not again crossing back), a flyer, none to the pierced foes, past the end or after 2.5s; M3 the pinprick and the needle, 35% to the near half only; M4 seals, 65% once together at 0.38s, none for a foe already dead; M5 the point and the second beam at 0.45s, 100% once to every foe on the same line then (one that stepped on, a flyer), none to one that stepped off or stands beside; nothing left behind and the light handed back; the Judgment and other casters untouched; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Magma Geyser's masteries, as Simon designed them (v410)
A block before the boot line, on top of v405. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the block) and asked for "no low effort effects, no bland 2d explosions" and for it to feel more powerful each mastery. It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.geyser` (Hotter Magma, Magma Bombs, Burning Seams, Obsidian Crust, Twin Geyser). Magma Geyser is Rare, so its masteries stay developer-mode only (`SM_OPEN`, v405); the What's new popup is unchanged, the changelog has the entry.
- **Every cast:** a `castMagmaGeyser` wrapper runs every cast through `gmCast`: the same aim (`nsLead` 0.62s), seams, eruption (230% within 1.1, burn 25%/s for 3s), column and six blobs (`gmBlob`, 60% within 0.45, then `nsLavaPool`, so Firestorm still drinks the pools) as `castMagmaGeyser`, but the eruption and every landing are 3D fireballs (`cfmBoom`, v401) instead of `flash`/`shockwave`; the column (`gmColumn`, the NS_LAVA shader), the seams and the blasts grow with the mastery level.
- **Molten Shrapnel (M1, 85%):** 0.1s after the eruption three crusted chunks (`gmChunk`) are hurled high, 2.2-3.2 tiles out (onto the road where it is at least 2 tiles from the crater); halfway their crust cracks and flakes off (`gmFlake`) and the core blazes; they smash down in a fireball and sparks: 85% to foes within 0.55 (flyers too).
- **Lava Veins (M2, 30% + 20%/s):** 0.2s after the eruption lava wells out of the crater in three streams (`gmFlow`): along the road both ways and a third braiding beside one of them (Simon: they should flow slowly from the eruption, only along the path, in random patterns). Each meanders in its own pattern (two waves and a slow random drift) but stays within 0.32 of the road's middle and stops at the road's end; its molten front creeps on at 0.7 tiles a second until it is 2.2 long, and they cool 4.6s after the eruption. Each is drawn as a soft band of scorched ground with a thin molten core glowing through cracks in a dark crust (`GM_VEIN_FS`), feathered at its edges, with a brighter creeping front that spits embers. A ground foe within 0.15 of the part that has flowed takes 30% and burns for 20%/s for 2s, at most once every 0.75s from all the streams.
- **Heart of the Volcano (M3, 120%):** when the column sinks the seams dim, the heart pulses ever faster and the ground bulges (`gmBulge`); 1.5s later it erupts: a shorter column, a fireball and crust flakes, 120% to foes within 0.85, and 3 more blobs.
- **Fissure Cascade (M4, 100%):** 0.25s after the eruption a glowing crack races along the road (toward the side with more foes within 3.5, else toward the keep); vents burst 1, 2 and 3 tiles out, 0.35s apart, each a small column and fireball with glowing cracks (`gmVentSeams`): 100% to foes within 0.6, and 2 blobs.
- **Caldera (M5, 100% + 45% + 150%):** the seams crack wider (1.6) with a ring fracture and the eruption blasts 100% to foes within 1 of the centre. When the column sinks, a caldera rises (`gmCaldera`: a ragged rim of black crust round a churning lava lake, `GM_LAKE_FS`, boulders on its crest; built above the ground, which would hide a real hollow) for 3s: surges at 0.5, 1.5 and 2.5s hit ground foes within 1.25 for 45% and tip boulders into the lake; then it caves in (0.3s) and explodes outward: a fireball with a mushroom column, a ring of fireballs, `myFireRing` and molten stones, 150% to every foe within 1.7.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs, without factors): 1.00 / 1.13 / 1.53 / 1.78 / 2.93 against the Rare ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. The shrapnel lands beyond a packed group and the veins only catch foes crossing them, so Masteries 1 and 2 get hidden factors (`SMG.geyser=[1.12,1.11,1,1,1]`); from Mastery 3 Simon's numbers carry it above the ladder (`SM.free.geyser`).
- Tests: `tests/gmm-test.js` (28, in run-all as 'gmm'): Mastery 0 hits as before with no masteries; three chunks landing far out, 85% within 0.55; three streams from the crater just after the eruption, both ways along the road, staying within 0.32 of its middle and meandering, creeping on at about 0.7 tiles a second to full length, 30% to ground foes on the part that has flowed, never flyers, at most once every 0.75s; the heart 1.5s after the column sinks, 120% within 0.85, nine pools; three fissures 0.35s apart, 1, 2 and 3 tiles along the road, 100% within 0.6, twelve pools; the caldera's 100% burst within 1, three surges a second apart (45%, ground only), the 150% collapse within 1.7 (flyers too); each needs its level; unmastered casts get no masteries; every level runs clean; nothing left behind. `tests/smgate-test.js`'s changelog row checks that v405 sits above v404 instead of pinning it on top. `tests/smast3-test.js` (the Rare ladder) now lets a scroll on its designer's own numbers (`SM.free`) sit above the ladder as long as it reaches 3/4 of it, as `smast-test` and `smast2-test` already did.

## Murder of Crows' masteries, as Simon designed them (v411)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Murder of Crows' masteries`, just before the boot line), on main v405 (alongside v407, Spatial Deflection, on its own branch; they share nothing). Simon's text, abridged, is at the top of the block; he asked: "no low effort effects or bland 2d effects, scroll should 'feel' more powerful each mastery". It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.crows` (Sharper Beaks, Omen, Larger Murder, Carrion, Raven King, whose "more crows" and "great raven" were glowing projectiles). Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old flock (`crmC(o)`: the `SM.ctx` entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`); Rare masteries still only work in developer mode. Percentages are of the scroll's power (a peck is 8%); the numbers live in `CRM`.
- **How it hangs together:** the masteries change how each crow flies, so from Mastery 1 `castCrows` is replaced by `crmCast`: the old flock line for line (18 crows within 4.2 of the hero; climb, circle, dive, peck; the Ashen Flock combo; the scatter at 5.4s) with more states for the masteries (`dbl`, `dive2`, `sync`, `gale`, `rise`, `hop`, `fdive`). Each crow has a violet eye glow at its head (a sprite) that flashes when it does something special. Feathers are real: `crmFeathers`, a pool of 300 instanced 3D feathers (a shaft and tapering vane, black with a violet sheen) that tumble, sway and drift down, or circle a foe; every mastery's feathers go through it.
- **Ravenous Beaks (M1, 8%):** every 5th peck of the flock, the crow that made it flashes its eyes, snaps its wings shut, rises 0.13s and dives again at the same foe (faster): 8% more and a scatter of feathers. Simon wrote "every fifth successful peck from a crow", but a crow only pecks 3-4 times in a cast, so counted per crow it would hardly ever happen; counted on the flock it happens about a dozen times a cast.
- **Omen Circles (M2, 35% + 20%):** when 3 crows circle the same foe, 14 feathers start circling it like a cyclone, tightening and spinning faster as more crows gather; the next peck on it bursts it: 35% to the foe, 20% to every foe within 1.1, the feathers flung outward, a violet-black puff and a dark band racing out (`crmBand`). Once per foe every 1.2s.
- **Unkindness (M3, 8%):** after its peck a crow caws (`crmCaw`: two thin rings of sound spreading from it, a puff of feathers) and calls the two nearest crows that are climbing or circling; the three spiral in to its next target in a turning triangle for 0.55s, trailing violet sparks, and dive together; the two called each deal 8% more on that peck. Once per crow (as caller) per cast.
- **Black Gale (M4, 40%):** at 1, 2, 3 and 4s half the flock (alternate halves, so the hunt never stops; the whole flock interrupted every second never got to peck) breaks off into a spiral, each crow on its own arm, sweeping out from the hero to 4.2 in 0.6s with smoke trailing, a band of shadow streaked like feathers on the ground and a ring of feathers left behind: 40% to each foe the front crosses, once per sweep; then they go straight back to circling.
- **The Last Omen (M5, 8% x 3):** at 5s the flock stops and rises into a dark violet haze above the hero (a dark cloud and a violet glow with drifting smoke; the board darkens a little), then dives in three waves (0.65, 0.9, 1.15s later); each crow picks a foe in reach and pecks it three times (8% each), hopping up between, then bursts into feathers and violet sparks (a crow with no foe bursts at once). A foe whose count of crows (each crow counts once, on its first peck) reaches 3 is marked by a raven sigil (`crmSigil`: a raven with spread wings in a ring of marks, drawn once; glowing on the ground under it and floating above it) that dissolves into smoke. The sigil does nothing more: Simon gave it no effect.
- **Visual gotchas:** feathers as big as the crows made the swarm unreadable; they are drawn at 0.58 of the model and bursts throw 60% as many. The gale's ground band first read as a plain violet ring: it is mostly shadow streaks now, with no hot line.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, all SMA/SMG are 1 and `SM.free.crows` is set. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 2 runs): 1.19 / 2.94 / 3.40 / 6.48 / 7.65 against the ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74. Black Gale carries Mastery 4: 40% to every foe within 4.2 of the hero, four times a cast.
- **Older tests:** `tests/smast3-test.js` gives a scroll in `SM.free` the same 3/4-of-the-ladder rule as `smast2-test.js`, and `tests/smgate-test.js` accepts a later update than v405 on top of the changelog (the same two edits as v407).
- Tests: `tests/crm-test.js` (29, in run-all as 'crm'). The crows pick their foes at random, so the rows check the rules on what the log shows: the card; Mastery 0 as before; doubles after the flock's 5th, 10th... peck by that crow, 8%; rings only where 3 crows circle, bursts for 35% and 20% within 1.1, never twice within 1.2s on a foe; calls with two other crows, each crow calling at most once, bonus pecks only by called crows, 8%; four sweeps a second apart, 40% once per sweep to every foe in reach and none beyond, pecking between them; the rise at 5s, at most three final pecks a crow and a burst after the third, sigils exactly on the foes three crows struck, Masteries 1-4 riding it, the haze and the light back; the crows, feathers and everything else gone; other casters untouched; the real cast path at Masteries 1, 3 and 5.

## Lightning Cloud's masteries, as Simon designed them, and a see-through cloud (v412)
Simon designed five new masteries for the Rare scroll Lightning Cloud (two storm clouds, one over the leading foe and one over the strongest, each striking a random spot under it about every 0.3s for 5.4s: 100% within 40% of a strike's reach, 70% out to its edge, 0.55 tiles), with "no low effort effects, no bland 2D explosions", and asked for the cloud to be "slightly translucent, like in the old stormcaller, cause it gets actually annoying when you cant see anything through it". Written in a cloud session straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Lightning Cloud's masteries`).
- **See-through, higher cloud (every cast):** the cloud's puffs are drawn at `CLOUD.op` (0.45) opacity without writing depth, so the road and the foes show through it, like Stormcaller's clouds. Simon: "raise the cloud like at least twice the height": it floats at `CLOUD.h` (5.2, was 2.55). The camera can come down to about 6.8 when zoomed in, so the puffs fade as it comes close (`cloudCamF`, down to a fifth); the lightning stays.
- **Where it hooks in:** `castStormCloud` runs once per cloud (twice a cast) and calls `scmStart(o, K)` with the cloud's parts and place; it gets `S` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old cloud). `S.aim` may move a strike (M1), `S.pre` sees who a strike will hit, `S.struck` runs after it, `S.tick` every frame after the cloud is drawn, `S.until` ends the normal strikes early (M5), `S.extra` lengthens its life (M5). The two clouds of a cast share `G` (the cast's context) for the Stormlink.
- **M1 Stormseeker:** after a strike hits, its toughest foe becomes the cloud's quarry (a crackling ring of light marks it); while it stays under the cloud each next strike lands on it with `SCM.seek` chance by hits in a row (40 / 60 / 80 / 85%). Two strikes in a row that hit no one reset it. The quarry can be struck again and again.
- **M2 Forked Tempest:** every 4th strike of a cloud splits at impact: two smaller branching bolts leap to two different foes within `SCM.forkR` (2 tiles; foes the strike missed first), 45% each, flyers too, with lightning crawling over the road to them.
- **M3 Supercharged Skies:** a core glow inside the cloud grows with each strike; after 5 it blazes and crackles, and the next strike is a Thunderclap: the heavy bolt, 175% within the inner reach (the strike's 100% and 75% more), 70% at the edge as before, a wide blue-white burst, rings of light racing over the road, sparks and glowing scars.
- **M4 Stormlink:** while both clouds rage (from 1.2s, every 1.6s) lightning drops from one cloud to the road and runs low across to the other for 0.8s, crackling over the ground under it: 90% to a foe it crosses (within `SCM.linkW` of the line between the clouds, flyers too), at most once per foe every 1.2s. No link if the clouds are under a tile apart; it ends at once if either cloud ends.
- **M5 Wrath of the Heavens:** 0.5s before its end a cloud stops striking and contracts round a blazing plasma core, light and lightning rushing in; then three bolts tear down to separate spots in its area (0.55 apart at least): 160% within the inner reach, 70% at the edge, each with a burst and scars; the last with a great shock flash, rings racing out and a heavy shake. Then the core bursts and the cloud tears apart. The second cloud's finale comes 0.15s after the first's.
- **The looks:** the Ball Lightning kit (soft lightning ribbons with branches, struck out by a leader and re-struck; fresnel bursts; a plasma orb; the shared light, blue-white).
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept (`SM.free.stormcloud`). They are well above the Rare ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 5 runs: 1.91 / 2.24 / 2.29 / 2.48 / 2.47 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74): the old cloud spends most strikes on empty road and Stormseeker sends them into foes, which alone nearly doubles it. Simon was told.
- Tests: `tests/scm-test.js` (29, in run-all as 'scm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old cloud, and the see-through cloud; M1 a foe under a cloud hit far more often than at Mastery 0, strikes only seeking after a hit; M2 every fourth strike splits, two forks of 45% to different foes, a flyer and a ground foe nearby but not one out of reach; M3 the 6th and 12th strikes are Thunderclaps, 75% more in the inner reach only; M4 links from 1.2s every 1.6s, 90% once per link to a foe on the line and a flyer, none beside it, none when a cloud has ended; M5 the strikes stop early, each cloud compresses and fires three bolts on separate spots in its area, 160% / 70% by distance, the second cloud a beat later, nothing left behind and the light handed back; other casters untouched; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Ice Tomb's masteries, as Simon designed them (v413)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Ice Tomb's masteries`, just before the boot line), on main v405 (alongside v407 Spatial Deflection and v411 Murder of Crows on their own branches; they share nothing). Simon's text, abridged, is at the top of the block; he asked again for no low-effort or bland 2D effects and for the scroll to feel stronger at each mastery. It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.icetomb` (Hoarfrost, Frozen Shards, Brittle, Glacial Prison, Twin Tombs). Rare, so developer mode only for now.
- **How it hangs together:** Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old tomb (`itmC(o)`: the SM.ctx entry of `o.smId`, not `o.smx`).
