From Mastery 1 `castIceTomb` is wrapped: the old tomb is built and explodes exactly as before (the wrapper catches its group from `scene.add` and its `NTOMB` entry, and reads the hold back from `stunE`), `itmTomb` dresses it and watches for the moment it explodes (`T.done`; the comet's x3 is caught on `T.shatter`), then `itmBoom` plays the masteries. Percentages are of the scroll's power (the prisoner takes 400%, the foes within 1.2 take 150%); the bigger splash is dealt as the difference from the old one.
- **Glacial Overgrowth (M1, 75%):** from the moment the tomb closes, ten more crystals (broad overlapping plates and tall spires) grow round it one after another and the whole tomb stretches 32% taller; at the explosion they are flung out with the rest. 75% more to the prisoner; 150% to the foes beyond 1.2 out to 1.5.
- **Heart of Winter (M2, 20% to 120%):** veins of blue-white light (two layers of lines with beads of light) climb from the prisoner through the crystals and a cold heart glows inside; every 0.25s held adds 20% (six steps at most), each step a pulse of the heart, the veins and a soft cold light. A boss (held 1.15s) stores 80%. At the explosion a brilliant frost burst (sparks and mist, bigger with the charge) releases it.
- **Shatterstorm (M3, 90%):** twelve great fragments, each with a glow, hang round the burst for 0.2s turning, then shoot out to 1.8 trailing sparks over a frost wave on the ground: 90% to each foe they reach (flyers too), on top of the explosion.
- **Permafrost Rupture (M4, 100% + 45% slow):** eight branching frost fissures (`itmCrackNet`, a crack mesh revealed along its length: a deep blue crack under a cyan glow) race out 2 tiles in 0.5s, ice spikes bursting up along them and sinking again: 100% and 45% slower for 2s to each ground foe a fissure reaches (within 0.3 of it). Flyers are skipped.
- **Absolute Cataclysm (M5, 200% / 225% / 30%):** in the last 0.35s the tomb locks, compresses inward and turns white (a white shell over it, sparks drawn in), then detonates: a frost wave to 1.9, a dome of cold light, 80 shards flung out, a white flash and a shake. 200% more to the prisoner, the splash 225% to 1.6, and a field of 56 ice fragments swirling low round the spot in frost mist for 2s: 30% a second (7.5% every 0.25s) to the foes inside 1.6.
- **Visual gotchas:** the cold light on the plains washed the grass lime: it is kept low. The ground waves first read as plain white rings (and three stacked at Mastery 5): the hot line is faint and broken by noise now, the rime is the body, and Mastery 1 adds no wave of its own.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, all SMA/SMG are 1 and `SM.free.icetomb` is set. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 2 runs): 1.20 / 1.35 / 1.99 / 2.86 / 3.70 against the ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74.
- **Older tests:** `tests/smast3-test.js` gives a scroll in `SM.free` the same 3/4-of-the-ladder rule as `smast2-test.js`, and `tests/smgate-test.js` accepts a later update than v405 on top of the changelog (the same two edits as v407 and v411).
- Tests: `tests/itm-test.js` (33, in run-all as 'itm'): the card; Mastery 0 as before; the old explosion kept; 75% to the prisoner and the splash to 1.5; the tomb taller; heart steps every 0.25s, 195% for a full hold and 155% for a boss; the fragments hanging 0.2s and 90% within 1.8 (flyers too); fissures for 100% and the slow, never flyers or beyond 2; at Mastery 5 395% to the prisoner, 75%/225% splash to 1.6, eight 7.5% field ticks; a comet x3 tripling the masteries; echoes and other casters as the old tomb; the real cast path; nothing left behind.

## Fire Tornado's masteries, as Simon designed them (v414)
Simon designed five new masteries for the Rare scroll Fire Tornado (a whirling column of fire rises 1.3 down the road from the leading foe and drifts back up it at 0.72 a second for about 4.3s: 30% every 0.3s within 0.95 and a burn, a pull within 1.45 that slows 45%, and a 120% burst when it collapses, 0.3s before its end), with "no low effort effects, no 2D bland effects or explosions". For Molten Orbit: "careful not to overdo it, should be fairly subtle, not to outshine the tornado". The old masteries (Hotter Core, Ember Trail, Whirling Embers, Updraft, Firestorm Collapse) are gone. Firestorm (the combo with Wall of Fire, Cinder Scatter, Magma Geyser or Phoenix) stays apart, as Simon asked: the masteries leave no burning ground it can drink, and its +50% does not touch their damage (only its growth carries their reach).
- **Where it hooks in:** `castFireTornado` calls `ftmStart(o, K)` with the funnel's parts and gets `M` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old tornado). `M.zone` runs on each of its fire ticks, `M.tick` every frame after the funnel is drawn, `M.collapse` at its collapse; `M.spin` turns the funnel's layers (M5 reverses them) and `M.pin` draws its rocks in. A rock's place in its orbit is `slot(b, v, q)`; a rock flying (`b.out`) or set in the ring (`b.ring`) is left alone by the funnel. The cast's own effects ride in `M.W`, which follows the funnel. Percentages are of the scroll's power. `FTM.dbg` logs the events for the tests.
- **M1 Molten Orbit:** from 0.55s, every 0.22s at most, a rock (the one nearest its foe) breaks out of the orbit: a glowing coal with a short trail of streaming flame and embers, curving into a foe within 2 tiles of the tornado (the one with the fewest rocks so far; flyers too) for 45% with a small burst of fire, then flying back into its place in the orbit, or the ring. Twelve a cast, three at most on one foe; none leave after 3s, so all are home before the collapse.
- **M2 Infernal Vortex:** a furnace at the funnel's heart: a white-hot noise fireball inside a fast twisting column of fire, glowing out through the flames and lighting the road. On each fire tick foes within 0.4 (times the tornado's size) take 25% more, fire licking up where it scorches them; each such hit heats the core (`FTM.heat`), which brightens and cools slowly. A foe held there 0.6s takes a 60% burst (a burst of fire bursting out of it) when it breaks free, or when the tornado collapses with it still inside.
- **M3 Trail of Ruin:** at 0.9s and every 0.8s after (four at most) the stretch of road it has just crossed ignites: charred earth split by a molten seam and a web of glowing veins, tapering at its ends, with glowing cracks round it, flames standing along it, embers and smoke, lit from the tornado's side outward. Each burns 2.5s: 25% every 0.5s to ground foes on it (within 0.26 of its middle line).
- **M4 Ring of Ruin:** at 2.15s the funnel's rocks are flung out of it one after another and spiral out from where they are, and 28 more chunks are torn up out of the road round its base (grit, sparks and a scorch where each leaves it): a ragged belt of tumbling molten rock (rough solids with the Ember Titan's glowing lava cracks), 1.35 wide, every chunk at its own reach, height and pace, a third of them streaming flame, all shedding embers and smoke. Every 0.6s it flares: 70% to each foe within 1.35 (flyers too), chunks of rock bursting off them. It stays flat round the funnel the whole time (Simon did not want it standing up "spinning like a coin"): in the last second it whirls faster, then is drawn in and up into the heart; at the collapse 100% to foes within 1.35, and the debris is flung out burning, splashing fire where it lands.
- **M5 Eye of the Inferno:** 0.5s before the collapse the funnel's layers spin the other way, it narrows and shudders, flames and smoke rush in to the heart, fire streams in over the road from a closing ring, and the core swells into a blazing eye; each ground foe in the pull (1.45) is marked by flames standing round its feet. Then it detonates: a billowing, climbing mass of fire with jets of flame bursting out of it, a column of fire bursting upward, a ring of fire racing 2.8 out (billows of fire bursting up along its front), glowing cracks, burning debris, sparks, smoke and a heavy shake: 180% to all within 1.5 (flyers too), and each marked foe's flames shoot up for 60% more, once.
- **The looks:** Simon, on the first look: "I dont like the 2d explosions" and the ring "should appear more naturally and look more like a ring of molten debris rather than just a perfect fire ring". So there are no flat rings of fire, shockwaves or flat glows in any burst: every burst is `ftmBoom` (noise fireballs, `ftmBall`, one a moment after another and climbing so it billows, and burning out instead of charring to soot; jets of flame made of streaming fire; sparks; smoke; burning chunks, `ftmChunk`), the Eye's ring of fire is `ftmWave` (billows of fire bursting up along a racing front), and a mastered tornado's collapse is an `ftmBoom` in place of its old flat ring of fire. Built on the fire kit of the Fire Bolt and the Ember Titan (noise fireballs, flame sheets, lava), with a shader for the burning strips (`FTM_STRIP_FS`); the shared effect light (`blmLight`, now with a colour) burns orange.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept (`SM.free.firetornado`). They are above the Rare ladder at every level (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 5 runs: 1.13 / 1.42 / 1.58 / 2.12 / 2.48 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74); the Ring of Ruin is the big step. Simon was told.
- Tests: `tests/ftm-test.js` (31, in run-all as 'ftm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old tornado; M1 rocks of 45%, twelve at most, only at foes within 2 tiles, three at most on one, one at a time and all home; M2 25% more only at the heart, a 60% burst once for a foe held 0.6s (and at the collapse for one still held), the core heating and cooling; M3 four strips end to end along its path, 25% every 0.5s for a foe on one, none for a flyer or a foe beside it, no burning ground for Firestorm; M4 the ring at 2.15s, a flare every 0.6s, 70% to a foe within 1.35 and none beyond, flat throughout and whirling faster in its last second, 100% at the collapse, the debris flung; M5 the funnel reversed, narrowed and gone, marks only in the pull in the last 0.5s, 180% within 1.5 and 60% more to the marked; Firestorm still 50% hotter but not the masteries; nothing left behind at any level.

## Frost Wolves' masteries, as Simon designed them (v415)
A block before the boot line, on top of v405. Simon redesigned all five (his text, abridged, is at the top of the block) and asked for "no low effort effects or bland 2d explosions". It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.frostwolves` (Frostbite, Pack Mark, Ice Fangs, Hamstring, Winter Howl). Frost Wolves is Rare, so its masteries stay developer-mode only (`SM_OPEN`, v405); the What's new popup is unchanged, the changelog has the entry.
- **Every cast:** a `castFrostWolves` wrapper runs every cast through `fwmCast`: the same rune, wolves (`s6WolfModel`), howl, 4.6s hunt, target choice, bites (28%, 30% slower for 1.5s) and final leap and shatter (60% within 0.8) as `castFrostWolves`, but bites and shatters are 3D frost bursts: `cfmBoom` (v401) has a palette table (`CFM_PAL`) and an `ice` palette (`CFM_BOOM_FS_ICE`: white, pale cyan, deep blue, pale frost mist), with ice spikes bursting from the ground (`fwmSpikes`) instead of `flash`.
- **Razor Fangs (M1, 60% / 40%):** the wolves' jaws grow serrated ice blades (`fwmFangs`). Each wolf counts its bites in a row on one foe (a new foe starts it again); every 3rd deals 60% more, and frost cracks (`fwmCracks`, glowing strokes billboarded on the foe) spread with each bite and burst on the 3rd. A wolf's shatter deals 40% more to the foes it bit 3 times or more.
- **Flanking Instinct (M2, 45%):** a wolf joins the other's prey if that foe can survive more than one bite (Simon: only then); it circles to the far side before it lunges. While both hunt one foe, every 2nd bite between them deals 45% more (an ice cross-slash); the count ends when either turns to another foe.
- **Winter's Trail (M3, 35%):** a running wolf leaves a glowing ice sigil (`fwmSigil`) every 0.22s; 0.45s later it bursts into crystals: 35% and 20% slower for 1s to ground foes within 0.32, each foe at most once every 0.6s from the whole trail.
- **Howl of the North (M4, 75% / 50%):** after the first howl the wolves howl again (the hunt starts 0.45s later): a wave of frost rolls out from the hero (`fwmFrostWave`: rings of ice spikes and mist): 75% and 35% slower for 2s to every foe within 1.4 (flyers too); each wolf's first bite on a howled foe deals 50% more.
- **Ragnarok Pack (M5, 100% / 100%):** when the hunt ends both wolves stop, turn to the strongest foe within 3 tiles of each, blaze white (their own copies of the ice material) as crackling runs over them (0.5s), and leap 0.25s apart: 100% to each one's target, then each shatters for 100% within 1.1 (instead of 60% within 0.8), a bigger frost burst and ring of spikes; a foe caught by both takes both.
- **Balance:** power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.31 / 1.73 / 1.78 / 2.32 / 3.00 against the Rare ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74; Simon's numbers are above the ladder at every level, so no hidden factors (`SM.free.frostwolves`).
- Tests: `tests/fwm-test.js` (27, in run-all as 'fwm'): Mastery 0 bites and shatters as before with no masteries; every 3rd bite in a row per wolf (a new foe starts again) 60% more, matching the hits, and the shatter's 40% to foes a wolf bit 3 times; both wolves on a foe that survives a bite, every 2nd bite 45% more, never on foes one bite kills; trail bursts 35% to ground foes within 0.32, never flyers, at most once every 0.6s; the second howl, its 75% wave within 1.4 (flyers too), each wolf's first bite on a howled foe 50% more, the hunt 0.45s later; Ragnarok on the strongest foe, 100% leaps, shatters 0.25s apart for 100% within 1.1 each; each needs its level; unmastered casts get no masteries; every level runs clean; nothing left behind. `tests/smgate-test.js`'s changelog row checks that v405 sits above v404 instead of pinning it on top, and `tests/smast3-test.js` lets a Rare scroll on its designer's own numbers (`SM.free`) sit above the ladder as long as it reaches 3/4 of it (as on the v410 branch).

## Entangling Roots' masteries, as Simon designed them (v416)
Written straight into `src/kingsroad-keep.html` (search `Entangling Roots' masteries`, just before the boot line), on main v405 (alongside v407 Spatial Deflection, v411 Murder of Crows, v413 Ice Tomb and v414 Fire Tornado and v415 Frost Wolves on their own branches; they share nothing but the same two small test edits). Simon's text, abridged, is at the top of the block; he asked again for no low-effort or bland 2D effects, and warned against reapplying Overgrown too often (only Mastery 2, 0.5s twice, and Mastery 5, 3s once, touch it). It replaces the v370 `SM_D/SMF/SMA/SMG.roots` (Thorned Roots, Deep Roots, Root Network, Sap Drain, Sapling). Rare, so developer mode only for now.
- **How it hangs together:** Mastery 0, echoes and other casters are the old roots (`rtmC(o)`). From Mastery 1 `castRoots` is replaced by `rtmCast`, the old cast line for line (rune, ground burst, five roots and their side roots growing 0.2s and sinking in the last 0.45s of 1.9s; 170%, the hold, the 50% slow, Overgrown 3s) that keeps each root's curve, and `rtmRun` plays the masteries on one clock from the cast. Percentages are of the scroll's power. Nature hits on a held foe still set off the Deep Roots combo (+40%), as before.
- **Lift:** the old lift (`e.liftT`) always lasted 1.6s; it now takes an optional length `e.liftD` (cleared when it lands), so Earth's Reprisal can throw a foe for 0.9s (`rtmLift`, never over a lift already running).
- **Thornbound (M1, 100% / 150%):** nine thorns per root (an instanced mesh, dark wood fading to bone at the tip) push out along every root from 0.25s to 1.2s, pointing away from the prisoner; at 1.32s they swing inward and stab (0.08s, stretching), then each prisoner takes 100%, or 150% if it was Overgrown before the cast, with sap, dark red flecks and a few splinters. The thorns sink with the roots.
- **Root Network (M2, 55% x 2):** with two or more prisoners, from 0.3s thick thorny roots tunnel between them (a tree joining the nearest ones), arching out of the soil and diving back with heaps of soil over the buried stretches and dirt thrown up at the tip. At 0.55s and 1.25s one prisoner struggles and a band of light runs out through the network from it at 4 tiles a second, along the roots and along a glowing vein in the soil above them, with a spark at its head; as it reaches each prisoner, a burst of light, 55% and Overgrown kept to at least 0.5s (no hold). The additive glow vanished on the grass, so the band is painted (lime to white).
- **Verdant Reclamation (M3, 35% a second):** at 1.6s the ground where each prisoner was caught blooms for 3s: a moss stain spreading with a ragged edge (merging where patches overlap) and five low thorny bramble arcs with leaves, flowers and berries; every 0.5s each ground foe on any patch takes 17.5% (once, however many patches) and is slowed 25%.
- **Earth's Reprisal (M4, 130% / 40%):** at 1.45s a great root (with four splayed roots at its base) bursts up under each prisoner with cracks, dirt, leaves, a green flash and a small shake; 130%, a non-boss is thrown up for 0.9s and held 0.75s, a boss only staggers; a wave of churned earth runs out 0.9: 40% to the other ground foes it reaches (not prisoners, not flyers).
- **Heart of the Wildwood (M5, 180% / 100%):** at 0.35s a great rune (two druid circles) glows under the group with motes rising; two ancient roots per prisoner surge in from the rim, arching over the ground, and coil round it; at 1.15s they burst into 150 wood splinters (from along the roots, tumbling and settling), leaves, dust and an earth wave: 180% more to the prisoners, 100% to the other foes within 1.5 of any prisoner (flyers too), and every foe it hits gets 3s more Overgrown.
- **Balance:** Simon's numbers are kept, all SMA/SMG are 1 and `SM.free.roots` is set. Power (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 2 runs): 1.94 / 2.34 / 3.60 / 4.65 / 6.23 against the ladder 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74.
- **Older tests:** `tests/smast3-test.js` gives a scroll in `SM.free` the 3/4-of-the-ladder rule and `tests/smgate-test.js` accepts a later update than v405 on top of the changelog (the same two edits as v407, v411 and v413).
- Tests: `tests/rtm-test.js` (36, in run-all as 'rtm'): the card; Mastery 0 as before; the thorns' 100% at 1.4s and 150% to a foe Overgrown before; the network's four links, two struggles 0.7s apart, 55% twice to each, the struggling one first, Overgrown kept to 0.5s, none for a lone prisoner; patches at 1.6s, six 17.5% ticks, no stacking, flyers and foes off them spared, the slow; 130%, throws for all but a boss, the 40% wave only to other ground foes; at Mastery 5 the eruption at 1.15s, 180%, 100% within 1.5 (flyers too), 3s more Overgrown, Masteries 1-4 riding it; echoes and other casters as the old roots; the real cast path; nothing left behind.

## The Rare masteries update: v406 to v416 in one (published as update 45)
Simon: "merge them all together and give me download link so we can publish update". Branch `rare-masteries-update`, merged into main: the Hall of Champions and profiles (v406, `friends-leaderboard`) and the ten Rare scrolls' masteries as Simon designed them, each from its own branch: Spatial Deflection (v407), Riftfang (v408), Piercing Verdict (v409), Magma Geyser (v410), Murder of Crows (v411), Lightning Cloud (v412), Ice Tomb (v413), Fire Tornado (v414), Frost Wolves (v415) and Entangling Roots (v416). The Rare masteries stay in developer mode (`SM_OPEN` is still Common and Uncommon) until Simon opens them.
- **How the branches met:** each was built on v405 and added the same kinds of things, so the merges met in the same places: a changelog entry at the top of `CLOG` (the newer one on top, so CLOG runs v416 down to v405), a module just before the boot line (in version order), a README section (in version order), the build check and the suite list in `tests/run-all.js` (all eleven checks, newest first, and every suite: lb, prof, dfm, rfm, pvm, gmm, crm, scm, itm, ftm, fwm, rtm) and the same relaxed rows in `tests/smgate-test.js` and `tests/smast3-test.js`. Piercing Verdict and Fire Tornado gave `blmLight` the same colour argument, which merged as one.
- **The blast palettes:** Riftfang (void) and Frost Wolves (ice) both gave Catch Flame's blast (`cfmBoom`, v401) a second colour scheme. They now sit side by side: `CFM_PAL` holds fire, ice and void (sparks, embers, kicks, dust, scorch, smoke), and `cfmBoomMat` picks `CFM_BOOM_FS_ICE`, `CFM_BOOM_FS_VOID` or the fire shader by `o.pal`.
- **Patch notes (`masteries-3`):** several branches had added their own line (and their own closing of the notes), which would have stopped the game from loading; the notes are one block again: the Hall of Champions and profiles, the Common and Uncommon reworks, one line naming the ten reworked Rare scrolls (developer mode for now) and Lightning Cloud's higher, see-through cloud (every cast). `NOTES_V` moved on to `masteries-3`, so a player who saw the last notes sees these once. `tests/smgate-test.js` checks the one Rare line and that no per-scroll "(Rare)" lines are left.

## Rare masteries for everyone (v417, update 45.1)
Simon: "change the rare scrolls to be public now". `SM_OPEN` is now Common, Uncommon and Rare: the ten Rare scrolls earn mastery XP and use their masteries in every game, not only in developer mode (the mastery panel's line now reads "above Rare"). The patch notes (`masteries-4`, so they show once more) say the Uncommon and Rare masteries are open to everyone and drop "developer mode" from the Rare line; the changelog has a v417 entry.
- Tests: an Epic scroll is now the example of a scroll that waits (`tests/smgate-test.js`, `tests/mpg-test.js`, `tests/spm-test.js`); `tests/smgate-test.js` also checks that every Rare scroll is open and earns XP outside developer mode.

## How masteries are designed now: Simon's framework (MASTERY_DESIGN.md)
Simon (28 Oct): "heres new thought process you remember please when making new masteries". Written down in `MASTERY_DESIGN.md` for every session: name the spell's identity first, grow the masteries out of what it already has (a cosmetic detail made to matter is a favourite), change behaviour rather than numbers, give each mastery its own design question and part of the spell, check that it creates a decision, check the numbers (trigger frequency, targets, overlap, control, bosses, synergies), design the look and the mechanic together (readability, anticipation, payoff), respect the game's limits, and run the eight-point checklist (identity, novelty, gameplay, visuals, balance, compatibility, rarity, roster fit) before presenting a set.

## Frostfall Comet's masteries (v419), the first set designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Frostfall Comet: one slow, telegraphed strike from the sky (the sky dims, a rune and a growing shadow mark the spot, the comet falls on where the densest foe will be 1.45s later), 600% within 1.6 and a 50% slow, a crown of ice spikes and a frozen crater that slows 40%. Each mastery grows a different part of it and asks its own question; none takes another scroll's identity (Skyfall: a rain of meteors; Glacial Rift: crystals racing along the road; Cataclysm: a battlefield shockwave; Ice Tomb: the ticking prison). The old masteries (Frost Crater, Ice Spikes, Brittle, Deep Freeze, Meteor Shower) are gone; Absolute Zero (Ice Tomb + comet) is untouched.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Comet's Eye | what if it could correct its aim? | targeting | behaviour change |
| 2 | Frostfall Tail | what if the frost it trails actually fell? | the approach and the tail (only looks before) | new phase |
| 3 | Crown of Spikes | what if the spikes impaled? | the crown (only looks before) | cosmetic made real |
| 4 | Faultlines | what if the impact set foes up for other ice? | statuses, other scrolls and the Frost Spire | interaction |
| 5 | Rebound | what if its energy were not spent? | the finale | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `castComet` calls `fcmStart(o, K)` and gets `M` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old comet). `M.fly` runs every frame of the fall (it may move `E`, the landing spot, with the rune and the shadow), `M.impact` at the blow with the foes it hit, `M.after` every frame after it. `cx/cz` now follow `E`.
- **M1 Comet's Eye:** every 0.08s of the fall (until 85% of it) it takes where each foe will be when it lands, scores each spot (taken as near as it can steer, 1.4 tiles from its first aim) by how many the blast would take in, centres on them if that loses none, and steers there at 2.6 tiles a second; never for a spot no better than its aim. A faint shaft of cold light runs from the comet to the spot and a ring of crystals over it closes as it locks. It adds no damage of its own: it mends the spell's weakness, a nuke that misses when the pack changes pace.
- **M2 Frostfall Tail:** it comes in low (4.4 high) from 4.6 tiles up the road, over the foes still marching in; from a quarter to near the end of its fall its tail sheds shards every 0.06s that fall onto the road at the point matching how far it has come (it follows the road's bends): 50% to each ground foe within 0.55 of where one lands, once a cast, and a 30% chill for 1.5s.
- **M3 Crown of Spikes:** a spike (with two side blades) bursts up under every ground foe the blast hits, through its body: 80% and pinned 0.5s (`smStun`: Resolve applies, bosses 40%). The spikes crumble with the crown. Flyers are above them.
- **M4 Faultlines:** every foe the impact hits glows with ice fractures for 4s (cracks of light across it, plates of ice, a cold glow that swells near the end). The next ice hit from anything else (`o.el==='ice'` and not the comet: another scroll, a Frost Spire bolt) shatters them, 100% and frozen 0.4s (Resolve applies); otherwise they give way at the end for 50%. So it pays alone and more beside other ice. A `hurt` wrapper watches for the ice hit; damage over time does not count.
- **M5 Rebound:** 1.2s after the impact the comet's heart wakes in the crater (a core and halos glowing brighter and pulsing faster, frost drawn in, light); at 2.3s, as the crown gives way, it bursts out (splinters, mist) and arcs with a frost tail onto the toughest foe within 4 tiles (flyers too, where it will be): 250%, a burst of ice, a small crown of spikes, frost mist and a thud; 0.3s later it bounces once more onto the toughest other foe within 4 tiles of there for 150%, then shatters. With nobody to hit it shatters in the crater.
- **The looks:** ice shards and splinters (`nsIceGeos`), crystal spikes, frost mist and glitter, the gaze and its ring of crystals, the heart (rock, core, halos) with its own frost tail (`nsTrail`). No flat rings or shockwaves of its own.
- **Balance:** each mastery's own numbers follow from what it does, and the hidden factor `SMG.comet` [1.15, 1.3, 1.3, 1.42, 1.37] brings it onto the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 4 runs: 1.10 / 1.24 / 1.36 / 1.53 / 1.75 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to 1.00 / 1.03 / 1.12 / 1.17 / 1.36: the comet already kills the ladder's churn of 600-health foes outright, so damage added on top of its blast counts for nothing there, and the Eye only pays when the pack changes pace (the ladder's foes stand still).
- Tests: `tests/fcm-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'fcm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old comet; M1 steers onto a knot out of its first reach (at most 1.4) and does not wander off a good aim; M2 comes in from up the road, 50% once to the foes under its path, none to a flyer or a foe off the road; M3 80% and a pin for each ground foe, none for a flyer; M4 fractures on each foe hit, an ice hit from elsewhere shatters them for 100% (a fire hit does not, nor the comet's own spikes), the rest give way at 4s for 50%; M5 the heart wakes and bursts on time, 250% on the toughest within 4 tiles (none further), then 150% on another, and shatters with nobody left; nothing left behind at any level.

## v420: every explosion is 3D (Simon's rule, applied to the old effects)
Simon: "theres gonna be a rule I have to set for all future and current explosion effects, no bland 2d effect, it should be 3d even if just a little bit, no too much to obstruct the entire vision but what we have right now just sucks". The rule is written into `MASTERY_DESIGN.md` for every new effect. For the effects that already exist, the flat building blocks most of them share get height, so every old blast built from them is 3D at once (module `XFX`, just before the boot line):

| Piece | Used by | Before | Now |
|---|---|---|---|
| `shockwave` | 112 casts | flat arcs on the ground | the arcs stay as a fainter accent, and a wall of light rises and races out with them, ragged at its top, sinking as it goes; reach 1.2+ also throws stones; a battlefield-wide wave keeps its wall faint |
| `ringFx` | 85 casts | a flat ring | a lower band of light rises with it |
| `myFireRing`, `ebFireRing` | Ember Titan, Fire Bolt | a flat ring of fire | a wall of fire rises with it |
| `smNova` | the old mastery bursts | flat rings | built from the two above, so it rises too |
| `flash` | 386 calls | a hard-edged faceted ball that read as a flat white disc | a soft ball of light, hot at its heart and clear at its edge (`FLASH_VS`/`FLASH_FS`) |
| Thunderclap | its strike | a 1.2-wide white ball hiding the strike | a quick small glow; the bolt, the wall of thunder and three stones are the blast |
| Frostfall Comet | its impact and its masteries' bursts | a 2.6-wide glow and a screen flash | `xfxFrostBoom`: billowing frost clouds (`xfxFrostBall`), jets of frost, splinters of ice and a low cold mist |

- The walls are see-through, short (at most half a tile) and fade as they grow, so they never cover the road or the foes. `XFX.off=true` turns them off for side-by-side comparisons.
- Checked in screenshots at normal zoom and close up with a crowd: Thunderclap, Cataclysm, Magma Geyser, Ember Bolt and the comet. Cataclysm's own blast still has big bright shells and leaves a dark purple cloud for about a second; it is in the Epic round (v423), and its base cast is to be brought up to the rule there.
- Tests: `tests/xfx-test.js` (9, in run-all as 'xfx'): a shockwave raises one wall standing on the ground with height, which races out, sinks and fades and is gone with the wave; a wide wave's wall stays faint; rings of light and fire rise too; `XFX.off` turns the walls off; the frost blast is clouds with body and leaves nothing; a flash is a soft ball of light that fades and is gone; no errors.
## Rite of the Phoenix's masteries (v421), designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Rite of the Phoenix: a phoenix is born from a burning rune above the hero, dives in front of the foe nearest the keep and skims 9 tiles back along the road through the enemy (350% to each foe it passes, burning 40% a second for 4s), leaving a river of fire, then climbs and bursts into burning feathers. A phoenix is also the bird that comes back from its ashes. Each mastery grows a different part of the spell and asks its own question. None takes another scroll's identity: Catch Flame spreads fire from foe to foe and the Fire Tornado drinks burning ground, while these masteries stay with the bird, its river, its feathers and its rebirth. Replaces the v370 set (Brighter Flames, Ember Wake, Phoenix Feathers, Pyre, Rebirth).

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rising Heat | what does the phoenix gain from the crowd? | the skim | behaviour change |
| 2 | Wildfire River | what does the road remember? | the river of fire | new phase |
| 3 | Burning Plumage | what if the feathers it bursts into were real? | the death burst (only looks before) | cosmetic made real |
| 4 | Pyre | what does it do with fire that is already burning? | its strikes, the river and the feathers, and other fire | interaction |
| 5 | Rebirth | what comes back from the ashes? | the whole spell, a second life | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** a `castPhoenix` wrapper runs every cast through `phmCast` (search `Rite of the Phoenix's masteries`, just before the boot line), the old cast line for line (birth, aim, dive, skim, river, climb, burst) with hooks; `phmC(o)` gives the mastery context (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters). Even at Mastery 0 its birth, its strikes and its death burst are now 3D fireballs (`cfmBoom`) instead of flat flashes.
- **M1 Rising Heat (7%):** every strike deals 7% more for each foe it struck before, up to 5 (35% from the 6th foe on), and the bird grows 8% a foe, its body runs white-gold and its glow and embers swell.
- **M2 Wildfire River (60%):** 0.25s after it climbs away a front of flame races back along the river from where the skim began to where it ended (10 tiles a second), throwing up twisting flame tongues (`phmTongue`) and small fireballs every 0.45 tiles: 60% to each ground foe within 0.45 of the road's middle as it passes, and burning again (40% a second for 2s). Not flyers.
- **M3 Burning Plumage (50%):** 8 real feathers (glowing, trailing fire) flutter out of the burst and dart down at the foes it struck that still stand, toughest first; with fewer than 8 some take two (at most two a foe); with none struck, the nearest foes within 6 tiles. 50% each, burning for 2s, a small fireball on impact.
- **M4 Pyre (10%):** when its fire (a strike, the river's front, a feather) hits a foe that was already burning, from any source, the foe erupts (a 3D fireball with a skirt of flame and a flame tongue): 10% to every foe within 0.75, once per foe a cast. Alone, its strikes set foes burning for the river and the feathers to set off; cast after other fire, foes erupt at the first strike.
- **M5 Rebirth (15% / 50%):** after the burst its heart (a white-hot core in halos) falls as an ember to the road just behind the rearmost foe it struck, a pyre burns there for 0.6s (a rune, a ring of flame tongues, embers spiralling up), it erupts and the phoenix is reborn from it, a third bigger and golden. It skims toward the keep (7 tiles a second, up to 1.2s, until it passes the foremost foe): 15% to each foe it passes, with Rising Heat still counting and Pyre still erupting; then it climbs and dives where the foes it passed are thickest: a mushroom fireball with a skirt of flame, flame tongues and feathers, 50% within 1.3 tiles.
- **Balance:** the numbers are kept low because each mastery adds whole extra hits to a spell that already strikes every foe on 9 tiles of road. The hidden factor `SMG.phoenix` [1.025, 1.021, 1.016, 1.015, 1.01] tops them up to the Epic ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs): 1.10 / 1.24 / 1.35 / 1.51 / 1.75 against 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742. Not in `SM.free`.
- Tests: `tests/phm-test.js` (33, in run-all as 'phm'): the card and its numbers; Mastery 0 the same skim with 3D fire and no flat flashes; M1's count (0 to 5) and 7% per foe; M2's front after the climb, 60% once to the ground foes on the river, none to a flyer or a foe 0.6 off the middle; M3's feathers, two each for 3 foes, one each for the 8 toughest of 10; M4 erupting at the strike for a foe already burning, at the river's front for the others, once each, the 10% hits within 0.75; M5's heart, pyre and rebirth, 15% to each foe on the way, Rising Heat carried over, the stop past the foremost foe, the 50% dive where they are thickest, nothing left behind; non-mastered casts get no masteries; every mastery with deaths without errors.

## Spatial Loop's masteries (v422), designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Spatial Loop: space folds. The road ahead of the foe nearest the keep loops back onto an earlier stretch for 10 seconds, so foes that reach the fold walk out 3 to 8 tiles back (the world-space warp of the SDP shader shows the road meeting itself) and cross ground they already crossed. It deals no damage: it buys time and gives the towers a second go. Each mastery grows a different part of it and asks its own question. None takes another scroll's identity: Spatial Compression squeezes and whips back, Deflection turns blows aside, Chronostasis stops time; these stay with the fold, its seam, the laps and the unfolding. Replaces the v370 set (Dizzy Return, Torn Space, Echo Fold, Fold Rift, Collapse).

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Long Fold | what if it did not leave the distance to chance? | how far back the fold reaches | behaviour change |
| 2 | Shear | what if crossing the seam hurt? | the seam (only a picture before) | cosmetic made real |
| 3 | Groundhog Lap | what if the loop remembered who it had already sent round? | the laps | new rule |
| 4 | Displaced | what if the second pass was worth more to the towers? | towers, heroes and other scrolls | interaction |
| 5 | Snapback | what happens when space lets go? | the unfolding | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** a `castLoop` wrapper runs every cast through `lpmCast` (search `Spatial Loop's masteries`, just before the boot line), the old cast line for line with the mastery hooks; `lpmC(o)` gives the mastery context (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters). A `foldHop` wrapper tells the current fold (`LPM.cur`) about each foe it sends back; a new loop (which ends the old one) or the end of the 10.8s clears it.
- **M1 Long Fold:** the fold always reaches back 8 tiles (or as far as the road allows) instead of a random 3 to 8. From Mastery 1 the seam shows (`lpmSeam`): a curtain of tall panes of folded light across the road (soft gradient panes, shimmering, glints drifting up), fading in and out with the fold.
- **M2 Shear (40%):** each time the fold sends a foe back it takes 40%, and space tears open where it went in and where it came out (`lpmTear`: a burst of spinning shards of folded space, a shell of bent light snapping shut, a flaring core).
- **M3 Groundhog Lap (50%):** the fold counts each foe's laps; from the second on, each lap deals 50% more for every lap before it (lap 2: 50%, lap 3: 100%, ...), and a ring of folded light closes round the foe for each lap past the first (up to 4, `lpmRings`).
- **M4 Displaced (15%):** for 3s after the fold sends a foe back it takes 15% more from everything with a source (towers, heroes, other scrolls), through the same `smMk['*']` mark the scroll marks use, while splinters of space orbit it (`lpmOrbit`).
- **M5 Snapback (120% / 40%):** when the 10 seconds end, a wave of shattering space runs along the looped stretch from where the foes came out up to the fold in 0.6s (`lpmPanes`: panes bursting up out of the road and breaking, shards): 120% to each foe within 0.6 of the road as it passes, plus 40% for each lap it walked, and held 0.5s (`smStun`, Resolve applies).
- **Balance:** the ladder does not apply: the loop itself deals no damage, so its factors stay 1 (as in v372) and the new numbers sit beside the damage of the set they replace. In a pack of 12 walking foes (3 runs, effects stepped), mastery damage per cast was old 0 / 0 / 547 / 559 / 901 / 1806 and is now 0 / 0 / 453 / 480 / 480 / 2480 (Displaced pays through other damage, so it adds nothing here). Not in `SM.free`.
- Tests: `tests/lpm-test.js` (27, in run-all as 'lpm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the same random fold, no damage, nothing added; M1's 8 tiles, its seam (gone with the fold), no damage; M2's 40% for each send-back; M3 laps 1 to 3 and 50% for each lap before, none on the first; M4's mark (3s, 15%), a tower-like hit dealing 115 instead of 100 and 100 after; M5's snap on the looped stretch only, 120% plus 40% a lap, the laps it counts, nothing left behind; non-mastered casts get none; a second loop ends the first cleanly; every mastery with deaths without errors.

## Cataclysm's masteries (v423), designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Cataclysm: the sky breaks the earth. A rune opens in the sky over the thickest knot of foes, the ground cracks under it (1.9 tiles), eight black obsidian pillars erupt (200% each to the foes on them), the cracked ground deals 100%, and then the sky rune falls into the ground in a collapse that hurls the foes into the air (300%, falling to half at 3.4 tiles; a push, a 1s stun and a lift). Each mastery grows a different part of it and asks its own question. None takes another scroll's identity: Voidfall and Singularity pull foes in, Grave Burst throws the dead; these stay with the sky rune, the pillars and the collapse. Replaces the v370 set (Abyssal Cracks, Black Pillars, Doom Mark, Aftershock, Eclipse Rune).

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Drawn Down | what if a pillar rose where the foe stands? | where the pillars rise | behaviour change |
| 2 | Falling Sky | what if the sky started breaking before the rune fell? | the 2.2s charge | new phase |
| 3 | Obsidian Chains | what if the pillars held them? | the standing pillars (only scenery before) | cosmetic made real |
| 4 | Sundered | what if the towers could finish what the collapse broke? | towers and soldiers after the collapse | interaction |
| 5 | World Breaker | what if the pillars came back down? | the finale | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** a `castCataclysm` wrapper runs every cast through `ccmCast` and `ccmExplode` (search `Cataclysm's masteries`, just before the boot line): copies of `castCataclysm` and `cataExplode` with the mastery hooks. `ccmC(o)` gives the mastery context (null at Mastery 0, for echoes, combos and other casters). Even at Mastery 0 the pillars burst out of the ground and shatter in 3D void fireballs (`cfmBoom`, void palette) instead of flat flashes, the collapse is a brief 3D void fireball with a skirt, and it no longer whites out or smokes over the field: the screen flash is .3 instead of .8, the white core and the plain sphere are smaller and dimmer, the rising ring fainter, the after-blast is a thin violet shell gone in 0.75s, the smoke and dust are light violet-grey and gone in about a second (they lingered for 2s, near black), and the camera-facing ring is gone (main session's review of v420). The shared `shockwave` calls are left as they are (v420 upgrades the helper).
- **M1 Drawn Down:** the first of the outer pillars rises right under the toughest foe inside the rune, where it is when the pillar rises (a random spot as before if there is none).
- **M2 Falling Sky (70%):** from 0.6s, every 0.35s, a shard breaks off the sky rune and falls as an obsidian meteor (a black glass shard with a glowing crown, a violet trail and smoke) onto a different foe inside the rune: 70% within 0.5 tiles and a 3D void burst; 4 shards.
- **M3 Obsidian Chains:** a rising pillar chains every ground foe within 0.9 of it with links of black glass (`ccmChain`) that hold it until the collapse (`smStun`: Resolve applies, bosses 40%); the collapse then hits a chained foe at full strength (300%, no falloff), and the chains burst.
- **M4 Sundered (25%):** foes the collapse hits are Sundered for 5s, shards of obsidian sticking out of them (`ccmSunder`): physical, piercing and siege hits (archers, ballistas, cannons, soldiers) deal 25% more to them (a `hurt` wrapper on `e.ccmSu`). Magic is unchanged.
- **M5 World Breaker (35%):** the collapse tears the risen pillars out of the ground instead of shattering them: they spin up into the sky (0.55s), hang, and slam down one after another (0.12s apart) onto the toughest foes within 4.5 tiles, a different foe each while there are enough: 35% within 0.75 tiles each, a 3D void explosion with debris and sparks.
- **Balance:** the hidden factor `SMG.cataclysm` [1, 1, 1.06, 1.19, 1.15] tops the masteries up to the Epic ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 5 runs): 1.10 / 1.22 / 1.37 / 1.49 / 1.74 against 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742. Sundered pays only through towers, which the ladder does not have, so Mastery 4 leans on its factor. Not in `SM.free`.
- Tests: `tests/ccm-test.js` (29, in run-all as 'ccm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 eight pillars at 200%, the collapse's 300% with its falloff, no masteries, no flat flashes; M1's pillar under the toughest foe; M2's four shards before the collapse on four foes, 70% within 0.5; M3's chains within 0.9, never a flyer, held until the collapse, full 300% for the chained; M4's 5s, 125 for physical and piercing, 100 for magic and after; M5's eight pillars on different toughest foes 0.12s apart, 35% within 0.75, nothing left behind; non-mastered casts get none; every mastery with deaths without errors.
## Thunder Wyrm's masteries (v424), designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Thunder Wyrm: a serpent of living lightning rises from a storm rune at the hero's feet, dives onto the road, weaves back through the enemy line for about 2s lashing what it passes (120% and Charged, each foe at most every 0.5s) and rears up to strike the densest knot like a thunderbolt (400% within 1.3). Identity: **a hunting serpent of living lightning**. Design space: its rise and dive, the weave (where it goes, how fast, what it lashes), its long crackling body (only looks before), the Charge it leaves, the rear-up and the strike. Roster: no battlefield storm (Stormcaller), no bolts from a cloud (Lightning Cloud), no chain hopping foe to foe (Ball Lightning, Static Spark): every mastery is about the serpent itself. The old masteries (Static Scales, Chain Lash, Stun Coil, Storm Trail, Twin Wyrm) are gone.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Crackling Coils | what if the arcs between its coils were real? | the body (only looks before) | cosmetic made real |
| 2 | Hunter's Weave | what if it chose its path? | the weave: speed and sway | behaviour change |
| 3 | Constrict | what if it seized its prey? | a new phase in the weave | control |
| 4 | Grounding Strike | what if every lash built toward the strike? | the Charge, the strike, other lightning | interaction / build-up |
| 5 | Thunderhead | what if the strike split the sky? | the rear-up and the strike | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `castWyrm` calls `wymStart(o, {P, HD})` and gets `M` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old wyrm). `M.lash` on every lash, `M.speed`/`M.side` drive the weave, `M.coil` may take over the head for the coil, `M.rearH`/`M.rear` shape the rear-up, `M.strike` runs after the strike's damage and `M.frame` every frame.
- **M1 Crackling Coils (35%):** the arcs that flicker between its coils leap (violet-white) to foes within 0.6 of any part of its body behind the head: 35% and Charged, each foe at most every 0.4s. Its whole length becomes a weapon, so foes it has already passed still burn.
- **M2 Hunter's Weave:** it lingers in packs (half speed while a foe is within 0.9 of its head) and darts across empty road (1.6x), swaying toward the nearest foe instead of a blind zig-zag; amber sparks at its eyes while it hunts. No damage of its own: more lashes where the foes are (the tests count them on the same crowd).
- **M3 Constrict (50% x 3):** once a cast, between 1.45s and 2.7s, it seizes the toughest foe within 1.1 of its head and coils round it for 0.6s (its body winds up the foe in a helix of lightning): held (`smStun`: Resolve applies, bosses 40%) and three squeezes of 50%, each a ring of arcs round the foe.
- **M4 Grounding Strike (35% a mark):** every lash leaves a charge mark (gold sparks circling the foe, up to three); the strike grounds through every marked foe within 4 tiles: a gold fork races to each (later the further it is) and bursts on it in a small plasma shell with forks, 35% per mark. A foe Charged by anything else (Static Spark, Ball Lightning, a Stormglass, its own coils) counts as one mark.
- **M5 Thunderhead (250% / 120%):** it rears to 3.6 instead of 2.2, its head swelling, a crown of arcs winding round it, an eight-pointed storm rune spinning under its target, arcs crawling in over the ground and the sky darkening; then it falls as a column of lightning (four great bolts, a churning shaft of light that stands 0.5s): 250% more within 1.3 and 120% out to 2.2, two plasma shells, nine forks thrown up and out, ten forks raking the ground outward, 24 rocks thrown and dust climbing.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** the dive (1.2s) and the strike now burst as 3D storms (`wymBurst`: a churning plasma shell, see-through veins and rim, that swells, rises and thins out; forks of lightning thrown up and outward; rock torn out and flung in arcs; dust rising) instead of `shockwave` rings; the cracks and the scorch stay as the aftermath. The first shells were too big and too opaque at their end and hid the foes: they are smaller, thinner and gone in about half a second.
- **Balance:** each mastery's own numbers follow from what it does, and the hidden factor `SMG.wyrm` [0.95, 1.1, 0.86, 0.9, 0.86] brings it onto the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs: about 1.10 / 1.24 / 1.36 / 1.53 / 1.74 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to 1.14 / 1.12 / 1.57 / 1.67 / 1.98: Hunter's Weave shows little on the ladder (its foes stand still, so lingering gains less than in play), while Constrict and Thunderhead land hard on a pack.
- Tests: `tests/wym-test.js` (26, in run-all as 'wym'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old wyrm, its dive and strike as 3D bursts with no shockwave; M1 arcs of 35% to foes near the body, a foe at most every 0.4s; M2 more lashes than Mastery 0 on the same crowd; M3 one coil on the toughest foe near the head, stunned, three 50% squeezes; M4 35% per lash (a foe only Charged once), a foe Charged elsewhere counted once, none beyond 4 tiles; M5 the rear-up before the column, 250% within 1.3 and 120% out to 2.2, Masteries 1-4 riding it; echoes and other casters as the old wyrm; the real cast path; nothing left behind.

## Prism of Dawn's masteries (v425), designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Prism of Dawn: coloured motes lock into a crystal prism over the foes, it catches a shaft of sunlight and splits it into six coloured beams (red, orange, gold, green, blue, violet) that sweep round over the ground for about 3s, faster and faster (45% each time one passes a foe, at most every 0.22s), then fold back into one lance of light: 180% within 1.8 and a rainbow. Identity: **a crystal that splits the dawn into a wheel of colour**. Design space: the six colours (only looks before), where the prism hangs (it never moved), the sweeps over each foe, the fold, the lance and its rainbow. Roster: no lances raining on the strongest (Lance of the Seraph), no split beams or mirrors (Basic Beam's Prism Split and Lightbound Mirrors), no sunlit weakening ground (Sunflare). The old masteries (Dawn Burn, Refraction, Seventh Beam, Blinding Flash, Rainbow Burst) are gone.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Spectrum | what if each colour did what it looks like? | the six colours (only looks before) | cosmetic made real |
| 2 | Sun-Tracking | what if it followed the foes? | where the prism hangs | behaviour change |
| 3 | Blinding Fold | what if the fold dazzled them for the lance? | the fold | control, sets up the finale |
| 4 | Full Spectrum | what if catching every colour mattered? | the sweeps over one foe | build-up payoff |
| 5 | Sunrise | what if the lance were the dawn itself? | the lance | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `castPrism` calls `prmStart(o, {P, R, COLS})` and gets `PM` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old prism; not `M`, which is the cast's own material helper). `PM.hit(e, colour, folding)` on every pass, `PM.drift` may move the centre (`cx/cz/cy` are now `let`, and the prism, its rune and its sunbeam follow), `PM.lance` after the lance and `PM.frame` every frame.
- **M1 Spectrum (20% burn):** the warm beams (red, orange, gold) set the foes they sweep alight: 20% over 2s (Burning, fire), a foe at most every 1.2s; the cool beams (green, blue, violet) are light, so the next one over a burning foe sets off the Flare combo already in the game (Flashpoint: the burn is spent at once for 150% of what was left, and a little splashes round it). The wheel feeds itself, and Burning sets up the fire scrolls. With no limit on re-igniting, a foe flared on almost every turn of the wheel (1.9x on the ladder): the 1.2s limit keeps it to about three flares a cast.
- **M2 Sun-Tracking:** while the wheel turns, the prism drifts toward the middle of the foes within reach of its beams, 0.7 tiles a second, up to 1.6 tiles from where it formed, its rune and sunbeam with it (golden motes trail along the ground). No damage of its own: it mends the spell's weakness, a fixed wheel that the pack walks out of.
- **M3 Blinding Fold:** as the beams fold back in (from 4s), each foe they cross is blinded: held 0.5s (`smStun`: Resolve applies, bosses 40%), then 30% slower for 1.5s, so it is still under the prism when the lance falls. A ring of light flares round its head.
- **M4 Full Spectrum (120%):** each foe remembers the colours that have swept it (motes of those colours circle it); one swept by all six in a cast bursts with them: 120% and a small 3D rainbow (`prmNova`) out of it, once per foe.
- **M5 Sunrise (140% / 15% x 4):** after the lance the folded light shoots up into the sky (a beam racing upward), and 0.35s later the dawn comes down: a pillar of sunrise 1.5 wide, gold with a white rim, see-through, with coloured crystal spiralling up inside it, stands 1.2s: 140% more within 1.8 as it lands (and a bigger 3D rainbow), then 15% every 0.3s (four times) to the foes inside 1.5. The first pillar was taller, pinker and more solid and covered the road: it is lower, gold and mostly rim now.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** the lance's rainbow was six flat `shockwave` rings; it is `prmNova` now: 48 crystal shards in the six colours racing out in a ring with height, tumbling and falling, and fourteen rays of colour bursting up and outward; the flash is smaller. The scorch decals stay as the aftermath.
- **Balance:** each mastery's own numbers follow from what it does, and the hidden factor `SMG.prism` [0.87, 0.89, 0.96, 0.98, 0.84] brings it onto the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs: about 1.09 / 1.22 / 1.34 / 1.52 / 1.74 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to about 1.27 / 1.37 / 1.40 / 1.55 / 2.06: Spectrum leans on the Flare combo, which pays well on a pack, and Sun-Tracking adds nothing where the ladder's foes stand still.
- Tests: `tests/prm-test.js` (26, in run-all as 'prm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old prism, its rainbow in 3D with no shockwave; M1 burns only from the warm beams, and flares; M2 the prism drifts after the foes, never more than 1.6 tiles; M3 blinding only from the folding beams, once a foe, held and slowed; M4 120% once to foes swept six times or more; M5 the light rising then the pillar, 140% as it lands and up to four 15% ticks, Masteries 1-4 riding it; echoes and other casters as the old prism; the real cast path; nothing left behind.

## Oakheart Treant's masteries (v426), designed with the framework
The Epic scroll Oakheart Treant: an old oak wakes and heaves itself out of the road where the hero stands, then walks up the road toward the foes for 10s (slowing foes at its feet), and every 1.3s with foes near it smashes the ground with both arms: 240% within 1.25; foes already Overgrown are stunned (Warden Oak), the rest knocked back, all Overgrown; at the end it sinks back into the earth. Identity: **an old oak walking into the enemy line**. Design space: its walk and its heavy steps (only dust before), its body standing on the road, the slam and its reach, the Overgrown it feeds on, its size, and its end. Roster: no map-wide forest (Wrath of Gaia), no world root (the Eldritch Tree), no roots that bind (Entangling Roots), no colossus stomping across the map (the Ember Titan). The old masteries (Thorny Bark, Heavy Steps, Acorn Volley, Rootbound, Elder Wrath) are gone.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Heavy Tread | what if its footsteps were felt? | the steps (only dust before) | cosmetic made real |
| 2 | Bark Bulwark | what if it truly stood in their way? | its body on the road | behaviour change, control |
| 3 | Uprooted Boulder | what if it could strike beyond its reach? | the slam's reach | new attack |
| 4 | Sap Harvest | what if it grew from what it crushed? | Overgrown, other Nature scrolls, its size | interaction, build-up |
| 5 | Timber! | what if it fell instead of sinking? | its end | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `castTreant` (the v3xx nature rework) calls `trmStart(o, {P, W, g, rt})` and gets `T` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old treant). `T.size()` scales the slam's reach, `T.slam(cx, cz, hit, wasOvergrown)` after each slam, `T.step` on every step, `T.frame` every frame and `T.fall` may take over its last 1.45s.
- **M1 Heavy Tread (15%):** every step (about every 0.75s while it walks) shakes the road: 15% to the ground foes within 1 of it, with a puff of dust and pebbles and a small shake.
- **M2 Bark Bulwark:** ground foes cannot get past it: those that walk into it are held 0.45 in front of its bark (and shoved back up the road as it walks), each for up to 2s before it squeezes past. Bosses are too big to hold: they are only slowed 60%. The old slam landed 0.45 behind it (its walk faces its local -Z): from this mastery on it slams the foes held at its bark, in front of it. Without that, holding the pack in front pulled it out of the slam (0.81 on the ladder).
- **M3 Uprooted Boulder (160%):** every third slam, 0.3s later, it tears a boulder (rock with clods of earth) out of the road (a burst of earth at its feet) and hurls it in an arc at the thickest knot of ground foes 1.4 to 4.5 tiles away: 160% within 1, a 3D burst of earth, cracks and a thud where it lands.
- **M4 Sap Harvest (60%):** each foe its slam hits that was already Overgrown (by it, Entangling Roots, Bramble Patch, Thorn Whip, a Briarband...) is drained: 60% more, green sap streaming from it into the oak, and the treant grows 10% (up to 40% bigger), its slam reaching further with it.
- **M5 Timber! (300%):** with 1.45s left it does not sink: it creaks and rocks back (leaves shaking loose), then topples forward along the road like a felled oak: 300% to every foe under its length (2.6 tiles, wider and longer if it has grown; flyers too), and they are Overgrown; it crashes in a line of earth bursts, splinters and a storm of leaves, lies a moment and crumbles away into leaves.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** the slam was a flat `shockwave` ring; it is `trmQuake` now: clods and turf (earth, grass and stone, instanced) thrown in arcs that bounce and settle, dust billowing out and up, and the ground's cracks, mound and root spikes as before.
- **Artifact sets:** a burn only drew in a set's colours under the fire set; a burn set by a scroll of the set's own element (Spectrum's, from Prism of Dawn, under the light set) now does too (`tests/smsets-test.js` caught it). That test's "no errors" row also fails on main when the suite runs on its own (Spatial Deflection's "warp shader not found"); in the full run it passes.
- **Balance:** each mastery's own numbers follow from what it does, and the hidden factor `SMG.treant` [0.95, 1.2, 1.21, 1.19, 0.88] brings it onto the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs: about 1.09 / 1.25 / 1.37 / 1.50 / 1.72 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to about 1.15 / 1.01 / 1.14 / 1.37 / 2.07: Bark Bulwark is worth lives rather than damage (and holding the pack costs it some steps), which the ladder does not count; Timber! lands on a whole pack.
- Tests: `tests/trm-test.js` (27, in run-all as 'trm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old treant, every slam a 3D burst with no shockwave; M1 15% steps to ground foes within 1, not flyers; M2 a foe held and shoved back up the road, a boss only slowed; M3 boulders at foes further off, 160% where they land; M4 60% to Overgrown foes and growth up to 40%; M5 the creak before the fall, 300% to foes under its length (Overgrown) and none beside it; echoes and other casters as the old treant; the real cast path; nothing left behind.

## Voidfall's masteries (v430), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Voidfall: the world falls silent, a black sphere swallows the ground over the densest spot, collapses, and a pillar of void crashes down: 800% within 2.2 tiles, lifting the foes under it into the air with shards of void orbiting them, then a wave of 300% within 4.5. Simon asked for the Legendary scrolls (all but Dimensional Slash) to be split between the four sessions; this one and Grim Harvest are this session's.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Gravity Well | what if the sphere swallowed foes, not just stones? | the sphere (the earth it drew in was only looks) | cosmetic made real, coverage |
| 2 | Hard Landing | what if what goes up came down hard? | the fall after the lift | a new phase |
| 3 | Shattered Orbit | what if the shards orbiting them were weapons? | the orbiting shards (only looks) | cosmetic made real |
| 4 | Hollow | what if the void stayed inside them? | the foes it struck, the player's other damage | interaction |
| 5 | Event Horizon | what if the void came back for the foes it threw? | the lift, the sphere | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** a `castVoidfall` wrapper runs `vfmRun` beside the old cast, on its clock (the sphere 0.15 to 1.75s, the pillar at 2.02s, the wave at 2.25s). An `orbitFrags` wrapper keeps each lifted foe's orbiting shards, a `hurt` wrapper fills the hollows, an `updFx` wrapper releases them. The old masteries (Void Touched, Event Horizon slow, Void Shards, Long Fall, Second Void) are gone.
- **M1 Gravity Well:** while the sphere forms, ground foes within 4 tiles that have walked past the point are dragged back along the road toward it, 1.5 tiles a second, up to 1.5 tiles each (bosses a third of both), with threads of void tugging at them. Like the other Legendary scrolls it moves them itself, so it spends no Resolve. No damage: it mends the spell's weakness (the front of a fast pack walking out of a 2s wind-up).
- **M2 Hard Landing (60% / 30%):** each foe the pillar threw up slams back to earth when its lift ends: 60% to it and 30% to foes within 0.9, a 3D burst of void (`cfmBoom` in the void palette with a skirt), stones thrown in arcs, dust and a dark mark.
- **M3 Shattered Orbit (25%):** 0.75s after the impact (the top of the flight) the five shards orbiting each lifted foe tear free (the old orbit hides) and spear the nearest other foes within 3 tiles, 25% each, bursting into splinters of void glass.
- **M4 Hollow (25%, 5s, up to 300%):** foes the pillar strikes are Hollowed: a slow cage of void glass turns round them, faster and brighter the more it holds. A quarter of all damage they take from anything else (towers, heroes, other scrolls, the wave) is drawn in; when it ends a lance of void falls on the foe and gives it all back at once.
- **M5 Event Horizon (150% + 30% per other foe, up to 8):** at the top of their flight a second black sphere opens 2.5 tiles over the crater, holds every lifted foe (not bosses) in the air, bound by threads of void, and raises them to 1.45; 1.45s after the impact it implodes: shards rush up from each foe into it, 150% to each plus 30% for every other foe it holds, a 3D void burst in the air, and drops them (Hard Landing follows).
- **Balance:** `SMG.voidfall` [1.135, 1.2, 1.285, 1.43, 1.4] brings it onto the Legendary ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs: about 1.08 / 1.23 / 1.36 / 1.54 / 1.79 before the last tweak, against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to about 0.98 / 1.03 / 1.05 / 1.07 / 1.22: the ladder's foes stand still, so the Well adds nothing there.
- Tests: `tests/vfm-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'vfm'): the cards and numbers; Mastery 0 the old Voidfall; M1 a foe past the point dragged back (at most 1.5), a boss a third, a foe still coming untouched, no Resolve spent; M2 every lifted foe lands once, 60% and 30% beside it; M3 five shards a lifted foe, 25% each; M4 every struck foe Hollowed, a quarter of an outside hit given back as one hit, capped; M5 the foes held high, 150% + 30% each, then they land; nothing left behind; rendering.

## Grim Harvest's masteries (v431), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Grim Harvest: darkness falls and a giant reaper rises from the road ahead of the strongest foe, sweeps up the path twice (600%, then 300%, radius 4.3) leaving clouds of Wither (60% a second), and every soul he reaps flies back to the hero, hurrying its other scrolls.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Culling Edge | what if the reaper did what reapers do? | the scythe | behaviour change |
| 2 | Soul Swarm | what if the souls he took fought for him? | the souls circling him (only looks) | cosmetic made real |
| 3 | Creeping Wither | what if the rot spread? | the clouds of Wither | coverage, interaction |
| 4 | Borrowed Time | what if the souls were not only time but power? | the hero, the player's other scrolls | interaction |
| 5 | Final Toll | what if death came for the strongest himself? | the mark | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `SMF.harvest.hit` (each reap) and `.cast` (after the old cast, with its own clock), and wrappers on `nsReaper` (where he stands), `nsWitherCloud` (his clouds), `kill` (Creeping Wither), `castScrollNow` (Borrowed Time) and `clearWorld` (nothing carries over to a new game). The old masteries (Soul Rend, Marked for Death, Lost Souls, Third Sweep, Death's Embrace) are gone.
- **M1 Culling Edge (15%; bosses 25%):** a foe the scythe leaves under 15% of its health (not a boss) is reaped outright, its soul (a pale wraith) torn up out of it in a burst of soul fire; a boss the scythe touches takes 25% of the hit more instead.
- **M2 Soul Swarm (40%):** every foe the first sweep reaps leaves a soul (a white heart, a violet halo and a ribbon trail) circling the reaper at chest height (up to 8); on the backhand they hurl out in arcs at the foes in reach, 40% each, bursting in 3D soul fire.
- **M3 Creeping Wither (60% a second, up to 6 a cast):** a foe that dies while withering in his clouds bursts into a green-black billow of spores and a new cloud of Wither where it falls (radius 1.3, the same rot), so the clouds creep after the pack.
- **M4 Borrowed Time (4% a soul, up to 10):** each soul that reaches the hero stays with it, circling its head, for up to 20s: the next other scroll it casts is 4% stronger for each, and the souls dive into that cast.
- **M5 Final Toll (300%; 6 x 60%):** after the backhand five long finger bones rise out of the ground in a ring round his mark (or the strongest foe left in reach), a ring of knuckles and cracks of violet light round them, and curl in over it like a cage: it is held 1.3s (Resolve applies). Then the hand tears its soul out: 300% to it, the wraith rips upward and bursts into 6 shards that arc onto the foes within 3.5, 60% each; the bones open and sink.
- **Balance:** `SMG.harvest` [1.12, 1.255, 1.33, 1.48, 1.46] brings it onto the Legendary ladder (3 runs: about 1.07 / 1.23 / 1.22 to 1.42 / 1.40 to 1.58 / 1.75; Creeping Wither makes the churn vary). Borrowed Time and Culling Edge add nothing the ladder can see (it casts only this scroll, at foes it cannot cull).
- Tests: `tests/ghm-test.js` (24, in run-all as 'ghm'): the cards and numbers; Mastery 0 the old harvest; M1 a foe under 15% culled, one above lives, a boss takes 25% more; M2 one soul per foe the first sweep reaps, 40% each on the backhand; M3 withering foes that die leave new clouds that wither too, at most 6; M4 the souls stay with the hero and the next other scroll is 4% stronger each, once; M5 the hand holds the mark, 300%, 6 shards of 60%, the strongest left if the mark died; nothing left behind; rendering.
## Stormcaller's masteries (v434), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Stormcaller: the sky turns against the enemy. A vast storm rolls in over the whole battlefield (clouds drifting in from every edge of the map, their shadows sliding over it), darkens it, and for 15 seconds hurls giant bolts at random stretches of the road from the clouds overhead: 250% near the middle and 175% out to 1.05, a 25% slow, every 0.36-0.66s (sometimes two at once). Identity: **the sky itself turning against the enemy**. Design space: where the bolts fall (blind chance before), the clouds (only looks before), the thunder of each strike, the storm's long life (one even rain of bolts) and its end (it simply drifted away). Roster: no serpent (Thunder Wyrm), no single cloud over a pack (Lightning Cloud), no chain hopping foe to foe at will (Ball Lightning), no first storm of the world (the Primordial Tempest). The old masteries (Charged Air, Forking Storm, Ball Storm, Paralysis, Eye of the Storm) are gone.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Seeking Bolts | what if the storm saw them? | where the bolts fall | behaviour change, targeting |
| 2 | Downpour | what if its clouds rained? | the clouds (only looks before) | cosmetic made real, interaction |
| 3 | Rolling Thunder | what if the thunder followed the road? | each strike's thunder | new effect |
| 4 | Storm Front | what if the storm marched? | its long life | a new phase |
| 5 | Last Thunder | what if it ended in one blow? | its end | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `castStormcaller` calls `stcStart(o, {P, clouds, ARR, DUR})` and gets `S` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old storm). `S.frame` every frame, `S.aim(x, z)` may move the first bolt of a strike, `S.front(tt)` pauses the random strikes, `S.struck(x, z, R)` after each strike's damage.
- **M1 Seeking Bolts:** the bolts still fall at random, but one that would land within 1.2 tiles of a foe bends onto it: 0.3s before, a crackling sigil (two turning hexagons, sparks climbing from it) marks the foe, and the bolt lands on it. No damage of its own: near misses become hits. The first version aimed every other bolt at a foe outright: six times stronger on the ladder, whose foes stand in one small knot, which no hidden factor could sensibly bring back.
- **M2 Downpour (45%):** the clouds that drift over the road (checked every second, up to four) rain on it: streaks falling from the cloud to the ground and splashes; foes under one are Soaked for 2s (drips falling from them). A bolt that hits a Soaked foe runs through the water to every other Soaked foe within 2 tiles: a low arc along the ground and 45% each.
- **M3 Rolling Thunder (40%):** the thunder of each strike rolls 1.6 tiles along the road both ways in 0.4s, tearing it up as it goes (dust climbing, clods of earth thrown up, crackles): 40% to each ground foe it rolls over and a 0.25 knock back (not bosses).
- **M4 Storm Front (110%):** from 8s to 11s of the storm the random strikes stop and it marches down every road from its start to the keep in those 3s, a bolt every 0.12s on the road under the front (with its impact and forks): 110% within 0.9 each. (At a fixed 4 tiles a second it never reached the foes on a long road.)
- **M5 Last Thunder (30% / 180% / 70%):** when the storm ends, its clouds draw together into one thunderhead over the thickest pack (sixteen dark puffs turning and closing in, flickering from inside, faded near the camera); eight bolts fall in a ring that closes in on it (30% within 0.7 each), then a colossal bolt (three great bolts and a 3D storm burst): 180% within 1.4 and stunned 0.6s (Resolve applies), 70% out to 2.6. The first thunderhead was bright blue, opaque and low: it is high, dark and see-through now.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** each strike already had its bolt, crack net, dust, smoke, debris and (since v420) a shockwave that rises as a wall; it now also throws forks of lightning up and out and tears stones from the road (`wymForks`, `wymRocks`, from Thunder Wyrm). Its flat light disc stays as the accent under it.
- **Balance:** each mastery's own numbers follow from what it does, and the hidden factor `SMG.stormcaller` [0.83, 0.92, 0.78, 0.87, 0.75] brings it onto the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`; its bolts fall at random, so one run swings by 10-15%: aiming for 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to about 1.25 / 1.3 / 1.7 / 1.75 / 2.3: Seeking Bolts, Rolling Thunder and Last Thunder all land well on the ladder's one knot of foes.
- Tests: `tests/stc-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'stc'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old storm; M1 bolts bending onto foes and landing on them; M2 rain over the road, 45% arcs between Soaked foes; M3 thunder rolling along the road, 40% to ground foes, not flyers; M4 the front from 8s, 110% hits, no random strikes while it marches; M5 the thunderhead after the storm, 180% within 1.4, 70% out to 2.6, 30% from the ring, nothing for a foe far off, Masteries 1-4 riding it; echoes and other casters as the old storm; the real cast path; nothing left behind.

## Skyfall's masteries (v435), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Skyfall: the sky darkens and begins to burn (embers drifting down over the whole field), a rune of fire marks the densest foes, and nine meteors tear through the sky and hammer them one after another, faster and faster (0.8s to 3.35s; 90% within 1 and a 3s burn each, a trail of fire where each lands), then a giant meteor falls last (at 3.9s; 420% within 2 and a stronger burn). Identity: **the burning sky falling on the enemy, quickening to its end**. Design space: the burning sky (its embers only looked before), the quickening rhythm (only timing before), each meteor's flight and impact, the impacts' force, and the giant meteor. Roster: no single telegraphed comet with a crater (Frostfall Comet), no obsidian meteors breaking off a rune (Cataclysm's Falling Sky), no bursting of burning foes (Rite of the Phoenix's Pyre), no battlefield storm (Stormcaller). The old masteries (Burning Sky, Scorched Earth, Shrapnel, Stunning Impact, Second Giant) are gone.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Ember Rain | what if the burning sky really burned? | the embers falling everywhere (only looks before) | cosmetic made real |
| 2 | Crescendo | what if the quickening meant something? | the rhythm of the barrage | build-up |
| 3 | Ricochet | what if a meteor did not stop where it hit? | each meteor's flight | behaviour change |
| 4 | Shockfall | what if the impacts knocked them flat? | the force of each impact | control |
| 5 | Extinction | what if the last one ended the world? | the giant meteor | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** `castSkyfall` calls `skmStart(o, {P, cx, cz, K})` and gets `SK` (null at Mastery 0, for echoes and other casters: the old barrage). `SK.next()` sizes each meteor as it is launched, `SK.landed(fromX, fromZ, x, z, giant)` runs after each impact's damage and `SK.frame` every frame.
- **M1 Ember Rain (15%):** the embers the burning sky sheds are real: every 0.4s while it burns, each foe within 6 tiles of the barrage has a one-in-three chance that a glowing ember drops onto it: 15%, a puff of sparks and Burning 1.5s, so the fire scrolls and combos find it alight.
- **M2 Crescendo (15% a meteor):** each meteor hits 15% harder than the one before (90%, 103.5%, 117%... the ninth 198%, its burn too) and is 6% bigger (its rock, fireball and trail), so the barrage visibly swells toward the giant.
- **M3 Ricochet (50%):** a meteor does not stop where it lands: its rock bounces on along its line of flight, 1.3 tiles further in an arc trailing fire and smoke, and crashes again (a small 3D fire burst and cracks): 50% within 0.8 (ground foes).
- **M4 Shockfall:** each impact knocks the ground foes within 1 flat: stunned 0.35s (`smStun`: Resolve applies, bosses 40%), dust and stones thrown up round them; the giant knocks down everything within 2 for 0.8s.
- **M5 Extinction (150% / 100%):** 0.25s after the giant's crash its molten heart bursts up in a column of fire that billows into a mushroom cloud (`cfmBoom` with its cap): 150% more within 2; and a ring of sixteen streams of fireballs races out along the ground from 2 to 3.5 tiles in 0.7s: 100% to each foe it reaches. The first mushroom was big and slow and ended as a dark red blob over the foes: it is smaller and gone in about a second.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** each impact had soft flashes, shockwaves (rising walls since v420), glowing seams, scorch and dark smoke hanging for 2-3s; it now also bursts as billowing fireballs with jets of flame (`ftmBoom`, from Fire Tornado, via `skmBoom`: three for a meteor, five for the giant), and its smoke is a lighter brown, thinner and gone in about 1.2-1.6s.
- **Balance:** each mastery's own numbers follow from what it does, and the hidden factor `SMG.skyfall` [1.04, 0.88, 0.93, 1.05, 1.1] brings it onto the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs: aiming for 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74). Without it the masteries alone come to 1.06 / 1.40 / 1.45 / 1.45 / 1.58: Crescendo swells the whole barrage, and Shockfall is control, which the ladder does not count.
- Tests: `tests/skm-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'skm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old barrage, every impact a 3D burst; M1 embers of 15% on foes near the barrage; M2 meteors climbing in steps of 13.5 from 90 up to 198 at most; M3 nine bounces, 50% where they crash; M4 foes knocked flat, longer by the giant; M5 the heart bursting 0.25s after the crash, 150% within 2 and 100% from 2 to 3.5, none further, Masteries 1-4 riding it; echoes and other casters as the old barrage; the real cast path; nothing left behind.
## Spatial Compression's masteries (v432), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Spatial Compression: space is squeezed. For 8 seconds a large irregular stretch of the field is drawn tighter and tighter (a world-space warp; foes and trees keep their size), foes slow to a crawl and take pressure ticks, space holds one unnaturally still moment (0.3s), then snaps back in a violent release that strikes everything inside for 500%. Each mastery grows a different part of it and asks its own question. None takes another scroll's identity: the Spatial Loop sends foes back, Chronostasis slows time; these stay with the squeeze, its bands and satellites, the snap and where the pressure goes. Replaces the v372 set (Crushing Weight, Dense Space, Pressure Shards, Implosion, Starburst). Legendary masteries stay in developer mode (`SM_OPEN` untouched).

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Gathering Pull | what if the squeeze drew the foes together? | how foes sit in the squeeze | behaviour change |
| 2 | Contraction Bands | what if the ripples carried weight? | the travelling bands (only a picture before) | cosmetic made real |
| 3 | Satellite Snaps | what if the smaller knots let go first? | the two satellite squeezes | new phase |
