| 4 | Implosion | what if the foes it packed together hit each other? | the crowd at the snap | interaction |
| 5 | Pressed Star | where does all that pressure go? | the release | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** a `castCompress` wrapper starts `cpmStart` beside every cast (search `Spatial Compression's masteries`, just before the boot line). It reads the squeeze from `SDP.cmp` (centre, axes, irregular edge, the bands `wv` and the satellites `sat`), mirrors the shader's local frame (`cpmLoc`), and runs on the same clock (release at 8.38s); a new squeeze ends the old one. `cpmC(o)` gives the mastery context. Even at Mastery 0 the release now bursts in 3D (a pale fireball in the ice palette and shards of space, `cpmBurst`, `lpmTear`) on top of the warp.
- **M1 Gathering Pull:** foes inside drift toward the point of the road nearest the centre, up to 0.3 tiles a second (deeper in, stronger; bosses half; backward through `pushE`, so Resolve applies), never past it; strands of pressed light run from them to the centre.
- **M2 Contraction Bands (100%):** each band crushes the foes it rolls over (within 0.6 of its width) for 100%, at most once a second per foe, with a burst of space shards.
- **M3 Satellite Snaps (200%):** 0.6s before the main release, each satellite snaps at its own centre with a 3D burst and a space quake: 200% to foes within its reach (at least 1.1 tiles).
- **M4 Implosion (50%):** at the snap each foe inside takes 50% for every other foe within 0.5 tiles (up to 3), with a crack of shards between them; Gathering Pull and the squeeze itself pack them.
- **M5 Pressed Star (200%):** in the held moment a star of pressed light (a white core in halos, spikes of space) gathers at the centre, spinning faster; 0.45s after the snap it bursts in a large pale 3D blast and shards: 200% within 1.6 tiles.
- **Balance:** the base snap (500%) and its pressure ticks are most of its power, so the masteries' numbers are large and the hidden factor `SMG.compress` [1.13, 1.21, 1.09, 1.18, 1.08] does the rest (Gathering Pull deals no damage itself). Legendary ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 6 runs): 1.10 / 1.24 / 1.32 / 1.56 / 1.70 against 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742; the region's random shape and satellites swing it about 0.05 run to run. Not in `SM.free`.
- Tests: `tests/cpm-test.js` (24, in run-all as 'cpm'): the card and numbers; still developer-mode; Mastery 0 the 500% release with its 3D burst, no masteries; M1 the drift toward the middle (never past it, at most 0.3 a second, no damage); M2 100% per band, once a second per foe; M3 both satellites 0.6s early at their centres, 200% within reach; M4 50% per neighbour within 0.5 (up to 3) at the snap; M5 the star 0.45s after the snap, 200% within 1.6, nothing left behind; non-mastered casts get none; a second squeeze ends the first cleanly; every mastery with deaths without errors.

## Chronostasis's masteries (v433), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Chronostasis: time breaks. Over 2.6 tiles of road everything crawls for 10 seconds (foes at a fifth of their speed, their arrows hanging in the air), afterimages flicker where time cannot decide where they are, the stopped time suffocates them (2.5% of their health a second, half on bosses), the last quarter second stands still, and then time catches up at once in a Temporal Collapse (500%) as the field shatters. A great clock turns behind the hero and a clock face hangs over the field. Each mastery grows a different part of it and asks its own question; none takes another scroll's identity (Spatial Compression squeezes, the Spatial Loop folds; these stay with time: rewinding, the clock, the afterimages, the stolen time and the moment replaying). Replaces the v372 set (Frozen Moments, Heavier Time, Time Shards, Paradox, Rewind). Developer mode only, like every Legendary.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rewind | what if time ran backward first? | the moment time breaks | behaviour change |
| 2 | Sweeping Hand | what if the clock's hand cut? | the clock (only a picture before) | cosmetic made real |
| 3 | Echoes | what if the afterimages were real? | the afterimages | new rule |
| 4 | Stolen Time | what if the time taken from the foes went somewhere? | the towers | interaction |
| 5 | Second Collapse | what if the moment happened again? | the collapse | signature payoff |

- **Where it hooks in:** a `castChronostasis` wrapper starts `ctmStart` beside every cast (search `Chronostasis's masteries`, just before the boot line), on the same clock (the field forms at 0.6s, holds from 1.15s, collapses at 11.15s); `ctmC(o)` gives the mastery context. A `towerUpdate` wrapper gives the stolen time to the towers. The base collapse's flat triangles are gone: every cast now throws real 3D shards of glass made of time (`ctmShards`) and a pale 3D blast (ice palette), and the screen flash is .3 instead of .45.
- **M1 Rewind (180%):** as the field forms, every foe inside is wound back 0.8 tiles along the road (`pushE`, Resolve applies) and takes 180%, with a golden trail and a small burst where it stood.
- **M2 Sweeping Hand (35%):** a great golden hand of light (a tapering blade, a bright edge and a glow) sweeps the field once every 2s while time crawls (it stops in the still moment): 35% to each foe it passes over, once a turn.
- **M3 Echoes (40%):** every second a foe spends inside leaves an echo (up to 6; the first three show as faint golden ghosts trailing behind it). At the collapse the echoes rush back into it: 40% each, 0.25s later.
- **M4 Stolen Time (30%):** while the field holds, towers whose range reaches into it (within their range plus half the field's radius) attack 30% faster, golden clock rings turning round them.
- **M5 Second Collapse (140%):** 1.2s after the collapse a golden ghost of the field (the base field's shimmering glass shader) flickers back and collapses again: 140% to every foe within it, frozen 0.5s (Resolve applies), more shards and a pale 3D blast.
- **Balance:** its suffocation (a share of each foe's health, not of the scroll's power) is most of its strength, so the hidden factors move it little and the masteries carry the climb: `SMG.chronostasis` [1.05, 1.05, 1.05, 1.07, 1.1]. Legendary ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 6 runs): 1.09 / 1.22 / 1.34 / 1.50 / 1.75 against 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742 (Stolen Time pays only through towers, which the ladder does not have). Not in `SM.free`.
- Tests: `tests/ctm-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'ctm'): the card and numbers; still developer-mode; Mastery 0 the 500% collapse with real shards, a 3D blast and a flash of at most .3, no masteries; M1 0.8 tiles back and 180% inside only; M2 4 to 6 passes in 10s, 35% once a turn, none in the still moment; M3 six echoes for a foe inside all along, 40% each as time catches up; M4 a tower in reach found and its cooldown running 1.3 times as fast, and only while the field holds; M5 the second collapse 1.2s on, 140% inside, nothing left behind; non-mastered casts get none; every mastery with deaths without errors.
## Glacial Rift's masteries (v436), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Glacial Rift: a seam cracks down a long stretch of road ahead of the first foe, a wave of towering crystals erupts out of it and races back through the enemy (550%), freezing the foes where they stand; a blizzard sweeps the field while the ridge stands, then at 3.7s the whole ridge shatters from the front to the back (400%). Identity: **the road itself turns to glacier**. Design space: the seam, the wave, the frozen foes, the standing ridge, the blizzard and the shatter. Roster: no falling strike and crater (Frostfall Comet), no single prison (Ice Tomb), no frozen patch (Rime Bloom), no hunt (Frost Wolves). Simon asked for it with the other Legendary scrolls, split between the sessions: "can you seperate all the legendary scrolls between the 3 parallel workers and you to finish them all except the dimensional slash please". The masteries stay in developer mode.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Frozen Sentinels | what if the frozen foes became part of the glacier? | the frozen foes | behaviour change, control |
| 2 | Undertow | what if the wave carried them? | the wave | behaviour change, control |
| 3 | Calving | what if the ridge didn't just stand there? | the standing ridge (only looks before) | new phase |
| 4 | Deep Cold | what if the blizzard really froze things? | the blizzard (map-wide) | interaction with the field |
| 5 | Glacial Maw | what if the rift swallowed them? | the shatter | signature payoff |

- **M1 Frozen Sentinels:** the foes the wave freezes (not bosses) stay frozen until the ridge shatters, then stand on the bare road as ice statues for 1.5s more, crystals bursting out of them. While one stands, ground foes behind it cannot walk past it (they stop 0.38 behind it; bosses push through). The statues form at the shatter, where they can be seen.
- **M2 Undertow (45%):** the wave surges under each foe it reaches and drags it a tile back up the road over 0.3s (not bosses or flyers), lifted on a crest of ice, grinding it for 45% before it freezes there.
- **M3 Calving (130%):** while the ridge stands, a great glowing slab breaks off it every 0.4s, rising and then toppling at a foe on the road (most of the time; now and then anywhere along the ridge), as tall as the fall needs, aimed where the foe will be as it lands: 130% to the ground foes under it (a foe at most every 0.7s).
- **M4 Deep Cold (120%):** rime gathers on every foe out in the blizzard, anywhere on the map, a white crust growing over it; after 2s it freezes solid for 0.6s (bosses less, Resolve applies), and as it thaws the ice cracks off it for 120%. Once a foe each cast, flyers too.
- **M5 Glacial Maw (350%):** 0.3s after the shatter the rift tears open along the whole stretch, a dark crevasse with great ice fangs rising along its lips; the ground foes along it are dragged over it, sinking, and held 1s (bosses are not held), then the fangs slam shut from the front to the back: 350% to each.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** where the ridge bursts a billowing frost cloud (`xfxFrostBoom`) rises with the flying crystals.
- **Balance:** the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 4 runs: a pack, a churn of fresh 600-health foes, a single foe) comes to 1.05 / 1.01 / 1.35 / 1.50 / 1.78 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74, with the hidden factor `SMG.glacialrift` [1.08, 1, 1, 1, 1]. The churn is a step for this two-phase nuke (once the wave reaches a fresh foe's 600, another walks in for the shatter), so the factors stay at 1 and the masteries' own damage carries the ladder. Undertow stays under its rung: its worth is a tile of road regained on every foe, which the ladder does not count.
- Tests: `tests/grm-test.js` (23, in run-all as 'grm'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old rift (550 then 400, no mastery); M1 a statue that holds the queue behind it, a boss pushing through, the queue walking on when it thaws; M2 the push and the 45%, not flyers or bosses; M3 slabs while the ridge stands, 130% under them; M4 a far foe and a flyer frozen after 2s, the 120% once; M5 the maw just after the shatter, the hold (not a boss), the drag, 350% each; nothing left behind; no errors.

## Hundred-Year Bloom's masteries (v437), designed with the framework
The Legendary scroll Hundred-Year Bloom: a giant bud pushes out of the earth among the foes, its vines hold them (slowed, rooted) while its petals open, then at 1.8s it bursts (700% within 2.2 tiles, Overgrown) in a storm of petals, and its golden pollen hangs in the air for 5s, choking what is in it (50% a second; lightning sets it off as a Pollen Blast). Identity: **a flower a hundred years in the making, opening in one long breath among the foes**. Design space: the opening bud and its scent, the vines that hold, the petals of the burst, the pollen, and the flower's own life (it grows, opens and goes to seed). Roster: no carnivorous flower snapping shut (Bramble Patch), no roots holding five foes (Entangling Roots), no lashing vine (Thorn Whip), no fire spreading from foe to foe (Catch Flame), no frozen patch (Rime Bloom). The old masteries (Clinging Vines, Nectar, Thorn Ring, Long Hold, Second Bloom) are gone. The masteries stay in developer mode.

| # | Mastery | Question | Part of the spell | Kind |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| 1 | Scent of Nectar | what if the bloom called them in? | the opening bud | behaviour change, targeting |
| 2 | Vine Lattice | what if the vines bound them together? | the vines | interaction with every other hit |
| 3 | Petal Storm | what if the storm of petals was real? | the burst's petals (only looks before) | cosmetic made real |
| 4 | Golden Haze | what if the pollen chased them? | the pollen cloud | behaviour change |
| 5 | A Hundred Years in a Breath | what if the bloom went to seed? | the flower's life | signature payoff |

- **M1 Scent of Nectar:** from the moment the bud breaks the ground until it bursts, streams of golden scent weave out from the flower to the foes up to 3.5 tiles away, and they walk to it along the road (1.1 tiles a second, bosses half that): a foe past the flower turns back, one short of it hurries on, until its vines take them. The burst catches the stragglers of a loose group too.
- **M2 Vine Lattice (25%):** the vines tie every foe the bloom holds to its two nearest held neighbours with thorned vines, while it holds and for 3s after the burst. A hit on a bound foe from anything else (a building, another scroll, the hero) runs 25% of itself along the vines to every other bound foe (up to 6), a bead of green light racing along them. The bloom's own hits (the burst, the petals, the pollen) do not run.
- **M3 Petal Storm (80%):** the burst throws 16 razor petals that whirl out in a rising spiral with pink ribbons behind them, then turn and slice into the foes within 3.2 tiles: 80% each, at most 3 on a foe.
- **M4 Golden Haze (40%):** the pollen cloud is 30% wider and 40% thicker, and it drifts down the road after the foes in it (0.7 tiles a second), so they choke for all of its 5 seconds, and they are left Overgrown while they breathe it.
- **M5 A Hundred Years in a Breath (50%):** as the flower fades its heart ripens into a glowing dandelion seed head that rises and bursts; 10 seeds drift out on the wind, one onto each foe nearest it within 5 tiles (the rest settle on the road around them), and each sprouts a small bloom that grabs the foes around it for 0.6s (Resolve applies, not bosses or flyers) and bursts for 50% in a puff of golden pollen.
- **The explosion rule, base cast too:** the burst was a flat flash and rings under a hard white bar of light; it now billows up as a golden cloud of pollen in 3D (`cfmBoom` with a new 'pollen' palette: cream, gold, a warm white heart and a rose edge), the flash is a small accent, and the shaft of light fades as it rises.
- **Balance:** the ladder (`tests/sm-ladder.js`, 3 runs) comes to 1.06 / 1.16 / 1.27 / 1.54 / 1.79 against 1.09 / 1.23 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.74, with the hidden factor `SMG.bloom` [1.094, 1.234, 1.234, 1.234, 1.234]. The scent and the lattice do no damage of their own (their worth is foes brought into the burst and hits shared along the vines, which the ladder cannot count), so the factor carries them; from the petals on the masteries' own damage climbs the rungs. The pack and the single foe land on them; the churn lags because the burst kills a fresh foe either way.
- Tests: `tests/hyb-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'hyb'): the card and numbers; Mastery 0 the old bloom (700 each, pollen 12.5 a tick, no mastery) with its golden cloud; M1 a foe past the flower turned back, one farther out walking on, a boss at half the pace; M2 the binding, 25% shared, the bloom's own hits not, still bound just under 3s after the burst and free after; M3 16 petals, 3 on each of 4 foes, none past 3.2 tiles; M4 the wider, thicker cloud drifting with its foes, Overgrown; M5 the seed head after the burst, 10 seeds one on each foe, 50% each, the grab (not a boss); nothing left behind; no errors. `tests/grm-test.js` and this test move the clock only through `update` in their walking rows (it moved twice as fast there before).
## Arena 6: the Stormcrag Highlands (v418)
Simon: "prepare the last arena the highlands? includeing all new troop models etc, they should (except bosses) like normal people looking troops wear leather jackets like its cold up there and bosses just make them look all look a bit withered". One block of modules inserted before the boot line (between the markers `Arena 6: the Stormcrag Highlands (v418)` and `end of the Stormcrag Highlands (v418)`), built from five parts: the land and the haar, the foes' dressing, the troops, the bosses, the landmarks. A few one-line edits in the arena core (the same places the taiga touched).
- **The land.** The painted map site `highlands` becomes playable: `ARENAS.highlands` (no 6, the last), `SITE_ARENA`, `arenaNow`. It opens with a wave-200 win in the Deep Taiga (`arenaWin` wrapper; `arenaFix` opens it for accounts with `wonA.taiga`); the lock text, arrival plate, `arenaUnlockDev`, header class (`.arena.highlands`), Bestiary biome cycle (Plains > Frostvale > Tundra > Whitewaste > Taiga > Highlands), night toast, tree name (Wind-bent Scots Pine), Hall of Champions short name and the developer menu row ('Haar') all know it. The trip runs along the roads (`wmpVia` from v356).
- **Tougher.** `HL`: troops x1.35 health and x1.05 speed, bosses x1.6 health (spawnEnemy, and `bossHpAt` agrees); three gates; gems x2.5 and rarity +20% (`ARENA_REWARD.highlands`); lightning spells +20% (`LAND_EL.highlands={ice:1,slow:1,lightning:1.2}` plus its own `hurt` wrapper, like the taiga's fire). Peat hags: `terMarshLand` includes the Highlands (`TERP.highlands`: granite tors, black peat water, a fallen broch).
- **Board (`highlandsBoard`).** Moor grass 0x5a6640, a road of grey gravel 0xa49a86 (lum about .37 vs .60), `hlGround` shader (heather and bleached-grass patches at the same brightness, quartz and heather-bell specks; gravel, stones and ruts on the road; moor, heather, bracken and rock around), `HL_REMAP` (Scots pine blue-green with red bark, tan moor grass, granite pebbles), `hlLichen` on rocks (pale and orange lichen crusts, a little moss). Outside the grid, instanced: granite tors (four stacked-slab shapes), about 600 heather clumps, bracken, gorse with yellow flowers, wind-bent Scots pines, lichened boulders, runs of dry-stone wall (gaps where they fell), cairns, snow-capped bens on the horizon, a flock of sheep and two red Highland cattle grazing (heads down, heads up), mist in the hollows and cloud caught on the big tors. `HL_LIGHT` (cold silver day, heather-pink dusk, readable blue night), `HL_PV`. `hlAtmos`: grass seed and heather bits on the west wind by day, sleet blown sideways at night.
- **The haar (`HLH`).** Every 32-44 s with a wave on the field (`HL.gap0/gap1`): 2.6 s of warning (`sfxHaar`, a low moan; the `#hlPill` sign; the bank gathering at the board's edge), then a fog bank 5 tiles wide rolls across the board on the wind (west to east three times in four) at 1.05 tiles/s. `hlFogAt(x,z)` is its depth at a point; a tower inside it (`hlInFog`) has 70% range (`tStats` wrapper) and wears a pale ring. A lit Beacon Cairn (4 tiles) keeps it off. `hlCalm(n)` keeps it away n waves (saved with the run, `hlh`); `hlHaarAt(x)` drops it on a point (the Grey Man). Looks: one `THREE.Points` of 760 puffs with its own shader (fbm-edged soft puffs lit from above, rolling at the front, piled in the middle), a ground haze plane, tendrils off the front; the colour follows the day (white, rose, slate). Developer menu row 'Haar' (`haarNow`).
- **The people of the hills (troops).** Every troop type of the waves and every variant has its own model up here: peasants 0-7 (Crofter, Shepherd, Beacon-bearer, Old Ghillie, Peat Cutter, Herd-wife, the drover's storm lantern Gem Carrier, the seanchaidh Scroll Bearer), soldiers, knights, monks, riders (garrons: shaggy hill ponies), ravens (Crag Raven, Red-billed Chough, Storm Raven) and rams (sheepskin, heather-thatch and slate roofs, a horned ram's head, a granite boulder), each with its Gem Carrier and Scroll Bearer. Ordinary folk dressed for the cold: leather coats and jerkins, sheepskin collars and lapels, scarves, wool bonnets with toories, knitted caps, hoods, fur hats, deerstalkers, steel bonnets, kilts and trews, plaids, gloves and boots, wind-reddened cheeks and noses (`hlBody`, `hlHat`, `hlFace`, `hlTarge`). Built like every troop (`kitBuilder` parts merged per pivot, the same pivots, poses, `K.special`, `K.glows`, `K.legs4`, `K.wheels`), so walking, hits, deaths, elites, Gem Carriers and Scroll Bearers work unchanged. `peasantKit`/`unitKit` wrappers return `hlPeasantKit`/`hlUnitKit` (caches `HL_PEAS_CACHE`, `HL_UNIT_CACHE`) while `HLX.n>0`: a spawn in the Highlands (`spawnEnemy` wrapper) or the Bestiary showing the Highlands biome (`buildGallery`/`openVar` wrappers). Anything else (the profile icon's knight, other lands) keeps the usual models. Variant numbers, tiers and resistances are unchanged. `HL_VARS` holds the Bestiary names and words (a `galVarUI` wrapper; 'Highland variant.'). Troops breathe out little puffs in the cold.
- **Withered bosses.** Simon's words read as: the Highlands' own bosses are withered, and so is every boss that climbs up here. `hlWitherMat(m,mode)`: colours drained toward ash and bone, a cold blue rim, rime on the faces that look up; mode 'skin' adds object-space cracks (the edges of a Voronoi pattern, `HLW_GLSL`, with a pale raised lip, in patches), mode 'cloth' faded fibres instead. `hlDress` withers every boss but the Highlands' two (they are built withered); withered bosses shed frost and ash. The Bestiary's Highlands biome shows them withered too ('Withered in the Highlands.').
- **Bosses.** `MSB_AT.highlands={100:['greyman',15500],200:['cailleach',40000]}`. The Grey Man (`greyManModel`, from Am Fear Liath Mor of Ben Macdui): gaunt and stooped on long rag-bound legs, a torn belted plaid, bare ribs, arms to his knees with long bony fingers, a ragged grey hood and cloak, white eyes in deep sockets, lank white hair and beard, a bog-oak staff, mist rising off him. Dread every 10 s (4 towers within 3 tiles attack at half speed for 4 s: `hlDread`, `tStats` wrapper; grey wisps and a pale eye over each, `HLDR`); every 13 s he steps into the mist (1.5 s untargetable, `burrow`) and out of it 1.3 tiles on; at half health the haar answers him (`hlHaarAt`) and a cold shroud (15%). The Cailleach (`cailleachModel`): bent double over her staff of winter, layered skirts torn to rags, a tartan shawl and hood rimed and hung with icicles, a wicker creel of granite boulders, a blue withered face with a hooked nose, rust-red teeth, one eye of ice, wild white hair. Every 11 s she hurls up to 3 boulders at towers within 3.6 tiles (`hlHurl`: real stones on an arc with a ground shadow and a target ring; the tower hit is crushed under the stone for 2.5 s, `hlSeize(t,sec,'stone')` on the frozen-tower mechanism, released by the `msbThaw` wrapper); every 14 s the slachdan (`hlIceRing`: clusters of ice crystals burst out 2.8 tiles over a frost decal, then shatter; towers in it freeze 1.6 s, foes in it get 8% frost shields); at half health the well of youth (`hlWell`: she kneels 4 s, heals 4% a second, takes x1.5; 10% of her health in damage breaks it and she staggers 1.5 s). Her death calls `hlCalm(3)`. Cloth uses canvas tartans (`hlTartanTex`, moth holes through `alphaTest`), torn hems (`hlRagGeo`), hair `hlStrands`. Abilities run in `MSB_ACT`; standing still uses `hlHold` (dt kept, `dist` put back).
- **Places.** Lore props: a Pictish symbol stone, a peat stack, bagpipes on a rock, a claymore in a stone, a stag's skull in the heather, a ruined shieling. Landmarks (`MK_SETS.highlands`; the keys are new because Frostvale, the tundra and the Whitewaste already own 'stones', 'seer' and 'beacon'): Old Broch (`broch`: slingers, riders x2), Beacon Cairn (`firecairn`: the haar cannot blind towers within 4 tiles), Standing Stones (`standing`: towers within 3 tiles +10% attack speed), Bothy Still (`still`: sell the casks, or +40+3w gold after each cleared wave), Seer's Bothy (`brahan`: +3 lives, or foes within 3 tiles take +15%), Clootie Well (`clootie`: towers within 3 tiles shake off frost, stones and dread twice as fast, or take the offerings: gold, 5 gems, foes +10% speed for 3 waves). Chest: `MK_CHEST.highlands` with a tartan strap and a sprig of heather.
Nature resistances: the Grey Man .9, the Cailleach .7.
Tests: `tests/highlands-test.js` (88, in run-all: unlock, board, every one of the 38 troop models on the same rig as the usual one, walking, gems, scrolls, elites, withering, the haar and the beacon, the landmarks, both bosses, the Bestiary); `tests/arena-test.js` cycles six biomes; `tests/boss-test.js` expects ten Bestiary boss pages.

## Boards at ground level (v427)
Simon: "make it so that EVERY map will sit on the bottom on hover over the background but the background becomes part of the map, that means the background needs to match texturewise the texture of the tiles so its not that distinct yet not be tiles". One module inserted before the boot line, after the Highlands (markers `Boards at ground level (v427)` and `end of boards at ground level (v427)`), and two tags in `buildMap`.
- **Before:** the board stood on an earthen slab (`box(W+.2,1.3,...)` at y -.95 with a grass lip) and the land around it was a 420x420 plane 1.6 below the tiles; every land put its scenery on that low ground (y -1.6).
- **The ground:** `glLevel` (an outermost `buildMap` wrapper, so it runs after every land has dressed its board) hides the low plane and lays a new one at y -.002, just under the tile tops, as a `ShapeGeometry` with a hole traced round the board's cells and the lead-in road cells (`glHoleLoop`). The tiles keep their seams and the road its lower bed; the slab and lip (tagged `userData.glBase`) stay under the new ground, out of sight.
- **The look (`glGroundMat`):** the new ground takes the mean colour of the board's non-road tiles, soft patches about a tile across whose strength follows the tiles' own spread (no edges, no grid), then runs the tiles' own land shader (`TILES.material.onBeforeCompile`: heather, moss, snow glint, gold grass, frost...), so the board and the land around it read as one. In the tundra the sea along the left edge is painted on top (the shore formula `TUN_SHORE_GLSL`, the same waves and foam as before).
- **The scenery:** everything that stood on the low ground is lifted by `GLY` (1.598): `TERRAIN` children below y -.6 (and the mist planes), and per instance every instanced-mesh entry below -.6 (spruce, tors, heather, cliffs, beasts...). Runtime uses of the old height now add `GLY`: the wolf pack's eyes and their leaf fall, the tundra's, the Whitewaste's and the taiga's outer weather, the Eldritch Tree's fallback spot beside the board.
Tests: `tests/ground-test.js` (11, in run-all: in all six lands the board sits on the ground, the tiles show through the hole and the ground everywhere round it, the lead roads stay open, nothing is left on the low ground, the ground's colour matches the tiles; the tundra's sea; the pack's eyes; rendering; ten more boards).

## The world map, redrawn (v429)
Simon: "can you now rework the map where you select arenas? it looks wierd right now, make it more natural and a better shape". One module inserted before the boot line, after the ground-level boards (markers `The world map, redrawn (v429)` and `end of the world map, redrawn (v429)`). It replaces the painter and the land field behind the same API (`WMP.sites`, `WMP.paths`, `wmpPaint`, `wmpField`, `wmpBioRaw`/`wmpBioSoft`, `wmpTop`, `wmpVia`), so the world map screen, the keep buttons and the trips are unchanged.
- **The keeps** are moved into a natural layout: Plains in the south, Frostvale west, Greywind Tundra east, the Deep Taiga north-west, the Whitewaste in the middle and the Stormcrag Highlands north-east.
- **The coast (`wm2Land`, cached on a 400x560 grid as `wm2F`):** a domain-warped ellipse plus three octaves of noise, a list of bays and capes (`WM2_FEAT`, Gaussian dents and bumps, including two isles off the Highlands and the tundra), a rise round each keep so no keep sits on the shore, and a fade towards the frame so the island never touches it. The coast is inked twice, with three water lines offshore.
- **Territories:** a warped, weighted Voronoi over seeds per land (`WM2_SEED`) with soft weights across a narrow wandering band (`exp(-(d-dmin)/.028)`), so borders mingle without blotches.
- **Relief:** a height and a mountainness grid (`wm2Height`, 125x175): ridged noise scaled per land (`WM2_MK`: the Highlands 1, the Plains .06) plus spines along the borders. The land is hill-shaded (clamped), peaks are drawn only where mountainness is over .5, foothills from .3 to .5, and vegetation per land elsewhere.
- **Rivers** run downhill on a distance-to-sea grid from six sources to the coast, then are smoothed and given meanders. **Lakes** sit only on land. **Roads** are found with A* (`wm2Heap`) on an 8 px grid that avoids the sea, mountains, the coast and rivers, then are simplified, smoothed and meandered on land; a bridge is drawn wherever a road crosses a river.
- Painting takes about 0.7 s, once per session (cached on `WMP.cv`).
Tests: `tests/worldmap-test.js` (18, in run-all: it paints and quickly; six keeps well inside the land; the frame is all sea; land is 30-70% of the page; one mainland holds every keep; the coast winds; isles offshore; each keep in its own territory with room; the Highlands are mountains and the Plains are open; rivers run to the sea; a road from the Plains to every keep, on land and winding; the screen opens; the changelog). The flaky Scroll Bearer check in `tests/highlands-test.js` now re-spawns when a random Gem Carrier roll takes the scroll's place.

## v428: recorded scroll mastery previews
Simon: "the previes needs to be recorded not happened in realtime when clicked - this reduces the long loading time and we can make a much nicer UI for it aswell", "if a mastery says something like "on kill effect" and it actually may not be strong enaugh to kill an enemy in the preview, we need to spawn one or two enemies and one will be the targeted one that will spawn with 1hp so the ability kills him" and "if a mastery says something like "targets the toughest enemy" you need to spawn a ram or just a tougher enemy so its distinct which one is being targeted".

The live previews (v367, v374) started a second copy of the whole game in a hidden frame on the first hover, which took a few seconds. Every published scroll mastery is now a short recorded clip: 32 scrolls (Common, Uncommon, Rare), each with no mastery and Masteries 1 to 5, so 192 clips.

**How a clip is made** (`node tools/previews/record.js`, needs the dev server and ffmpeg):
- The real game runs in headless Chromium, stepped frame by frame (its own loop is stopped), with a seeded `Math.random`, so a clip is the same every time.
- The stage is a straight stretch of road running across the view (map seed 98), with the hero (Aldric) in front of it and a pack of foes walking down it. The scroll is cast when the pack reaches the hero's side.
- Each clip is first played without pictures. That run picks the seed (a mastery's moment must happen in it) and the framing: the hero, the pack at the cast and every place a foe was hit, with the road kept across the middle. A few scrolls lean the view (Lightning Cloud up to its clouds, Dark Pulse toward the hero it rolls out from, Fire Tornado up and back).
- The camera then holds still; the clip runs from just before the cast until the scroll's effects are done (at least 3 s, at most 9.5 s).
- Each clip is encoded as H.264 (`previews/<scroll>-<level>.mp4`, 480x320, 30 fps). `previews/manifest.json` lists them with their captions; `--sheet` writes contact sheets for checking them by eye.

**The stage for each mastery** (`tools/previews/scenarios.js`):

| What the mastery says | The stage |
|---|---|
| On a kill or a death (Hexspark 4, Wither 3, Ember Bolt 5, Static Spark 2 and 4, Soul Lantern 4, Catch Flame 2) | the target spawns with 1 HP, or with just enough to survive the first hit when the mastery needs it marked first (Hexspark's Hex, Wither's withering), labelled **1 HP** / **Low HP** |
| The toughest or strongest foe (Thorn Whip 5, Ice Tomb, Lightning Cloud, Frost Wolves 5) | a ram in the pack, labelled **Toughest** / **Strongest** |
| Every Nth cast (Hexspark 5, Light Bolt 5, Frost Shard 5) | the cast counter starts one short, so the recorded cast is the special one |
| A follow-up hit (Light Bolt 2, Rime Bloom 3) | a second cast, or a Frost Shard after the bloom |
| Flyers (Cinder Scatter 2, Dark Pulse 4, Riftfang 4, Lightning Cloud 2, Piercing Verdict 5) | a raven, labelled **Flying** |
| A count (Catch Flame 5: ten foes; Wall of Fire 4: six crossings) | a bigger pack, foes still arriving |
| A chance (Static Spark 3 and 4, Frost Shard 4) | the seed is chosen so it happens on camera |

Every special stage has a check: a spy counts calls of the function that draws the mastery's moment (`hxNova`, `ebMolten`, `sskBurst`, `cfmErupt` and so on). If the moment never happened, the next seed is tried, and a clip that never manages it is reported.

**Where the clips live:** Simon chose to load them from GitHub, so the game file stays small. The clip list is read a few seconds after the game starts: from `previews/` beside the page when it is served from the repository (the dev page, a githack link), else from jsDelivr's copy of the main branch, else from raw GitHub. Opening a scroll's mastery card fetches its clips, so they play at once. Offline the masteries keep the live preview, and so do scrolls without clips (Epic and up, in developer mode) and buildings (a building and a scroll can share a key: `lantern`).

**The panel** (`PVC`, `pvcShow`): the scroll and the mastery, the clip (looping, with a progress line), tabs for no mastery and Masteries I to V (the ones you may watch are lit), the mastery's text and a line saying what the clip sets up. Hovering a mastery shows it; a click or a tap pins it open; the close button or a second click closes it. `smPvCan` still decides which masteries can be watched outside developer mode.

**Re-recording:** after a change to a published mastery or to the board, run `node tools/previews/record.js <scroll>` (or with no arguments for all, about 30 to 60 minutes) and commit `previews/`. The clips reach the published game once they are on main.

Tests: `tests/pvc-test.js` (in run-all as 'pvc'):
- every published scroll has its 6 clips and every file exists;
- a hover plays the clip at once, without the live preview's frame;
- the panel shows the mastery, its text and its note;
- the tabs switch clips and pin the panel; pinning and closing work;
- outside developer mode only reached masteries play;
- a scroll without clips keeps the live preview, and so does a building;
- the dev page reads `previews/`, and opening a card pre-fetches its clips;
- offline it falls back to the live preview.

`tests/smprev-test.js` now checks the live preview on an Epic scroll (published scrolls play clips), and `tests/pvown-test.js` accepts either panel for a reached mastery.

## Recorded previews for the Epic and Legendary masteries, with a tracking camera (v439)
Simon: "re-record the previews for the new legendary masteries and please keep in mind what they show, even for epics, if the pheonix has a explode animation and it runs of screen the previews should track it to always display what is said in the description". The v428 previews covered the published scrolls (Common, Uncommon, Rare); the Epic and Legendary scrolls still started the slow live preview. Now all 17 of them (8 Epic, 9 Legendary: Spatial Loop, Piercing Judgment, Cataclysm, Rite of the Phoenix, Frostfall Comet, Thunder Wyrm, Prism of Dawn, Oakheart Treant, Spatial Compression, Voidfall, Stormcaller, Chronostasis, Skyfall, Glacial Rift, Grim Harvest, Dimensional Slash, Hundred-Year Bloom) have a recorded clip for no mastery and each of Masteries 1-5: 102 clips in `previews/`, listed in `previews/manifest.json`. They play in developer mode, like their masteries. Like the published clips since Simon asked for them in higher quality ("at least 720p"), they are 1080x720, x264 at CRF 26; the busiest come to about 2 MB.

**The tracking camera** (`tools/previews/record.js`, `camPath`). The v428 recorder framed each clip once, still, around the hero and every foe hit; a cast that travels (the Phoenix reborn skimming toward the keep, the Comet's rebound, the Storm Front marching down the road, the Treant walking) ran out of the picture. Now the run without pictures logs, every frame, where the action is, and the camera follows it:

| What the view keeps in it | When |
|---|---|
| the hero and the pack | from the start until just after the cast |
| the foes hit (where they show: a lifted or flying foe higher up the screen) | from 0.3s before each hit to 0.8s after |
| every effect the cast made that is new (under 0.8s old), moving or growing (where it shows: a flying one higher up by its height) | from 0.1s before to 0.6s after |
| towers on the stage | always |
| an effect that only lingers (a burning river, a cloud), a foe that is only burning | only when nothing else is going on |

What it leaves out: effects high in the sky (above 2.4 tiles: a phoenix climbing away, a meteor far up; a stage can raise it, Voidfall's sphere at 4), a stray more than 5 tiles from the rest of the action or from where the action was a frame before (an ember thrown across the map). When it all does not fit in the widest view, it keeps what comes next. The zoom opens a little ahead of time and closes slowly, and the middle glides (two passes of a 0.6s average), so it reads like a camera operator, not a jump cut. A stage can follow only the foes it hits (`follow: 'hits'`, the Stormcaller whose bolts fall all over the map) or the foes themselves (`follow: 'foes'`, Spatial Loop sending them 8 tiles back).

**What each clip shows** (`tools/previews/scenarios.js`), built from the mastery's own words:

| Scroll | Stages set up for its masteries |
|---|---|
| Spatial Loop | brisk foes so they reach the fold, the view following where most of them are (it pans up the road to where the fold sends them); fast ones for Groundhog Lap (more than one lap); archer towers for Displaced; the whole 10s for Snapback |
| Piercing Judgment | a Light Bolt after the line (Condemned); the ram as the strongest foe (Afterimage, Final Judgment); three foes under a quarter of their health (Harsh Sentence) |
| Cataclysm | the ram as the toughest foe in the rune (Drawn Down, World Breaker); an archer tower and a cannon (Sundered) |
| Rite of the Phoenix | eleven foes (Rising Heat); the ram as the toughest it struck (Burning Plumage); its rebirth and dive followed down the road |
| Frostfall Comet | a brisk pack (Comet's Eye); foes marching in under the tail; a Frost Spire (Faultlines); the ram near the crater (Rebound) |
| Thunder Wyrm | two knots with empty road between (Hunter's Weave); the ram near its head (Constrict) |
| Prism of Dawn | a pack walking on (Sun-Tracking) |
| Oakheart Treant | every clip holds its 10s walk, from a step back; foes walking into it (Bark Bulwark); a second knot further back (Uprooted Boulder); Entangling Roots first (Sap Harvest); the fall (Timber!) |
| Spatial Compression | a spread-out pack (Gathering Pull); every clip runs on past the snap until the star has burst (11.3s) |
| Voidfall | the front of a brisk pack past the impact (Gravity Well); archer towers (Hollow); the sphere over the crater (Event Horizon) |
| Stormcaller | eleven foes, the view on the foes it hits; a cloud steered over the pack so it rains on them (Downpour); the Storm Front and the Last Thunder are clips centred on them (they come 10s and 17s into the storm) |
| Chronostasis | archer towers in reach (Stolen Time); the Echoes and the Second Collapse are clips centred on the collapse (the field holds 10s) |
| Glacial Rift | three foes walking up just after the shatter, stopped behind the statues until they thaw (Frozen Sentinels); a raven and foes far behind the rift (Deep Cold); a wider view for its 8.5-tile ridge |
| Grim Harvest | a step back from the giant reaper; low-health foes (Culling Edge, Creeping Wither); 1 HP foes for the souls (Soul Swarm, Borrowed Time, with a Dark Pulse once they reach the hero); the ram as the mark (Final Toll) |
| Dimensional Slash | three foes under 30% of their health (Severed) |
| Hundred-Year Bloom | foes past the flower and behind it (Scent of Nectar); an archer tower (Vine Lattice); a pack walking on (Golden Haze) |

The Epic and Legendary stages use tougher foes (30 times the spell power, against 12) so the pack lives through the long casts and the later masteries still find it. Each mastery module's own log (FCM.dbg, PHM.dbg, ...) is read after every clip: the recorder prints which moments happened, `manifest.json` keeps them (`ev`), and a stage that must show one moment (the Phoenix reborn, the Comet's rebound, a culled foe) tries the next seed until it does.

**The recorded run is the planned run.** The camera is planned on a run without pictures, so the recorded run must be the very same run. Three things broke that, and each is fixed: three.js draws on Math.random for the id of every new object, and how many objects a frame makes depends on the camera (so the game now keeps its seeded stream to itself and Math.random becomes a second seeded stream once the game has loaded); a first cast in a page builds and caches textures and geometry, drawing on the random as it does (a warm-up run comes first); and a page reused for the next clip kept what the last one left behind (a space warp, trails), so every clip gets a fresh page. The recorder reports any drift between the planned and the recorded run (none now).

**A game fix found on the way:** the Stormcaller's Downpour (Mastery 2) rained only from clouds the camera could see, and the clouds fade out when the camera is low, so a close view stopped the rain altogether (in the previews, and for a player zoomed in). Its clouds now rain, and flicker, while they are in the sky.

New in the recorder: `towers` beside the road, foes at a fraction of their health (`frac`), foes that walk in later (`enter`), clips that start later (`from`) or run longer (`cap`), clips centred on a mastery's moment (`focus`: the Storm Front, the Last Thunder, Chronostasis' collapse, Downpour's arcs), a script run every frame on the stage (`tick`: a storm cloud steered over the pack for Downpour), `epic` and `legendary` on the command line (`node tools/previews/record.js epic legendary`), and loading the recorder from another script records nothing (only running it does).

- Tests: `tests/pvc-test.js` checks that every Epic and Legendary scroll has its six clips and that an Epic scroll's card plays its clip in developer mode; the live preview of a scroll without clips is now checked with the Comet's clips put aside (also in `tests/smprev-test.js`, which used the Comet as the scroll without clips).
## Performance: static batching (v446)
Simon: "please now work on just optimalizations". Measured with Chrome's CPU profiler on a wave-150 board with ten towers and the hero (the battery harness's setup): the frame was about 97% rendering, and three.js spent most of that on per-draw work (uniform uploads and draw calls), not on the game's own logic (update, effects and anim together were under half a millisecond). With shadows on, a Kingsroad Plains board made about 540 draw calls a frame: towers about 290 (main and shadow pass), board trees about 220, lore props about 140, the hero about 100, grass tufts about 80; the 150 outer trees were about 450 meshes, most outside the view.
One module inserted before the boot line (markers `Performance (v446)` and `end of performance (v446)`), one tag in `buildMap` (the outer trees get `userData.ot`).
- **The outer forest (`pfBatch`):** after every land has dressed its board (an outermost `buildMap` wrapper), the still-shown outer trees are baked into the terrain's space and merged into one mesh per material and shadow setting. The lands that hide the outer forest (tundra, Whitewaste, taiga) leave nothing to batch.
- **Props (`pfFreeze`, `pfProps`):** inside one prop (a lore prop, a board tree, a gate) the meshes that never move are merged by material, as children of the prop, so clicks and removal work as before. Skipped: parts the prop animates (a lore prop's flaps), anything with its own userData, hidden, transparent, instanced or skinned meshes. Board trees are merged per tree only (the Firestorm scorches them one by one, the player clears them).
- **Landmarks, the keep, chests (also in `pfProps`):** a landmark animates and changes state only through the parts its model object points at (fire, core, glow, stones, fix, goods, tree, stump, lamp...), so those are skipped and the rest is merged; the keep skips its waving flag (`castleFlag`), a chest its lid, shine, halo and pin.
- **Specks (`pfLoose`):** grass tufts (by their cone) and road pebbles (dodecahedra smaller than any rock) straight under the terrain are merged by material; the pebbles' batch keeps `voidKeep`. Rocks (found by `rockIndex` by their shapes) are untouched.
- **Towers (`pfTower`, a `buildTowerMesh` wrapper, so every upgrade too):** everything the tower object points at (the turning head, spinner, beacon, the hero's rig, `refs`, the mastery flag, `userData.stone`) is skipped; the rest of the body is merged by material, then the head's own still parts inside the head. The merged meshes carry `userData.tower`.
- **Shaders:** a material is merged only if it is plain, the snow shader (world space) or the snow caps. The snow caps read each mesh's own height (`vStO=position`), so a merged mesh carries that as a `pfLoc` attribute and uses a variant of the material that reads it (`pfMat`). Any other custom shader is left alone.
- **The hero (`pfHeroFreeze`, a `spawnHero` wrapper):** a hero is 40 to 65 meshes in about a dozen joints, each part its own colour, so merging by material saved little. Inside each joint the plain parts (MeshPhong, no texture, no shader of its own, opaque) are drawn as one mesh: each part's colour goes into the vertices and the merged mesh uses one white vertex-coloured MeshPhong per finish (shininess, specular, emissive, flat shading; registered in `MC` so the shadow switch updates it too). The rig points only at joints, so every joint still moves its parts; the originals stay in place, hidden; glowing or transparent parts stay their own. Every hero renders the same (pixel-compared), with 30 to 55% fewer draw calls (Robin 122 to 84, Gunnar 78 to 35, the Sentinel 144 to 71, both passes).
- **Health bars (`PFB`, an `updBar` replacement closed by an `anim` wrapper):** every hurt foe had its own bar of three meshes (the back, the fill and a shield line drawn even when empty), up to 150 draw calls in a busy wave. Now all foes' bars are four instanced meshes (back, fill, boss fill, shield), filled each frame with the same sizes, colours, draw order and facing; `e.bar` stays null. 30 hurt foes: 490 to 427 draw calls, the same picture.
- **Build time:** the merging bakes vertices with plain array maths (no Vector3 per vertex), so a board builds only a few milliseconds slower (`tests/tidy-test.js`'s cost row passes).
- **Memory:** merged geometry whose mesh has left the scene (an old board, a sold or rebuilt tower) is freed every 3 seconds (`pfSweep`, an `anim` wrapper); three.js uploads it again if it ever returns.
- **Result:** the same picture (pixel-compared in every land: at most a few dozen pixels of anti-aliasing differ) with far fewer draw calls, ten towers and shadows on: Kingsroad Plains 538 to 252, Frostvale 569 to 235, Greywind Tundra 408 to 242, the Whitewaste 495 to 213, the Deep Taiga 572 to 233. In a running game on the wave-150 Plains board (ten towers, the hero, foes, shadows on), per frame: draw calls 540 to 217, WebGL calls 3979 to 1986 (counted, so it does not depend on the machine's load; frame times in the test container swing too much to quote). Triangles go up a little (27k to 35k: the merged forest is drawn whole instead of culled tree by tree), which costs far less than the draw calls saved. `PF.on=false` before a board is built turns it all off.
- Note: `tests/sets-test.js` fails its no-errors rows (Citadel, 'dfm: warp shader not found') when run on its own, on main too; it passes in the full run.
Tests: `tests/perf-test.js` (34, in run-all as 'perf'): in all five lands the board looks the same after batching with at most 70% of the draw calls; a tower's merged meshes still point at it, a ray hits it, its head stays its own part, a rebuilt tower is merged again, a removed tower's geometry is freed; lore props still take clicks; board trees keep their own meshes; landmarks merge their still parts and every part they animate or change stays in place, with their states still applying; the keep's flag and a chest's lid stay their own parts (the lid still opens); the outer forest is a few meshes; snow caps read pfLoc; the Void dissolves the forest and the pebbles keep voidKeep; every hero looks the same with its parts merged and takes fewer draw calls, a merged part moves with its joint, the merged material is white and vertex-coloured; thirty hurt foes' bars are one instance each in the four instanced meshes, placed over the foe, facing the camera, at their health; a game runs and renders without errors.

## Rite of the Phoenix: White-Hot and From the Ashes (v440)
After Simon's review of the Epic masteries, two of the Phoenix's are replaced. Rising Heat (7% more per foe struck before, up to 5) was hard to read, and Rebirth (the phoenix reborn from a pyre to skim and dive again) played like a second cast.

| Mastery | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Rising Heat: each strike +7% for every foe struck before it (up to 5) | **White-Hot**: each strike stokes its fire (the bird grows a little, runs paler); its 5th strike sets it white-hot in a flash of white fire. From then on it is a third larger and white, its strikes deal 40% more and its river of fire burns 50% hotter |
| 5 | Rebirth: reborn from a pyre, skims for 15% a foe, dives for 50% | **From the Ashes**: the death burst rains ashes on every foe it struck that still stands (a ring of smouldering coals at their feet for 4s). A foe that dies with ashes on it is reborn as a small firebird that dives at the nearest foe within 4 tiles for 60% and spreads the ashes to it (up to 6 a cast) |

Like a match and a fuse: White-Hot is one moment you can see (the fifth strike), and From the Ashes lights a fuse on every foe the phoenix touched, so the kills it sets up keep paying out instead of it flying again.

- The ashes are put on at once (a falling ember shows them landing); a firebird's foe gets the ashes before its 60% lands, so a foe it kills is reborn in turn. The firebirds are built from the phoenix model at 0.42 scale, gold, rising from a 3D fire burst and diving into another.
- Power on the Epic ladder (6 runs): 1.09 / 1.24 / 1.35 / 1.52 / 1.70 at a Mastery 5 factor of 1.1, now 1.125 (targets 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742). Hidden factors `SMG.phoenix` = 1.017 / 1.032 / 1.016 / 1.055 / 1.125.
- Tests: `tests/phm-test.js` (39 rows): the 5th strike and only the 5th sets it white-hot, 40% on strikes 5 on and none before, the river at 150% after; ashes on the foes struck and no others, a firebird from a marked death only, at the nearest foe for 60%, the chain (a foe the firebird kills is reborn), the 6 cap, the 4s limit, no target within 4 tiles, nothing left behind.

## Spatial Loop: Turned Around (v441)
After Simon's review, Mastery 1 is replaced. Long Fold made the fold always reach 8 tiles back, a number change you could not see in play.

| Mastery | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Long Fold: the fold always reaches 8 tiles back | **Turned Around**: a foe the fold sends back comes out walking the wrong way, back up the road, for 1.2s (bosses 0.5s, Resolve applies) |

Like a revolving door that spins you out facing the street you came from: the fold already sends them back, and now they keep going back for a moment before they realise.

- How it works: `e.revT` is a new foe state in `moveEnemy`: while it is above 0 the foe's step along the road is reversed and it faces back up the road. The fold's hop sets it (`lpmCast`, `S.hop`), and it is paid for from the Resolve budget like a hold of the same length (`rsvLeft`, `rsvSpend`): a fresh foe walks back 1.2s, a foe with less Resolve left walks back for what is left, and an Unstoppable foe walks on.
- The look (`lpmTurn`): a U-turn arrow of folded light (a 3D tube bent through three-quarters of a circle with a cone for its head, in a soft glow) circles the foe's waist, tilting as it chases its own head round, with shards of space trailing it and glints streaming off toward the keep. It grows in and shrinks away with the turn. The seam still shows as a curtain of folded light, and the fold reaches 3 to 8 tiles back again, at random.
- The loop deals no damage, so it stays off the ladder (factors 1).
- Tests: `tests/lpm-test.js` (31 rows): a foe comes out walking back for 1.2s, faces back up the road, walks on afterwards; it costs 1.2s of Resolve; a boss 0.5s; an Unstoppable foe walks on; no damage; nothing left behind; no turn at Mastery 0.

## Cataclysm: Standing Stones and Shrapnel (v442)
After Simon's review, two Cataclysm masteries are replaced.

| Mastery | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 1 | Drawn Down: one pillar rises under the toughest foe | **Standing Stones**: seven rune-carved standing stones rise early (from 0.55s) in a ring at the rune's edge, with void lightning crackling from stone to stone. Ground foes inside when the ring closes are fenced in until the collapse: one that reaches the stones is held there (Resolve applies; one with no Resolve left breaks out). Bosses push through |
| 4 | Sundered: physical hits deal 25% more for 5s | **Shrapnel**: foes the collapse hits are studded with four obsidian shards for 5s. Each tower or hero attack on a studded foe knocks a shard into the nearest other foe within 1.5 tiles for 20% (up to 4 a foe) |

Like a sheepdog and a pinata: the stones keep the flock where the sky will land, and every arrow that hits a studded foe knocks a shard into its neighbour.

- One change from the plan: the table said the pillars would rise in the ring. Moved to the edge, the eight obsidian pillars struck mostly empty ground and the cast lost a fifth of its damage (0.80 of Mastery 0 on the ladder, against a target of 1.09). So the ring is seven separate standing stones (grey, rune-carved, no damage, crumbling back into the ground after the collapse), and the pillars rise inside as before (Falling Sky, Obsidian Chains and World Breaker still use them).
- What counts for Shrapnel (`ccmAtk`): a hit whose source is a tower or the hero, that is not a scroll, a burn or a mastery hit. A shard flies as a spinning splinter of black glass (0.2s) and bursts in a small 3D void blast; the four shards stuck in the foe go one by one.
- Power on the Epic ladder (4 runs): 1.12 / 1.23 / 1.34 / 1.53 / 1.73 (targets 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742). Hidden factors `SMG.cataclysm` = 1.094 / 1.089 / 1.157 / 1.325 / 1.244. The stones and the shards deal nothing on the ladder (its foes stand still and no towers fire), so the factors carry them.
- Tests: `tests/ccm-test.js` (38 rows): the stones in an even ring at the edge, up within 0.9s, no damage, gone afterwards; walking foes fenced in, held at the stones, a boss and a flyer walking out, an Unstoppable foe breaking out; studs for 5s; a tower hit and a hero attack each knock a shard into the nearest foe for 20%; scrolls, burns and mastery hits do not; 4 at most; no neighbour, no shard; nothing after 5s.

## Thunder Wyrm: Snap, and a clearer Grounding Strike (v443)

| Mastery | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | Hunter's Weave: slower in packs, faster on empty road, swaying toward foes | **Snap**: as it weaves, its head lunges at foes within 1.2 tiles (jaws wide, eyes burning amber) and snaps shut: 80% and Charged. Foes it has not lashed come first, then the nearest; each foe once a cast |
| 4 | Grounding Strike: lash marks (sparks), plus 1 for a foe Charged by anything else | **Grounding Strike**: each lash rings its foe with a gold hoop of lightning (up to 3, at waist, chest and head); the strike forks to every ringed foe within 4 tiles for 35% per ring. Only its own lashes count |

- The lunge takes 0.18s and the weave goes on under it, so it snaps on the move. Constrict judges its reach from the serpent's path, not the lunging head, and a lunge in progress gives way when Constrict seizes a foe.
- Power on the Epic ladder (5 runs) at the old factors: 1.10 / 1.27 / 1.28 / 1.45 / 1.65; the hidden factors are now `SMG.wyrm` = 0.95 / 1.07 / 0.908 / 0.946 / 0.911 (targets 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742).
- The rings (`wymRings`) are 3D hoops that spin against each other and flicker, flare and go when the strike grounds through them (or the foe dies).
- Tests: `tests/wym-test.js`: snaps at 80% each, once per foe, none out of reach, Charged, during the weave, reaching a foe a tile off the road; Grounding Strike counts its own lashes only (a foe Charged by something else is not grounded); Constrict's test now puts its toughest foe where the head is (it used to rely on Hunter's Weave slowing the head in the pack).

## Prism of Dawn: Heliotrope and Resonant Fold (v444)
After Simon's review, two Prism masteries are replaced.

| Mastery | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | Sun-Tracking: the prism drifts after the foes (up to 1.6 tiles) | **Heliotrope**: a seventh, white sunbeam bends down out of the shaft of sunlight over the prism and locks onto the toughest foe under the wheel, burning hotter the longer it holds: 10% a second, rising to 40% over 2s (it relocks, starting cool, if its foe dies or leaves) |
| 3 | Blinding Fold: the folding beams blind for 0.5s, then slow | **Resonant Fold**: foes the folding beams cross ring like bells. When the lance lands, its power runs through each ringing foe into the foes beside it (within 1 tile): 50% each, once a foe |

Like a magnifying glass on a sunny day, and a row of tuning forks: Heliotrope holds one point of sunlight on one foe until it smokes, and the fold strikes the foes like bells, so the lance's note carries to their neighbours.

- The look: the sunbeam (`prmSunbeam`) is a white-hot core in a gold glow with a cone of light narrowing onto the foe; a pool of light at its feet draws in to a point as it heats (a flattened sphere, with a ring of light round it), sparks and smoke rise and a scorch grows. A first version ran the beam from the prism itself, and from the game's camera it was lost in the prism's own glare; bent down from the shaft of sunlight above, it spans the screen. The bells (`prmBell`) are two hoops of gold light humming round the foe; the toll (`prmToll`) swells a ring of light out of it and throws a ribbon of light to each foe beside it.
- Power on the Epic ladder (5 runs) at the old factors: 1.09 / 1.27 / 1.44 / 1.60 / 1.81; the hidden factors are now `SMG.prism` = 0.87 / 0.862 / 0.901 / 0.93 / 0.807 (targets 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742).
- Tests: `tests/prm-test.js` (31 rows): the sunbeam locks the toughest foe, ticks every 0.25s with a rate rising from 10% to 40%, only while the wheel turns, relocks cool on the next toughest when its foe dies; only the folding beams set foes ringing, each once; every ringing foe tolls when the lance lands; 50% to each foe within 1 tile of a ringing foe, once a foe.

## Oakheart Treant: Battering Trunk and Growth Rings (v445)
After Simon's review, two Treant masteries are replaced.

| Mastery | Was | Now |
|---|---|---|
| 2 | Bark Bulwark: foes held against its bark for up to 2s | **Battering Trunk**: when 3 or more ground foes stand within a tile in front of it, it lowers its trunk and charges 1.5 tiles through them (0.45s): 60% each, and they are thrown up and over to the sides of the road; bosses take the blow but hold their ground. 2.5s before it can charge again |
| 4 | Sap Harvest: Overgrown foes drained for 60%, the oak grows up to 40% | **Growth Rings**: each Overgrown foe its slam hits adds a ring of glowing sap round its trunk (up to 4), and it grows 8% a ring. At 4 rings its next slam is a Grove Slam: roots burst up in a 2.2-tile circle and hold the ground foes 0.8s (Resolve applies; bosses 40%) for 120%; then the rings are spent |

Like a bull through a hedge, and the rings of a tree trunk: the oak shoulders its way through and leaves the pack on the verges, and every Overgrown foe it crushes adds a ring until the ground itself answers.

- Found on the ladder: after charging through a pack the foes are beside and behind the oak, and from Mastery 2 its slam always landed in front of it, so it swung at empty road (0.62 of Mastery 0's power where foes keep coming). From Mastery 2 the slam now lands on whichever side the foes are thickest, in front or behind (`side()`).
- The base model was 1.25 times its size and the masteries used to set it back to 1; the oak now keeps its size at every mastery (`s0`), and Growth Rings grow it from there.
- Power on the Epic ladder (5 runs) at the old factors: 1.09 / 1.55 / 1.78 / 2.05 / 1.59; the hidden factors are now `SMG.treant` = 0.95 / 0.958 / 0.92 / 0.886 / 0.965 (targets 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742).
- Tests: `tests/trm-test.js` (33 rows): it charges with 3 or more foes in front (not 2), 60% to each foe it runs through, they end on the verge (0.85 off the middle) and a boss does not; rings 1 to 4, then a Grove Slam, 120% to the foes in it and holding them, and the rings start again from one.

## Previews for the reworked Epic masteries, and every Epic and Legendary clip at 720p (v440-v445)
The 102 Epic and Legendary clips are re-recorded at 1080x720 (they were 480p), and the 29 clips that show the masteries reworked in v440-v445 are recorded from the new game, each set up for its new mastery (`tools/previews/scenarios.js`):

| Clip | What it shows |
|---|---|
| Phoenix 1-5 | eleven foes so its fifth strike sets it white-hot (White-Hot); four low-health foes that fall to the feathers soon after the death burst and are reborn as firebirds diving at the two whole ones (From the Ashes: the clip must show two firebirds strike) |
| Spatial Loop 1-5 | the foes the fold sends back coming out turned around, a U-turn of light circling them (Turned Around) |
| Cataclysm 0-5 | a pack walking through the rune as the ring of standing stones rises (Standing Stones); an archer tower and a cannon knocking shards out of the studded foes (Shrapnel) |
| Thunder Wyrm 2-5 | the pack, its head lunging at them (Snap); the gold rings of lightning its strike grounds through (Grounding Strike) |
| Prism of Dawn 2-5 | the ram as the toughest foe, the white sunbeam on it (Heliotrope); the folding beams setting the pack ringing and the tolls (Resonant Fold) |
| Oakheart Treant 1-5 | the oak at its full size again; foes walking into it as it charges and throws them to the verges (Battering Trunk); Entangling Roots first, then four rings and a Grove Slam (Growth Rings) |

Each clip's probe (the moment its mastery must show) passed on the first seed; the line for each lists what its module logged (manifest.json, `ev`). Every clip was looked at on its contact sheet.

- The recorder now writes `manifest.json` after every clip: a run stopped at a background job's 2-hour limit used to lose the whole manifest (it was written only at the end). The manifest of the 79 clips recorded before that stop was rebuilt from the run's log.
- Size: the Epic and Legendary clips come to 184 MB (1.8 MB a clip, 210 KB a second, against 122 KB a second for the published clips at 720p): their effects are heavier and the tracking camera keeps the picture moving. The panel loads one clip at a time.
- Also merged here: the third session's preview fixes from all-latest-test (v438: clips preloaded in the background, nothing else drawn while a clip plays). `tests/pvc-test.js` keeps both sets of rows. The full suite passes on the merged build (96 suites).

## Piercing Judgment: a volley of lines (v447)
Simon: "piercing judgment really cannot lose its identity so masteries should make it more powerful but not by adding crazy effects but picture it like fern from frieren where she fires alot of em and just more powerful ones". The v371 set (a burn, a Condemned mark, an afterimage line, +50% under a quarter of health, a pillar of light) is replaced by more of the same line, and stronger lines.

| Mastery | What it does |
|---|---|
| 1 Follow-Up | the circle snaps back to a point and fires a second line through the foes the first one missed (a foe already pierced counts for a third when it picks its line): 50% |
| 2 Honed Line | every line strikes 5% harder, pierces 0.08 tiles further off its path (0.48 to 0.56 for the Judgment's line, 0.34 to 0.42 for the smaller ones) and is drawn a quarter thicker |
| 3 Volley | three more circles open in a fan behind the hand and fire one after another, each at the best line through the foes pierced least: 35% each |
| 4 Executioner | every line finishes off non-boss foes under 25% of their health (the Judgment's own line: 15% before) |
| 5 Barrage | ten circles open in an arc in the air behind the hero (all within half a second) and fire in turn over 1.5s, each at the best line through the foes still standing: 25% each |

Like Fern's Zoltraak: the same killing spell, cast again and again. Every extra line is the Judgment's own smaller circle (the Verdict's), spinning up and compressing to a point before it fires the same white-violet line across the whole field; at Mastery 5 a cast is 15 lines.

- How: `jvCast` (the live Judgment since v357) takes an optional set of options for one line (`X`: width, execution threshold, aim, where its circle opens, timing, thickness, callbacks); without it a cast is exactly as before (the Verdict tests still compare it with the old cast). `jdmCast` fires the Judgment's line with its options and schedules the others; the extra lines are `jvCast`'s smaller line given the Judgment's length (32 tiles) and its options. Every line of a mastered cast executes at the Judgment's threshold.
- Numbers: on the Epic ladder (6 runs) 1.09 / 1.25 / 1.36 / 1.52 / 1.74 against 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742, hidden factors `SMG.judgment` = 1.059 / 0.936 / 0.944 / 1.099 / 1.013. The first numbers (+15% and +0.14 wide, 40% and 35% lines) came out far over: where the foes stand close a wider line takes whole extra foes, and every line takes the whole pack.
- Tests: `tests/jdm-test.js` (28 rows): each line logs where it ran, so the foes it pierces are checked exactly; the follow-up through the foes the first missed; the honed numbers and widths (and the width option itself, with a fixed aim); the volley's three lines at the rays no line had reached; execution at 25% and 15%, never a boss; ten barrage lines over 1.5s, 15 in all; nothing left behind; other casters and the Verdict unchanged. `tests/verdict-test.js` accepts the masteries' wrapper in front of `jvCast`.

## Dimensional Slash: the same cut, deeper (v448)
Simon: "its important you dont change the heart of the scroll, no aditional slashes, maybe just mor VFX by enhancing how it looks while making it more powerful without changing how it looks so it still 'feels' like the same spell". So the v371 set (a bleed, a Void Mark, an Echo Cut, +50% under 30%, a singularity) is replaced by masteries that each make one part of the cut more lethal and draw that same part a little stronger. There is still one draw and one cut.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn stronger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Keen Edge | the cut | strikes foes up to 0.75 tiles off the line (0.5 before) | the blade's edge burns white, its glow brighter, the line a quarter bolder |
| 2 Deeper Rift | the rift | open 7.5s (6 before), 50% a tick (45% before), slows 60% (45% before) | the tear opens wider and darker, smoke seeps out faster, twice the shards of broken space |
| 3 The Still Heartbeat | the stillness | the foes on the line at the draw are held until space splits; the split strikes 25% harder | the stillness lasts a quarter second longer, the world drained of colour |
| 4 Severance | the split | non-boss foes it leaves under 20% are cut down | their slice marks flare bigger and brighter |
| 5 Worldsplitter | the split and the seal | the split strikes 50% harder; when the rift seals it snaps shut on every foe still inside it for 300% | the tear yawns open far wider; it closes with a crack of white light down the line |

Like a sword being sharpened, not swapped: the same draw, the same black line, the same rift, a little more lethal at every mastery.

- How: `castDSlash` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cut (`XO`, over `DSL`); without it the cut is exactly as before (`tests/dslash-test.js` passes unchanged). `dsmOpts` builds it from the mastery.
- A bug found on the way: the stillness's grey is a filter on the game's canvas, and the browser writes `grayscale(.62)` back as `grayscale(0.62)`, so the first version never cleared it; it now does (the test checks the colour comes back).
- Numbers: the rift is most of the cast's damage where foes stay in it, and 8s at 70% took Mastery 2 to 1.75 times Mastery 0 (target 1.23), so it is 7.5s at 50%. On the Legendary scroll's ladder (4 runs) the hidden factors `SMG.dslash` = 1.065 / 0.915 / 0.942 / 1.111 / 0.989 bring it to 1.09 / 1.28 / 1.38 / 1.53 / 1.72, against 1.094 / 1.234 / 1.35 / 1.522 / 1.742 (within the ladder's noise); Severance counts for little there (its foes do not drop under 20%).
- Tests: `tests/dsm-test.js` (26 rows): the wider cut takes the foes beside the road; the rift's ticks, length and slow; the hold, the later split, the grey that comes and goes, 1500%; severance at 20% (not at 30%, not a boss); 2250% and the seal's 300% on the foes inside; nothing left behind; Mastery 0 and other casters unchanged, checked against the cut's own line.

### Previews (v447, v448)

All twelve clips (Piercing Judgment and Dimensional Slash, Mastery 0 to 5) are recorded again for the new masteries. The clips are drawn from the game's canvas, so the grey of Dimensional Slash's stillness (a filter on the page) is not in them.

| Clip | What the recorder needed |
|---|---|
| Dimensional Slash, every mastery | A least length (`minLen`): in the stilled moment the world runs at a twelfth of its speed, the count of effects can fall to where it was before the cast, and that ended the clip there. Each clip now runs to the seal. |
| Dimensional Slash 5 | Foes with 70 health, so some are still inside the rift when it snaps shut; the hero and the pack held in view (`trackHero`), because between the cast and the cut almost nothing moves and a single mote off the blade had steered the view down the field. |
| Piercing Judgment 5 | A wide view (zoom 1 to 1.1) set half a tile toward the hero. The camera frames the foes the lines hit, and the arc of circles behind the hero is more than 2.5 tiles from all of them, so it counted them as a sideshow and they drifted off the right edge. |

## The lands round the board (v449)
Simon: "visually improve and perfect all arena backgrounds when youre playing, not the tiled arena but rather the rest". Since v427 the land round the board sits at its height and wears the tiles' colours, but it was mostly empty. One module before the performance module (markers `Arena surroundings (v449)` and `end of arena surroundings (v449)`), run by an outermost `buildMap` wrapper after every land has built its board (so the v446 batching still runs after it).
- **The map (`arsField`, baked by `arsBake` on a 160x160 grid over x -28..28, z -38..26):** fbm noise on the board's seed gives woods (with a framing band that thickens from about 1 tile off the board, so every side has woods, carved into glades), groves (trees gather), patches (flowers, heather, ferns gather), meadows, bare ground, stone, a fringe of bushes and flowers along the board's edge with gaps, and worn tracks from each gate out into the land (`arsTrack`, wandering a little).
- **The ground (`arsGround`):** the board-level ground's shader (v427's `glGroundMat`) reads the map as a texture (`arsTex`): darker forest floor where the trees stand (each tree's canopy is stamped into the map after it is placed), lighter meadows, dirt and stone where they lie, and the tracks.
- **The props (`ARS_KITS`):** per land, a dozen kinds of low-poly vertex-coloured pieces (merged once per kind), each one `InstancedMesh` with a tint, size and turn per instance; flat-shaded Phong like the rest of the game. Plants sway (a vertex bend that grows with height, its phase from the instance's position; `ARS.T` ticks in `anim`).
  - Kingsroad Plains: oaks, pines, bushes, berry bushes, grass, yellow and purple wild flowers, mossy rocks, fallen logs, stumps.
  - Frostvale: snow-capped spruce, snow-topped rocks, bare twigs, dry grass, winter berry bushes, logs.
  - Greywind Tundra: red and gold shrubs, tussocks, lichen rocks, autumn birches (kept out of the sea with `tunShore`).
  - The Whitewaste: ice spires, dead trees, frost-capped rocks, frozen grass.
  - The Deep Taiga: ferns, red and brown mushrooms, mossy logs, rocks, bilberry bushes, grass.
  - The Stormcrag Highlands: heather clumps (replacing the old pebble heather, which is hidden), gorse, bracken, tussocks, rocks.
- **Where not:** never on the board or within 0.3 of it, on the lead roads, in the tundra's sea, or on what the land already put there (`arsOcc` rasterises the land's own props); trees stay off the first tile and are smaller near the board, so the board stays clear and readable. The plains' and snow's old outer forest (three meshes a tree at random) is hidden for the woods.
- **Cost:** one draw call a kind (about 6 to 11 a land, twice with shadows); 100 to 330k triangles a land after keeping the pieces low-poly (one-triangle grass blades, simple leaf blobs); building a board takes about 0.1 to 0.2 s longer.
- Tests: `tests/ars-test.js` (15, in run-all as 'ars'): in all six lands every kind is placed, at least 1500 props, none on the board, the lead roads or the sea, instanced in the terrain, and the ground reads the map; the old outer trees and pebble heather are hidden; the same board dresses the same way and nothing piles up; the ground darkens under the trees; one draw call a kind; the wind ticks; every land renders. `tests/perf-test.js` accepts the outer forest hidden (v449) as well as batched.

## Masteries count in play for every scroll (v450)

A fix. A scroll's power is spell power x (1 + 5% a scroll level) x the Aevorian's time-and-space bonus x the mastery's own factor (`smP`: its hidden factor `SMG`, or its ladder). Epic and greater scrolls are cast a moment after the button (a banner, a cinematic beat), and work their power out again then, in case the hero changed; that second sum left `smP` out. So in play no Epic, Legendary, Mythic, Ancient or Secret mastery ever got its factor, while every ladder and test (which call `castScrollNow` with `100*smP(k)`) measured it with the factor. Common to Rare scrolls are cast at once and always had it.

| Scroll | Mastery | Factor | Power in play before | Now |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Piercing Judgment (Epic) | 3 | 0.944 | 12.21 | 11.53 |
| Piercing Judgment (Epic), scroll level 3 | 4 | 1.099 | 13.43 | 14.76 |
| Dimensional Slash (Legendary) | 5 | 0.989 | 12.21 | 12.08 |
| Spatial Shatter (Mythic) | 2 | 1.16 | 12.21 | 14.16 |

- How: one function, `scrollPow(t,k,s,lv)`, works the power out for both paths, so they cannot drift apart again. The factor is applied once (artifact recasts such as Sun Disc reuse the power they are given).
- Masteries above Rare are still developer mode only: outside it the factor is 1, as before.
- Tests: `tests/smpow-test.js` (9 rows, in run-all) fires real casts through `fireScroll` with `castScrollNow` swapped for a recorder: a Common scroll at once with its factor once; delayed Epic, Legendary and Mythic casts with theirs; Mastery 0 and non-developer play unchanged; and the page's own `fireScroll` uses `scrollPow` on both paths.

## Eclipse of the Damned: more stars, and none escape (v451)
Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". So the old generic set (Umbral Mark, Black Corona, Grasping Dead, Dying Stars, Second Sunset) is replaced by masteries that each make one part of the rite stronger and draw that same part richer. The black sun still swallows the sky, its ritual circle still holds the foes, its stars still fall on them as beams and the sun still shatters over the whole map, in the same 11.5 seconds.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Starfire | the star beams | 12% harder (168%, a gold beam 252%) and a little wider: 0.6 tiles (0.55 before), a gold beam 0.9 (0.85) | the beams a quarter thicker with wider white cores, the sigil under each star larger with its reach, and each impact bursts up in starfire: two churning balls of violet light (gold for a gold beam) that swell, stretch and climb, with jets of sparks streaming up and out |
| 2 Greater Stars | the gold beams | the heavy gold beams (225%, wider) fall 30% of the time (18% before) | more of the gold beams the cast already has |
| 3 Falling Sky | how many fall | a star every 0.0625s (0.07 before): about 35 beams instead of 32, in the same window (4.75s to 6.95s) | the sky holds 400 stars (320 before), twinkling 1.3 times as bright while they wait |
| 4 None Escape | where the stars aim | the stars that fall on foes pick one no star has struck yet (then the least struck; a foe a falling star is locked on counts as struck) and land on it without scatter (0.45 before) | the sigil locks under its foe and follows it while the star charges and the beam drops, so a walking foe (or one the circle drags in) is struck where it is |
| 5 The Sun Breaks | the shatter | every foe on the map for 1200% (1000% before) | 90 glass shards instead of 60, 1.3 times as large; a darker, deeper wave across the map (its dark band 0.3 of its reach instead of 0.22, 1.2 times as dark) |

- How: `castEclipse` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `ECLD`, the old numbers); without it the rite is exactly as before. `ecmOpts` builds it from the mastery, and `castEclipse` is wrapped once (`ECM_OLD`). The base cast also makes one ring geometry for every star's collapse instead of one per beam (more beams cost less).
- Numbers: the approved numbers were starting points.
