As first drawn up (0.65 / 0.95 tiles, 40% gold, a star every 0.055s, every star on a foe) the ladder measured 1.20 / 1.32 / 1.63 / 2.21 / 2.31 against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35, which would have needed hidden factors near 0.6 (a Mastery 5 shatter of 1300% would then have struck weaker than the plain 1000%). None Escape alone was +35% when every star fell on a foe: a quarter of the stars fall anywhere on the ritual ground, and on the ladder's lone foe or small pack those mostly miss. So that quarter still falls where it did (None Escape changes which foe the other stars pick, takes away their scatter and keeps them on their foe until they strike: about +9%), and the others are tuned down: 0.6 / 0.9 tiles, 30% gold, a star every 0.0625s, a shatter of 1200%. The 12% stays. With the hidden factors no part of a mastered cast strikes weaker than at Mastery 0 (a beam at Mastery 5 is 168% x 0.896 = 150.5%, the shatter 1075%).
- Ladder (Mythic half steps, the standing pack and the lone foe, `SMW` .6 / .4; `ladder.js eclipse 8 6 14.5`): the ladder is noisy for this scroll (the stars pick their stars and foes at random; one run of 8 x 6 casts swings by about 3%), so the hidden factors come from six runs pooled (33 repetitions).

| Mastery | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| Hidden factor `SMG.eclipse` | 0.947 | 0.954 | 0.935 | 0.904 | 0.897 |
| Without the factor (pooled) | 1.137 | 1.210 | 1.313 | 1.440 | 1.510 |
| With it (pooled) | 1.077 | 1.154 | 1.228 | 1.302 | 1.355 |
| With it (the last run, 8 x 6 casts) | 1.073 | 1.128 | 1.231 | 1.302 | 1.366 |

- Tests: `tests/ecm-test.js` (32 rows, in run-all). Every beam's impact is caught (it calls `shockwave`, 1.2 for a beam and 2 for a gold one) with where every foe stood at that moment, so the foes each beam should strike, and for how much, are checked exactly at every mastery, Mastery 0 too: 150% / 225% within 0.55 / 0.85 at Mastery 0 (and for casts that are not a mastered scroll cast), 168% / 252% within 0.6 / 0.9 from Starfire on, with a neighbour on the road 0.6 away taken by the wider reach; a starfire burst per impact; the gold share over five casts each (30% against 18%); 35-36 beams in the same window, 400 stars and their brightness; None Escape replayed star by star (each star on a foe picks a least struck foe, a quarter still fall on the ground, each lands right on its foe and strikes it, walkers and foes the circle drags in are followed, every foe is struck, the ones outside the circle too); 1200% to every foe once, 90 shards of the larger size, the wider, darker wave; the rite's 11.5s and its darkness lifting; nothing left behind; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.
- Looked at: contact sheets of Mastery 0 and 5 over the whole board at the game's zoom (the rite, the stars waiting, the beams, the shatter and its wave), Mastery 0, 1 and 4 at middle and close range among the foes, and a beam's impact alone (Mastery 0's flat flash and shockwave against Starfire's burst, violet and gold). The first Starfire beams were tinted white all through and two crossing beams read as a white sheet, so only the cores are whiter now; the first burst was a solid, orange fireball that hid the foe, so it is smaller, see-through at its edge, violet and gold, and gone in under half a second.

## Wrath of the Ember Titan: one blow, a river of fire (v452)
Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". So the v373 set (White-Hot Blade, Ember Rain, Magma Fists, Molten Spray, Titan's Roar: add-ons any fire scroll could have) is replaced by the set Simon approved: there is still one titan, one blow and one fissure, and each mastery makes one part of the blow stronger and draws that same part richer.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn stronger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Molten Edge | the blade | strikes 2450% (2200% before) and reaches 0.6 tiles further (1.8-7.5 along it) | the sword drawn 12% longer, its rims white-hot, its glow brighter, embers streaming off its edges; the swing ends a little flatter so the longer blade meets the road further on |
| 2 Deeper Fissure | the fissure | runs 14 tiles from the titan (11.5), burns 5.5s after the blow (4.3s), 55% of its geysers leave a patch of fire (35%) | seams 40% wider with a hot core, embers climbing off them while it burns, taller geysers (a white-hot jet and a plume of flame up each) |
| 3 Furnace Heart | the tip blast | 1300% (1000%) within 3.5 tiles (3) | a bigger fireball, ring of fire and crater; 7 more lava rocks thrown (cosmetic, as before) |
| 4 Titan's Weight | the stun | foes under the blade stunned 1.2s (0.6s), bosses now 0.4s (not at all) | a wider crater of cracks, chunks of the road and dust thrown up the whole length of the blade, a heavier shake with an aftershock |
| 5 The Road Splits | the blow and the fissure | the fissure is torn open whole as the sword lands: every geyser bursts in that moment, 390% (300%), and the burn covers it all from the first tick | the seams flare white down the whole road and cool back to orange; one line of tall geysers with fire running between them |

- How: `castEmberTitan` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `ETL`); without it the cast is exactly as before (the test checks the blade, tip, stun, geysers and burn against the titan's own position and facing). `emtOpts` builds it from the mastery; the masteries' looks live in the module (`emtBuild`, `emtFrame`, `emtBlow`, `emtGeyser`, `emtChunk`). `myTitanSpot` reads the cast's numbers, so with Molten Edge the titan picks its spot for the longer blade.
- A bug found on the way: the fissure's glowing seams (`myRoadSeams`, like `nsSeams` and `nsPathSeams`) were built with their quads facing the ground, so the camera never saw them: the "river of fire" was only its geysers and fire patches. The titan's fissure is now drawn facing the sky (`o.up`) at every mastery, Mastery 0 too, as the scroll's card promises. The crater cracks (`nsSeamFx`, shared with many spells) are left unseen as before: seen, the titan's would lie over the grass round the blow as a flat orange web for 6s. Only Titan's Weight's wider crater is drawn facing up, smaller and dimmer. Other spells' seams are not touched.
- The mastery preview waits for the titan to sink and its fissure to cool (`SMPV_BUSY`): the cast lives 8.6s (9.8s from Deeper Fissure), longer than its 5s cinematic lock.
- Numbers: on the Mythic ladder (pack 0.6 / single 0.4, 4 runs of 4 casts 13s apart) the hidden factors `SMG.embertitan` = 1.027 / 1.067 / 1.05 / 1.12 / 1.128 bring it to 1.085 / 1.162 / 1.229 / 1.312 / 1.364, against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. The stun counts for nothing there (the ladder's foes stand still), so Titan's Weight's factor carries it; The Road Splits adds about 3.5% of its own (its geysers, and the burn over the whole fissure from the start).
- Tests: `tests/emt-test.js` (33 rows, in run-all): Mastery 0 exactly as before (the blade's zone, the tip's circle, the stun, geysers once per foe, the burn's length), the seams facing the sky; 2450% to exactly the foes 1.8-7.5 along the blade, foes past the old reach among them; the fissure 14 tiles long, burning 5.5s, the cast living 9.8s, about 55% of geysers leaving fire; 1300% within 3.5; stuns of 1.2s and 0.4s for bosses; every geyser in the frame of the blow at 390%; nothing left behind; other casters unchanged; the preview's wait; the real cast path at every mastery.

## Eternal Winter: the same winter, colder (v453)
Simon: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear, for example the abyss having 3rd leviathan or something but just nothing that over shines the spell".

The Heart of Winter falls, frost freezes the whole world, and the Heart bursts so a wave of breaking ice shatters everything frozen. Each mastery strengthens one part of that and draws the same part richer; the one new thing, Shards of the Heart, is six of the thirty shards the burst already throws. They replace the generic set (Deep Cold, Snowfall, Ice Spires, Frostbite, Heart's Echo).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn stronger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Biting Frost | the frost's touch | the frost strikes 320% as it reaches each foe (200% before) | larger ice blocks (x1.18) with longer spikes (x1.7); the cracks just behind the frost's front light up |
| 2 The Long Freeze | the hold | the Heart gathers 0.6s longer (it bursts at 5.1s, not 4.5s), so every foe held until the wave is held 0.6s longer, and stays slowed 2.5s after (1.5s before); bosses as before | longer rays from the Heart as it gathers (11x, not 7.5x); a brighter aurora |
| 3 Shards of the Heart | the burst's shards | 6 of its 30 shards fly at the 6 nearest foes, one each: 100% each where they land (fewer foes, fewer shards) | those shards larger and brighter, with a soft glow and frost trails |
| 4 Winter's Hold | the boss rule | bosses stay frozen until the wave reaches them too, up to 2.4s (1.2s before, and never less) | thicker ice on bosses: a second shell, crystals at its foot, more splinters when it thaws |
| 5 The Heart Breaks | the shatter | the wave strikes 1500% (1200% before) | the sheet of ice bursts up in light, see-through plates that tumble and are gone within about half a second of the wave; all 30 shards 1.25x larger |

The same winter at every mastery: the same Heart falling out of the same storm of shards, the same frost racing across the board, the same burst and wave.

- How: `castEternalWinter` takes an optional set of numbers and hooks for one cast (`XO`, over `EWL`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `ewmOpts` builds it from the mastery (masteries 1 to L). The Long Freeze moves the whole end of the cast with the burst: its length (6.8s to 7.4s), the floor's and the sky's fade, and the cinematic lock (5.8s to 6.4s). The frost floor's shader has one more uniform (`uF`, the light in the cracks behind the front; 0 for the plain cast) and `myAurora` takes a brightness. A frozen foe's ice is the plain block (`nsFreezeFoe`) made larger by `ewmFreeze`. The Heart Breaks' plates are one instanced mesh (about 230 plates, one draw call), moved only while they fly. The mastery preview also waits for the cast's fade now (the frost floor is shared, so a cast on top of the last one's fade would lose it).
- Holds still ignore Resolve, as the plain cast's do, so Winter's Hold is capped at 2.4s.
- Names: none of the five is a scroll's, another mastery's or a synergy's (the old Deep Cold was also a Glacial Rift mastery). The fifth was approved as Absolute Zero, but that is already the synergy of an Ice Tomb caught in the Frostfall Comet, so it is The Heart Breaks (it pairs with Shards of the Heart).
- Numbers: on the Mythic half-step ladder (pack .6 / one .4; its foes stand still, so runs agree) the masteries measure 1.086 / 1.086 / 1.136 / 1.136 / 1.35 without factors: Biting Frost +8.6%, the six shards about +5%, The Heart Breaks +21%; the holds and slows count nothing there. The hidden factors `SMG.eternalwinter` = 0.993 / 1.07 / 1.079 / 1.146 / 1 bring it to 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.225 / 1.302 / 1.35 against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. The factor falls back to 1 at Mastery 5, where The Heart Breaks' own damage reaches the target.
- Tests: `tests/ewm-test.js` (42 rows, in run-all): every hit logged and every foe's hold and slow read the frame the frost reaches it. Mastery 0 is the old cast (200% then 1200% once each, held until the wave, the boss 1.2s, the burst at 4.5s, the lock 5.8s, the floor gone at 6.8s, the plain ice); 320% and the larger ice; the burst 0.6s later, holds 0.6s longer, slows 2.5s, the lock and the floor 0.6s later, the brighter aurora; the six shards at the six nearest foes, 100% each, one each with two foes; the boss held until the wave up to 2.4s and never less than 1.2s, in thicker ice; 1500% and the plates; nothing left behind at any mastery; the plain cast with Mastery 5 on the account; the preview's wait; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.

## The Aeon Dial: twelve strokes at midnight (v454)
A great clock is laid over the field; its hand sweeps every foe, ageing it slower and slower, while the towers live through the years faster; at midnight the last hour strikes everything. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". So the old generic set (Weathered, Sands of Ages, Clockwork Shards, Heavy Hours, The Thirteenth Hour) is replaced by masteries that each strengthen one part the dial already has and draw that same part richer. The only new pieces are midnight's twelve strokes, which are the dial's own midnight.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 The Toll of Years | each sweep | each pass takes 2% of a foe's max health (1% before; bosses 0.5%, 0.3% before) | the hand's trail runs longer (1.3 rad, 0.8 before) and brighter, its light brighter; 24 motes of gold dust off every foe it passes (10), 1.6 times as large |
| 2 Deep Age | the ageing | each pass ages foes 12.5% slower, up to 50% (10%, up to 40% before) | aged foes shed twice the dust, paler and larger, from head to foot |
| 3 Quickening | the turning | the hand turns every 2.56s (3.2s before): five sweeps before the same midnight | gears and rune rings spin 1.6 times as fast (the hands faster by themselves) |
| 4 Borrowed Years | the towers' time | towers fight 30% faster while the dial turns (20% before) | each tower glints gold as the hand passes over it: a four-pointed star on its crown, gold motes climbing its walls |
| 5 Twelve Strokes | midnight | midnight tolls twelve times over 2.2s, 95% to every foe each (1140% in all, one 1000% strike before); every stroke crumbles the non-boss foes left under 25% (20% before) | the numerals flare one by one, I to XII, each with a column of light and a rise of gold; each stroke sends a golden ring with a wall (sixty ribs of light, like the dial's ticks) and a band of motes out across the field, with a bell; on XII every numeral flares and the dial fades |

- How: `castAeon` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one dial (`XO`, over `AEOD`); without it the dial is exactly as before (`tests/ancient-test.js` passes unchanged, with the Aeon mastery pinned to none). `aemOpts` builds it from the mastery; `aemFx` builds the masteries' own pieces (the glint stars, the flaring numerals and their columns, the twelve rings) in the dial's root, so they are freed with it. The strokes run on the dial's own clock (game time), not timers. `AEON_ACT` is unchanged: Quickening turns faster within the same window, and the towers' speed is read from the running dial (`live.tr`).
- A recast while the dial turns winds it back, as before; the running dial keeps the masteries it was cast with.
- With the toll the dial holds 2.2s longer (until its last stroke) and then fades as it did after its one strike.
- Numbers: on the ladder's standing foes a cast is four passes (350% + 1% of 4000) and the 1000% midnight; Quickening adds a fifth pass and the toll 140%. Deep Age (a slow) and Borrowed Years (the towers) count for nothing there, so their factors carry them. On the Mythic ladder (pack .6 / one .4, 3 casts 25s apart, 2 runs) the hidden factors `SMG.aeon` = 1.017 / 1.106 / 1.003 / 1.074 / 1.064 bring it to 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35 (the foes stand still and nothing there is random, so the factors follow from these sums), against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35.
- Tests: `tests/aem-test.js` (36 rows, in run-all): the card and its numbers; the defaults table is the old dial (the same passes and hits as a cast without options); Mastery 0 unchanged (350% and 1% a pass, 0.3% a boss, four turns, ageing to 40%, towers 20%, one 1000% midnight); 2% and 0.5% at Mastery 1; ageing 0.125 / 0.25 / 0.375 / 0.5 and half speed at Mastery 2; five passes 2.56s apart at Mastery 3; towers 30% (20% at Mastery 3) and a glint once a turn at Mastery 4; twelve strokes 0.2s apart, 95% each, no 1000%, a foe under 25% crumbling at the first stroke, one just over it at a later one, not one at 30% nor a boss; nothing left behind; a recast keeps its masteries; other casters cast the old dial; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.

## Wrath of Gaia: the forest takes them down (v455)
From the keep outward a forest tears up along every road, each tree striking and pinning the foes it rises through; then it sheds its leaves over the whole field and the keep drinks the life of the green. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". So the old generic set (Thorned Bark, Pollen Storm, Blossoms, Strangling Roots, Heart of the Forest) is replaced by masteries that each strengthen one part the forest already has and draw that same part richer. Nothing new walks, coils or blooms (the Treant, Entangling Roots, Bramble Patch and the Hundred-Year Bloom own those); the only new event is the drag as the forest sinks, which is the forest's own sinking.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Heartwood | each tree's strike | each tree strikes for 650% (500% before) the foes within 1.05 tiles of it (0.9 before), once per foe | the trees pop up harder (overshoot 1.22, 1.12 before); a bigger 3D burst of earth at every trunk (7 longer cracks, 4 before; a mound more than twice as tall; more clods, stones and motes). Not taller trees (World Root owns that) |
| 2 Rooted Fast | the pin | struck foes are pinned 1.6s (1s before), bosses 0.6s (0.4s before), Resolve still applying (`stunE`); Held 2.1s (1.5s before) | five thick roots splay out from every trunk's foot and down into the ground, birches too (three thin ones before, none on a birch); the ground under each tree darker (0.75, 0.55 before) |
| 3 The Forest Stands | the standing forest | until it sheds, a foe that walks into a standing tree's reach is struck by it too: the same strike and pin, once per foe (a foe struck as the forest rose is not struck again) | that tree replays its rise pop (a heave and a swell, a burst of earth at its foot, a few leaves shaken loose); the leaves fall twice as thick (a leaf every 0.06s, 0.12s before) |
| 4 The Keep Drinks Deep | the mend | the keep mends 3 lives (2 before) | the green column at the keep rises twice as tall (12, 6 before), fading softly toward its top; the mend's spray thicker, climbing the keep's walls and bursting over its roof |
| 5 Return to the Earth | the leaf-fall and the sink | every tree sheds (every second tree before), 375% to every foe (300% before); as the forest sinks each tree drags the ground foes in its reach down with it: 150% and pinned 0.5s (bosses 0.2s; Resolve applies; once per foe; not flyers) | a storm of leaves twice as thick (five a tree), drawn in batches; the trees go down heavier and faster (an accelerating plunge in 0.62s, leaning as they go, all the way under; 0.9s and part way before), the earth bursting at every trunk: a low heave of soil, clods and stones thrown out in arcs, dust |

- How: `castGaia` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one forest (`XO`, over `GAIAD`); without it the forest is exactly as before. `wgmOpts` builds it from the mastery; `wgmFx` hooks the masteries' own pieces into the forest (the taller column's fade, the standing forest's strikes and pops, the heavier sink, the storm, the burst of earth, the drag, the deeper mend). The strike is one function (`hit`) shared by the rising trees and the standing ones. `nfxTreeRaw`/`natTree` take optional roots and `nfxBurst` an optional mound height; both are unchanged without them.
- Cost: The Forest Stands keeps the risen trees by tile (cells as wide as a tree's reach), so a foe only looks at the trees in the nine cells round it. The storm of Return to the Earth draws its leaves as one instanced mesh per shape and colour (five draws for some 265 leaves on the plains, where every second tree used to shed four leaves as a mesh each), and the burst of earth is three instanced meshes for the whole forest: at the shed Mastery 5 costs fewer draw calls than Mastery 0 (112 against 174 on the plains), and at its natfx check (1.6s) 88 against 84 (budget 170).
- Numbers: on the ladder's standing foes a cast is one 500% strike for every foe and the 300% shed (about 330% with Overgrown); Heartwood adds 150%, Return to the Earth 75% to the shed and the 150% drag. Rooted Fast (a hold), The Forest Stands (foes walking in) and The Keep Drinks Deep (lives) count for nothing there, so their factors carry them. The approved table had Heartwood at 575%: with it the masteries measured 1.092 at Mastery 1-4 and the factors would have had to climb to 1.19 at Mastery 4, so the strike is 650% and every factor stays between 0.91 and 1.1. On the Mythic ladder (pack .6 / one .4, 3 casts 10s apart, 2 runs) the masteries alone measure 1.184 / 1.184 / 1.184 / 1.184 / 1.48, and the hidden factors `SMG.gaia` = 0.911 / 0.981 / 1.035 / 1.1 / 0.912 bring them to 1.078 / 1.161 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.349 against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35.
- In play: Rooted Fast's longer Held lets Deep Roots (+40% to nature on a Held foe) catch the shed and the drag on foes struck in the last half second of the rise (far up long roads), and on foes The Forest Stands strikes late. The drag's pin on top of Rooted Fast's can use up a foe's Resolve, so some foes turn Unstoppable after the forest sinks, as with any other hold that does. World Root (the Eldritch Tree standing) still makes the trees bigger and doubles every strike, the shed and the drag.
- Tests: `tests/wgm-test.js` (43 rows, in run-all): the card and its numbers; the defaults table is the old forest (each foe within 0.9 tiles of a tree struck once for 500%, none further, 300% shed, 1s / 0.4s pins, the keep mending 2); Mastery 0 unchanged; 650% within 1.05 tiles (and foes between 0.9 and 1.05 struck) at Mastery 1; 1.6s / 0.6s pins and 2.1s Held, five roots on every kind of tree, still one mesh, at Mastery 2; a foe walking in struck with the same strike and pin, its tree popping, once per foe, not after the shed nor as the forest sinks, and not at Mastery 2; 3 lives and the 12-tall column at Mastery 4 (2 at Mastery 3); every tree shedding five leaves, 375% once, the 150% drag on every ground foe in reach 0.15s after the shed, not a flyer nor a foe out of reach, 0.5s / 0.2s pins, the storm and the earth in batches, the trees all the way under 0.6s after the shed, nothing left behind, and none of it at Mastery 4; World Root doubling the strike, the shed and the drag; other casters casting the old forest; the draw calls; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery. `tests/nature-test.js` and `tests/natfx-test.js` pin the Gaia mastery to none (they test the scroll's own cast and looks).

## Lance of the Seraph: the host of heaven (v456)
A seraph of light appears over the mightiest foe and hurls one great lance into it like a falling star, then six smaller lances leave its wings for the next strongest. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". So the old generic set (Holy Brand, Feathers of Light, Radiant Wings, Eighth Lance, Choir of Lances) is replaced by the set Simon approved: there is still one seraph, one great lance and its volley from the wings, and each mastery makes one part of the cast stronger and draws that same part richer.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Star-Forged Lance | the great lance | strikes 4200% (3500% before) | the lance 4.1 long and 1.5 wide (3.4 and 1.25), its halo and the glow on its head brighter, a brighter trail and more motes as it forms and falls; the column of light where it lands wider (0.75, 0.6 before) |
| 2 Falling Star | the impact | its blast reaches 4 tiles (3.2) and strikes 900% (800%) | the ring of ten pillars of light rises at 3.2 tiles (2.4), 6 tall (4.5); the rune 3.3 (2.6); a see-through dome of light swells out to the blast's reach and light is thrown out low over the ground (the blast drawn in 3D, the explosion rule) |
| 3 Host of Lances | the lances | nine lances leave the wings (six) | three more of the same lances: from the tips of the great wings, then from above the halo where the upper wings meet |
| 4 Pinned by Light | the lances left standing | the great lance holds its foe 1.5s; each lance holds its foe 0.5s (`stunE`, so Resolve applies: a boss is held 0.6s at most) | the lances stay standing in their foes, glowing, until the hold ends (the small ones vanished the moment they landed); light climbs the great lance while it holds |
| 5 Wrath of the Host | the small lances | every lance strikes like a lesser great lance: 1500% (1200%) and 350% around it (250%) | the lances larger (2.2 by 1.05; 1.7 by 0.8), brighter trails, taller beams where they land (11, 8 before), and a small dome of light and a burst of sparks where each lands |

- How: `castSeraphLance` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `SLD`); without it the cast is exactly as before (the test checks every hit of a Mastery 0 cast against where the foes stand). `srlOpts` builds it from the mastery; the masteries' own pieces (the lance's brighter halo and glows, the domes, the standing lances' light) live in the module (`srlBuild`, `srlFrame`, `srlImpact`, `srlLand`, `srlStand`) and hang from the cast's own lances, so they are freed with them.
- A trim: Simon approved Wrath of the Host at 1800% and 400% around it. Measured on the ladder, the set came out far over it with those numbers (1.54 at Mastery 5 against a target of 1.35, which would have needed a hidden factor of 0.88), so the two numbers are trimmed to 1500% and 350%. The mechanics are as approved, and the card shows the numbers in play.
- The cast still lives 6s: with nine lances and their holds the last lance is gone about 4.6s in, before the seraph has risen away. The mastery preview waits for the seraph to rise away (`SMPV_BUSY`), as it outlives its 4.8s cinematic lock.
- Numbers: on the Mythic ladder (pack 0.6 / single 0.4, 8 runs of 4 casts 9s apart) the hidden factors `SMG.seraphlance` = 1 / 1.045 / 0.96 / 1.024 / 0.94 bring it to 1.074 / 1.166 / 1.226 / 1.31 / 1.354, against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. The holds count for nothing there (the ladder's foes stand still), so Pinned by Light's factor carries it. The factors stay close to 1: only at Mastery 3 (0.96) does each lance from the wings strike a little less than at Mastery 0 (1152% and 240% around it, against 1200% and 250%), and there are nine of them.
- Tests: `tests/srl-test.js` (32 rows, in run-all): the card and its numbers; the defaults table is the old cast; Mastery 0 exactly as before (3500% to the mark, 800% to exactly the foes within 3.2, 1200% to the six next strongest and 250% within 0.8 of each, no holds, nothing left behind); 4200% at Mastery 1; 900% to exactly the foes within 4 at Mastery 2, foes 3.2-4 tiles away among them; nine lances at Mastery 3, the last three from the wing tips, the list repeating over fewer foes; holds of 1.5s and 0.5s at Mastery 4, only on those foes, a boss held its Resolve (0.6s), a lance standing 0.5s and gone after; 1500% and 350% at Mastery 5 with Masteries 1-4 riding it; nothing left behind; other casters cast the old cast; the preview's wait; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.

## Spatial Shatter: the world falls (v457)
A tarot card of space lands and turns, the world twists and freezes, a crack races through, and the whole region breaks into shards that carry the frozen picture: 800% to every foe inside, once. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". So the old generic set (Glass Echo, Starlit Cracks, Falling Space, Prism Halo, Mirror World) is replaced by masteries that each strengthen one part the spell already has and draw that same part richer. There is still one card, one twist, one freeze and one break: no second shatter, no ice look (Eternal Winter's), no grey stillness (Dimensional Slash's).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Heavier Space | the break | the break strikes 940% (800% before) | the shards are cut thicker (0.09-0.2 tiles, 0.05-0.14 before), and the shard shader's own chromatic split and Fresnel rim (both zeroed before) are switched on: each piece bends the frozen picture with coloured edges, its side faces show as a darker band with a faint cold light |
| 2 Absolute Stillness | the freeze | foes stay held 1.2s after the break (bosses 0.5s), through `stunE`, so Resolve applies | every shard hangs a quarter second, perfectly still, at the top of its flight before it falls (no grey: that is Dimensional Slash's stillness) |
| 3 Torsion | the twist | foes inside are slowed while the twist turns, up to 50% at its fastest, following its speed curve (it did nothing to them before); a stronger slow from elsewhere is kept | the twist turns a quarter turn further, and motes swirl with it: 150 drawn after the twist's warp, in the shards' own pass, orbiting its centre on its speed curve, fastest near the middle, freezing with the world (the base cast's own motes are drawn before the warp, which smears them away: they have never been seen) |
| 4 Clean Break | the break | non-boss foes the break leaves under 25% of their health break apart with the region | their burst flares bigger: 30 pieces (12), a flash twice as large, a spray of light; the sheet still covers them at the break, so a flare and chips of the frozen picture are thrown up over it, in the shards' own pass |
| 5 The World Falls | the shards | the sheet of shards bursts up to twice its height and rains back down; every large shard that lands strikes the foes under it, 250%, once each | the pieces climb to about 3 tiles (from a sheet 1.5 tiles up; they barely rose before) and fall back over the region, half see-through at their highest so the foes show through them; heavier landings: twice the dust, up to 8 flashes a frame (3) and a shake; a large slab pushes dust out from under its outline and a heavy one glints, and every foe it strikes breaks a few chips of the picture out over it |

- How: `castShatter` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `SHD`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `shmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L). The changes inside the base cast are small: the break's strike, a hold, the twist's slow and extra angle, the finish, the shard thickness and shader, a hang at the top of the flight, how high and how far the shards fly, and the strike of a landing shard. The twist's speed comes from its own curve (`shSpd`, the curve `SH_SV` that `shAng` integrates); "under a shard" is its outline where it lies, seen from above, with a foe's half-width round it (`shUnder`). The stale "ANCIENT" header over the cast is now "MYTHIC".
- Seen, not hidden: the shards are drawn in a pass of their own over the world, so the base cast's burst at each foe at the break, and the dust and flash of a landing shard, are mostly under the shards; the new accents (Torsion's motes, Clean Break's flare and chips, the landing dust pushed out from under a slab and its glint, the chips of a struck foe) are drawn in that pass, over them. The lifted pieces of The World Falls fade to half at their highest, so the raised world does not bury the foes (Eternal Winter's lesson).
- Timeline: unchanged. Every shard lands within 2s of the break, before the fade, and the cast still ends 3.6s after it (10.05s); it holds `SGL.n` until then, which the mastery preview already waits on. One twist, one captured frame, one region: none of the singletons gains a second user.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: the break is 940%, not 880%. The hold, the slow and the finish count for nothing on the ladder (its foes stand still with huge health), so Masteries 1-4 all measure as the break alone, and the hidden factor has to climb 1.21 times from Mastery 1 to 4 whatever the break is: at 880% that put Mastery 4's factor at 1.18 (0.98 to 1.18 in all); 940% centres the factors in 0.92-1.11, as the Eclipse lesson asks. "Large" for the falling shards is every shard that lands as a piece (0.55 tiles and up, the base cast's own line between chips that dissolve in the air and pieces), not only the heaviest (over 1.7, the ones that flash): the breakup is finest at its centre, where the densest foes stand (cells there are 0.5-1.2 tiles), so the heaviest alone would almost never fall on them. "Twice as high" is twice the sheet's height: twice the old rise (a tenth of a tile) could not be seen; the shards fly out half as fast so they still land about where they did.
- Ladder (Mythic, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, SMW .6 / .4; `ladder.js shatter 3 4 13.5`): with the hidden factors `SMG.shatter` = 0.9174 / 0.9889 / 1.0434 / 1.1081 / 0.915, it measures 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.337 (Mastery 5 swings about 0.02 from run to run: the run before, with its factor rescaled to this one, gave 1.358) against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. Masteries 1-4 are the break alone (1.175 without the factors), so they land exactly. At Mastery 5 a falling shard strikes 96% of the pack and 97% of lone foes (pooled over 38 casts each; one cast can strike anywhere from half to all of them), about 1.476 without its factor.
- Tests: `tests/shm-test.js` (36 rows, in run-all): the card and its numbers; the defaults table is the old cast and a cast with only a log is the same cast (800% to every foe inside once, the old shards and shader, no slow, the old hold); Mastery 0 unchanged; 940% and the thicker shards with their split and rim at Mastery 1; the hold of 1.2s (a boss 0.5s, a foe short of Resolve only what it has left), foes standing still through it and walking on after, and every shard hanging still a quarter second at the top of its flight at Mastery 2; the slow following the twist's speed curve exactly, 50% at its peak, none before, after or outside, a stronger slow kept, and the twist a quarter turn further with its motes drawn over the warp at Mastery 3; a foe left at 21% breaking apart, not one at 29% nor a boss, its bigger burst with a flare and chips drawn over the sheet, at Mastery 4; at Mastery 5 every large landing shard striking exactly the foes under it (checked against the shard's own transform), 250% once each, the pieces climbing to about 3 tiles and half see-through up there, every landing before the fade and nothing left behind; each mastery needing its level; echoes cast the old spell; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.

## Vael'Thoruun: the storm that follows (v458)
The hero becomes the source of a living storm: lightning erupts from the staff, wilder with every strike, arcs between the foes until everything joins one network, and one final strike falls from the apex star above it. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". So the old generic set (Charged Sky, Thunder Crown, Static Field, Ball Lightning, Storm Lattice) is replaced by masteries that each strengthen one part the storm already has and draw that same part richer. The bolts still come only from the staff and the apex star, never from clouds, and the storm keeps its timing: the opening at 1s, four bursts from 2s to 6s, the network from 6s, the final strike at 8s (the hero's pose still kicks on it).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Wilder Storm | the escalation | the four bursts bring about 3, 5, 7 and 10 strikes (2, 4, 6 and 9 before; each max(1, round(q x 0.6-1.5))): some 26 in all instead of 22 | sparks spray from the staff tip, thickening as the storm builds (none before), and it zaps twice as often (16 a second); the purple deepens faster (0.26 a strike up to 0.66, 0.18 up to 0.6 before; its floor rises 0.075 a second, 0.06 before) |
| 2 Living Current | the arcs | every strike that lands on a foe arcs on (4 in 10 before), into 1-2 foes within 2.2 tiles (1.8 before), for 60% of the strike (55%): 90% within 0.4 of each | the arcs thicker (0.8, 0.55 before) with two branches (1), ten sparks where each lands (6) and a small flash |
| 3 The Great Circuit | the network | every foe in the storm's area joins it (at most 10 before), four links from a node (3), and it tears at them for 75% a tick (60%) | its edges thicker (1.3, 1.05 before) under a bigger apex star (2.6 at its peak, 2.2); at most 64 links are drawn, every node linked to its nearest first, so a big crowd does not flood the frame |
| 4 The Storm Follows | the storm's area | at each burst and at 6s it re-centres on the thickest foes near it (within 5.2 tiles) and glides after them at up to 1.5 tiles a second, keeping within half a tile of them, until the final strike falls where they are | the area glow, the strikes' focus and the apex star glide with it, and the bolts climbing to the star turn to keep reaching it |
| 5 Wrath of the Ancient Sky | the final strike | 1260% (900% before) within 3.2 tiles (2.6), knockback 1.4 (1.1); the crater stays live 4.5s (2.6s; still 25% every 0.3s within 2.4 tiles), and the storm ends with it | the apex star peaks at 3 (2.2), the final bolt is wider (7, 5.5) with 12 branches (9), the crater's disc and crack nets a quarter larger |

- Name: the approved table called Mastery 3 "Overload", but Overload is already a synergy (fire on a Charged foe) and a Wizard Tower mastery, so it is The Great Circuit.
- How: `castVael` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one storm (`XO`, over `VAELD`, the old numbers); without it the storm is exactly as before. `vtmOpts` builds it from the mastery, and `castVael` is wrapped once (`VTM_OLD`). `vBolt` now returns its root group (its callers ignored the result; nothing else changed in it or in `vImpact`, which The Primordial Tempest shares). A Tempest Heart recast is a second storm with the same masteries (the recast keeps the cast's mastery context); both end clean. Stuns still write `e.stunT` directly (no Resolve, never bosses), as the base storm always did. The longer crater ends the storm with it (8s + 4.5s): `VAEL.on`, the light and the mastery preview's wait follow it.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: Living Current arcs into 1-2 foes for 60% (approved 1-3 for 70%), and The Storm Follows keeps within half a tile of the thickest foes instead of landing right on them. As approved, Living Current measured five times the old arcs' damage and took Mastery 2 to about 1.26 against 1.162 (a hidden factor near 0.92 would have made every strike and the final strike weaker than at Mastery 0); every strike still arcs, further and harder. On the ladder's standing foes (pushed back by every opening and final strike until they pile up), a storm centring exactly on the thickest foe shifted where the final strike's knockback fell and left the later casts about 12% weaker; keeping within half a tile is neutral there (within 1%) and a walking pack still stays inside. The Storm Follows is worth nothing on standing foes, so its factor carries it.
- Ladder (Mythic half steps, the standing pack and the lone foe, `SMW` .6 / .4; `ladder.js vael <reps> 6 13.6`): knockback piles make one run swing by about 3%, so the factors come from five runs pooled (240 to 470 casts a mode). Masteries 1 and 2 keep a factor of 1, so no part of a mastered storm strikes weaker than at Mastery 0.

| Mastery | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| Hidden factor `SMG.vael` | 1 | 1 | 1.015 | 1.078 | 1.004 |
| Without the factor (pooled) | 1.087 | 1.181 | 1.207 | 1.207 | 1.345 |
| With it (pooled) | 1.087 | 1.181 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.350 |
| With it (the last run, 8 x 6 casts) | 1.078 | 1.196 | 1.251 | 1.336 | 1.335 |

- Tests: `tests/vtm-test.js` (38 rows, in run-all). `vHit` is wrapped to note every hit of the storm with the foes in its reach and what each took, so every strike, arc, opening and final strike is checked exactly at every mastery (Mastery 0 too), with the final strike's knockback: the card and its numbers; `VAELD` is the old storm; Mastery 0 (400% opening, 12-31 strikes at 150%, arcs 82.5%, at most 10 foes in the network at 60% a tick, 900% within 2.6 with knockback 1.1, a 2.6s crater, the end at 10.6s, the light back); the bursts' counts against 3, 5, 7 and 10 and every strike landing, the light's floor and cap, the tip's sparks and doubled zaps; every strike on a foe arcing into 1-2 foes within 2.2 for 90%; every foe in the area in the network with four links from a node and 75% a tick to each once, the bigger star; on walking foes the re-centring at each burst and at 6s, the glide at up to 1.5 tiles a second, the final strike within half a tile of the foe it follows and taking more of the pack than without, the glow and the star gliding together, and (the pack moved off as the network forms) every bolt climbing to the star turned to end exactly at it; 1260% within 3.2 with knockback 1.4, the crater's ticks for 4.5s, the end at 12.5s; nothing left behind at any mastery; casts that are not a mastered scroll cast are the old storm; a Tempest Heart recast is a second mastered storm and both end clean; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.
- Looked at: contact sheets over the whole storm at the game's zoom with a crowd (Mastery 0 and 5), the staff tip close up (Mastery 0 and 1), the bursts and arcs (Mastery 1 and 2), the network (Mastery 2 and 3, 22 foes), a walking pack (Mastery 3 and 4, and the pack moved off as the network forms) and the final strike and crater (Mastery 4 and 5). The first sparks at the tip were too small to read even close up, so they are larger, faster and live a little longer. On a crowd of 30 the network's peak draws no more than Mastery 0's (about 1,100 draw calls, 60 effects).

## Abyssal Genesis: the eldest wakes (v459)
The hero sits on the anchor of the first sea while it floods the battlefield, crushing and slowly drowning every foe as leviathans hunt the roads; then one backwash throws the army back. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and then "new stuff CAN appear, for example the abyss having 3rd leviathan or something but just nothing that over shines the spell". So each mastery strengthens one part the sea already has and draws that same part richer. The only new piece is his own example, a third leviathan, and it is one of the spell's own leviathans: the eldest, larger and older. There is still exactly one sea and exactly one backwash.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Drowning Depths | the drowning | foes drown 1.6% of their max health a second (1.2% before; bosses 0.5%, 0.4% before) | a stream of bubbles rises off every foe (real bubbles, a bright rim and a glint, in their own particle pool) instead of a bubble now and then; the marine snow falls twice as thick |
| 2 The Weight of the Sea | the sea's slow | foes move 70% slower (60% before) | the surface stands at 2.7 tiles (2.2; the seated hero still rides above it), the light a deeper teal and the water over the field a little deeper, the caustics 1.5 times and the shafts of light 1.35 times as bright |
| 3 Leviathan's Hunger | the lunges | a lunge every 2.2s (2.6s before): 7 instead of 6, biting 650% (600%) within 1.35 tiles (1.26) | both leviathans 8% larger, their spots 30% larger, every eye and spot burning with a glow, twice the spray |
| 4 Blood in the Water | the lunges and the drowning | a foe a leviathan bites drowns 50% faster for 4s | a heavy column of bubbles spirals up off it while it does, and a burst of bubbles flies out of it at the bite |
| 5 The Third Leviathan | the hunt and the backwash | at the first lunge after 12s (halfway) the eldest rises and from then on takes every other lunge, biting 900%; the backwash strikes 1300% (1000%) | the eldest, hidden in the deep until then, bursts up out of the seabed beside its prey's road (a column of great bubbles, silt and stones thrown up, a low cloud of silt, a deep rumble); it is 1.2 times the size of the first two at Mastery 0 (a little larger than the two at Mastery 3), weathered grey, raked with claw scars, crusted with barnacles like the anchor, its spots a pale glow; at the end it swims back to the keep and rides the backwash up the field on the crest's lip, then dives; the crest stands taller (radius 2.4, 1.3 before; its strike 1.4 either side, 1.1 before) with foam pouring off its lip and churning at its foot |

- How: `castAbyss` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one sea (`XO`, over `ABYD`); without it the sea is exactly as before (`tests/ancient-test.js` and `tests/never-test.js` pass unchanged, with the Abyssal Genesis mastery pinned to none). `abmOpts` builds it from the mastery; `abmFx` builds the masteries' own pieces (the bubble pool, the glow round the eyes and spots, the eldest's scars and barnacles, its rise, the crest's foam, its ride) in the sea's root, so they are freed with it. The eldest is built by the sea's own `leviathan()` (the same geometry, a larger scale, its own skin, pale spots, the scars and barnacles merged into one draw per segment, a shadow from every other segment); it stays hidden in the deep until it rises. `ancPool` (the Ancient domains' particle pool) takes an optional fragment shader, for the bubbles; the other domains are unchanged.
- Never burying the foes: in every mastered sea a leviathan's body segment that lies over foes (as the camera sees it, at the foes' height) thins to about half and casts no shadow, so the foes and their health bars read through it; the head stays solid while it lunges. Each segment has its own copy of its materials for this (Mastery 0 is untouched). The first cut had the two 15% larger and the eldest 1.32 times; with three serpents over a thick crowd the middle of the field went dark, so they are 8% and 1.2 times now. Size and fade are looks only: reach, damage and timing are unchanged.
- A recast while the sea is up extends it, as before; the running sea keeps the masteries it was cast with.
- The backwash's push is unchanged (3 tiles, a boss 1, capped by Resolve), and the eldest never does damage outside its bites: there is no second wave and no damaging shadow.
- Numbers: built as approved (1.6% drowning; a lunge every 2s at 700% within 1.45; twice as fast for 4s; a third leviathan with lunges of its own at 900%; a 1500% backwash), the sea measured 1.05 / 1.05 / 1.51 / 1.69 / 2.26 on the ladder against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. Every mechanic is kept and the numbers trimmed: a lunge every 2.2s (7, not 8) at 650% within 1.35; 50% faster drowning (not twice as fast) for 4s; the eldest takes every other lunge instead of adding its own; a 1300% backwash. On the ladder's standing foes a cast is about 700 of drowning, 2870 of bites and 1000 of backwash on each foe of the pack; The Weight of the Sea (a slow) counts for nothing there, so its factor carries it. On the Ancient ladder (pack .6 / one .4, 3 casts 27s apart, 2 runs) the hidden factors `SMG.abyss` = 1.03 / 1.132 / 1.018 / 1.01 / 0.945 bring it to 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.349, against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. With its factor nothing in a mastered sea hits weaker than at Mastery 0 (at Mastery 5 a bite lands 614%, the eldest's 851%, the backwash 1229%).
- Tests: `tests/abm-test.js` (41 rows, in run-all): the card and its numbers, names used nowhere else; the defaults table is the old sea (six lunges 2.6s apart in turn, 600% within 1.26, a 1000% backwash once on every foe, 0.6% / 0.2% a tick); Mastery 0 unchanged (the same hits as the defaults, 60% slow, nothing left behind); 0.8% / 0.25% a tick and the bubble pool at Mastery 1 (none at Mastery 0); 70% slow, the sea at 2.7 and the deeper light at Mastery 2; seven lunges 2.2s apart, every bite 650%, within 1.35 and some beyond 1.26, the glow layer at Mastery 3 (six lunges at 600% at Mastery 2); at Mastery 4 every drowning tick of a foe bitten within the last 4s is 1.2%, every other 0.8%, and the foe no leviathan reaches never bleeds; at Mastery 5 the eldest hidden until it bursts up at the first lunge after 12s, every other lunge after that, its bites 900% and the others 650%, one 1300% backwash on every foe, the eldest riding the crest up the field, the bodies thinning over the foes with the eldest's head solid in its lunges, nothing left behind (no eldest and a 1000% backwash at Mastery 4); a recast keeps its masteries; other casters cast the old sea; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.

## Citadel of Orders: the Monarch's orders (v460)
The Time Monarch stands unmoving in a ring of golden light pillars; inside it time crawls for his foes while he throws single cuts through space, ever faster, and he ends with one final order: a fracture that snaps shut. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". The Citadel had no masteries before (Simon had kept Ancients and Secrets out of the generic sets); these each strengthen one part the domain already has and draw that same part richer. Still one domain, one cut at a time and one fracture: no "time catches up" burst (Chronostasis), no shards (Spatial Shatter), no grey stillness (Dimensional Slash).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Wider Dominion | its reach | the domain reaches 6 tiles (5.2 before), a third more ground; the crawl's reach (`CIT.R`) follows | the pillars rise a fifth taller (8.4-13.8) and 15% brighter (`uI`), still 10-12 of them (the warp has twelve pillar slots); the gold glow and haze widen with the ring |
| 2 Keener Orders | the cuts | each cut strikes 240% on its mark and 120% in its wake (200% and 100% before) | its wound bends space 1.3 times deeper, with a 1.3 times deeper quake; its seam is shown, with a white-hot core and a cold glow round it |
| 3 Time Crawls | the crawl | foes inside move at 40% (50% before), down to 5% at the end (10%); the opening slow lasts 4s (2.5s); `CIT.pf` keeps its old curve, so tower and hero shots inside are not slowed more | twice the afterimages (a pool of 28, up to 8 a beat: 14 and 4 before), living 0.6s (0.4s), in a brighter gold |
| 4 Edict | where the cuts land | each cut aims at the foe nearest the keep that no cut has aimed at yet (once every foe inside has been, the orders start again from the front) and throws it 0.6 tiles back (0.25; not bosses), never out of the domain | a larger quake of space on its mark (x1.4) |
| 5 The Final Order | the fracture | it strikes 1200% within 2 tiles of its main crack and 600% elsewhere in the domain (800% and 400%), and throws foes 1 tile back (0.55; not bosses) | its main crack is longer (R x2.2, 1.85 before), its two branches wider (0.45, 0.3) and deeper (1.1, 0.8); the pillars rise into a brighter column of light (x1.5); still one fracture |

- How: `castCitadel` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `CITD`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `cimOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`CIM`) wraps `castCitadel` by name, as v447/v448 did. The changes inside the base cast are small: the reach, the pillars' height and light, a cut's strike, throw, depth and quake, the Edict's aim and throw, the crawl and the opening slow, the afterimages, the fracture's strike, length, branches and throw, the pillars' last light, and a log for the tests.
- Seen, not hidden: the base cast makes a seam strip for every cut (`CIT_STRIP_FS`) and never shows it: the mesh is hidden when it is made and never switched on, so Keener Orders' "whiter cores in the strip" would have strengthened a look nobody has seen. It is shown at Mastery 2 and up through an opt-in option (`strip`), with its own shader (`CIM_STRIP_FS`); Mastery 0 and 1 keep it hidden. It is the cut's own seam, a thin line of light across the wound's front, seen almost edge-on from the game's camera, so it reads as a white edge in the bent space rather than a new object.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: none. One rule added: the Edict's throw stops at the domain's edge. Built as written, each cut threw a lone foe 0.6 tiles out of the domain, where no cut could follow it and time no longer crawled; on the ladder's lone foe Mastery 4 struck 0.59 times as hard as Mastery 0 (Mastery 3: 1.31). Now the throw goes back as far as 0.6 tiles while the foe stays inside, so the Edict holds the front line at the back of the domain instead of letting it walk in at full speed.
- Ladder (Ancient, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 20s apart), calibrated with the foes put back where they stood before every cast. In play the cooldown is 150s, so every cast meets fresh foes. The standard ladder (`ladder.js citadel 4 3 20 pack:.6,one:.4`) casts again at foes that never walk, and the throws skew its lone foe: the Edict holds it at the domain's edge, the fracture throws it out, and it is not there for the next cast, while a wider domain keeps one the old cast would lose. So the standard ladder reads Masteries 1-3 high and 4-5 low. The put-back measure is `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kingsroad-keep/5a683175-73c8-5b6a-8b5f-1cd5ede13798/scratchpad/anc-citadel/ladder-fresh.js` (the kit's `_ladder-modes.js`, with every foe returned to its spot and health before each cast), evaluated on the dev page. Time Crawls (a slow) and Edict (aim and throws) count for nothing on either, so their factors carry them.

| `SMG.citadel` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 1.081 | 1.029 | 1.085 | 1.152 | 1.077 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| foes put back before every cast (3 runs) | 1.076 | 1.164 | 1.232 | 1.299 | 1.353 |
| standard ladder (4 runs) | 1.145 | 1.227 | 1.292 | 1.25 | 1.277 |

- Tests: `tests/cim-test.js` (38 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers, that the defaults table is the old cast and that a cast with only a log is the old cast (the opening strike, every cut's 200% and 100% as it lands, the 0.25 throw, the fracture's 800% and 400% by where each foe stood against its main crack, the crawl curve, the seam never shown, nothing left behind), Mastery 0 unchanged, and each mastery's numbers exactly: the reach of 6 tiles (foes between 5.2 and 6 struck, those past 6 not), the taller pillars and wider glow; 240% and 120% cuts, the deeper wound and quake, the seam shown with its shader; the 40% / 5% crawl with `CIT.pf` on its old curve, the 4s slow and the 28 afterimages (walking foes leave up to 8 a beat and more at once than the old pool held); every Edict aim against the rule, the 0.6 throw that stops at the domain's edge, a boss not thrown, the larger quake; the 1200% / 600% fracture, its 1-tile throw, its longer crack and wider branches, still one fracture, nothing left behind; echoes cast the old spell, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery, with its factor in the opening strike.

## Infinite Void: reality, erased (v461)
The hero erases the battlefield: the world dissolves into an endless starry void, every foe crawls at a fifth of its speed, and blinding white cuts tear through everything until reality snaps back. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". These are the first masteries of an Ancient scroll; each strengthens one part the domain already has and draws that same part richer. The void and its timing stay as they are (the strain 0-4s, the snap at 4s, the cuts until 23.55s, the snap back at 24s); nothing scales with distance from the hero (that is Crimson Purgatory) and nothing stills the world grey (Dimensional Slash).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Blinding Edge | every cut | a cut on a foe strikes 88% + 2.2% of its max health (81% + 2.03% before; bosses 1.1%, 1.01% before); a ground cut 66% (60%) + 1% within half a tile of its line (0.42) | every stroke a fifth wider, with a whiter core (the strokes' core exponent 4, 6 before) |
| 2 Ceaseless Cuts | the stream of cuts | the focused stream brings 4.4 cuts a second and 0.33 more for each foe, up to 11 (4 and 0.3, up to 10): a tenth more | more of the same strokes |
| 3 The Void Holds | the crawl | every foe crawls at 15% of its speed (`DOM.f` 0.15, 0.2 before; bosses too, as the base domain) | the motes of dense space round each foe come 1.5x as often, a quarter larger and brighter |
| 4 Erasure | the cuts | a non-boss foe a cut (on a foe, on the ground, or the last cut) leaves under 15% of its health is erased: a killing blow | its cross of strokes twice as large; it dissolves in about half a second behind the same white-rimmed edge the world dissolved behind, white motes lifting off it where the edge passes |
| 5 Reality Returns | the snap back | in the last 3s a great screen-wide cut every 0.8s (x0.85-1.15), each bringing three cuts; as reality returns (0.12s before the snap) one last cut strikes every foe for 300%, bosses too (the snap back struck nothing before) | the great cuts are the base big cut's screen-wide stroke and distortion, flaring the light instead of blinking the silhouette (so the finale does not strobe); the last cut is the same stroke; the pinch before the snap 0.6s (0.35s) and the snap's ripple, colour split, shake and burst of light 30% stronger |

- How: `castExpanse` takes an optional set of numbers and looks (`XO`, over `EXD`, the old numbers); without it the domain is exactly as before (`tests/v354-test.js` and `tests/arena-test.js` pass unchanged). `ivmOpts` builds it from the mastery and `castExpanse` is wrapped once (`IVM_OLD`). The renewal (a recast while the domain holds) keeps the masteries the domain was cast with and cuts again as the renewed domain ends.
- Erasure's dissolve: the erased foe's own meshes are kept a moment under the domain's root and wear clones of their materials that discard behind a front racing out from the cut, its rim white-hot (the world dissolve's colour). One clone per material a cast (made once, outside the artifact sets' colour context so a foe keeps its colours), sharing one array of up to 8 fronts; glows and rings simply hide; with all 8 fronts busy a foe just goes. Its materials are its own again after.
- Cost: the strokes are now pooled (the base cast built a geometry and a shader material for every stroke, about 25 a second; now one of the same length and layer is reused), so more cuts allocate nothing once the pool has warmed up; the pool is freed with the domain. The base big cut's three cuts still come on timers; the finale's come on the domain's own clock (game time).
- Name: the approved table called Mastery 3 "Denser Space", too close to Spatial Compression's "Dense Space" (also a 15% slow), so it is The Void Holds.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: the cuts strike 88% + 2.2% (approved 90% + 2.25%; bosses 1.1%, approved 1.12%), and Ceaseless Cuts brings a tenth more cuts (4.4 + 0.33 a foe, up to 11; approved 5 + 0.4, up to 13, about 30% more). Built as approved the domain measured 1.11 / 1.384 / 1.37 / 1.361 / 1.559 against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35: the cuts are the whole of its damage, so 30% more of them was 25% more domain. Reality Returns: the great cuts flare the light rather than blinking the silhouette (the base big cut's 75ms inverted frame, every 0.8s, strobed), and the snap's screen flash is not made stronger (it is full white already at Mastery 0); the 30% went into the ripple, the colour split, the shake and the burst.
- The hidden factor scales the whole cut: on the ladder about half of a cut is its share of max health, which smP never scaled, so `ivmOpts` gives the max-health shares the factor too.
- Ladder (Ancient half steps, the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4; `ladder.js expanse <reps> 3 28`, three casts 28s apart; three runs pooled, 8 reps a mode). The Void Holds and Erasure count for nothing on standing foes that never drop under 15%, so their factors carry them. Masteries 1, 2 and 5 keep a factor of 1.

| Mastery | 1 | 2 | 3 | 4 | 5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| Hidden factor `SMG.expanse` | 1 | 1 | 1.035 | 1.105 | 1 |
| Without the factor (pooled) | 1.089 | 1.178 | 1.187 | 1.180 | 1.354 |
| With it (pooled) | 1.089 | 1.178 | 1.229 | 1.304 | 1.354 |
| The last run (1.04 / 1.105 / 0.99, 3 reps) | 1.087 | 1.176 | 1.241 | 1.315 | 1.332 |

- Tests: `tests/ivm-test.js` (32 rows, in run-all). `hurt` is wrapped to log every cut's share of the power, its share of max health and any erasure, and the domain's events say which cut each belongs to, so every cut is checked exactly at every mastery: the card and its numbers; `EXD` is the old domain; Mastery 0 (81% / 60% and 2.03% / 1%, bosses 1.01% / 0.5%; nobody erased, nothing at the snap back; a fifth of the speed; the strokes' core and widths; the 0.35s pinch); 88% + 2.2% on every cut and the boss share, every ground cut 66% on exactly the foes within half a tile of its line, the strokes a fifth wider with core 4; the stream's count over the held domain at Mastery 1 and 2 and its cap with 24 foes; a walking pack covering three quarters of the ground at Mastery 3, the denser motes, nobody erased yet; every non-boss foe left under 15% erased by a killing blow, never a boss, the cross twice as large, the foe dissolving under the domain's root for half a second and then gone with its own materials back; the great cuts' timing, their three cuts on game time, the last cut on every foe once for 300% bosses too, the longer pinch, the stronger ripple; nothing left behind at any mastery; a renewed domain cutting once as it ends; casts that are not a mastered scroll cast are the old domain; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.
- Looked at: contact sheets over the whole domain at the game's zoom with a crowd (Mastery 0, 4 and 5: the strain, the snap, the held cuts, the finale's great cuts, the pinch, the last cut and the snap back), the held cuts close up (Mastery 0, 1 and 3) and an erasure frame by frame close up (Mastery 4, one foe and a crowd). The first dissolve was over in a tenth of a second and read as a blink, so its front is sized to the body and runs over 0.6s; the finale's first great cuts strobed (the silhouette blink every 0.8s), so they flare the light instead.
- Seen while testing, not changed: since v427 the ground round the board (`GLD.ground`) carries `voidKeep`, so the Infinite Void dissolves the board and its props but leaves the grass round it (v354 had the land round the board dissolve too). Mastery 0 is left exactly as it is.

- **The land round the board dissolves too (a fix, at every mastery).** Since v427 the ground round the board (`GLD.ground`) carried `voidKeep`, so the void swallowed the board but left the grass and woods round it. It no longer carries it: the void now fills the whole view, and everything comes back when the void ends (v354, ivm, ars and arena tests pass).

## The Eldritch Tree: the world in bloom (v462)
A tree older than the stars tears out of the earth beside the field; its roots run under every road and burst up under the thickest knots of foes to bind them and drink their life, and at the end it blooms over the whole field and mends the keep. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". Each mastery strengthens one part the Tree already has and draws that same part richer. Still one trunk and one bloom: no other trees (Wrath of Gaia, Oakheart Treant), no Overgrown and no new kind of hold (Entangling Roots), no lingering pollen cloud (Hundred-Year Bloom).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Long Roots | each eruption's reach | an eruption strikes every foe within 1.65 tiles of its knot (1.35 before) | 8 root spikes (6), a fifth taller, set out wider round a wider ring |
| 2 Thirst | the drink | the roots drink 2% of each foe's max health (1%; bosses 0.7%, 0.4% before) | twice the motes of life fly to the trunk (12 a foe); as each draught reaches it the sap veins flash and a bright bead of sap climbs them; the veins a little bolder |
| 3 Restless Roots | how often they burst | an eruption every 0.45s (0.6s): about 31 instead of 24 when fresh foes are at hand; a foe may be struck again after 3s (4s); the roots hold a foe only while its Resolve allows the whole hold | more of the same eruptions |
| 4 Swallowed | the roots | a foe (not a boss) the roots leave under 20% of its health is dragged under the earth and dies there | the spikes clench round it (their own curl, closing) and sink with it; a puff of soil, and its last life flies to the trunk |
| 5 The World in Bloom | the bloom | it strikes 1100% (900%), and 3% more for every foe the roots drank from this cast, up to 45% more; still 3 lives | the veins glow brighter as the Tree drinks (up to 1.8x at 15 foes) and a blazing surge of sap climbs the trunk as it blooms; twice the blossoms, now five cupped petals round a gold heart in the old three pinks, in one instanced draw (the old cast draws about 130 meshes); a thicker, taller wave of pollen |

- How: `castFirstTree` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `TRD`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `etmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`ETM`) wraps `castFirstTree` by name, as v447/v448 did; `NSP.firsttree` calls it by name, and Wrath of Gaia's World Root still reads `ANC.live.firsttree`. Inside the base cast: the reach, the drink, the gap and the lockout, the Resolve rule, Swallowed, the bloom's strike and bonus, the spikes, motes and veins, the blossoms, the pollen wave, the particle pool, and a log for the tests. A recast while the Tree stands extends it, as before, keeping the standing Tree's masteries.
- A health bar is hidden while its foe is dragged under (`e.swal`), in the old bars and in the batched bars of v446 (one token in its `updBar`).
- Resolve (a rule added at Mastery 3). Built as written, Restless Roots' extra eruptions spent every foe's Resolve, so the foes were Unstoppable when the Tree bloomed and the bloom's hold did nothing: measured on 16 foes, the bloom held each 0.8s at Mastery 0 and 0s from Mastery 3, and foes spent about 9.8s of each cast Unstoppable. From Mastery 3 the roots hold a foe only while its Resolve can take the whole hold: the time held under the roots stays about 4s a foe, no foe is made Unstoppable by them (so the player's other holds keep working), and the bloom holds every foe its full 1.5s.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: the bloom strikes 1100%, not 1350%. Built as written, Mastery 5 measured 1.525 against 1.35, which would need a factor of 0.872 (below the 0.9-1.1 band, with every eruption 13% weaker than at Mastery 0); at 1100% (the 3% a foe, up to 45%, kept) the factor is 0.952. Every other number is as approved.
- Ladder (Ancient, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 26s apart, 3 runs). Calibrated with the foes put back where they stood and at full health before every cast (`/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kingsroad-keep/5a683175-73c8-5b6a-8b5f-1cd5ede13798/scratchpad/anc-tree/ladder-fresh.js`, Citadel's script with Resolve and burrowing reset too): in play the cooldown is 150s, so every cast meets fresh foes. The standard ladder (`ladder.js firsttree 3 3 26 pack:.6,one:.4`) reads exactly the same: the Tree pushes and throws no foe, and the ladder's foes never fall under 20%. Long Roots (the 4s lockout keeps each foe to one strike in four seconds however far the roots reach) and Swallowed (an execute) count for nothing on either, so their factors carry them. The drink is a share of max health, not scaled by the factor.

|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 1.083 | 1.108 | 0.987 | 1.056 | 0.952 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
