- Tests: `tests/etm-test.js` (37 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers, the names against every scroll, mastery and synergy, that the defaults table is the old Tree and that a cast with only a log is the old Tree (400% strikes within 1.35, each foe once every 4s, a 1% / 0.4% drink, 1.2s / 0.4s holds, a 900% bloom once on every foe), Mastery 0 unchanged (the same damage to every foe, the old vein shader, 6 spikes, 10 blossom meshes on each root, +3 lives, nothing left behind), and each mastery exactly: strikes landing between 1.35 and 1.65 and none further, 8 spikes a fifth taller round a wider ring; 2% and 0.7% draughts, 12 motes, the veins' own shader and its draughts; eruptions 0.45s apart (24 become 31 on a field with fresh foes), re-strikes after 3s and never sooner, no foe made Unstoppable by the roots and the bloom's full 1.5s hold on every foe (while at Mastery 2 the roots still spend Resolve as before); exactly the foes left under 20% swallowed (not a foe at 50%, not a boss), each sinking, held, passed over and without a health bar until it dies 0.9s later, nothing left burrowed; the 1100% bloom with its bonus (36% at 12 foes, capped at 45% with 24), 260 blossoms in one instanced draw with the old colours, the veins' glow and surge, nothing left behind; a recast extending a mastered Tree, casts that are not a mastered scroll cast, Wrath of Gaia still finding the Tree, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery. `ancient-test` and `never-test` check the Tree itself whatever mastery the dev account has earned.

## Never-Never Land: the hour that is the hero's (v463)
Sylph's dome of night sky over the whole field, where the hero owns time: time stops, then crawls; every foe takes more and the towers fight faster; stars fall on the leaders, a whip of four stars lashes the thickest crowd, and at the end every star falls. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". Never-Never Land had no masteries before; these each strengthen one part the domain already has and draw that same part richer. The stillness stays map-wide (a zone of stopped time is Chronostasis) and the constellations stay a picture (stars joined into a figure that fall as lances are the secret scroll Constellation).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Falling Stars | the shooting stars | a star every 0.36s (0.45s before): about 39 in a cast (31), splashing 0.6 tiles (0.5) | heads, halos and tails a quarter larger; a bigger glint where they strike (2.0, 1.6 before) |
| 2 The Stopped Hour | the stillness | time stands still 2.6s (1.8s), bosses at 15% (25%); foes held in it take 50% more from everything (25%), so the first lash (it snaps at 5.3s) lands inside it | while it holds, the stars stop twinkling, Sylph's wind stands still (the sky's own clock stops and goes on from there) and the dome's meridians glow, thin lines of light that fade as time moves again |
| 3 Lash of Stars | the whip | a lash every 2.8s (3.5s): 5 lashes (4), over a wider square (radius 1.6, 1.35 before), holding foes 0.8s (0.6s; bosses 0.2s as before, Resolve applies) | a wider ribbon (0.15, 0.11 before), a whiter snap (x1.5) with sparks along its longer sides, larger glints at the corners (1.5, 1.1) |
| 4 Sylph's Hour | the crawl and the towers | foes 50% slower (40%; bosses 25%, 20% before); towers attack 35% faster (25%) | the sky wheels faster (0.06 a second, 0.035 before); Sylph's wings beat faster |
| 5 Rain of Stars | the finale | the last fall strikes 1300% (1000%), and the great stars that fall in it, only a picture before (on real-time timers), land for real in game time: up to 36 (18 before), two a foe, 220% each, all before the last strike | the dome's falling stars swell larger (2.4x, 1.4x before); the great stars' glints are bigger (3, 2.4) |

- How: `castNever` takes an optional set of numbers (`XO`, over `NVR`, whose new keys default to the old cast); without it the cast is exactly as before. `nvmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`NVM`) wraps `castNever` by name, after the two older wrappers (the violet remap and the night under artifact sets), as v447/v448 did. The cast's numbers live on the cast (`NVR.S.K`), where the stillness and the crawl (`ancF`), the damage taken (`hurt`) and the towers (`tStats`) read them; with no masteries `NVR.S.K` is `NVR` itself.
- Pooled: a base glint clones a material for every star that strikes and keeps it to the end. Mastered casts take their glints from a pool of 32 made with the cast (so they turn violet with it too) and light the oldest again when all are lit.
- Seen, not hidden: built as written, Rain of Stars piled 28-36 great-star glints of size 3 on one crowd within half a second, a white sheet over the foes. Now where several land together their glints share the light (each is dimmed by the others lit within 1.6 tiles), a great star's halo draws in as it strikes, and its sparks are 12 (18 for the old picture). The meridian glow was first far too strong (a white cage over the board); it is now thin lines that glow at the start of the stillness and settle lower.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: none. Two names were already taken and were changed: Stolen Time is Chronostasis' fourth mastery (here Sylph's Hour: Borrowed Hours, tried first, sat too close to the Aeon Dial's Borrowed Years, also about towers fighting faster), and Starfall is the world event (here Rain of Stars). One rule added: the great stars fall faster when there are many (at most 0.055s apart, all within 0.6s), so all of them land before the last strike at 18.05s; they strike only the foe they fall on (no splash).
- Not seen by the ladder, worth remembering: the share of damage taken (25%, 50% in the stillness) and the towers' speed lift every other spell and tower on the field while the dome is up, and Masteries 2 and 4 make them larger.
- Ladder (Ancient, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 22s apart), calibrated with the foes put back where they stood before every cast (`ladder-fresh`): in play the cooldown is 160s, so every cast meets fresh foes. Nothing here throws or kills a ladder foe, so the standard ladder (`ladder.js never 4 3 22 pack:.6,one:.4`) reads the same. The stillness, the crawl and the towers count for nothing on the ladder (its foes stand still and it has no towers); the factors carry them.

| `SMG.never` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 0.954 | 0.999 | 0.962 | 1.022 | 0.951 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| foes put back before every cast (4 runs) | 1.078 | 1.168 | 1.231 | 1.308 | 1.357 |
| standard ladder (4 runs) | 1.076 | 1.164 | 1.23 | 1.306 | 1.356 |
| without the factors (all 1) | 1.13 | 1.163 | 1.274 | 1.274 | 1.42 |

- Tests: `tests/nvm-test.js` (41 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers, that the defaults table is the old cast and that a cast with only a log is the old cast (the stillness and the crawl, 25% more taken, about 31 stars with 220% on their foe and 100% within 0.5 tiles and nowhere else, 4 lashes with 500% to exactly the foes in their square, held 0.6s, the last fall 1000%, the great stars striking nothing, the sky's clock running, a glint material for each glint, nothing left behind), Mastery 0 unchanged, and each mastery's numbers exactly: 38 stars splashing their neighbours 0.55 tiles away, larger shots and glints, a pool of 32 glint materials and no more; a 2.6s stillness (bosses 15%), 150 taken from 100 in it and 125 after, the first lash inside it, the stars' and the wind's clocks and the wind's turn stopped while it holds, the meridians glowing; 5 lashes at 5.3, 8.1, 10.9, 13.7 and 16.5s over the wider square, held 0.8s (a boss 0.2s), the wider ribbon and larger corner glints; crawl 50% (bosses 25%), a tower 1.35 times faster, the sky 0.06 a second; the last fall 1300%, 36 great stars over 24 foes (10 over 5) each 220% on its foe, all landed before the last strike while the world is stepped (game time), the swell and the glints. Also a recast winding the domain back with its masteries kept, an echo cast as the old cast, the glint pool turning violet, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery. `tests/never-test.js` now pins Mastery 0 (its checks are of the domain itself).

## The Primordial Tempest: the hurricane (v465)
The hero is lifted into the eye of the first storm and the storm chooses: rune reticles lock onto the most dangerous foes and purple bolts land exactly there, in volleys that grow and quicken, each leaping on to the next charged foe; at the end every foe is locked at once and the hero comes down on one last bolt. Simon: "for the tempest spell make it feel like there's an actual storm like a hurricane around the hero at a greater hight and around him so it actually feels so incredibly powerful", and for the base cast: "yes upgrade the base tempest storm too". So the storm is now a hurricane at every mastery (its numbers unchanged), and each mastery strengthens one part the storm already has while the hurricane grows round the hero. Simon approved the table.

The base storm (Mastery 0, looks only):
- The hero rises to 2.2 tiles (1.55 before). The eye wall towers about 5 tiles (under 3 before), its inner face leaning out as it rises, like a stadium seen from above: billows of storm cloud (one instanced draw) over a cone of cloud along the inner face (drawn by its shader), lit from inside where the lightning runs.
- The wall is cut away on the camera's side, so the hero, the road and the foes stay in view; it reads from the far and side walls.
- Three spiral rain bands reach out over the ground to 8 tiles: a thin wet shadow with rain streaming out along it, rain curtains, and thin see-through arms of cloud over them. A faint outflow of cirrus wheels the other way high above.
- Everything that moves with the wind moves in its shader (one clock and one wind angle a frame, each puff's constants as attributes), so the bigger storm costs the CPU nothing more: the eye's frame stays well under 1 ms (`tests/tpeye-test.js`). Only the lightning (arcs, crawlers, strikes) and the wind streaks are written each frame.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | The storm grows |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Crown of Thunder | the opening | the crown bolt strikes every foe for 300% (200% before); Charged lasts 6s (4s) | the hero lifted to 2.8; the wall 6.2 tall (5) |
| 2 The Storm Chooses | the volleys | one more foe in every volley: 3 rising to 8 (2 rising to 7) | the rain bands sweep out from the wall across the whole field (4 bands to 22 tiles; 3 to 8); the wall thicker |
| 3 Leaping Bolts | the leap | each strike leaps on for 50% (35%), up to 3 tiles (2.2), on a thicker bolt (0.85, 0.6) | lightning crawls through the wall (more arcs, longer) and between the rain bands (jagged spider lightning along them) |
| 4 The Eye Wall | the wall's lightning | the arcs racing round the hero through the wall strike the foes they pass beneath: 80% and Charged, each foe at most once every 0.6s (before: only a picture) | each strike drops a bolt from the arc to the foe and the arc flares, sparks thrown out in every direction; debris (rocks and splinters) whirls round in the wind |
| 5 Hurricane | the last thunder and the fall | the last thunder 1300% (1000%); the fall rolls out over the whole field like the crown: 300% to every foe (only within 2.6 tiles before) | the full hurricane: the hero high in its eye (4), the wall towering out of view (10.5), the outflow across the sky; wider last bolts (2.4, 1.9); the fall's ground bolts reach across the field (28 at 4-10 tiles; 18 at 2.5-6) and a ring races out with its wave |

- How: `castTempest` takes an optional set of numbers and looks (`XO`, over `TPD`); without it the storm's gameplay is exactly as before (`tests/tempest-test.js` passes unchanged, with the Tempest's mastery pinned to none). `tpmOpts` builds it from the mastery. The hurricane is `tpEye`, which reads its shape from the same options. The Eye Wall only looks at foes within a few tiles of the hero, a few times a second, so the eye stays cheap.
- A recast while the storm rages winds it back, as before; the running storm keeps the masteries it was cast with.
- Numbers: the approved ones. Measured raw (no factor) the set came out under the ladder (1.019 / 1.081 / 1.131 / 1.225 / 1.262), so nothing was trimmed. On the Ancient half-step ladder (pack .6 / one .4, 3 runs of 3 casts 20s apart; its foes stand by the hero, so the fall reaching the whole field counts for nothing there) the hidden factors `SMG.tempest` = 1.06 / 1.077 / 1.086 / 1.065 / 1.072 bring it to 1.079 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.309 / 1.351, against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35.
- Tests: `tests/tpm-test.js` (36 rows, in run-all): the card and its numbers; the defaults table is the old storm (the same volleys as a cast without options); Mastery 0 unchanged (200% crown, Charged 4s, 450% strikes leaping for 35%, 1000% to every foe, 300% within 2.6 tiles) with the hero at 2.2 and the 5-tile wall; 300% and Charged 6s at Mastery 1; volleys of 3 rising to 8 and the bands to 22 tiles at Mastery 2; 50% leaps reaching 3 tiles (foes 2.6 apart: none at Mastery 0) and the crawlers at Mastery 3; wall strikes of 80%, at most once a foe every 0.6s, only near the hero, Charging the foe, at Mastery 4 (none at Mastery 3); 1300% and the fall reaching a foe 9 tiles off at Mastery 5 (not at Mastery 4); nothing left behind; the full hurricane's eye under 1 ms; a recast keeps its masteries; other casters cast the old storm; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery. `tests/tpeye-test.js` checks the new hurricane (38 rows).

## Crimson Purgatory: the judgment falls (v464)
The world turns crimson round the hero as judge. Six eyes watch, every foe moves at half speed, and invisible blades cut every foe, more often and harder the closer it stands to the hero, building in stages to one frozen, silent instant. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". Crimson Purgatory had no masteries before; these each strengthen one part the domain already has and draw that same part richer. Its cuts stay crimson and tied to the distance to the hero (Infinite Void's are white and everywhere), and its execution is tied to closeness (Dimensional Slash and Piercing Judgment keep theirs).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Closer Still | the closeness | closeness to the hero falls off over 3 tiles (2.8 before): about 10% more to a foe 3-4 tiles away (more cuts, each harder) | the aura's rings, shards, pulses and cracks reach x3/2.8 further, its glow 2 tiles (1.7) |
| 2 Swifter Judgment | the stages | the last stage begins at 13s (14s) and cuts 7% faster (1.6 x (0.35 + 2.8k) a second, 1.5 before) | the eyes swell more in it (0.09, 0.06 before); the red deepens, the thorns surge and the third stare come with it; the second stare moves to 10.6s (11.2s) so the stares stay spread; the hero's judgment pose follows the stages |
| 3 The Watching Eyes | the eyes' four stares (until now only a picture) | foes within 1.5 tiles of where the eyes look (the densest foe) are cut as if they stood 2.75 tiles from the hero (closeness at least exp(-2.75/3) = 0.4) | their cuts are drawn with the large blades of the foes beside the hero; the eyes flare and sparks run along their links |
| 4 Execution | the blades | a cut that leaves a non-boss foe under 8% + 12% x closeness of its health (20% beside the hero) cuts it down; a void knight still rises once | its crossing blade flares larger (a wide cross and a lingering trail), its scorch mark 1.7 times larger with a red-hot glow spreading under it, embers thrown out |
| 5 The Last Judgment | the silence | the silence holds 0.8s (0.32s; still map-wide, bosses too: 0.8s is the cap), then every foe takes one last cut, 150% x (1 + 2.2 x closeness): 150% far away, up to 480% beside the hero; Execution rides it | the six eyes open wide (their lids 1.45 times open) as blades cross on the forty nearest foes, in order from the hero; sparks burst on the rest |

- How: `castPurgatory` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `PURGD`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `pgmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`PGM`) wraps `castPurgatory` by name, as v447/v448 did. The changes inside the base cast are small: the falloff, the stage times and rates, the second stare, the silence and the end, a stare's closeness for the foes near where the eyes look, the execution check after a cut, the last cut, the aura's reach and glow, the eyes' swell, flare and wide-open look, and a log for the tests.
- Pooled: the base cast builds a new geometry and shader material for every blade (`mkSlash`). The executions and the last cut (up to 80 blades at once) instead use one mesh of 128 blades (48 at Mastery 4) with the same ribbon and shader look, each blade timed on the GPU by its own start, so they add one draw call and make nothing per blade; the execution glows are a pool of ten. They are drawn over the thorns: the base cast's blades sit among the thorns, and the last cut's crosses were lost in them.
- The pose: the hero's judgment pose was fixed at 15s-18.5s of the gesture (the cast arrives 1s after it starts). It now follows the stages through `PURG.pose` (14s at Mastery 2 and up; to 18.98s at Mastery 5), the same 15s-18.5s without masteries.
- Numbers changed from the approved table (built as written, the set measured 1.196 / 1.389 / 1.55 / 1.559 / 1.857 against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35 before any factor, so every mechanic is kept and its numbers trimmed): the falloff 3 tiles (3.2 approved); the last stage at 13s and 1.6 (12.5s and 1.7); the stare's closeness as at 2.75 tiles (about 2 tiles, 0.5); the last cut 150% (300%). Kept as approved: Execution (8% + 12% x closeness), the stares' 1.5-tile reach and the 0.8s silence. The silence holds 0.8s: it ends 0.48s later (the domain lasts 20.28s at Mastery 5), so the last stage keeps its full length and the fourth stare its cuts. One rule added: the frame the silence breaks in holds only the last cut (the base cast still cuts once in that frame).
- Ladder (Ancient, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 23s apart), calibrated with the foes put back where they stood before every cast (in play the cooldown is 120s, so every cast meets fresh foes). The cuts depend on the distance to the hero, so the foes stand at sensible distances: the pack of 12 on the road 2.6-3.4 tiles from him, the lone foe 3.2 tiles away. The measure is `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kingsroad-keep/5a683175-73c8-5b6a-8b5f-1cd5ede13798/scratchpad/anc-purgatory/ladder-purg.js` (Citadel's put-back ladder with those placements; `window.__smoneK` runs the lone foe more often, its count of cuts being noisier), evaluated on the dev page. The standard ladder (`ladder.js purgatory 3 3 23 pack:.6,one:.4`) stands its foes 0.8-2.5 tiles from the hero (the lone foe 1.4), where a longer reach or a stare adds little, so it reads lower. Execution and the longer silence count for nothing on either, so their factors carry them.

| `SMG.purgatory` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 0.99 | 0.975 | 0.99 | 1.051 | 0.981 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| foes put back before every cast, 2.6-3.4 tiles (4 runs, the lone foe 12) | 1.078 | 1.158 | 1.225 | 1.292 | 1.353 |
| standard ladder, 0.8-2.5 tiles (3 runs) | 1.034 | 1.114 | 1.129 | 1.196 | 1.203 |

- Tests: `tests/pgm-test.js` (37 rows, in run-all) steps the cut model frame by frame exactly as the cast does (the stages, the stares, the silence) and checks every cut against it: each foe's count within one cut, each cut's closeness and strike, its max-HP part, nothing else struck. It checks the card and its numbers, that the defaults table is the old cast and that a cast with only a log is the old cast (the stages, the stares at 7, 11.2, 14.2 and 16.9s, no cut in the silence, the half-speed slow and the hold, the old aura and pose, nothing left behind), Mastery 0 unchanged, and each mastery exactly: the 3-tile falloff, its 8-11% gain 3-4 tiles out and the wider aura; the last stage from 13s at 1.6 and the stares and pose that follow it; the stare cuts only in the stares, only within 1.5 tiles of the densest foe, only for foes further than 2.75 tiles, and the eyes' flare; every cut against the execution rule (bosses never, the pooled cross and the glow); the 0.8s silence with no cuts, the last cut on every foe by its distance in order from the hero, 40 crosses, the eyes wide open, the pose to 18.98s, the last cut's executions, nothing left behind; echoes cast the old spell, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery, with its factor in the cut.

## Blood Needles: more of them, and sharper (v466)
A crimson orbit of eight needles circles the hero over a blood rune, then hunts for 4 seconds: guided blood darts leave it one every eighth of a second, each going for the foe with the most life left after the darts already in flight. Simon's picture for Piercing Judgment fits this scroll best of all: "picture it like fern from frieren where she fires alot of em and just more powerful ones"; and for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". Blood Needles had no masteries before. These make the same swarm sharper or larger and draw it a little richer: nothing ricochets, pierces, bursts or bleeds (Murder of Crows, Frost Wolves, Vael, Seraph Lance).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Barbed Points | each needle | 135% of the hero's attack (125% before) | the needles a fifth longer in the orbit and in flight, a brighter sheen (0.45, 0.3 before), tips half again as large, longer trails (18 points, 14) |
| 2 Bloodhound | the flight | 25% faster, turning twice as tight, hunting a quarter further (reach x1.25), so they strike in about 0.8s on average instead of circling their mark for 2-3s (2.3s on average; one still circling at 3.6s expires) | longer, brighter trails (22 points, a brighter red at 0.68) and a sharper swoop |
| 3 Bloodletting | the focus | a needle striking a foe this swarm has already pierced strikes 2% harder for every needle already in it, up to 20% (from the 11th); the needles still spread by the life left, so it pays on the big foes that draw many | the hit spray thickens and darkens as they pile in (9 red and 5 dark sparks at first, 16 and 14 once ten are in) |
| 4 Patient Swarm | the orbit | a needle with no foe in reach waits in its slot instead of being lost, until 1.5s after the hunt's end (4.35s); when one comes, the waiting needles fly at 32 a second until the hunt has caught up | the waiting needles glow crimson and the orbit spins faster |
| 5 Crimson Halo | the orbit's end | the orbit fills to 12 needles (8); 24 fly one by one at the hunt's old pace (by 3.35s), then at 3.85s every needle still circling looses at once at the strongest foe: 36 needles in all (32) | the orbit spins to a blur (a thin torus of light streaked behind each needle), thicker blood smoke |

- How: `castBloodNeedles` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `BND`); without it the cast is as before. `bnmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`BNM`) wraps `castBloodNeedles` by name, as v447/v448 did. The changes inside the base cast are small: the strike, the flight's speed, turn and swoop, the reach, Bloodletting's count per foe, the wait and its rush, the orbit's size, the hunt's count and pace, the halo, the looks, and a log for the tests.
- A fix for every cast, Mastery 0 too: a needle's flight is now flown in steps of at most 0.02s (`BND.sub`). Effects step by the frame's time times the game speed, and in one long step a needle flew so far past its mark (0.2 wide) that it never met it: below 50 fps, or at 2x speed and up, most needles circled their mark until they expired (stepped at 0.05s, as at 20 fps or at 3x speed at 60 fps, 6-11 of the 32 struck). At 50 fps and up at 1x a frame is still one step, so nothing changes there; everywhere else the swarm now strikes as it does at 60 fps. Without it the ladder's own 0.05s steps would also have lost most needles at every mastery. `{sub:0}` flies as before.
- Also in the base cast, with no change to how it plays: the needles' shared body and sheen materials were freed with the orbit at 4.9s while needles still flew to about 7.9s (and, at Mastery 4, later); they now live until the last needle can be gone. Each trail allocated a new point every frame (`p.clone()`): it now reuses its oldest one.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: Crimson Halo's twelve come out of the hunt's own count (24 one by one, then 12 at once: 36 needles, 32 before). Built as written (the 32 one by one, then 12 more: 44) the halo added 41% and Mastery 5 measured 1.64 against 1.35, a factor of 0.82, so each needle would have struck weaker than at Mastery 0. The hunt keeps its pace, one every eighth of a second, and ends a second sooner, then the orbit spins up for half a second and looses. Bloodhound's "tighter turns" is a doubled turn: turning only 25% tighter with the 25% faster flight circled in the same loops and struck no sooner (2.3s on average).
- Ladder (Secret, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 12 casts 15s apart: `ladder.js bloodneedles 2 12 15 pack:.6,one:.4`). The needles' cooldown is 14s, so the standard ladder is the calibration; with the foes put back before every cast (the Citadel's `ladder-fresh.js`) it reads the same. Bloodhound's flight and reach and Patient Swarm count for nothing on either (the foes stand in reach and never die, and every needle strikes), so their factors carry them.

| `SMG.bloodneedles` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 1 | 1.076 | 1.054 | 1.12 | 1.005 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| standard ladder | 1.08 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.303 | 1.349 |
| foes put back before every cast | 1.08 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.343 |

- Tests: `tests/bnm-test.js` (41 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers; that the defaults table is the old cast and that a cast with only a log is the old cast (an orbit of 8 at 0.72, 32 needles one every 1/8 s from 0.35s, each striking 125% of the hero's attack once, none lost, the orbit gone at 4.9s, nothing left behind); Mastery 0 unchanged; the flight fix (flown in one long step most needles expire unstruck; now all 32 strike at 20 fps as at 60 fps, after the same circling hunt); and each mastery exactly: 135% strikes, the longer needles, sheen, tips and trails; the reach (foes between the old reach and 1.25 times it struck, none beyond), the far sooner strikes and the brighter trails; Bloodletting's ramp on a lone foe and in a pack, a new swarm starting again, the thickening spray; no needle leaving while no foe is in reach, the glow, the rush when they come back, the 1.5s limit, the orbit fading after it, the shared materials freed only after the last needle; the 12-needle orbit, 24 one by one, the halo of 12 at 3.85s at the strongest foe, 36 strikes on Bloodletting's ramp, the faster spin and the blur, the halo waiting for prey too; echoes cast the old spell, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery with its factor in each needle.

## Eye of the Blood Moon: the gaze that marks (v467)
A vast eye tears open in the sky, its gaze settles on the strongest foes and brands them (they take more from everything for a while), and blood lightning lashes down from the eye at its brands. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". The Eye had no masteries before; these each strengthen one part it already has and draw that same part richer. Still one eye, one gaze, brands and lightning from the eye: no lances (Seraph Lance), no stars over the strongest (Constellation), no more eyes (Crimson Purgatory).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Wider Gaze | the gaze | it brands 7 foes (6 before) within 10 tiles of where it looks (8 before) | the eye a tenth larger (1.32, 1.2 before), its first sweep over the field 1.3 times wider, a larger rune (1.85, 1.6 before) |
| 2 Bloodshot Brand | the brand | branded foes take 40% more damage from everything (35% before) for 10s (8s before) | the brands a fifth larger, in deeper reds: a brighter core, a deeper red halo whose light pulses, a see-through dark red glow round them; they pulse harder (45%, 25% before) and stay lit as long as they last |
| 3 Lashing Storm | the lightning | a lash every 0.34s, 7 of them (every 0.4s, 6 before), 140% each (150% before): 980% on each brand against 900% | thicker bolts (width 1.6, 1.3 before) with three branches (two) |
| 4 The Gaze Moves On | the brands | while the eye is open (until its lids begin to close at 4.1s), a branded foe that dies passes the gaze to the strongest unbranded foe in reach, which is branded like the first ones and struck by the lashes still to come (up to 6 more) | the gaze beam swings from the fallen brand to the new one over 0.3s, the eye turns to it, and its brand lights up as the beam arrives |
| 5 The Last Look | the lightning's end | at 4s, after the last lash and before the lids close, one great lash strikes every brand at once: 200%, renewing each brand (10s from then) | the iris flares (its light 0.5 to 1.6) and the same blood lightning, twice as thick (3.2), runs from every brand up to the eye |

- How: `castBloodEye` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `BED`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `beyOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`BEY`) wraps `castBloodEye` by name, as v447/v448 did. The changes inside the base cast are small: the brand's reach, count, length and strength, the lashes' interval, count, strike, width and branches, the gaze moving on, The Last Look, the eye's size and sweep, the rune, the brands' look, and a log for the tests. The brand's strength is now the foe's own (`e.eyeM`, 1.35 unless Bloodshot Brand set it) in the global hurt wrapper that makes branded foes take more from everything.
- Seen, not hidden: the old cast ends at 8.4s, so its brand marks vanished 1.2s before its brands ran out (9.6s). Mastered casts keep the marks for as long as the brands last (option `hold`), which Bloodshot Brand's 10s and The Last Look's renewal need; the eye, its rune and the screen's darkening still end at 8.4s, so another spell's darkness is never cut. Mastery 0 is unchanged.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: Bloodshot Brand +40% (+45% approved; 10s kept), Lashing Storm's lashes 140% each (the approved row kept the base 150%; 7 lashes every 0.34s kept), The Last Look 200% (250% approved). Built as written, the set measured 1.10 / 1.181 / 1.378 / 1.378 / 1.707 on the ladder against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35: the seventh brand, the seventh lash and the great lash each add a sixth to a quarter, so Mastery 5 would have needed a factor of 0.79 (every lash a fifth weaker than at Mastery 0). Trimmed, it measures 1.10 / 1.141 / 1.242 / 1.242 / 1.496, and the factors stay between 0.89 and 1.04. Wider Gaze and The Gaze Moves On are as approved. Rules added: the gaze moves on only while the eye is open (it has no gaze after), to the strongest unbranded foe within the brand's reach of where it looks (flyers too, not decoys); The Last Look comes at 4s (the last lash is at about 3.7s).

What the brand adds in play. The ladder sees only the Eye's own lightning (with the brand on it), but the brand lifts everything that strikes a branded foe: towers, the hero, other scrolls, damage over time. That part does not grow with the mastery's factor, and Mastery 0 already has it.
- Measured: each tower of the standard bot build (Robin level 12, 12 towers, the `scroll-dmg-sim.js` bot) firing at one foe deals, in units of the scroll's power a second: the best ones (archers, ballista) about 0.8 at wave 15, 1.1 at wave 25 and 1.5 at wave 35; mages and cannons about half that. So two good towers on the brands put about 2.2 times the scroll's power a second into them around wave 25 (1.6-3.2 over waves 15-35).
- At Mastery 0 the Eye's own lightning deals 6 brands x 6 lashes x 150% x 1.35 = 72.9 times its power; two such towers on the brands for the brand's 8s add 0.35 x 8 x 2.2 = 6.2 more: the brand adds about 8.5% to the cast's own damage (6-12% over waves 15-35). A whole bot game to wave 40 with the Eye measured 7.5-9.4% (health others took off branded foes x 0.26); after wave 24 its towers clear each wave before the brands land, so that game says little about late waves.
- Mastery 2 (40% for 10s) makes that bonus 1.43 times as large, about 12% of Mastery 0's own damage; the approved 45% for 10s would have made it 1.61 times (13.7%). The Last Look's renewal keeps the brands on to 14s, 1.77 times (15%).
- How it was weighed: the brand is at the low end (+40%, the duration kept for The Last Look's renewal), and the factors leave the ladder at the target at Mastery 1 and 0.010-0.018 under it from Mastery 2 up. Counting the brand's bonus to one good tower (1.1 times the power a second) the whole cast measures 1.075 / 1.156 / 1.221 / 1.298 / 1.354; to two, 1.072 / 1.166 / 1.229 / 1.303 / 1.370: within 0.02 of every target either way. (At Mastery 1 the brand's share is a little diluted by the larger own damage, so it reads a hair under.) Not counted anywhere: The Gaze Moves On (its foes must die; the ladder's never do).

| `SMG.bloodeye` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 0.98 | 1.003 | 0.976 | 1.04 | 0.893 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| standard ladder (pack .6 / one .4, 6 casts 12s apart, 2 runs) | 1.078 | 1.144 | 1.212 | 1.292 | 1.336 |
| foes put back before every cast (2 runs) | 1.078 | 1.144 | 1.212 | 1.292 | 1.336 |
| with the brand on one / two good towers (estimate) | 1.075 / 1.072 | 1.156 / 1.166 | 1.221 / 1.229 | 1.298 / 1.303 | 1.354 / 1.370 |

The two ladders agree exactly: the Eye moves and kills no foe, and its brands do not stack, so a cast meets the same foes either way.
- Tests: `tests/bey-test.js` (43 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers, that the defaults table is the old cast and that a cast with only a log is the old cast (the same aim, brands, lash moments and damage), Mastery 0 unchanged (the 6 strongest within 8 tiles branded at x1.35 for 8s while stronger foes 8-10 tiles off are left; 6 lashes of 150% that take 202.5 off each brand; bolts of width 1.3 with 2 branches; the brand lifting another source's hit 100 to 135; the cast ending at 8.4s with its marks; nothing left behind), and each mastery exactly: the 7 strongest within 10 tiles, the 1.32 eye and the 1.85 rune, the marks lasting the brand's 8s; x1.4 for 10s on the lashes (210) and on another source (140 at 9.8s, 100 at 10.15s), the larger, deeper red marks and their pulse, the eye, rune and darkness ending at 8.4s with the brands still on; 7 lashes 0.34-0.35s apart, each 140% (196 off), width 1.6 with 3 branches; the gaze moving on six times to the strongest unbranded foe in reach, not a seventh time nor after the lids begin to close, the new brands held 10s and lashed, their marks growing in; The Last Look once at 4s, 200% (280 off) on every brand including a moved one, renewing them, its bolts 3.2 wide from each brand up to the eye, the iris's light 0.5 to 1.6 and back, the marks kept to 14s; echoes casting the old spell, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery, with its factor in every lash.

## Moonfall: the moon breaks (v468)
Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". So Moonfall (Secret, Blood Moon) keeps one moon, one fall, one impact and one crater, and each mastery Simon approved makes one part of it stronger and draws that same part richer. Two parts that were only a picture, the moon's shards and its far wave of blood, now strike; there is still no rain and no spikes.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn stronger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Fuller Moon | the impact's reach | the impact (2600%, the 1.4s stun) reaches 4.6 tiles (4.2 before) | the moon a tenth larger with brighter coronas; its shadow, red glow and the impact's waves widened with its reach |
| 2 Molten Crater | the crater | spreads to 3 tiles (2.6), burns 7s (6s), 65% a tick (60%) | 16 cracks (12), a little further out and brighter; more embers, darker smoke; the scorch lasts as much longer |
| 3 Shattered Moon | the moon's shards | the 16 shards are thrown at foes, one each, the foes the impact did not reach first; each strikes the nearest foe within 0.5 tiles of where it lands, 80% | shards hotter (those thrown at foes a quarter smaller), with short ember trails; a little burst of sparks where each lands (blood on a foe); landed, they cool darker and crumble away |
| 4 Under Its Shadow | the fall | ground foes under the falling moon's shadow are slowed, harder as it darkens, 60% in its last second (Unstoppable foes too: it is a slow) | the shadow deeper and the red glow under it brighter; dust pressed up at the feet of the foes under it |
| 5 Moonbreaker | the impact | 20% harder (3120%), and the far wave of blood strikes every ground foe it passes, once each, out to 10.6 tiles, 300% | the wave drawn with height: a see-through ring of blood with a trailing slope, a crest and a curling lip rolls out on it, blood thrown off its crest |

- How: `castMoonfall` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `MFL`); without it the cast is exactly as before (the test checks the impact, stun and crater against where the moon landed). `mfmOpts` builds it from the mastery; the masteries' looks live in the module (`mfmAim`, `mfmHotMat`, `mfmWave`, `mfmImpact`, `mfmFall`, `mfmFrame`, `mfmLand`). The impact's stun is still set directly (Resolve is bypassed by design). The shards are aimed by giving each a velocity that lands it where its foe will be (a stunned or slowed foe is led less) and flying it on an exact arc; the strike itself is the rule "the nearest foe within 0.5 of where it lands". The wave strikes as the front of the existing `bmWave` passes (2.3 times the impact's reach, 1.9s), and its 3D ring rides that front.
- A look fixed on the way: the crater's glowing cracks (`nsSeamFx`, shared with many spells, as the Ember Titan found in v452) were drawn facing the ground, so they were never seen. Moonfall's are drawn facing the sky now (`nsSeamFx`'s `o.up`, the same change v452 made, so the branches merge cleanly), at every mastery, Mastery 0 too. Seen as they were set (12 cracks out to 1.4 times the crater), they lay over the grass as a bright web, so they are kept inside the crater, thin and dim: 12 within 0.75 of its radius at 40% (Molten Crater: 16 within 0.8 at 50%, a little wider and hotter). Other spells' cracks are unchanged (the test checks both).
- The mastery preview waits for the crater to cool (`SMPV_BUSY`): the cast lives 9.2s (10.2s from Molten Crater), longer than its 5s cinematic lock.
- Ladder (Secret, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 13.5s apart, 3 runs). Calibrated with the foes put back where they stood and at full health before every cast (Citadel's `ladder-fresh.js`, with stuns, slows and Resolve reset as the Eldritch Tree's copy does): in play the cooldown is 120s, so every cast meets fresh foes. The standard ladder (`ladder.js moonfall 3 3 13.5 pack:.6,one:.4`) reads exactly the same: the ladder's foes stand still and never die. Fuller Moon's reach and Under Its Shadow's slow count for nothing on either (every ladder foe stands inside 4.2 tiles, and none walks), and the shards for little (one each), so their factors carry them: Mastery 4's 1.204 is above the usual 0.9-1.1 band for that reason. Mastery 5 measures 1.333 without a factor, hence its 1.013.

| `SMG.moonfall` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 1.078 | 1.099 | 1.134 | 1.204 | 1.013 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| foes put back before every cast (3 runs) | 1.078 | 1.161 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| standard ladder (3 runs) | 1.078 | 1.161 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |

- One number changed from the approved table: Moonbreaker's impact strikes 20% harder (3120%), not 30% (3380%). At 30% Mastery 5 measured 1.412 before its factor, so the factor would fall from 1.204 at Mastery 4 to 0.956, and each crater tick and shard would strike about 21% weaker at Mastery 5 than at Mastery 4. At 20% it measures 1.333 and its factor is 1.013: the crater's ticks and the shards strike 16% lighter than at Mastery 4 (whose factor carries its slow), the impact a little harder. Every other number is as approved.
- Tests: `tests/mfm-test.js` (38 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers, Mastery 0 unchanged (the moon on the knot and not led, 2600% to exactly the foes within 4.2, flyers too, the 1.4s stun set directly on an Unstoppable foe and 0.5s on a boss, the crater's 60% ticks every 0.5s for 6s within 2.6, no slow, nothing else, nothing left behind), the cracks facing the sky for Moonfall only, and each mastery exactly: 2600% out to 4.6 with the moon 2.64 and its shadow and glow widened; the crater to 3 at 65% for 7s, 16 cracks, the cast living 10.2s; all 16 shards thrown at foes past the impact, each striking the nearest foe within 0.5 of where it lands for 80% (and on its own foe), one back onto a lone foe; the slow's ramp (0.34 at 1.2s, 0.6 in the last second) on ground foes under the shadow, an Unstoppable foe too, not flyers or foes outside it, and a walking foe covering 40% of its ground there; 3120% within 4.6 and 300% once on every ground foe out to 10.6 as the wave passes (the further, the later), not flyers; nothing left behind; Mastery 4 without the wave; casts that are not a mastered scroll cast; the preview's wait; and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery.

## Constellation: a brighter sky, and it falls twice (v470)
Stars kindle one by one over the strongest foes and lines of light join them into one figure; then the whole figure falls, a lance of light per star (1000% to its foe, 60% round it), and the lines burn into the ground beneath (200% once to every foe along them). Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better"; and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". Constellation had no masteries before. These strengthen a part the figure already has and draw it a little richer; the one new thing, a second fall, is the same figure again. The lances stay one a star (Lance of the Seraph owns lances stacked on the strongest) and the lines stay on the ground between the impacts (Piercing Judgment owns beams across the field).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Brighter Stars | the stars and lances | each lance 1100% (1000% before), 60% to the foes within 0.8 tiles of its foe (0.6) | the stars a quarter larger, a white core and a whiter halo, flares a tenth longer, lances half again as thick; each impact a short column of light rising off the ground inside a low wall of light racing out to 0.8, where a flat ring was |
| 2 A Larger Figure | the figure | 8 stars (7) over the strongest foes within 10 tiles of the densest one (9) | one more star and one more line, kindled the same way |
| 3 Burning Lines | the lines | 250% (200%) to every foe within 0.75 tiles of a line (0.55); alight 2s (the streaks lasted 1.3s), burning a foe that steps onto one meanwhile, once | wider streaks (0.3, 0.22) with a white core, a low band of light rising off them, embers along them |
| 4 Fixed Stars | the kindling | a non-boss foe is held under its star from its kindling until its star falls (about 2.05s for the first star to 1.5s for the eighth; `stunT` set directly, as the Dimensional Slash's stillness); a boss is slowed 50% instead | the halo tightens and pulses over a held foe (a little less over a slowed boss) |
| 5 The Sky Falls Twice | the fall | 0.25s after the lines burn, the figure kindles again over the strongest foes still standing and falls a second time at 12% of the power, with every mastery before it | the same figure, drawn faster (its timings times 0.6: it falls 1.3s after it kindles), its impacts a little softer; the sky stays dark until it has fallen |

- How: `castConstellation` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `CSL`); without it the cast is as before. `csmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`CSM`) wraps `castConstellation` by name, as v447/v448 did. The second figure is the same cast called again with the second figure's options (`second`, its timings scaled by `ts`), so it is drawn by the same code. `nsLance` takes an optional thickness and `nsStreak` an optional look (width, life, brightness, band); without them they draw as before.
- The explosion rule: the impact's only explosion was a flat `ringFx`. From Mastery 1 it is `csImpact`: an open column of light round the foe (brightest at its edges and foot, streaked, rising in 0.12s and thinning as it climbs) inside a low wall of light that races out to the splash's reach, both gone in 0.6s and see-through. Mastery 0 keeps the old ring, as the approved design asked.
- Burning Lines' later burns: while the lines stay alight, each foe's path since the last frame is checked against them in steps of at most 0.15 tiles, so a long frame at a high game speed (the lesson of Blood Needles, v466) cannot carry a foe across a line unburnt; a jump of 2.5 tiles or more (a foe sent back up the road) is checked only where it lands. Nothing else in the cast moves toward a target.
- The second figure outlives the cast's `cineLock` (the cast lasts about 6.6s at Mastery 5), so the mastery preview waits for it: `SMPV_BUSY.push(()=>fx.some(f=>f.csFig))`.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: The Sky Falls Twice falls at 12% of the power (40% approved). Built at 40% the second figure added 40% at every measure: Mastery 5 measured 1.79 against 1.35, a factor of 0.76, so each lance of the first fall would have struck about 830%, weaker than at Mastery 0. At 12% the factor is 0.945 (lances at 1040%). Masteries 1-4 keep the approved numbers; their factors sit a little below 1 (0.943-0.961), except Fixed Stars', which the ladder cannot see (1.02). The flares are a tenth longer (with the stars a quarter larger they are 1.4 times as long): "longer flares" built longer crowded the figure.
- Ladder (Secret, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 8 casts 8s apart: `ladder.js constellation 2 8 8 pack:.6,one:.4`). The cooldown is 50s, so the calibration also put the foes back before every cast (the Citadel's `ladder-fresh.js`): the foes stand still and never die, so it reads the same. Fixed Stars, the reach, the lines' later burns and the second figure's choice of foes count for nothing on either, so their factors carry them.

| `SMG.constellation` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 0.955 | 0.943 | 0.961 | 1.02 | 0.945 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| standard ladder | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.301 | 1.35 |
| foes put back before every cast | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.301 | 1.35 |
| with every factor at 1 | 1.129 | 1.232 | 1.276 | 1.276 | 1.786 (at 40%) |

- Tests: `tests/csm-test.js` (42 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers, that no mastered cast strikes weaker than Mastery 0, and that the defaults table is the old cast; Mastery 0 foe by foe against the spell's own rules worked out from the foes' places (7 lances of 1000%, the splash, the lines), with the old flat rings and nothing left behind, and a cast with only a log striking exactly as Mastery 0, frame by frame; then each mastery exactly: 1100% lances and the 0.8 splash foe by foe, the larger and whiter stars, longer flares, thicker lances and a column at every impact; a strongest foe 9.25-9.75 tiles off reached at Mastery 2 and not at Mastery 1, 8 stars and 7 lines; 250% lines to 0.75 (a foe 0.6-0.7 off a line burnt now and not before), the streaks and their bands alight 2s, a foe stepping onto a line later burnt once, none after 2s, a foe carried across a line in one long step burnt, none twice; walking foes standing still from kindling to fall and walking on, the hold's length, the boss walking at half speed, the tighter pulsing halo; the second figure 0.25s after the burn, only one, over the strongest foes still standing, drawn faster, at 12% foe by foe, the sky kept dark between, the preview waiting, nothing left behind, a lone foe struck twice, no second figure when no foe stands; echoes cast the old spell, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery with its factor in each lance.


## Curse of the Blood Moon: the price of blood (v469)
The hero is the centre of a 13-second ritual: an opening blast of fire and darkness, a vast spiked blood seal with a crest of blades round a red star overhead, crimson eruptions under the enemy escalating in four stages, one enormous final eruption, and the price: the hero's own greater magic is sealed while it burns. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better". The Curse had no masteries before; these each strengthen one part the ritual already has and draw that same part richer. About 160 eruptions fall in a cast, each with its own meshes and materials, so the masteries make them larger, never more: no new pattern and no domain (Crimson Purgatory, Eclipse and Pulsar keep theirs).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Wider Seal | the opening blast | reaches 5 tiles (4.4 before) and strikes 650% fire + 650% dark (600% + 600%); its push back along the road, 0.6 x (1 - d / 5), and its 0.5s hold reach as far | its dome, waves and rings drawn 5/4.4 as wide; the seal's 24 blade spikes 15% longer |
| 2 Hotter Blood | the eruptions | every eruption strikes 10% harder (its red lightning, 40% of it, too; the finale's as well) | its three fire columns rise a tenth taller and burn hotter (uHot 0.15 / 0.9 / 1.45, 0 / 0.55 / 1 before); the red lightning inside brighter (its glow ribbon 1.35 and its core 1.175 times as wide, from the same three materials) |
| 3 Rising Fury | the escalation | each stage grows the eruptions 18% (12%): the last stage's are 1.54 times their size (1.36), the finale's too, and strike and reach with it | the seal's rings and swirls spin 1 + stage times their speed (1 + 0.6 x stage); the crest's star pulses 0.15 + 0.1 x stage (0.15) and faster |
| 4 Blood Price | the seal on the hero's greater magic | each greater scroll (Rare and up; not the Curse or the Needles, as `cursedSeal`) the curse seals on its caster feeds it: every eruption 4% harder for each, up to 3 | the crest's 20 blades 8% longer and its star 10% larger and brighter for each |
| 5 The Great Eruption | the finale | the final eruption half again as large (3.4 x 1.54 x 1.5 = 7.85: it reaches 4.9 tiles) and so half again as hard; the blast after it 1500% within 4.2 tiles (1000% within 2.8) | the crest's star leaves the crest and dives down a shaft of light into the final eruption, landing as it bursts; a wider web of cracks (2.1 tiles, 14 branches; 1.4 and 9) and rings 1.5 times as wide |

- How: `castBloodCurse` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `BCD`); without it the cast is exactly as before. `bcmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L) and counts the sealed scrolls for Blood Price; the module (`BCM`) wraps `castBloodCurse` by name, as v447/v448 did. The changes inside the base cast are small: the blast's reach, strike and drawn size, the spikes' length, every eruption's strike and stage growth, the rings' spin, the crest's star pulse, blades and star, the finale's size, blast and cracks, the plunge, and a log for the tests.
- The eruptions (`bmErupt`, used only by the Curse) and their bolts (`bmBolt`) take an optional last argument too, and are exactly as before without it: the columns' height, hotter cores and brighter bolts; the size an eruption counts its shards, bolts and boom at, which the masteries keep at the size it would have without them, so a larger eruption allocates no more meshes or materials than before; and how much further a finale strikes than it is drawn.
- The finale is drawn smaller than it strikes: built as written, the half-again-as-large final eruption (7.85 across) swallowed most of the view at normal zoom for most of a second. It is drawn 1.12 times its stage's size (5.86, 4.62 at Mastery 0) and keeps its full reach; its wider rings and cracks show it. The plunging star and its shaft reuse the crest's own star and shaft materials (one shaft stretched from the crest's top down to the diving star), and are freed with the ritual.
- Numbers changed from the approved table: Blood Price is 4% a sealed scroll (8% approved). At 8% the gap between a hero with none sealed and one with three is about 20%, too wide to calibrate: set for three, Mastery 4 without sealed scrolls falls below Mastery 3; set for none, three sealed land about 20% over the target. At 4% (up to 12%) it is calibrated at the middle of the price, 1.5 sealed: with none Mastery 4 and 5 sit about 5% under their targets (still above the mastery before), with three about 5% over. The finale's drawn size (above) is a look, not a number of the table. Everything else is as approved.
- Ladder (Secret, half steps; the standing pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 17s apart), calibrated with the foes put back where they stood before every cast (in play the cooldown is 200s, so every cast meets fresh foes). The eruptions land at random, so the factors come from one set of sampled casts worked out at every level: the masteries never move an eruption or change a pattern, only its strike, its size by stage and the finale, so the casts are run once (at Mastery 2, which logs every eruption's base size and stage, 276 casts) and each level's damage is the same eruptions' (an eruption of size s strikes 150% x s to the foes within 0.62 s and 40% of that within 0.7 s); it reproduces the casts' own damage to 0.01%. The direct measure (`/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kingsroad-keep/5a683175-73c8-5b6a-8b5f-1cd5ede13798/scratchpad/sec-curse/ladder-curse.js`, Citadel's put-back ladder with `window.__smSeal` greater scrolls equipped on the hero, evaluated on the dev page; 16 runs) confirms it. The standard ladder (`ladder.js bloodcurse 4 3 17 pack:.6,one:.4`) reuses its foes, pushed back by the blast each cast. The push, the holds and the stagger count for nothing on either (the foes stand still), and the ladder hero carries no scrolls, so Blood Price is measured with three equipped.
