It checks the card and its numbers, the changelog, that the defaults table is the old cast and Mastery 0 the old curse (the blast's 600% + 600% to every foe within 4.4 and its push, every eruption 150% x its size with the old columns and bolts, the final eruption 4.62 across and the 1000% blast within 2.8, the old rings and cracks, the 14s hold, nothing left behind), and each mastery exactly: the 5-tile blast at 650% + 650% with its push, the wider dome and waves and the 24 longer spikes; 165% x size, the hotter columns and brighter bolts and the meshes counted at the old size; each eruption's stage and its 18% growth, the rings' spin and the star's pulse by stage; the sealed scrolls counted (none, 1, 3, 3 of 6; not the Needles, Common or Uncommon scrolls or another hero's), 184.8% x size with three, those scrolls sealed while it burns, the crest's blades and star, nothing below Mastery 4; the 7.85 finale drawn at 5.86 that strikes every foe within 4.9 tiles, the 1500% blast within 4.2, the star's plunge landing as it bursts, the wider cracks and rings, no more eruptions than Mastery 0; nothing left behind at any mastery; a cast that is not a mastered scroll cast is the old curse, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery (with deaths). `tests/smsets-test.js` now casts the Curse at Mastery 5 under the fire set and finds nothing in its old colours.

## Pulsar: the star spins up (v471)
A tiny, impossibly bright neutron star hangs over the road and spins itself into a frenzy; its beam sweeps a ring of ground like a lighthouse for 15s, burning it, then the star implodes into one stellar blast. Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". Pulsar had no masteries before; these each strengthen one part it already has and draw that same part richer. Still one star and a fixed sweeping ring (aimed beams belong to Basic Beam and Soul Lantern), burnt ground and one blast.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Hard Radiation | the beam | every hit 95% fire + 95% light (90% + 90% before), searing 0.04 tiles further off its path | its layers a fifth thicker, a wider, brighter white core (0.07 wide at 1.3, 0.045 at 1 before) and a whiter inner layer |
| 2 Spin-Up | the spin | every phase 10% sooner (ignition 0-1.8s, active 1.8-4.5s, frenzy 4.5-9s, critical 9-12.6s; the convergence still 14-15s) and the star 5% faster; a beam may strike a foe again 0.22s / 1.05 on, so its faster passes strike as often | the wind arcs whirl faster and the field loops brighten sooner, with the spin and the phases |
| 3 Both Poles | the second beam | the second beam pointed at the sky and struck nothing (the card promised two beams); it tips down and sweeps a second ring at 60% of the radius, half a turn behind the first: 20% fire + 20% light a hit | the same beam, with its ground glow and its own burnt ring (0.7 times as wide, a quarter lighter, fading 2s sooner, none in the convergence); the burn pool 1500 quads (900) |
| 4 Scorched Ring | the burnt ground | every 0.25s, each foe standing on ground the beams burnt in the last 2s takes 15% fire, at most every 0.5s (not flyers) | fresh burns glow a dull red and their embers burn brighter and longer; embers kick up round a seared foe's feet |
| 5 Supernova | the implosion and blast | the implosion 0.6s (0.35s); the blast 650% fire + 650% light within 8.5 tiles (+1 bosses; 500% + 500% within 6.5 before) | light streams up off both burnt rings into the star and the rings' embers fade into it (back over 2s after the blast); shells and rings a third wider, the ground shockwave out to the new reach, the screen waves 15% wider |

- How: `castPulsar` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `PSD`); without it the cast is exactly as before (but for the sweep's steps, below). `psmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`PSM`) wraps `castPulsar` by name, as v447/v448 did. The changes inside the base cast are small: the hit and its reach, the phase times and the spin, the second beam's direction and hit, the burn pool, the scorched ground, the implosion's length, the blast's strike and reach, the beam layers, the burn shader's embers, the pull, the blast's sizes, and a log for the tests.
- The second beam: both beams' directions come from one helper, `dirAt(phi, tilt)` round the down axis. The first beam is the old one exactly; the second was the first turned upside down (`-M`); with Both Poles it is `dirAt(phi + pi, tilt2)`, its tilt set so its ground spot circles at 0.6 of the radius.
- The scorched ground: the burn ribbon already kept 900 pooled quads. Scorched Ring marks, as each quad is laid, the quarter-tile cells whose centres lie within the burn's half width of its stroke, with the time; every 0.25s a foe's own cell (a boss's and the cells round it) is looked up. A foe costs one lookup, so the check is cheap however many quads are burning. The blast's 40 scorch marks count too, so the searing ends 2s after the blast.
- Seen, not hidden: two rings and the convergence's two spirals filled the disc between them with soot that lay over the road for 8s after the blast; the second ring's burn is narrower and lighter, fades 2s sooner and stops in the convergence, so the aftermath keeps Mastery 0's spiral plus one inner ring. The Scorched Ring embers were first far too bright (the rings read as lava) and were toned down to a dull red.
- A fix for every cast (Mastery 0 too): each frame's sweep is flown in steps of at most 0.02s along the spin (`PSD.sub`). One long frame used to sweep a straight chord across the ring, inside it, and past half a turn it swept backwards: at a 0.1s step (2x speed at 20 fps, 6x at 60 fps) a foe on the ring took 6-11 strikes where it takes about 32 at 60 fps, and 26-28 at the ladder's 0.05s. Now it takes about 30 at any step. A frame of 0.02s or less is one step, as before. The hit checks look only at the foes inside the box round the star and the frame's ground spots: a sweep between two downward directions lands on the straight line between their spots, so the box holds every foe it can reach.
- Numbers changed from the approved table. Built as written, the set measured 1.186 / 1.402 / 1.72 / 1.797 / 1.895 before any factor (the kit's pack; 1.153 / 1.338 / 1.584 / 1.669 / 1.771 spread along the road) against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35. The second ring alone added two fifths on a pack: the first ring meets the pack where the target foe stands, at its end, while the inner one often crosses its middle twice. So every mechanic is kept and its numbers trimmed: Hard Radiation 95% + 95% (100% + 100% approved) and 0.04 tiles (0.06); Spin-Up 10% sooner (25%) and 5% faster (25%); Both Poles 20% + 20% (45% + 45%); Supernova 650% + 650% (750% + 750%; 8.5 tiles and 0.6s kept); Scorched Ring as approved (15% every 0.5s, 2s).
- One rule added (Spin-Up): a beam may strike a foe again 0.22s / the spin on (0.22s before). With the old 0.22s, a faster star could not strike more: at the top of the spin a pass comes every 0.17-0.2s, so a foe was struck every other pass; at 15% faster a foe on the ring took the same 32 strikes. The approved "so the beam passes more often" now means it strikes more often too.

Ladder (Secret, half steps; pack .6 / one .4, 3 casts 24.5s apart), calibrated on fresh foes: every foe is put back before every cast (the cooldown is 150s, so every cast meets fresh foes). Pulsar places its star at random (a circle through one of the three strongest foes that crosses the road twice), so the measure is noisy. Each cast's placement is recorded at Mastery 0 and used again at every mastery, so each one is measured on the same rings. The ring always crosses its target foe and the road, so the foes stand where it can cross them. Two layouts are used: the kit's pack (12 foes 0.3 tiles apart round the road point nearest the hero, and the lone foe there) and the same pack spread 0.9 tiles apart along the road (a wave walking it), and the factors are set from the mean of the two. The measure is `/tmp/claude-0/-home-user-kingsroad-keep/5a683175-73c8-5b6a-8b5f-1cd5ede13798/scratchpad/sec-pulsar/ladder-psr.js` (Citadel's put-back ladder with the shared placements, evaluated on the dev page). Scorched Ring counts fully there (the foes stand on the burn); in play a foe walks across a ring in about a second.

| `SMG.pulsar` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 0.988 | 0.978 | 0.962 | 0.977 | 0.965 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| fresh foes, the kit's pack (32 runs) | 1.084 | 1.186 | 1.249 | 1.330 | 1.375 |
| fresh foes, the pack spread along the road (32 runs) | 1.074 | 1.153 | 1.228 | 1.304 | 1.356 |
| mean (calibrated) | 1.079 | 1.170 | 1.239 | 1.317 | 1.366 |
| the same pack, foes not put back (32 runs) | 1.076 | 1.153 | 1.174 | 1.256 | 1.293 |
| the kit's standard ladder (`ladder.js pulsar 12 3 26.35`, 12 runs) | 1.088 | 1.188 | 1.214 | 1.303 | 1.344 |

Not putting the foes back changes where the star goes: it aims at one of the three foes with the most health, so later casts follow the foes the first one missed. Pulsar pushes and kills no ladder foe, so that is the only difference.
- Tests: `tests/psm-test.js` (39 rows, in run-all) places the star by hand so its ring crosses the road through a chosen foe. It checks the card and its numbers, that the defaults table is the old cast and that a cast with only a log is the old cast: only the first beam strikes, 90% + 90%, each foe at most every 0.22s per beam, every ground hit within its reach of the beam's ground spot, the blast at 15.35s on the foes within 6.5 tiles and none further, cineLock 16.5s, the cast ending at 23.35s with nothing left behind and the shared light off. It checks Mastery 0 unchanged through `castScrollNow`, and the sweep at any frame rate: a 0.1s step with the old single chord misses most passes, while the new steps strike as often at 0.1s and 0.05s as at 60 fps. Each mastery is checked exactly: 95% + 95% and 0.04 tiles further (some hits beyond the old reach, none beyond the new), the layers' width x1.2 every frame sampled and the whiter core; every phase's ramp and the target spin frame by frame, more turns by 14s, strikes 0.21s apart, the brighter field loops and faster wind; the second beam's direction exactly at the spin angle + pi every frame sampled, its ring 0.6 of the first one's, the 1500-quad pool, the second ring's burn and none in the convergence, a foe on the inner ring struck by it at 20% + 20% (nothing touching it at Mastery 2 before the convergence); the sears on the foe on the ring (15% fire, at most every 0.5s, only on ground burnt in the last 2s), none on the far foe, none after 2s past the blast, the new ember shader; the 0.6s implosion, the blast at 15.6s with 650% + 650% on a foe 7.5 tiles out that Mastery 4 misses, the streams of motes and the embers fading, the cast lasting 23.6s, nothing left behind. Echoes cast the old spell, and the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery strikes only for the scroll's power with its factor times that mastery's hit, sear or blast. The mastery preview waits for the star: `SMPV_BUSY` holds `PSR.n>0`, true 17.5s into a cast (past its cineLock, with nothing else holding the preview) and false once it is gone.

## Singularity: a heavier hole (v472)
Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better", and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". So Singularity (Secret, Starfall) keeps one black hole, one grip on every foe on the map, one drain and one collapse, and each mastery Simon approved makes one of those parts stronger and draws it richer. There is no pull to a point and no local implosion (those are Voidfall's and Spatial Compression's).

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn stronger |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Greater Mass | the grip | it takes hold at 3.2s (4s before): every foe pinned and drained 8.3s (7.5s) | the hole 15% larger, its disk wider (10.8 horizon radii, 10.3), its ring of light 35% brighter |
| 2 Tidal Drain | the drain | 5.8% of a foe's max health a second (5% before), 3.5% of a boss's (3%) | twice as many gold sparks rise off the pinned |
| 3 Accretion | the disk | each foe that dies while pinned (to anything) makes the drain 2.5% stronger, up to 45% | a stream of gold into the hole from each; the disk burns hotter and brighter as it feeds |
| 4 Torn Earth | the ground tearing up | a rock tears up from beneath every pinned foe once a second and strikes it as it rises: 40% | rocks with glowing broken edges, hot as they tear loose, cooling as they fly into the hole |
| 5 Last Light | the collapse | the collapse strikes every foe on the map once: 500% | the hole draws in 0.15s longer; the flare, rays, shockwave and ground waves 30% larger |

- How: `castSingularity` takes an optional set of numbers for one cast (`XO`, over `SGX`); without it the cast is exactly as before (the test checks the 4s grip, 15 a frame at power 100, 5% / 3% of max health, the hole's shader numbers, the contraction's timing and the collapse's waves). `sgmOpts` builds it from the mastery. The hole's shader has one new number, `uHeat` (Accretion's heat, 0 before). The torn rocks are a pool of their own (`sgEdgeGeo`, `sgEdgeMat`: a lit rock whose rims, some of them, glow orange as it tears up). Everything lives inside the cast: the drain hits every foe every frame, so there is no hit hook.
- Frame cost: the hole is ray traced, 170 steps a pixel, inside a lensing sphere 14 horizon radii wide. Grown 15% with the hole, that sphere would draw 32% more pixels; Greater Mass keeps the sphere the world size it had (12.17 radii of the larger hole), so the hole grows inside the same lensed halo, and replaying the shader's march over the sphere it costs 6.8% more steps.
- A fix on the way: the scroll's card said foes are drained for 11.5 seconds; the pin and the drain last 7.5 seconds (from 4s to the collapse at 11.5s). The card and the scroll's short line say 7.5 now. A second fix: the collapse's burst (its core spheres, flare, god rays and column) hung on the scene itself, and an effect's end is skipped when the world is cleared or the game is not being played, so leaving a game during its 1.6s left it frozen over the next board (22 objects, at every mastery); its pieces hang under its own effect now and go with it, drawn exactly as before. Nothing else of Mastery 0 changed.
- The mastery preview waits for the hole to close (`SMPV_BUSY`: `SGL.n`, and the module's own `SGM.busy`): two Singularities at once would grade the scene's lights twice and leave them tinted.
- Ladder (Secret, half steps; the pack and the lone foe, .6 / .4, 3 casts 17s apart, 3 runs). In play the cooldown is 110s, so every cast meets fresh foes; and the drain takes a share of each foe's max health while Accretion needs foes to die, which the standard ladder's foes (1e9 health, never dying) cannot show. So it was calibrated with foes that have real health and can die, put back (the dead replaced, the living healed) before every cast: a pack of 12 that is 8 light foes dying during the pin, spread over it as a bot game to wave 60 shows (every cast met 8-21 deaths, through the whole pin), and 4 tough foes that live (8000-14000 health at power 100); the lone foe has 9000.

| `SMG.singularity` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 0.968 | 0.949 | 0.949 | 1.02 | 0.97 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| real health, put back before every cast (3 runs) | 1.078 | 1.163 | 1.226 | 1.301 | 1.347 |
| keep foes put back before every cast (3 runs) | 1.086 | 1.144 | 1.144 | 1.277 | 1.371 |
| standard ladder (3 runs) | 1.086 | 1.144 | 1.144 | 1.277 | 1.371 |

- Numbers changed from the approved table. As approved (6.5% / 3.75%, 3% a foe), the set measured 1.089 / 1.234 / 1.251 before its factors on a first pack (with the representative pack: Tidal Drain alone at 6% asked a factor of 0.88), so Tidal Drain is 5.8% / 3.5% and Accretion 2.5% a foe (its cap is 45% as approved). Every factor is now within 0.95-1.02, so no mastery's drain falls below the one before it. The fifth name, Collapse, was already Spatial Loop's; it is Last Light. The table gave Torn Earth 40% a rock; one rock a second is this build's.
- Tests: `tests/sgm-test.js` (41 rows, in run-all) checks the card and its numbers (the names used nowhere else), Mastery 0 unchanged (the 4s grip and the walker far down the road standing still, 15 a frame from 4s to 11.5s on every foe, flyers and bosses too, 5% / 3% of max health carrying only its source, nothing else, the hole's sphere, disk and heat, the contraction from 11.15s, the collapse's waves 7.5 / 9 / 14, nothing left behind, the lights graded back exactly, the preview waiting through the cast), each mastery with exact numbers (the grip at 3.2s and 8.3s; the hole 1.15 and the ring 1.35 with the lensing sphere's world size unchanged; 5.8% / 3.5%; each death feeding once, drained or not, the drain 15 x 1.025 ... and its cap at 1.45 with 22 fed; 8 rocks a foe 1s apart for 40, only while pinned; 500% once on every foe at the collapse, the contraction from 11s, the waves 9.75 / 11.7 / 18.2), the collapse's burst cut short by a cleared world or an ended game leaving nothing (it left 22 objects before), a direct cast at Mastery 0 whatever the account has, the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery (a second later, with its factor), and the mastery preview never casting a second Singularity over the first, with the lights back exactly.

## Wormhole: a hungrier mouth, and the last crossing (v473)
Space folds into twin wormholes on one road, one just ahead of the enemy's front and one 13 tiles back; whatever walks into the first is torn through the stars (400%) and thrown out of the second, until both collapse (300% round each). Simon, for every Mythic, Ancient and Secret scroll: "lets do the same way all the mythics ancients and secrets where their identity is preserved but their visuals barely change and change only to better"; and "new stuff CAN appear ... but just nothing that over shines the spell". Wormhole had no masteries before. These strengthen a part the wormholes already have and draw it a little richer; the one new piece, a small far mouth where a boss comes out, is a wormhole too. Spatial Loop owns sending foes back over and over, turning them round and marking them, so here each foe goes through once a cast, walks on the way it faced, and nothing is marked.

| Mastery | Part | Stronger | Drawn richer |
|---|---|---|---|
| 1 Hungry Mouth | the first mouth | it also draws in every foe within 1.6 tiles of it, on any road (before, only foes walking into it on its own road); a foe from another road comes out on the far mouth's road, and only if that sets it back | the mouth 30% wider, its swirl twice as thick and drawn from further out, motes drawn in from where each foe stood |
| 2 Held Open | how long it stays open | open 10.5s (9.2s), so more foes walk in; the Calm space setting covers it (11.8s) | the columns of light half again as tall, fading out above their old height, motes rising up them |
| 3 Torn Through the Stars | the crossing | 450% a crossing (400%), thrown 16 tiles back (13) | a comet of light (a thin core in a soft halo) runs the arc with each crossing, more stars on the arc, a wall of light rising at each exit |
| 4 No Weight Too Great | the boss rule | bosses go through too, once a cast (Resolve ignored, as for every setback of this spell), coming out 5 tiles behind the first mouth and still slowed 50% for 2s (before, only slowed) | the first mouth shudders; a small far mouth flickers open where the boss comes out; its comet runs a shorter, lower arc |
| 5 The Last Crossing | the collapse | as the mouths close, the first swallows every foe within 2.4 tiles of it that has not gone through and throws it out of the far one; both collapse for 350% (300%) within 2.4 tiles (1.6) | in its last half second the swirl pulls from 2.4 tiles out; a rush of streaks fans across the stars; the collapse half again as large (its shockwave and a wall of light racing out to 2.4) |

- How: `castWormhole` takes an optional set of numbers and looks for one cast (`XO`, over `WHL`); without it the cast is as before. `whmOpts` builds it from the mastery (cumulative: Mastery L has masteries 1..L), and the module (`WHM`) wraps `castWormhole` by name, as v447/v448 did. One `cross` function does every crossing (the road's, Hungry Mouth's, the swept one and the last), so each foe goes through once a cast by the same rules. `whPortal` takes an optional column height; without it the mouths are drawn as before.
- Hungry Mouth on other roads: a foe drawn in off the road it stands on comes out on the far mouth's road (`e.route`, `e.dist`, its block cleared), and only if that leaves it more road to the end than it had, on a road to the same place (never thrown forward, never sent from a village road to the keep's). Tested on board 777 at board version 5, where road 2 joins road 1: at Mastery 0 the other road's foes walk straight through the first mouth; at Mastery 1 they are drawn in and walk on along the far mouth's road.
- Moving foes and long frames (the lesson of Blood Needles, v466): the mouth's reach is a positional check, so each foe's way since the last frame is checked along its road in steps of at most 0.15 tiles, and a foe carried right over the first mouth in one long frame goes through too (every mastered cast). Mastery 0 keeps the old checks.
- The Last Crossing's foes are only thrown, not struck: the far mouth's collapse strikes them as it strikes the foes already out. They come out together, so each arrival's flash is softer and one wall of light rises for all of them (no white blot at the far mouth).
- The explosion rule: the collapse was already a shockwave with a wall of light and debris; from Mastery 3 each exit bursts as a rising wall of light with a fountain of motes, and Mastery 5's collapse adds a wall racing out to its reach. All see-through and gone in half a second.
- Every Wormhole cast stays open past its `cineLock` (3s): 9.6s, 10.9s from Held Open. So the mastery preview waits for it: `SMPV_BUSY.push(()=>fx.some(f=>f.whCast))`.
- Numbers changed from the approved table, all for the ladder. Built as approved, the walking measure gave 1.04 / 1.22 / 1.66 / 1.66 / 2.21 against 1.078 / 1.162 / 1.226 / 1.302 / 1.35, which needs factors down to 0.61. Held Open is 10.5s (12 approved): 12s alone added 18% where its step is 8%. Torn Through the Stars is 450% (550%): the crossing is nearly all of the spell's damage, so 550% added a third to everything. The Last Crossing's collapse is 350% (600%), and its swallowed foes take no crossing strike of their own: they still get the far mouth's collapse. Hungry Mouth, the 16 tiles, No Weight Too Great and the 2.4-tile reach are as approved.
- Ladder (Secret, half steps). The standard ladder's foes stand still, so at Mastery 0 nothing walks in. It strikes almost nothing, only the collapse on the few foes near the first mouth, and its ratios mean nothing (3.0 to 7.3 for Masteries 1-5).
  - So Wormhole has a walking-foe ladder (scratch `sec-wormhole/ladder-walk.js`). Foes walk the lead road into the first mouth at 1 tile/s, a Soldier's speed, with the board's marsh switched off. The hero is knocked out, so it neither blocks nor fights them. Every foe stands back where it started before each cast: the cooldown is 95s, so in play every cast meets fresh foes.
  - The pack is a column of 20 foes, 0.72 tiles apart (a wave's spawn gap at that speed), long enough that the longer opening never runs out of foes. The other measure is a lone foe. The weights are .6 / .4, with 3 casts each, 3 times over.
  - Damage is every point of health the foes lose: the crossings and the collapse.
  - Setback is the road each foe loses when it is thrown back, summed over a cast and averaged over the foes.
  - Mastery 1 wanders by about 0.01 between runs (1.079 and 1.091 on two runs): the exits' small random scatter decides which of the thrown foes the far mouth's collapse reaches.

| `SMG.wormhole` | M1 | M2 | M3 | M4 | M5 |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| factor | 1.038 | 1.029 | 0.971 | 1.032 | 0.939 |
| target | 1.078 | 1.162 | 1.226 | 1.302 | 1.35 |
| walking foes: damage | 1.079 | 1.162 | 1.225 | 1.302 | 1.351 |
| the same with the marsh on | 1.082 | 1.117 | 1.207 | 1.283 | 1.413 |
| with every factor at 1 | 1.039 | 1.129 | 1.262 | 1.262 | 1.438 |
| as approved, every factor at 1 | 1.047 | 1.224 | 1.656 | 1.656 | 2.209 |
| walking foes: setback (.6 / .4) | 1.006 | 1.119 | 1.429 | 1.428 | 1.497 |
| setback, column / lone foe (tiles a foe; M0 5.84 / 12.94) | 6.48 / 11.02 | 7.57 / 11.04 | 9.68 / 14.07 | 9.67 / 14.05 | 10.34 / 14.07 |

- How the setback was weighed.
  - `SMG` is set on the damage alone, as for every scroll.
  - The setback is the scroll's control, and no damage factor can scale it. It is measured and reported beside the damage, against Mastery 0 with the same .6 / .4 weights. It was weighed in the trims, not in F.
  - Hungry Mouth and Held Open add setback only by putting more foes through. The damage measure already counts each of those foes, so their setback climbs no faster than their damage: 1.01 / 1.12 against 1.08 / 1.16. A lone foe even loses a little less, 11 tiles instead of 13, because the mouth catches it 1.6 tiles before it reaches the mouth.
  - The 16-tile throw (Mastery 3) and bosses going through (Mastery 4, not on the measure) are setback alone. They are the heart of the scroll, so they were kept as approved and paid for in damage: Torn Through the Stars' strike was cut to 450%, and the swallowed foes take no crossing strike.
  - The damage therefore lands on the targets with every factor near 1, and no mastered strike is weaker than Mastery 0's (each crossing at least 400%, the last collapse at least 300%). From Mastery 3 the setback runs about 0.15-0.2 above the targets, which is the longer throw.
- Tests: `tests/whm-test.js` (47 rows, in run-all).
  - The card and its numbers; no mastered strike weaker than Mastery 0; the defaults table is the old cast; the names are their own.
  - Mastery 0 foe by foe against the spell's own rules: the mouths' places; each foe through once, 400% in the mouth, out 13 tiles back on its road, unblocked; the first as the lead reaches the mouth, none after 8.75s; the collapse at 8.75s, 300% within 1.6 of either mouth; a boss slowed 50% for 2s, not moved; nothing left behind. A cast with only a log makes the same crossings frame by frame.
  - Hungry Mouth: foes drawn in within 1.6, before the mouth, and the mouth 30% wider. On two roads, the other road's foes walk through at Mastery 0, and at Mastery 1 they are drawn in, come out on the far mouth's road, are let go by the hero's block, and walk on along it. Foes that would be thrown forward, or onto another place's road, are left. A foe carried past the edge of the reach in one long frame is drawn in, one 1.8 off is not, and a foe carried right over the mouth goes through.
  - Held Open: crossings until 10.05s, the collapse then, the end at 10.9s, Calm space 11.8s, the taller fading columns.
  - Torn Through the Stars: 450% and 16 tiles back, a comet and a burst per crossing, and a boss still only slowed.
  - No Weight Too Great: the boss through once (Unstoppable or not) to 5 tiles back, still slowed, not taken again when it walks back in; the mouth shudders and a small far mouth flickers where it lands.
  - The Last Crossing: the foes within 2.4 not yet sent swallowed and thrown with no strike; a foe sent before not taken again; 350% within 2.4 foe by foe, the swallowed among them; the rush of streaks and both walls; nothing left behind.
  - Echoes cast the old spell; the real `fireScroll` path at every mastery, with its factor in each crossing; the mastery preview waits for the cast.

## Six elemental heroes (v474)
Simon: "add more heros that have some sort of a specialization to a certain element, so a hero thats better with fire damage, another with nature, another with dark etc etc please so it would be 6 new heros please, price them similarily but no the same, based on their power but similar". A block before the boot line (search `Elemental specialists`) adds six 4-star spellcasters (`cl:'mage'`, they cast scrolls, 6 scroll slots), one for each element (`h.elem`):

| Hero | Element | Price | Power | Talent | Aura |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| Sylvara Thornwood, Grove Druid | nature | 2,600 | x2.5 | thorn bolts root a foe (20% chance, 0.6s, Resolve applies) | nearby towers +10% range |
| Isolde Rimeheart, Frost Sorceress | ice | 2,800 | x2.7 | bolts chill 30% slower for 1.5s | Frost Spires slow 10% more, towers +8% damage |
| Ignatia Emberveil, Pyromancer | fire | 3,000 | x2.9 | bolts set foes burning (25% of the hit a second for 2.5s) | Cannons and Wizard Towers +15% damage |
| Corvin Ashveil, Shadowcaller | dark | 3,100 | x3.0 | bolts wither (30% a second for 3s) | foes inside +8% damage taken, towers +6% damage |
| Vesper Stormborn, Storm Mage | lightning | 3,200 | x3.1 | bolts chain to 2 more foes (keeping 85% a jump) | Wizard Towers +1 chain jump and +10% attack speed |
| Seraphine Dawnveil, Lightbringer | light | 3,400 | x3.3 | every 6th bolt flares: 100% within 0.9 | towers +10% attack speed and +8% damage |

- **Pricing:** between Elowen (2,500, x2.4) and Thaddeus (3,500, x3.8), rising with power and all different, as Simon asked.
- **Element Mastery:** scrolls of their own element deal +30% and recharge 10% faster. It lives in `tsd`/`tsc` (the scroll damage and recharge factors, beside Aevorian's Temporal Sovereignty), so the cooldown bar follows (`ehMastery`).
- **Their attack:** a `heroFire` wrapper gives them `ehFire`: the mage's bolt and chain, but an orb of their element (`PJ.eh_<element>`: a glowing core, a halo and a tail, shared meshes) that hits as that element (`o.el`, so element reactions fire), a small 3D burst where it lands (`cfmBoom` in the fire, ice or void palette for fire, ice and dark; leaves for nature; sparks for lightning and light) and the touch of their element above. A rooted foe gets a ring of thorny vines (`ehRoot`). The light flare is a pale 3D blast.
- **Their looks:** `heroModel` hands its helpers to `ehModel` for them: Ignatia in red and char with a crown of flame, a burning hem and a flame-tipped staff; Isolde in white and ice blue with a crown and shoulders of ice crystals and a crystal staff; Vesper a white-bearded storm mage in a tall navy hat with lightning stitched on his robe and a forked staff; Sylvara in moss green with antlers, leaves and a staff wound in vines; Corvin in a deep horned hood with violet eyes and a skull staff; Seraphine in ivory and gold with a halo, small wings of light and a sun-disc staff. Each has motes of their element floating round them. Their cards carry a coloured element chip (`ehChip`) and border, and `heroCol` gives their element's colour.
- **Patch notes:** a "Six elemental heroes" section at the top of `NOTES_HTML`; `NOTES_V` is left as it is for whoever publishes the update.
- Tests: `tests/ehr-test.js` (25, in run-all as 'ehr'): six heroes, one per element; 4-star spellcasters with 6 slots; priced by power, all different, between Elowen and Thaddeus; aura, talent, thumbnail and a model of their own; the chip and colour; other heroes unchanged; Element Mastery +30% and 10% faster on their element only (and Ignatia's Catch Flame's cooldown); each shoots an orb of its element that hits as it; burning, chill, two extra chain jumps, wither, roots about one bolt in five, a flare every 6th light bolt; Elowen still shoots her violet bolt; each fights in the real loop without errors.

## Previews for the Mythic, Ancient and Secret masteries (v475)
Simon: "Why are the previews still not how they should be?", then "Yes, record them all". The 23 Mythic, Ancient and Secret scrolls (v451-v473) had no recorded clips, so their mastery cards fell back on the slow live preview. Now each has six clips (no mastery and Masteries 1 to 5): 138 more in `previews/`, made by `node tools/previews/record.js mythic ancient secret` on the stages in `tools/previews/scenarios.js`. They are developer mode only, like their masteries.

**Recorder changes** (`tools/previews/record.js`):
- `live`: the battle runs unpaused while it is stepped. The Ancient domains (`ancRun`) only advance while `state==='playing'&&!paused`, so the old paused stage froze them at their first frame.
- `st.gv`: a stage can build its board at another board version (`MAP_GV`). A foe can walk another road (`ri`, with `dshift` tiles along it).
- `crf` per clip: these clips are encoded at CRF 29 (the others 26). Their effects are heavier, and this keeps them about the size of the Epic clips.

**The stages.** The foes are tougher (60x spell power, up to 400x where a mastery needs them alive at the end). The tracking camera follows the action (v439), with a wider view for the domains that fill the board.

| Scroll | What the stages set up |
|---|---|
| Eclipse | 11 foes for None Escape; foes further back for The Sun Breaks |
| Ember Titan | foes far up the road for Deeper Fissure; an Ogre for Titan's Weight |
| Eternal Winter | an Ogre for Winter's Hold; seen from further back (the ice heart fills the board) |
| Aeon Dial | windows of the 20s dial; archer towers for Borrowed Years; worn-down foes and the window at midnight for Twelve Strokes |
| Gaia | foes behind walking into the standing trees for The Forest Stands |
| Seraph Lance | a ram for the great lance; 11 foes for the Host of Lances |
| Spatial Shatter | worn-down foes for Clean Break; a walking pack for Torsion |
| Vael'Thoruun | 11 foes for the current and the circuit; a walking pack for The Storm Follows |
| Abyss | windows of the 20s sea; the window when the eldest leviathan rises for The Third Leviathan |
| Citadel | spread foes for Wider Dominion; the window at the final fracture for The Final Order |
| Infinite Void | worn-down foes for Erasure; the last 3 seconds for Reality Returns |
| Eldritch Tree | worn-down foes for Swallowed; the window at the bloom for The World in Bloom |
| Never-Never Land | archer towers for Sylph's Hour; the window at the last fall for Rain of Stars |
| Primordial Tempest | 11 foes for The Storm Chooses; the window at the last thunder for Hurricane |
| Purgatory | the window at the last stage, the eyes, the executions and the last cut |
| Blood Needles | a ram for Bloodletting and Crimson Halo; one 1 HP foe, then foes walking in, for Patient Swarm |
| Eye of the Blood Moon | 11 foes for Wider Gaze; low-health foes for The Gaze Moves On |
| Moonfall | spread foes for Fuller Moon; foes far back for Moonbreaker's wave |
| Constellation | 11 foes for A Larger Figure; foes behind walking onto the lines for Burning Lines |
| Curse of the Blood Moon | spread foes for the Wider Seal |
| Pulsar | windows of the 15s star; a walking pack over the burnt ground for Scorched Ring; the window at the implosion for Supernova |
| Singularity | low-health foes for Accretion; foes further back for Last Light |
| Wormhole | board 777 at version 5 (a second road joins the first) for Hungry Mouth; an Ogre for No Weight Too Great; slow foes short of the mouth for The Last Crossing |

- The sky spells use a still, wide camera that looks up (`fixedCam`, `zoom`, `cz`): Singularity's hole, Constellation's figure, the Blood Moon's eye and the falling moon hang high over the field, and the tracking camera (which follows the ground) left them out of the picture. Singularity's hole settles in the upper part of the view the cast sees, so a moving camera lost it altogether.
- Each clip's moment was checked twice: in a run without pictures (every one of the 138 stages logs its mastery's event: a probe) and on its contact sheet.
- The recording was split across six cloud sessions (four browsers each) and merged here; `previews/manifest.json` lists all 432 clips (294 before).
- Known limits: Gaia's The Keep Drinks Deep heals the keep, which is off camera. Curse of the Blood Moon's Blood Price needs other great scrolls sealed on the hero, which the stage does not set up.
- Tests: `tests/mpv-test.js` (in run-all as 'mpv'): the 23 scrolls (8 Mythic, 7 Ancient, 8 Secret); a clip for every level and every file present; lengths 3-17 s; a caption on every mastery clip; every mastery clip logged its mastery at work, and no clip without one did; the changelog.

## The Primordial Tempest, a hurricane seen from above (v477)
Simon: "make the primordial tempest 10x better" (its visuals). From the game's camera the v465 storm read as a dim board, the sigil and a smudge of cloud on the far side of the hero (its eye wall is cut away on the camera's side). Now a hurricane fills the sky over the board. Looks only: `tpxBuild` uses no game random and hurts no foe, and the Tempest strikes exactly as before.

| Piece | What it does |
|---|---|
| The storm deck | One plane 5.6 over the board, its cloud drawn by the shader. Three spiral arms of storm cloud (fbm noise in a frame turned with the spin, a fixed log-spiral twist, a finer inner layer turning a little faster) round a clear eye, a dense overcast and a bright eye wall round it. Lit from above (the density a little toward the light), dark undersides. At most 70% opaque, so the board reads through it. |
| The eye frames the hero | The eye's centre is where the line from the camera to the hero crosses the deck, worked out every frame, so the lifted hero is always seen through it. |
| Lightning in the cloud | Each strike lights the cloud above it from within (8 flashes at once, each where the camera sees the strike through the deck), sheet lightning lights the whole deck, and thin filaments crawl along the arms. |
| The wind and rain sheet | Fast streaks swirling into the eye, 2.4 over the ground. |
| Strikes | Every bolt that lands leaves a flare of light rising off the ground and a glowing violet scorch (pools of 16 each). |
| The hero | A bright core of light and a halo while the hero is lifted. |
| The storm's life | It gathers (the eye closes from 7 to 2.3 tiles as the arms are drawn in), flashes white and spins up at the crown bolt, winds tight and bright and spins three times as fast before the last thunder, and blows outward and clears at the fall. |
| Storm clouds | Simon: "make the lightnings strike from translucent clouds like in stormcaller but make it a tiny bit more cloudy". 7 dark, see-through storm clouds in a ring over the eye wall (9 with the bands across the field, 1 more for the full hurricane), in the Stormcaller's storm greys (0x1b1f27 to 0x3a404c): low-poly puffs, flat shaded, 8-12 a cloud merged into one mesh (one draw a cloud), opacity 0.08-0.16 (the Stormcaller's 0.13-0.26 times CLOUD.op 0.45 is 0.06-0.12), no glow at rest, fading near the camera. They wheel round the eye. Every bolt that comes down leaves from the cloud over its target (the volleys, the finale, the Gathering Storm's called bolts, the crown bolt from the eye wall's cloud), its leader too; the cloud glows violet as it charges and flashes from inside as it fires. A target with no cloud over it draws the nearest free cloud over it while the bolt charges; foes close together share one. The view stays clear: a cloud thins away (to 8%) while the ground the camera sees through it holds a foe or the road, its flash still showing as it fires. Measured at the game camera (board 777, 3 s / 7 s / 11 s after the cast, pixels changed by more than 60/765): 0.1% / 3.3% / 2.8% (Mastery 5: 0.5% / 2.6% / 8.0%); a first try, 16 pale clouds, changed 29-30%. Their layout uses a seeded random of its own. The deck is thinner (at most 52% opaque, the dense ring round the eye lighter). |
| The masteries | Mastery 2's bands across the whole field: the arms reach across the whole board. The full hurricane (Mastery 5): denser, faster, more lightning in the cloud. |

- Hooks in `castTempest`: `tpxBuild` beside `tpEye`, `TX.step` each frame, `TX.strike` in `hit`, `TX.crown` and `TX.fall` in those bolts, `TX.end` when the storm ends.
- The 6 preview clips of the Tempest are re-recorded with it.
- Tests: `tests/tpx-test.js` (17, in run-all as 'tpx'): the storm clouds (7-10, opacity 0.08-0.16, see-through, fading near the camera, thinning away over a foe) and the bolts coming down from them; looks only (no random, no damage); nothing before the cast; the deck, the sheet and the core in the storm's group; it gathers and its eye closes; the eye frames the hero through the deck; the core; strikes light it; it winds tight and spins faster before the last thunder; it blows out at the fall and is gone after; the full hurricane is wider; it renders through a whole cast; the changelog. tempest, tpeye, tpm, ancient and perf pass.


## v476: gold buildings learn by their gold

Simon: "we have to rework how money earning buildings gain masteries, they should get masteries by how much money they produce".

- **The rule:** the Gold Mine, the Windmill and the Merchant's Post (`GOLD_T`) earn 1 XP for every `MAST_GOLD` = 10 gold they pay out. That is the gold that adds to a building's own tally (`t.earned`), whatever pays it:

  | Source | Where it is paid | Buildings |
  |---|---|---|
  | Income at each wave call, with its share of the interest | `callWave` | all three |
  | The bounty: the extra gold foes slain in its range drop | `kill` (`MERCH_HIT`) | Merchant's Post |
  | Mastery payouts after a cleared wave (Gold Rush, Mother Lode, Harvest Festival, Strongbox, Royal Treasury, Golden Caravan) | `mxPay` | all three |

- **Before:** like every support building they earned a flat `MAST_SUP` = 4 XP for each wave they stood through, however much they made. The Bell Tower, which makes no gold, still does.
- **How it is counted:** the v476 module wraps `callWave`, `kill` and `mxPay`. Each wrapper reads the gold buildings' tallies before and after the call and turns the difference into XP through `mastGain`, so nothing is counted twice and a resumed run's restored tallies are never counted again. `mastGain` gives XP only in play, as for every building. The support-XP hook skips `GOLD_T`.
- **The rate:** a new Windmill or Merchant's Post makes about 40 gold a wave, so it earns 4 XP a wave, the same as before; upgraded, it learns faster. Gold per wave, read from `tStats` (no Armory enhancement):

  | Special part | 0 | 5 | 10 | 15 | 20 |
  |---|---|---|---|---|---|
  | Gold Mine | 18 | 35 | 71 | 142 | 286 |
  | Windmill | 39 | 72 | 133 | 245 | 452 |
  | Merchant's Post (before its bounty) | 40 | 80 | 162 | 325 | 655 |

  An un-upgraded Gold Mine learns at about half its old pace (1.8 XP a wave), but a player can build five.
- **Card:** the mastery card of a gold building says it grows with the gold it makes, 1 XP for every 10 gold; the Merchant's Post's card also names its bounty. Changelog entry and a patch-notes section added (`NOTES_V` unchanged).
- **Saved XP:** XP already earned stays; nothing is migrated.
- **Tests:** `tests/gmx-test.js` (15 rows, in run-all as 'gmx'):
  - each payout source turns into XP exactly (wave income, interest share, bounty in range and none out of range, a Mastery 4 Mother Lode);
  - the Bell Tower keeps 4 XP a wave and the gold buildings get no flat XP;
  - a restored tally is not counted again, and there is no XP outside a run;
  - over five waves each type's XP equals its buildings' gold / 10;
  - kill XP is unchanged, and the cards say the right thing.

  `tests/mastery-test.js` now uses the Bell Tower as its support building.


## Elite looks, one builder per modifier (v479)
A refactor with no change to how anything looks or plays. It lets each elite modifier's look be reworked on its own branch.
- `eliteDress(type,affs,body,variant)` builds each modifier through `ELD[affix](C)`, where `C={type,variant,affs,B,g,fx,fz,tw,plate,rivets,body}`. Both callers now pass the unit's variant.
- `ELD.armored` uses `ARMOR_D[type](C)` when a unit has a design of its own. Otherwise it uses `armorClass(C)`, the old class templates.
- `ELA[affix](fx,t,dt,moving,e)` animates the gear; `e` is the foe, or null in the Bestiary. `ELP[affix](e,fx,dt)` emits the in-game particles.
- `eliteRing(a,i)` makes the marker under the foe.
- **Tests:** `tests/elb-test.js` (in run-all as 'elb'):
  - every Bestiary unit takes every modifier it can roll, in game and in the Bestiary, without an error;
  - an `ARMOR_D` entry is used and is told the variant;
  - the class template still dresses a unit that has no design of its own.

## Armoured, one design per troop (v482)
Simon: "armoured should not just be a template but rather a seperate design for every unit that can be armored".
- `ARMOR_D.peasant`, `.soldier`, `.rider`, `.raven`, `.ram` and `.wolf` each build their own armour, per variant (`C.variant`; the wolf by alpha or not). Knights and monks still never roll Armoured, and the modifier still gives +0.25 armour.
  - **Peasants** (improvised): Farmer: a cooking pot over the straw hat, a padded jack with a ploughshare and scrap nailed on, a barn-door shield, slat shin guards. Woodcutter: a barrel-stave cuirass in iron hoops, an iron skullcap over the hood, a log-round shield. Torchbearer: a smith's apron with horseshoes, a pail helm, a barrel-lid shield, a bracer on the torch arm. Elder: a rusted, dented heirloom breastplate, a kettle hat, a mail shawl, knee cops. Stable Lad: studded saddle leather, a copper barber's basin, a cart-wheel shield. Goodwife: a colander helm, pot lids on a quilted apron, a frying pan. Gem Carrier: a looted gold-edged breastplate, a crested morion, a gilt shield on his back. Scroll Bearer: book-board armour with brass corners, a studded cap, a lectern board on his back, parchment bracers.
  - **Soldiers**: Spearman: a red brigandine with rivet rows, a gorget, a broad kettle hat with a comb and a mail aventail, spaulders, splinted vambraces, knee cops and greaves. Swordsman: a mail hauberk, a coat of plates under the tabard, a nasal helm with cheek plates and a coif. Axeman: laced lamellar coat and skirt, a spangenhelm with a mail aventail, lamellar shoulder guards. Gem Carrier: gilded parade plate with a sunburst, crowned pauldrons, a crested gilt helm with a plume. Scroll Bearer: a blue jack, a breastplate with a gold device, a sallet.
  - **Riders**: chanfron with eye guards, crinet lames down the neck, peytral with bosses, crupper, flanchards and guards on each moving leg; the rider gets a breastplate, pauldrons, cuisses and a helm. Scout in boiled leather, Lancer in bright steel and a plumed great helm, Raider in black scale with spikes and a masked helm, Gem Courier in gilt, Scroll Courier in mail and a sallet.
  - **Ravens**: a riveted beak cap, a helm cap with a brow guard and crest, a feather-scale mantle, talon guards and scale lames on each wing; plus a crest (Raven), a mail hood (Hooded Crow), red spikes (Blood Raven), a gilded claw-cage round the gem (Gem Thief), a harness with a bronze buckle (Scroll Thief).
  - **Rams**: iron sheets on both roof planes with rivet rows and a ridge cap (gaps for the gem cradle and the banner pole), riveted bands on the posts and rails, side mantlets (not on the Spiked Ram, whose shields hang there), a riveted sleeve and flanges on the head, a front mantlet, studded iron tyres on the wheels. Bronze for the Ram's Head, blackened iron and roof spikes for the Spiked Ram, gilt rivets for the Gem Ram.
  - **Wolf**: a chain-mail barding coat (rows of rings with a leather trim) with a riveted spine plate, a spiked collar, a helm plate with ear guards on the moving head, greaves on the four legs. The alpha's is blackened with bone-white spikes.
- **Fit**: every piece is placed from the kit's own numbers (torso, head, arm and leg pivots, the horse's head and neck, the wheel and wing pivots) and measured from the body mesh by `arxMeas` (the head top, leaving out pompoms and feathers, and the chest depth), so it fits the Highlands kits as well. The elder's armour follows his hunch.
- **Moving parts**: `peasantModel`, `unitModel` and `tgWolfModel` are wrapped to record their pivots in `b.userData.arxL`. Pieces for an arm, leg, wing, horse leg, wheel or the wolf's head are parented to that pivot, so they cannot drift; they leave with the dress (its `removed` event), which the Bestiary relies on.
- **Performance**: `arxDress` builds a design once per (type, variant, body mesh) into `ARX_CACHE` (29 builds cover every Plains variant) and merges it, with vertex colours, into at most 2 meshes per part (`ARX_MET` steel, `ARX_SOFT` leather and wood), shared by every foe. Averages per foe: peasant 988 triangles in 5.8 meshes, soldier 1900 in 6.8, rider 1340 in 6.2, raven 1308 in 3.2, ram 4439 in 5, wolf 4108 in 7 (the old template used up to 13 separate meshes). Nothing is allocated per frame.
- An artefact that strips Armoured off a foe (`artStrip`) now hides its armour.
- **Tests**: `tests/arx-test.js` (in run-all as 'arx'): every variant (Plains and Highlands kits, both wolves) uses its own design with no class-template pieces, 1-2 static meshes and pieces on its moving parts, and +0.25 armour; within each troop the variants differ; caching and shared materials; pieces stay on their pivots over a walk cycle while the pivots turn; cleanup on `removeEnemy`, `clearWorld` and the Bestiary cycle; stripping; knights and monks; every arena. `tests/elb-test.js` now puts the soldier's design back after its template check.
- **Sheets**: `tests/arx-shots.js OUTDIR close|game|gal` makes the close-up, in-game and Bestiary sheets.

## Armoured bosses, one design each (v483)
Simon: "armoured should not just be a template but rather a seperate design for every unit that can be armored". Every boss that can roll the Armoured modifier (from wave 100, half of the time, never a milestone boss) has its own `ARMOR_D[type]`:

| Boss | Armour |
|---|---|
| Dragon, Twin Drake, Brood Mother, Pale Elder | scale-plate barding, peytral, lamellar neck collars on every neck, a helm per dragon (brow spike, strapped skull plate, coronet, bronze blades), chin guards, wing-arm guards and couters, tail rings, a spade sheath |
| Ironclad Twin Drake, Armoured Brood Mother | the same in gilded overplates over their own steel (a gilded crest fin; a golden face mask with swept horns on obsidian) |
| Ogre | Brute: scrap (cauldron helm, barrel-lid, pot-lid pauldron); Chieftain: skull-bossed plate, barred visor, spiked pauldrons, greaves; Scarred: riveted slabs, one-slit bucket helm, chain round the arm; the Gem Carrier keeps his crown bare |
| Kikimora | bark slabs lashed with vine, bone ribs, horned skull-cap, bark greaves and bone bands on all eight legs |
| Stone and Runebound Golem | riveted iron bands, rune plates bolted into the stone (copper runes; cyan runes framing the sigil) |
| Spined Behemoth | plates laid between its spines, brow and snout plates, greaves and pauldrons on all four legs |
| Arachne | chitin capping plates with silver edges and her hourglass repainted, greaves on every leg, gorget |
| Colossus, Dread Knight, Void Knight | darker, heavier plate in their own style (visored helm and siege pauldrons; red-rune blackened plate; obsidian with violet seams) |
| Mire Wyrm, Armoured Mire Wyrm, Wyrmfiend | per-segment hoops: rusted iron split round the spines, a gilded crest over steel, bone-iron with magma seams plus greaves and wing guards |
| Ophanim, Seraphim | gilded bands on every wheel rim between the eyes, a collar round the great eye, steel feather-plates edged in gold |

How it works: `arbKit(key, build)` builds each design once as one merged vertex-coloured geometry per moving part and material (four shared materials: steel, gilded, matte, glowing). `arbDress(C, kit, targets)` puts each part's mesh on the model's own bone (wing, neck/head group, jaw, tail segment, leg or arm pivot, snake segment, wheel, wing flap), found through wrappers on `enemyModel` and `unitModel` (`ARB_M`). Pieces not in the dress group leave with it (a `removed` listener), so the Bestiary elite button takes them off. 23 kits, 5 to 29 extra meshes per boss. Tests: `tests/arb-test.js`; sheets: `tests/arb-shots.js`.

## Regenerating variants, the Shielded bubble and 3D elite markers (v484)
Simon: "fix all elite modifiers ... regenerating should have more variants and add some more features".
- **Regenerating, five looks.** Each foe gets one, picked from its spawn (a run serial hashed with the unit; the Bestiary's Elite button steps through all five, and the label names it, e.g. "Elite: Regenerating (Troll-blood)").
  - Verdant: moss, leaves and sprouts with glowing buds that shrink when it is hurt and grow back as it heals.
  - Troll-blood: warty hide, scars and glowing wounds that gape when hurt and knit shut as it heals, with faint steam.
  - Blessed: a slowly turning ring of golden runes and motes round the chest.
  - Blood-leech: dark veins and a heart-stone pulsing with a heartbeat (0.85 a second, 1.5 while healing).
  - Fungal: glowing caps on the shoulders and back, and spore puffs.
- **Fitted to every unit.** `rgxSurf(b,key)` reads the triangles of the unit's three biggest solid meshes (cached per model) and samples points on them by area; gear is placed along the surface normal on the mesh's own parent, so it moves with animated parts. Static gear is merged per parent, cached per model and look, and shared by every foe of that kind. Glow levels are 12 shared materials per look.
- **The heal shows.** Every 3% of max health healed (or every 1.25 s of a smaller heal) a soft additive shell of the model's own meshes pulses for 0.75 s, at most 0.18 opacity, scaled to the heal, with a few particles.
- **Counterplay.** Fire damage (`o.el==='fire'`) and any burn set `e.rgSup=2`: no regeneration of any kind (elite or Starfall) for 2 s after the last fire hit or the end of the burn. The update line reads `if(e.regen&&!(e.rgSup>1e-6))`. The look sears meanwhile. The heal is unchanged (2.5% a second).
- **Shielded.** An ellipsoid bubble fitted to the model's bounds, drawn with one shared hex-cell shader (rim, faint twinkle). Hits ripple from the attacker's side (at most one new ripple per 0.1 s, two at once, 0.45 s). Cracks show under 50% and 25%. Breaking it shows a 0.09 s crack-up, then 20 curved glass shards (80 on a big bubble) that fly off and shrink in 0.55 to 0.85 s. A bubble removed any other way (the split, a strip) shatters too.
- **Markers.** `eliteRing(a,i)` is a bevelled band 0.022 high with a motif per modifier and an additive glow ring. The geometry is shared per modifier and slot. Each foe has its own band and glow material, so `en.aura.material.opacity`, `.visible` and `.scale` still work; `removeEnemy` now also disposes the second marker's material and both glows.
- Draw calls with 30 elites on the road: 315 (was 400).
- **Tests:** `tests/rgx-test.js` (in run-all as 'rgx'): every look on every unit, the pick from the spawn, the exact heal and the 2 s fire and burn stops, the pulse and sear, the ripple, cracks, shatter and clean-up, the marker API, the other lands and the Bestiary.


## Three new elite modifiers, and Invisible reviewed (v485)
Simon asked to "fix all elite modifiers" and to "add some more features". One module, just before `Performance (v446)`; all numbers live in `NMX`.
- **Splitting** (`NMX.min.splitting` 15, never on bosses): a glowing pink seam, stitched shut, traced around the model's midline. On `kill` it tears in two (`nmxSplit`): 2 copies of the same unit and variant, 0.7 scale, 30% of its max HP each, no modifiers, 25% of a plain troop's gold and XP, at most 1 life, on its road at its distance, wave tag kept. A jagged rift of light opens (`nmxTear`) while the halves lean apart and settle over 0.42 s. Halves never split again, never roll as Gem Carriers or Scroll Bearers, and a foe that leaks or is stripped does not split.
- **Blinking** (from 25): `e.nmxBlink.t` counts down from 1.6 to 4 s, then every 4 s it blinks 1.25 tiles (`TS`) forward (`nmxBlink`): an after-image of the model, a small wire implosion, a streak and a landing ring. It is untargetable (`e.nmxUT`, filtered in `inRange`) for 0.18 s. A stun, hold, freeze, hero block, lift, Turned Around, the taiga sleep or the Singularity stops the charge, which then waits at least 0.6 s. No blink with less than 0.75 tiles of road left. The crystal brightens, the motes speed up and sparks gather in the last second, so the blink can be seen coming.
- **Warded** (from 40): `e.nmxWard.el` is the element of the last elemental hit; hits of that element take 40% less. A new element shifts the ward at most once every 0.4 s (damage-over-time ticks never shift it); physical damage and magic without an element are never warded. The orbiting rune stone and the circlet gem take the element's colour, with a faceted flash shell on each shift. The Bestiary cycles the elements by itself.
- **Looks fitted by rays:** each look is built by `ELD[affix]`, animated by `ELA`, with particles from `ELP`. The gear is placed by casting rays against the unit's own triangles (`nmxTris`, `nmxHit`), so the seam, crystal and circlet sit on every model, armour included. The geometry is merged into 1 to 3 meshes and cached per model shape (`NMX_G`); materials are shared (`NMX_M`).
- **Rolls:** the elite chance is unchanged. `nmxOk`/`nmxFix` replace a modifier a foe may not have (too early, Splitting or Invisible on a boss, Armoured on a knight or monk), in `buildWave` and in `spawnEnemy` (formations too). A spawn or wave item with `invOK` is decided (or forced by the developer tools and tests) and is left as it is. Invisible keeps its own odds.
- **artStrip** takes the new modifiers off with their gear and effect.
- **Invisible, reviewed:** `artGhostMat` is now a rim-lit shimmering cloak (a shared shader per material) instead of the grey see-through clone. `invisLook` cloaks every part on each call, so gear added after the spawn (the formation's tower shield and banner) is cloaked too, and hides lines, sprites, instanced and additive parts while unseen. The marker dims when Invisible is the second modifier. Old Tobin, the melee hero, no longer blocks or hits an unseen foe. Revealing it and losing it again both show.
- **Saves:** foes are not saved; the run is saved when a wave is called, so a resumed run rolls that wave again.
- **Tests:** `tests/nmx-test.js` (run-all 'nmx', 53 rows): each mechanic with its numbers, roll rules and rates, artStrip, the Bestiary cycle, all six lands and the world map, the Invisible fixes, cleanup and no errors.

## Swift ribbons as cloth, Giant review (v481)
Simon: "fix all elite modifiers, the waves behind swift should actually sway in the correct direction and sway more realistically".
- **The bug:** the old ribbons were rigid planes on pivots turned by `rotation.x=-1.15`. A plane hanging down (-y), turned that way, points to the model's front (+z, the way `posAt`'s angle faces it), while the pivots sat on its back. So the ribbons flapped forward through the body.
- **Now:** each ribbon is a verlet chain of `SWX.N`=5 segments, simulated in world space (`swxStep`):
  - the root node is pinned to a tie point on the unit, or on an animated part (a dragon's tail tip, a wyrm's head and tail; `swxTie`);
  - the other nodes feel gravity, quadratic air drag normalised by the foe's own full speed (`SWX.K`), a little lift, and a flutter wave that runs from root to tip, faster with speed. Follow-the-leader length constraints, a bend smoothing and a ground floor hold them in shape;
  - so the ribbons trail opposite the real frame-to-frame motion, swing outward at a road corner, and hang and settle when the foe is stopped or stunned. In the Bestiary a breeze lifts them, and turning the model swings them;
  - the strips are written with a helical twist that grows to the tip, so they read as cloth from the side as well as from above; the tips are swallowtails, or pennant points on the ram.
- **Cost:**
  - one mesh per foe (13 vertices and 11 triangles per ribbon); every foe with the same ribbon count shares one index buffer;
  - the buffers are updated in place, with no allocation per frame; about 11 microseconds per foe per frame;
  - the records are pooled. `removeEnemy`, `clearWorld`, the Bestiary dress (`galElite`, `closeVar`) and the Artificer's strip return them to the pool, and a reused mesh gets the plain cloth back after an arena recoloured it.
- **Per unit (`SWX_AT`, else `swxHum` from the measured body):**
  - humanoids: a headband, its two knot tails and two back streamers;
  - rider: a sash, two mane streamers and a tail ribbon. Raven: two tail streamers. Ram: two pennants on poles. Wolf: a neckerchief;
  - bosses: two big streamers that fit each one, plus one on the tail of the dragons, the beast and the wyrms (tied to the tail node), and on arachne's abdomen.
- **Dust:** `ELP.swift` kicks dust backward, opposite the motion, sized per unit (`SWX_DUST`), with none for flyers.
- **Giant:**
  - health bar, hit height, shadow blob and marker rings all scale with the model, on every unit (checked);
  - the Artificer's strip used to leave the 1.12 width, the 1.5x hit height, the extra life, the big blob and the rings: `artStrip` is wrapped to give them back;
  - the Bestiary keeps 1.3, because 1.5 crops the head off the stand;
  - a walking giant now has footfalls (`gntStep`, on the low point of the walk bob): a ring of dust puffs and a faint dark ground ring, one ring mesh per giant, reused. There are none when stunned, flying or on wheels.
- **Tests:** `tests/swx-test.js` (in run-all as 'swx') covers:
  - the tips against the velocity on every road heading and through a corner;
  - streaming at full and half speed, and hanging when stunned;
  - every unit in game and in the Bestiary;
  - no allocation per frame, cleanup, pooling and shared index;
  - the Swift and Giant strips, the dust direction and the footfalls;
  - the five arenas.
- **Sheets:** `tests/swx-shots.js` is the screenshot helper.

## Five new formations, and a Formations page in the Bestiary (v480)
Simon asked to "add 5 more formations" and "add some more features". The module sits right before `Performance (v446)` in src/kingsroad-keep.html (search `v480:`); its numbers are in `FM5`.

| Formation | From wave | Members | Mechanic | Counterplay |
|---|---|---|---|---|
| Peasant Mob (`mob`) | 6 | 9 peasants (10 from wave 12), each with a raised torch | each member slain makes the rest 5% faster, up to +30% (6 deaths); torches flare, embers, a "Frenzy +N%" label | kill them together |
| Raven Murder (`murder`) | 8 | a crested blood raven (tier 3, 1.18x size) and 7 ravens in a V (0.8x size), joined by a line of light | while the lead lives, a hit on a V raven is shared: it takes 40%, the other 60% is split evenly over the rest of the V (never the lead). When the lead goes the flock scatters: 45% slower for 1.6s, veering aside, no more sharing | kill the lead, or use splash (the share does not weaken it) |
| Holy Procession (`procession`) | 16 | 3 wardens (white tabards), a friar holding a gilded reliquary overhead (1.6x HP and gold), 2 hooded monks with censers | a pool of light (radius 1.35) under the reliquary: every 0.5s every foe in it (not bosses, not the bearer) heals 1.5% max HP. The procession's monks do not cast their own heals. When the bearer falls the reliquary drops and the light goes out | focus the bearer |
| Pike Phalanx (`phalanx`) | 18 | 9 soldiers in a 3x3 block; pikes level, at 30 degrees and upright by rank | while 5 or more stand: push-backs (`pushE`) do nothing and slows on them are halved. Below 5 it breaks: pikes go up, the block loosens | kill 5, or use damage rather than control |
| Knight's Wedge (`wedge`) | 25 | 5 knights in an arrowhead; the tip has a gilded crown, a crimson plume and cloak; a gold chevron lies under it | while 2 or more flank knights stand the tip takes 60% less damage (`formDR`). When the tip falls the rest charge for 3s (1.7x speed, as the Rider Charge) | break the flanks first, or kill the tip and be ready |

- Hooks: `formLayout` and `spawnFormation` are wrapped for the new ids (`FM5_LAY`, `fm5Spawn`); `fm5Gear(m,id,role,type)` builds the hand-made gear (merged, cached geometries in `FM5.geo`) on a foe or a Bestiary model. `update` runs `fm5Tick` on every live formation; `removeEnemy` and `kill` fire the moments (`fm5Lost`, `fm5Slain`); `hurt` shares the V's hits; `formDR` gives the tip its cut; `pushE` and `moveEnemy` give the phalanx its hold. `clearWorld` drops everything quietly.
- Per formation in the scene: one V line, one light pool or one chevron (each one mesh, its material disposed when the formation is gone). No lights, no per-frame allocation.
- The wedge comes from wave 25 (with the Siege Column), not 22: at 6573 HP it is the heaviest formation.
- Rates: `buildWave` is unchanged and `rollFormation` picks evenly among the open formations. `__kk.formStats(w,3000)` before / after: wave 8 0.36 / 0.34 per wave, wave 15 0.35 / 0.34, wave 22 0.34 / 0.36, wave 28 0.56 / 0.53, wave 35 0.86 / 0.85, wave 60 0.83 / 0.83; from wave 25 each of the nine is about 1/9 of them.
- Power at wave 30 (members, total HP, total gold): Shield Wall 5, 2888, 158; Rider Charge 6, 1850, 171; Peasant Mob 10, 1694, 209; Raven Murder 8, 1909, 197; Royal Escort 5, 3225, 173; Holy Procession 6, 3469, 218; Pike Phalanx 9, 3811, 238; Knight's Wedge 5, 6573, 282; Siege Column 5, 5442, 235. HP and gold per member are those of normal foes (the bearer 1.6x, the lead raven is a tier-3 raven).
- Bestiary: the Formations button on the Bestiary bar (an icon on narrow screens) opens nine dioramas on plinths (`fm5GalOpen`), using the variant viewer's panel and arrows: name, "From wave N", the member count and what it does. They idle and turn slowly; drag to turn them. Back returns to the Bestiary.
- Tests: `tests/fm5-test.js` (layouts, gear, every mechanic with its numbers, rates, elites, every arena, the world map, cleanup, the page).


## The Tempest's winds, smoothed (v487)
Simon: "The inside winds of the tempest are cut, look unfinished, can you smooth it out more please?"
- **The cause:** the inner face of the eye wall (the open cone `wface`/`wfM` in `tpEye`).
  - Its cloud was a thresholded fbm (smoothstep .34 to .64) read in an angle measured from the camera. It came out as torn, hard-edged blotches.
  - The camera-side cut then left only two chunks of it.
- **The change, in that shader only:**
  - The noise wraps round the circle (N=7 cells, world angle), so the wall has no seam anywhere.
  - It is stretched into long wind streaks that climb as they turn (slant .32 a tile), with a domain warp to keep them uneven.
  - A thin veil (24%) lies between the streaks, so the wall reads as one sheet of wind, with finer wind lines on top for the light.
  - It fades into the camera-side cut more softly: smoothstep -.12 to .62 (it was -.05 to .5).
  - Its overall opacity is 0.8 of before, so the veil does not thicken the wall.
- The puffs, the cloud bands, the rain bands, the storm deck and the storm clouds are untouched. So are all the numbers and the timing.
- **Tests:** tpeye 38/38, tempest 43/43, tpm 36/36, tpx 17/17, ancient 46/46, perf 34/34. The 6 tempest preview clips are re-recorded.
